# Queue

- **`scripts/agent.mjs` is at 1,499 of its 1,500 lines** (2026-10-23, PR #961's review). An operation added to it now has to move code out first. Its carriers (`openWebUnlocked`, `openStdio`, `openIOS`) or the session object's input methods (`tap`, `type`) are each a file's worth: move one out, as `agent-inspect.mjs`, `agent-keys.mjs` and `agent-launch.mjs` were.
- **`__exact_gate_decode` goes when `VideoDecoder` comes** (2026-10-23, LLP 1133.003 §15 stage 0). The throughput gate's stub decode is a global under the agent only, natively (`js/src/binary.js`, installed by `js/src/prelude.js`; bytes door op 8, `js/src/crypto.rs`) and on the web JS target (`host/web-js/agent.js`), called by `apps/stream-fixture/app.ts`. Stage 1 (the web) and stage 2 (Apple) replace it with `@exact/webcodecs`'s `VideoDecoder.decode`; delete the global, op 8 and the fixture's declaration then, and point the fixture and `gate.mjs` at the module's names. Until then a native production binary launched with `EXACT_AGENT=1` has the stub too: the runtime knows the agent flag, not the build's trust.
- **The root workspace needs Snapback's private repository since Harmony moved in** (2026-10-22, a Linux box with no GitHub access to `expo/snapback`). c7522f3002 made `apps/harmony/app/{web,apple,linux}` root members, so the root `Cargo.lock` carries `snapback4-core` and `snapback4-device` (through `exact-snapback4`, which `app-apple` and `app-linux` depend on) from `git+https://github.com/expo/snapback.git?rev=3653cae8…`, and every cargo command in the root workspace (the gate's first check included) fails on a machine that cannot fetch it: "revision 3653cae… not found … failed to authenticate". `snapback4/Cargo.toml` says those sources resolve "outside exact2's root lock" and is excluded for that reason, and `docs/reference.md` says root Cargo commands need no Snapback access. Either keep Harmony's crates out of the root workspace (`exclude`, a lock of their own as `snapback4/` has) or accept that the gate needs that access; with the commit in a local Snapback clone, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=1 GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0=url.file://<clone>.insteadOf GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0=https://github.com/expo/snapback.git CARGO_NET_GIT_FETCH_WITH_CLI=true cargo fetch --locked` seeds the cache once. Even then a clean checkout's workspace check (`cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, ceeb9276a3) fails Harmony's three build scripts: `snapback4/ts/web.ts(18,39): error TS2307: Cannot find module './generated/device.ts'`, which `snapback4/ts/.gitignore` ignores and only `snapback4/web/build.mjs` writes, though c7522f3002 committed `apps/harmony/snapback/generated/` so that a clean checkout's workspace would build.
- **iOS and the Mac: scroll anchoring doesn't register a box's `popover` prop set or cleared as a change into or out of flow** (2026-10-22, the popover out-of-flow change's round 3, Astra). `ScrollAnchoring.Changes.note` (host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/ScrollAnchoring.swift) suppresses an anchor correction when a box becomes or stops being out of flow; it reads `NodeView.layoutPosition`'s static form with the box's popover flag, but only on a style change, and a popover is out of flow by its prop (the kernel's top layer), so opening a popover whose prop is set in the same batch as a sibling moves can make one unwanted correction. Linux's `out_of_flow` reads the prop. The fix: note a popover prop change as a positioned change.
- **The top layer as a computed-style default, (B)** (2026-10-22, theme2 10:42). How a box is positioned for layout is one accessor today: `style::top_layer::layout_position` in the kernel (`of` over rows and props), `NodeRef::layout_position` on the Rust hosts, `NodeView.layoutPosition` in Swift, with `scripts/position-reads.test.mjs` holding every layout read to it. (B) would make the rule part of the computed style instead: a structural UA default in `arena/defaults.rs` (`structural_starts`) giving a popover `position: absolute` unless its author wrote one, so `effective_style` and what the kernel publishes from it say absolute. The accessor is the seam: (B) changes its body and the allowlist, not its callers. Owed with it: the rows sent to hosts would carry the default, so the web export must skip it to keep the browser's UA `fixed`, and own-style readers (`arena.style`) need the same audit. Kernel change: 3 rounds. With it, a compile-time guard in place of `scripts/position-reads.test.mjs`'s source scan (theme2 12:59): the authored row reachable only through a named accessor, so no read of it compiles elsewhere.
- **PRIORITY: an open popover's containing block on kernel hosts is its positioned ancestor, not the viewport** (2026-10-22, theme2 08:59; the popover out-of-flow change, fa4343f928). HTML's top layer sizes and places a popover against the viewport; the kernel lays it out `position: absolute` in its parent, so a popover with no width, or a percentage one, sizes inside its nearest positioned ancestor (or the root), and its static position there trims its available width. Repro: a 100-point `position: relative` box holding a `popover` with `width: 50%` lays out 50 points wide on iOS and the Mac, 50% of the viewport on the web; a kernel test in that shape (`cover_tests.rs`) fails once the rule is in. A first fix (hoisting top-layer boxes to the root in `layout/hoist.rs`, vendored Taffy patch 63) was built and dropped after round 2 (Astra): hoisted ancestors must precede descendants in the hoist list, the root is not the viewport (an authored root size), the static position still trims the width, top-layer overflow still unions into the root's and the hosts' scroll extents, and a content region's trial owns its descendants. The fix needs a top-layer pass of its own: each open popover laid out against the viewport offer with a zero static position, outside every ancestor's overflow and region trial.
- **A computed `-exact-layout-transition` on the web JS target is unset** (2026-10-21, GitHub #809). A bound row writes the kernel's value for each string its code names (`style.rs` `layout_transitions`, read from css.rs's own `layout_transition_css`), which covers a reduced-motion switch (`calm ? "none" : "260ms ease"`); lowering refuses a literal the kernel would. A string the code builds (`"${ms}ms ease"`, or a named one written another way, such as `"260ms  ease"`) is unset with a journal line saying the JS target writes only the values the code names, where the wasm host and native hosts parse it. The fix is the transition grammar in JS (`exact_motion::Transitions::parse` and `matching(Layout)`, to the presence module's `<ms> <delay> <easing>`), tested against the kernel over the cases paint_tests.rs's `bound_transition_stacking_matches_kernel` uses. A row of a list or match whose own value turns a curve on (an item's field or its index, not state outside it) in the commit that moves it jumps on this target where wasm slides it: `lt` asks the binding before the tree update, when the region's item and index signals still hold the last commit's values (`each`, `match` set them in `flush`); a gain read after them needs the row's box from before the update.
- **A Release build after a Contract edit that changes the archive's linked capabilities compiles the whole Rust world again under fat LTO** (2026-10-21, euxy port 21). A plain Contract edit's `ios --release` is ~8.5 s (the plan and bytecode are linked outside the archive, `scripts/linked-plan.mjs`). An edit that adds a capability (a gradient on a row, measured: 57 s, cargo 48.5 s; port 21's 84 s release was one) changes the bake's `linked.rs` (`EXACT_LINKED`, `host/apple/src/link.rs` `Links`), so Cargo compiles the archive crate and fat LTO runs over everything. Making it seconds means choosing the linked set at link time: the archive built once with every capability's entry exported, a per-plan object outside the LTO unit holding the table of the ones the plan uses, and the linker's dead strip removing the rest. Measure the binary, TTI and idle footprint against today's before taking it (LLP 1047.001 D2 decides what ships).
- **An `agent ios` drive still launches its app, ~0.5 s of its ~3 s** (2026-10-21). The rest is the window capture's three `simctl io` reads (~1.3 s; the touch runner's `XCUIScreen.main.screenshot()` takes ~0.12 s when it is up, measured), the app's own drawing (~0.45 s, mostly `settleMotion`'s quiet window) and the build check. A drive could find the next app already launched: once a drive's app has ended, a kept process launches another with the same environment (plan, facts, store and its fresh flag; never under `--touch platform` or platform timing), and the next drive with that environment connects to it instead. Its store's lock and the fresh store's emptying at boot have to move to that process, and a launch by any other drive on the simulator has to retire it. LLP 1012.000 proposes the resident driver this would sit beside.
- **iOS: a custom grouped row right to left, and its leading symbol at accessibility sizes** (2026-10-21, the Harmony custom-row fix's round 3). (1) Right to left, a custom row's views are carried into their cell unmirrored and the sheet's insets are physical (`padding-left` 56 past a leading symbol), so `GroupedCustomRowsIOS.swift` leaves the line under it UIKit's own rather than guess which edge "under its content" means; a mirrored custom row (the sheet's insets logical, the carried views placed from the right) would let the line follow it. (2) The sheet's leading-symbol slot (`grouped-symbol-width`, `-height`, `grouped-row-inset-symbol`: 24, 20 and 56) is fixed at every body size, while UIKit's cell grows its image's reserved space and the text's inset with Dynamic Type; so at an accessibility size a custom row keeps its leading symbol's old drawing (the slot's medium scale, in its box; theme2 20:51) rather than the list's scale, which would overflow toward the title. Ramps for those three rows, measured as `accessory-checkmark-width` was, would fix both the standard and the custom row's geometry.
- **A popover's default surface: two cases it leaves as today** (2026-10-22, theme2 00:54). (1) A popover with no role that a node only later names as its context menu (`contextPopover`) isn't re-checked for the default surface, which shows only in the driver's painted presentation (a person sees UIKit's context menu); a re-check belongs in the presenter's batch before its flush. (2) A conditional class with a surface row on one arm only counts as authored on both arms (the class expands to a written `value`/`none` row), so the other arm gets no default surface; one plan boolean can't follow the arm, a bound prop could.
- **Web: exact2's menu rule takes a row as it is at the click** (2026-10-22, theme2 02:54, option B). `host/web/menu-rows.js` intercepts only a menu row that names a popover when it's clicked, and acts after the row's press on the popover it names then; every other row keeps HTML's own activation. So a press that retargets its row from a popover to a dialog's command still opens the popover (the click-time target), and one that retargets it from a dialog's command to a popover leaves that popover nested in the menu, closing with it. Replaying every command after the press instead (option A) would re-implement HTML's command dispatch, which its re-checks kept finding gaps in. The native hosts resolve a row when it's chosen.
- **A grouped row's ink on the web is one for every state, while the kernel reads the row as it shows now** (2026-10-21, stage 13 C fix 1, theme2 17:32). The sheet's action-cell ink (`contract/lower/src/grouped.rs` `ink`, `grouped/actions.rs` `action_cell`) is decided once from the source; `GroupedRow::action` (`kernel/src/grouped.rs`) reads the row's shown children. So two shapes can differ between the web and iOS in one state. (1) A "split" `if`, a symbol in one branch and a text in the other: the sheet refuses it, and iOS tints it while the symbol shows (`contract/cli/tests/it/grouped_action.rs`'s `split` row). (2) A checkmark row written without `aria-selected` (`scripts/fixtures/grouped-list.contract`'s Dark and System rows): unpicked, it shows only its title, so iOS tints it as an action while the sheet, which sees the `if`, does not. A row that says `aria-selected` is a choice on both. Either the sheet's ink follows the state (a class on the shown shape), or the kernel reads the source's shape.
- **Linux: a bar item's symbol at the bar's size** (2026-10-21, LLP 1115.000 D4.15). The web draws a symbol that is all of a pressable's face in a route's bar (a header iOS makes one of, `kernel/src/bars.rs` `header_bar`) at the neutral look's `bar-symbol-size`, 22/17 of the root's font size, over the size written on it, as UIKit draws a bar button item's image. The Linux desktop, whose kernel lays the bar out, draws it at the written size; `Kernel::header_shape` finds the bar there.
- **Conformance's `intersect` fixture fails now and then on the JS target under load** (2026-10-21, the stage 12 gate's no-regressions run on `205714fd31`). In one full `host/web-js/conform.mjs --synthetic --strict --linux --jobs 1` run, `synthetic-intersect` failed at boot: `state slots.calls: wasm 1 js 0`, `state slots.seen: wasm "row:false:0" js "none"`, its `seen` text `1 row:false:0` against `0 none`. It passed in the full run before it and on the branch's base. The JS runner's first intersection callback seems to land after the boot comparison when the machine is busy, as `startend`'s first end report does (the entry above). Find where the JS target first reports an intersection at boot and make the comparison wait for it.
- **Web: an authored `appearance: none` on a route's header or its Back control** (2026-10-21, LLP 1075.003 §9.10, LLP 1116.000 D4). On iOS it keeps the authored bar: a header that says so is no bar, and a Back control that says so stays where it is while UIKit's Back is hidden (`HeaderShape.authoredBack`). The web makes no bar of a header whose `appearance: none` is a literal row (`data-exact-said`, element.rs) and draws its route no Back, but still draws its bar look (neutral.css) there; it does not read a Back control's own, nor a header's bound on the JS target, which marks no bound `appearance` (`rows.rs`).
- **Web: a page built ahead of time shows a pushed route's Back only once the navigation glue runs** (2026-10-21, LLP 1116.000 D4). `document.rs` mirrors the projection's route visibility but not the bar's Back, which is the header's shadow root's, nor the header-held Back control it does not show, so the Back appears, and that control goes, at hydration. A declarative shadow root (`<template shadowrootmode>`) with the Back in it, adopted by `appBarBack`, and the control's mark would show them as the glue will.
- **Web: a Back control hidden on its own in a header iOS makes no bar of leaves the page no way back** (2026-10-21, LLP 1116.000 D4). The web draws no Back beside a control such a header holds unless the control is in the header's menu or a dialog, or the header is not shown (`appBarBack`, navigation.js). A control hidden in a shown header by other means (`hidden` or `display: none` on it or a box around it, or `visibility: hidden`) gets no Back either, so nothing on the page goes back; the browser's Back still presses it, and iOS draws UIKit's Back there. Drawing the Back wherever such a control has no box or is not visible would close it, with care for a control the bar hid on an earlier projection (`data-exact-bar-back`), which has no box until the projection clears that mark.
- **A `sent` reader off screen still costs euxy 0.7-0.9% CPU while it plays** (2026-10-21, euxy port 18, iPhone 17 simulator, Release, no MIDI device; `ps` over 20 s, interleaved with builds before and after). A reader that declares `exactMIDI().sent` is answered at each message's time (LLP 1125 D6): about sixteen commits a second for the default pattern, each changing no node while the activity log is not shown. It was 2.3-2.6% before `Frames.tick` skipped their presenter pass, then 1.2-1.45%; the display link no longer runs for them (`timersQuiet`) and the host no longer lays them out (`inert_layout`). With `sent` left out of the shape, 0.05%. What is left is a timer's wake and a commit per message: the runner's settle and the host's turn. Reaching 0.1% needs fewer commits, which D6 decides: answer `sent` at most every 250 ms or so (four commits a second), or only while something mounted reads it.
- **A derive over `sent` that nothing shows costs euxy port 21 2.9% CPU while it plays** (2026-10-21, iPhone 17 simulator, Release, medians of 5; port 20, which reads `sent` only in the shown log, 0.5%). Its activity log derive (`logRows`: the 128 messages formatted and sorted) is evaluated at every message's commit though the log is not shown, about 118,000 VM instructions and 97% of the commit (985 µs in a release bench; 36 µs without it). Settlement evaluates every derive whose inputs changed, read or not, and a derive that traps refuses the commit (LLP 1005 §6; the web JS target's `settle` pulls every derive too), so evaluating only the derives something mounted reads would change what commits: Charlie's ruling. Within the semantics, what is left is the VM's own cost per instruction: `machine.rs`'s `pop_n` and `collected` build each list in a vector and copy it into its shared allocation, `first` allocates a `Some`, and the extent walk measures again each small record a body copies out of a list. `machine.rs` is extracted to Lean (`semantics/vm-extract`), so changing it needs Charon and Aeneas.
- **euxy's activity log costs 13-18% CPU while it plays** (2026-10-21, iPhone 17 simulator, Release; `ps` moves with the machine's load). The log is `for s, i in lines key=i` over the newest 100 `sent` messages, so each note gives every row its neighbour's words: about 100 `props`, 100 `paragraph` and 70 `content` ops a batch, sixteen batches a second. The main thread does about three quarters of it (`sample`). Per row, `NodeView.applyProps` marks the layer for display whatever changed, so Core Animation's pass runs `applyBoxLayer`, `applyImageLayer`, `applyGradientLayer` and `applyBoxMask` for a change of words; and showing a kept raster in a one-colour paragraph's `TintInk` is a `UIImageView.image` set, about a quarter of the presenter's samples. The owner's share is the runner's settle and the kernel laying out 100 paragraphs. Keyed by the message rather than its index, a note would make one row and move the others. In the host: a change of words that skips the layer's display pass, and a tinted ink shown without `UIImageView`'s image resolution.
- **A restored `app.contract` older than its bake is not baked again** (2026-10-21). In an app outside the repo, `cp app.contract app.contract.orig`, an edit, a Release build, then `mv app.contract.orig app.contract` and another build: the second build kept the edited plan (its `target/bake/…/production/*.plan` unchanged until `touch app.contract`), so every later build measured the edit. The plan is baked by a build script under Cargo's `rerun-if-changed` (`js/bake/src/lib.rs`), which reruns only for a file whose mtime is newer than its last run; a digest of the sources would not be fooled. Until then, `touch` a restored source.
- **Body text whose weight changes every frame drops frames as the motion starts** (2026-10-20, iPhone 17 Pro Max, ~/bench/weightbench mode 2: twelve 17 px paragraphs, the weight eased 100 to 1000 and back with a 100 ms rest at each end). Exact 18-26 late frames in 10 s, UIKit 2-11, at equal main-thread work in motion (3.3 against 3.4 ms a frame). Every late frame is in the first frames after a rest: the frame after it takes 11-14 ms and the cost falls over about ten frames to 3.4 ms, the CPU's clock and caches coming back after 100 ms idle. UIKit's falls the same way from 8-10 ms. It is not new fonts: with the weight in steps of 25 (37 weights, all made in the first second) the first frames are as slow and late frames 18-20. Exact's frame costs about 3.5 times its steady work there against UIKit's 2.5: find what in a relaid paragraph's frame is memory-bound (`Presenter.apply`, the paragraphs' layout and rasters) with a frame-level trace on the phone, which Instruments did not attach to while it was locked.
- **The Linux painter's neutral control sizes at a root other than 16 px** (2026-10-20, LLP 1069.011.001 D8). The web writes a control size's title in `rem` (`host/web/src/css/control_size.rs`), so it follows a root the app or the agent sets; the Linux painter titles it in fixed px, the web's at a 16 px root (`said_size`, `host/linux/src/paint/button.rs`). To follow the root it needs the root font size for every neutral button, which `ButtonFaceStyle` carries only for a neutral tab (`neutral_tab_root`).
- **Game pads have not met a real pad** (2026-10-18, LLP 1127.003 §4's hardware half). Every host's adapter is built (web, macOS, iOS, tvOS, Android, Linux evdev, Windows XInput) and the engine is tested, but no physical pad has been driven: none is attached to the Mac that built them. With a pad connected to each device, `bun game/dev.mjs beacons --lan` serves Beacons to a browser and prints Open in native for the Mac and for an iPad or a Pixel on the network: the left stick moves the player, `GamepadSouth` jumps, `GamepadL1` held runs, `GamepadWest` lights a beacon in reach, and the journal (`logs`) says `pad 0 connected standard` and `pad 0 disconnected` on unplugging. On the Mac, an iPad and a Pixel, check each: the delivery the driver reports as recognized, movement, both connection journal lines, focus lost while a button is held (the world releases it, and the pad reports itself when focus returns), and a disconnect while held. Linux needs an Xbox pad (xpad, letters) and a DualSense (positional) at launch: their X and Square are both `GamepadWest`.
- **Android pad readings carry no event time** (2026-10-18, LLP 1127.003's final review). `Pads.kt` sends only the reading (`:44`, `:93`), `Owner.kt` queues it (`:467`) and `canvas/keys.rs` stamps it on arrival (`:33`), so a press and release delivered late together can cross the 200 ms tap/hold boundary. Carry `KeyEvent`'s and `MotionEvent`'s `eventTime` through Kotlin, the owner's queue and JNI into the host's clock, and test a delayed delivery of 150 ms and 210 ms presses.
- **Android pads are released only when the activity stops** (2026-10-18, LLP 1127.003's final review). `ExactActivity.kt:454` releases them in `onStop`; a dialog or the notification shade takes the window's focus without stopping the activity, and the world keeps what was held. Release them on `onWindowFocusChanged(false)`, which matches the recorded resume behaviour (a pad reports itself with its next event).
- **No game stores a player's bindings** (2026-10-18, LLP 1127.003 D7). `Game::bindings` has only the engine's `Seeded` test consumer. Wire one real game's binding preference through app storage, and prove that a new game is seeded from it while an existing save keeps its own bindings.
- **Linux pad timing has no test through production dispatch** (2026-10-18, LLP 1127.003's final review). The route is tested in two halves joined at the canvas's JSON row (`host/linux`'s `pad_report_times_survive_the_read_and_the_first_owner`, `game/render`'s `pad_rows_tap_and_hold_by_their_report_times`), because no test binary links the Linux presenter with a game surface. A test the shell generator writes beside a game's Linux shell (`game/app/shells.mjs`), feeding `gamepad::read` through the presenter into the game's own surface, would join them, if it needs no new apparatus.
- **`metrics.mjs --long`'s iOS hello app fails to build** (2026-10-20, origin/main `77a3081944`). Both "optimized for speed" and "for size" report `FAILED exit 1` after `Build complete!`, so the iOS size number is unmeasured; the failing step prints nothing the metrics line keeps.
- **A bound `transition`'s spring on the web JS target plays only on the five spring properties** (2026-10-20, LLP 1019 §13 D10). `TRANSITION_ENTRIES` (`host/web-js/src/style.rs`) drops every entry with `spring(` and leaves it to `motion.js`, which drives `translate`, `scale`, `rotate`, `opacity` and `height`. Any other property under a bound spring (`font-weight`, a colour) lands at once on the web, where native hosts and a static row play the spring's curve from rest as `linear()` (`Transition::governing`). The fix is `SpringConfig::easing` in JS beside `transition-paint.js`, writing the entry as CSS's `linear()`.
- **The compiler's check of a bundled SVG image, and the icon reader on the parser crate** (2026-10-20, LLP 1011.001 stages 4 and 1). Every host now draws an `.svg` image source (`host/svg-file`; Apple's `RasterImage.decodeSVG`, Linux's `image/svg.rs`), and the `lower-image-svg` warning is retired, so a file the parser refuses or trims (a DOCTYPE entity, an external `image`, a `script`) is known only from the host's log at run time. Stage 4: the compiler parses a bundled `.svg` a literal source names (`contract/lower`, where `icons.rs` already reads asset SVGs) and names what the parser drops. Stage 1's leftover: `icon_svg.rs` onto the crate's tokenizer.
- **Linux and Windows do not read the neutral look's symbol scales or button image gap** (2026-10-20, LLP 1011 §3; LLP 1115.000 D17). The web draws a symbol for an SF Symbol in a 1.2em box (`symbol-scale`), and on a button's face in a 1.5em one (`button-symbol-scale`). Linux lays a symbol out in a box its font size square (`host/linux/src/host.rs:1081-1082`, and `image.rs:237-239` when it syncs an image) and paints it at that size (`paint/svg.rs:140-141`): five sixths of the web's size, and two thirds on a button's face. Nor does it read `button-image-gap`, the 8 px the web puts between a button's image and title: it puts a space in the title's type there (`paint/button.rs:308-315`, whose comment says the page does). Windows paints with Linux's painter (`host/windows/Cargo.toml`).
- **A native button whose title is empty is laid out on the web as a titled face** (2026-10-21, LLP 1069.011.001 D3; LLP 1011 §3). A title bound to `""` keeps the face's title column, the image padding and the symbol's full width: a symbol beside an empty title is 69.5 by 38.25 px at 17 px in Chromium and WebKit (57.22 on origin/main), where the same symbol alone is 38.25 square. iOS lays an empty title out as a symbol alone (`host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/ButtonConfigurationIOS.swift:238`, `face.title?.isEmpty ?? true`).
- **One-colour text rasters on the Mac, and wide lines' rasters** (2026-10-20, after iOS's alpha-only rasters). (1) iOS and tvOS paint a one-colour paragraph as coverage alone and show it through a template image in its colour (`TextRasterJob.tints`, `TintInk`), as a `UILabel` does: a 13-row poster of ~97 pt type whose weight changes every frame went from 7,410 to 3,019 main-thread samples in 10 s, UIKit's 3,045. The Mac paints into IOSurfaces (`TextRasterMac.swift`) and still uses four bytes a pixel, on purpose (measured 2026-10-20, weight bench's poster on this Mac, Time Profiler 10 s): its rasters run on workers through Core Animation's CG queue, where the glyph copy that cost iOS's main thread 65% (`CGSColorMaskCopyARGB8888`) is 2.2% of worker samples; the main thread's raster cost is IOSurface creation and registration and the commit. AppKit's public tint (a template image in an `NSImageView` with `contentTintColor`, shown with a multiply colour and no mask, and honoured by `cacheDisplay` and `render(in:)`) works only for an image view in the view tree, never its layer alone (probe: ~/bench/weightbench/a8mac), and shows a `CGImage`: a subview per paragraph, and the main-thread copy at commit that the IOSurface path exists to avoid. Not worth it unless a Mac profile shows the glyph copy on the main thread. (2) A raster is cut to a band vertically for a tall paragraph, never horizontally: a nowrap line wider than what shows (a marquee's) is painted whole every time it changes. Cutting it to the visible width needs the visible rect under a running animation, which `Presenter.textBand` reads from the model layer, so a row a keyframe animation slides would show blank past its cut. Not built: since (1) the weight bench's poster, rows about five times the screen's width, matches UIKit (119 fps, about 10 late frames in 10 s, 4.3 against 4.2 ms of main-thread work a frame), and a `UILabel`'s backing store also covers its whole width. The rows that would gain are the ones a keyframe slides, whose cut would have to cover the range the animation sweeps.
- **The Linux and Windows painter washes a plain or borderless button** (2026-10-20, after the web's plain-button fade). `host/web/neutral.css` draws a `plain` or `borderless` button with no wash, hovered or held, and fades its face to `button-pressed-alpha` (0.75, UIKit's highlighted alpha) while it is held. The painter's `text` look (`host/linux/src/paint/button.rs`, the `"text"` arm) washes it, against LLP 1115.000 D17's one designed look; it should draw no wash and fade its ink to `button-pressed-alpha` while pressed.
- **On the wasm runtime a bar's large-title line is the large title's only at a 16 px root** (2026-10-20, the web's large-title line in `host/web/neutral.css`). A level-1 bar title takes the large title's line, `2.0625rem` (the large title at a 16 px root) at a line height of 1.2, so it follows the large title at any root on the JS target, which writes every text style in `rem`. The wasm runtime sizes text styles by Apple's ramp at the root (`text_style_size`, `kernel/src/style/relative.rs`), which does not follow the root in proportion, so its line is the large title's only at 16 px: at a 20 px root it is a 41 px title's line where the ramp's large title is 37 px, at 53 a 109 px title's against 60, and at 14 a 29 px title's against 31. Wants the large title's size at the root where the rule can read it (a custom property each runtime writes, or a `calc()` of the ramp over `1rem`).
- **The painted hosts' route bars keep the title's own line, where the web's keeps the large title's** (2026-10-20, LLP 1115.000 D17; the web's rule is the large-title line in `host/web/neutral.css`). On the web a level-1 bar title is `title1` centred in the large title's line by equal margins, so the bar is as tall as with the large title. The Linux desktop and Windows paint the bar from the same metric rows but lay the title out on its own line, so their bars are about 7 px shorter at a 16 px root. Wants the kernel to give the bar's level-1 heading the large title's line, so every painting host keeps it.
- **Files dragged in or pasted on the hosts that take none** (2026-10-20, LLP 1069.010 "Files dropped or pasted"). Web and macOS take `drop`, `dragenter` and `dragleave`; web, macOS and iOS give a paste its `files`. Linux (GTK's `drag-data-received`, `text/uri-list`), Windows (`IDropTarget`, `CF_HDROP`) and Android (`View.OnDragListener`, `ClipData` content URIs) refuse by name and paste text only; iOS's `drop` would be `UIDropInteraction` (iPad drags from Files). Each needs its host's minting of a `doc:` handle for a URL it did not pick.
- **`radius.full` squares a terminal box, so @exact/cards' avatar corner is 999px** (2026-10-20, the stage 12 E follow-up; x2-perf4's round 2). The base scale's terminal cell for every radius is 0 (`kernel/tables/schema.json` `_scale`), and the terminal draws a rounded box only for a radius above 0 (`host/terminal/src/paint.rs`), so a pill written `radius.full` gets square corners there where a px radius got rounded ones. `cards.avatar-corner` is `999px` until the terminal draws `full` as rounded (its cell `full`, or the painter reading `RADIUS_FULL`); then it can be `radius.full`, and the clone's 999px corners too.
- **Rivals fails its ordinary native proof on origin/main** (verified on `f2fab719a`, 2026-10-20). At ceeb9276a3 (2026-10-22, Linux) the proof stops first at its captured lock: `game/games/rivals/Cargo.lock`, copied to `.shells/Cargo.lock`, no longer matches its dependencies under `--locked` (`ownDrift`, `game/app/shells.mjs`); refresh it with `bun game/app/shells.mjs game/games/rivals --lock`. On `f2fab719a` the proof passed its simulation but failed `particles-uncovered`: 528 particles live, zero pixels covered. Diagnose that and restore a passing proof. (Shortcut Fixture's proof passes on Linux with the `after-shortcuts` pin 1afbc5f640 set.)
- **The relay in Rust, once the hosted one needs it** (2026-10-20, LLP 1121 §5 stage 10c R3; theme2's 14:34 approval; owner rt1). The relay is one Bun file (`scripts/tamber-relay-server.mjs`): one EXACT-RELAY/1 implementation shared with the connector, an I/O-bound pipe. A Rust relay behind the same protocol buys less memory per socket and no GC pauses; the trigger is measured on the hosted instance: more than a few thousand concurrent streams, or memory per idle connector connection past what the host can hold.
- **A warning when a certificate for a relay account's names appears that this server didn't ask for** (2026-10-20, LLP 1121 §5 stage 10c R2; theme2's plan change 8 and the lead's answer to Q3; owner rt1). With the relay's default domain, its operator controls DNS for the account's names and could get a certificate for them unseen; a server could watch certificate transparency for certificates under `*.<label>.<relay>` whose key isn't its own and warn. That needs a CT search service at runtime (crt.sh, or a commercial monitor), since the logs themselves only offer whole-log scans: a developer's opt-in, naming the service. `--relay-name` with `--relay-dns-hook` (names of their own), the CAA records it prints set (`issue` and `issuewild`) and the names pointed by A/AAAA (no CNAME), already removes the residual, for the CAs that honour CAA (every public one must).
- **The agent cannot drag a real touch into the software keyboard** (2026-10-20, round 28's mood journal). Under `--touch platform` a drag must end inside the viewport, which stops at the keyboard's top (`scripts/agent-drag.mjs`, `host/apple/touches.mjs`), so a drive cannot show a scroller's interactive keyboard dismissal (`keyboardDismissMode` `.interactive`, LLP 1115.000 D9.1), which works: with both guards lifted, a drag from a sheet's content into the keyboard put it away. A drag whose end is in the keyboard could be admitted when the keyboard is up.
- **`font-variation-settings` in a transition, and the font axes in keyframes** (2026-10-20, LLP 1019 §13 D9, D10). D10 transitions `font-weight` and `font-stretch` through the runner; `font-variation-settings` takes no transition, and keyframes refuse all three. The settings row is eight (tag, value) pairs, so a transition between two values naming the same tags would run each axis as D10 runs the weight.
- **Firefox: `showPopover({ source })` after a `commandfor` open anchors at the earlier button** (2026-10-20, the review of the web chooser at its invoker). Once a `commandfor` button has opened a popover, a later `showPopover({ source: other })`, as the runtimes' `popoverCommand` opens one, draws it under the `commandfor` button rather than `other` in Firefox 157; Chrome 156 and WebKit 27.2, and Firefox after a `popovertarget` open or on a fresh page, draw it at `other`. The implicit anchor is the engine's, so a menu and a chooser meet it alike; `neutral-look.test.mjs` opens by `commandfor` last.
- **iOS `check`: a top row scrolled partly off-screen reported as "under List#6 … nothing scrolls it clear"** (2026-10-19, the #799 review, iPhone 18 Pro simulator, iOS 27.0). After a real drag in a mail-style list under a large title, `check` reported the partly scrolled-off top row as covered by the list itself, and said nothing scrolls it clear, though scrolling back down does. Unrelated to the floating bar fixed in `51e461973`; not traced. Fixtures: the review's scratchpad `pr799/` (gone with the session), so start from a large-title list with a bottom search field.
- **The exact-linux executor tests flake on origin/main** (2026-10-19, the euxy friction lane, on `2adc6c57d`). `cargo test -p exact-linux --lib` failed in 3 of 6 runs on a clean checkout, each in `executor::core::async_tests` (`host/apple/src/executor_async_tests.rs`, compiled into the Linux crate): `reader_spawn_failure_and_panics_settle_and_release_the_lane` twice and `portable_upload_reserves_the_blocking_workers_additional_copy` once, never alone (each passes when run by itself). Load-dependent, so chase the waits those two make under the full suite.
- **Apple: a line holding a separately shadowed run drops every decoration on it** (2026-10-20, review of the text-decoration propagation fix). `TextLinePaint.drawShadowed` draws a line whose runs carry different `text-shadow`s run by run with `CTRunDraw` (`TextRunPaint.swift`), which draws no underline or line-through, so the lines on that line vanish. Draw them as the hidden-run case does: the line clipped to each stretch of runs.

- **The retention key and a token named like a literal's digits** (2026-10-20, LLP 1121 stage 12 B1, Astra's sixth re-check; contrived, so QUEUE'd under theme2's ordinary-edit cutoff). The design server's retention key (`tamber_walk::retention_key`) keeps a literal value (`pick = #aabbcc`) by its kind, but the compiler's native-param word closure (`contract/lower/src/theme.rs` ~335) records the literal's words (`aabbcc`). Repro: a native component `Map.pin` defaulting to `pick`; prime with `pick = #aabbcc`, edit it to `#ddeeff` (the key is unchanged), then propose `add-token ddeeff = #112233`: the cached retention answers `live` where a fresh server answers `rebuild`. Fix: recompute the native-param word closure from the current parsed designs each proposal, caching only the view-derived retention; or keep hex words out of the closure.
- **Harmony in conform, with a backend of its own** (2026-10-21, the lead's Harmony task; skipped for now, the lane's in-memory web tests cover it). Conform compares the JS target with the wasm runner step by step on each app's own data. Harmony's data is a Snapback client: with no backend it shows only the never-synced state, and with one, sync timing makes the two runs differ. Conform also builds an app's Linux crate at `apps/<app>/linux`, where Harmony's is `apps/harmony/app/linux`. Doing it means a conform mode that starts `snapback4 dev --memory` per run (as `scripts/memory-backend-test.mjs` does, the server proven its own and the build's grants checked), seeds it with fixed times rather than `seed.mjs`'s now-relative ones, waits each step for the device's sync to settle on both targets, and finds the Linux crate beside the app.
- **The server's write check: what it still leaves to the build** (2026-10-20, theme2's 14:19 ruling; narrowed by LLP 1121 stage 13 D, 2026-10-21). `contract_lower::rows::check` refuses a write that breaks an element's style row that passed, in the compiler's words, at the row: literal rows and every arm of a literal choice (stage 12), `font`/`font-family`/`font-weight`/`font-style` rows with the node's fonts through `type_tokens::tokens_named` and `fonts::font_use` (D1), computed rows (a name, a template, a choice with a computed arm) judged whole in the root's scope and shapes where their form gives their type (D2), and an `svg` subtree's rows renamed and coerced as lowering does (D3). Still only at the build: a computed row under a `for` or `match` binding, or one whose form doesn't give its type (a call), or through a `let`; a row that failed before the write; a `style`'s rows wherever one element applying the style is one whose class rows lowering rewrites or judges otherwise first (an SVG element's, a box's `transform`, a grouped list's, a `button`'s, an `audio`'s, a `delete=` row's), chooses it (`class=(c ? A : B)`), or sits under a `for` or `match` (a style all of whose users are none of these is judged at each since 2026-10-22, theme2's 02:08 ruling; a write's refusals are said as the build reaches them, but a shorthand or `flex` row the build refuses before them, own or a style's, is not among them, so the first said may be the build's second); a shorthand or `flex` (`font` naming no type token among them); `transition`/`animation` rows; a state part's row; a refusal outside a style row; a write that also edits a Contract source; font rows after a font file the build read changed. **The window before the background build's verdict**: a file whose whole build fails before the write for a reason of its own (an element lowering refuses before its rows, its children never lowered: a positional `svg`, a leaf with children, a grouped list's malformed section, an unknown tag) has its rows left to the build once the background whole build says it fails (`Checker::seen_failed`), but a write in the moments before that verdict can be refused at a row in other words than the build's first refusal. Closing it means the walk sharing every pre-style exit of `prepare_element` (dozens), or the check waiting for the verdict.
- **Fresh macOS game receipts can reject their generated declarations** (2026-10-19, LLP 1132.A-proof-fix round 1). A macOS-first cold build on parent `2fdf7e077` refuses `.shells/keys.json` and `.shells/surfaces.json` as stale against its just-built Apple receipt. The refusal persists on cached ordinary and collecting runs, including after Linux/web proofs. Retained evidence: `.lane/r1-parent-cold-macos/`, `.lane/r1-parent-matrix-macos/` and `.lane/r1-parent-collecting-macos/` in the 1132.A-first-hour worktree; `.lane/r1-parent-source-check.json` verifies unchanged parent product sources. Fresh Linux/web/macOS edit-lifetime matrices pass on both HEAD and the parent. Qualify the generated declarations before issuing a cold Apple receipt.
- **Apple: a word longer than its line in a plain text, broken as a UILabel breaks it** (2026-10-19, round 26's hyphenation tells). A plain UILabel on iOS 27 wraps at words and breaks only a word longer than its line, between syllables with a hyphen ("antidisestab-lishmentarian-ism"); CSS has no value for that. `hyphens: auto` hyphenates every line end (a cell's way, which grouped rows now take) and `overflow-wrap: break-word` breaks at the last letter that fits with no hyphen, so verify can offer a heading of several words neither. On Apple, `break-word`'s break inside a word could take the last hyphenation point that fits and show a hyphen (the soft hyphens `Spec.hyphenateAuto` inserts, taken only when no other opportunity fits the line, with min-content kept at whole words): a declared deviation in LLP 1001, since the web and Linux break at a letter. UIKit reference screens: a scratch app of `UILabel`s at accessibility-extra-extra-extra-large.
- **Several native copies, and `navigate` on the native hosts** (2026-10-19, LLP 1122 T7; theme2's Q4 ruling). The design tool's grid of live copies is web copies of the dev page, each pinned to one mode; a native build stays the single connected build, pinned as before. Several simulators (a boot, memory and a runner slot each) would show the native rendering of every mode at once, and need each native host to take `navigate {seq, location}` through its own router (a deep link in place) and report `location` in its status, as the web pages do (docs/tamber-protocol.md, the build channel). Until then a native build lacks the `navigate` capability, and the server answers it 409 `unsupported`.
- **iOS: a root stack without tabs that shows no bar still gets a navigation controller** (2026-10-19, after the tab case landed). A tab whose stack shows no bar and has pushed nothing now holds its route in a plain container (`HeldStackIOS.swift`) until it pushes, its route shows a bar, or a hook reads `ExactTab.controller`: euxy port 12 (Release, a fresh iPhone 17 simulator, interleaved launches, each footprint after `simctl erase`) went from 25,010 to 23,986 KB (`footprint`, medians of 5), `EUXY_TTI` 1,234.5 to 1,223 ms (medians of 10), playing CPU 0.1% either way. The same holds for an app whose root is one stack (`installPrimary`'s path without tabs): a barless root there still builds a `UINavigationController`, whose view in a window lays out the SwiftUI floating bar host. Holding it the same way means a held stack under the root itself (the primary owner, `placeOwner`, `ownerFrame`, the sheet's presenting controller) rather than in a tab controller's slot; `realize` would put the new controller in the held one's place under the root. A tab the runner has not built yet still gets one at install, out of the window, which costs nothing at launch.
- **`smoke.mjs host-ios` fails at its blur step** (2026-10-19, iPhone 17 simulator, iOS 27.0; the same on `675cca3e8`): `tap caltrain-main at 2 2` is refused, "ScrollEdgeEffectView covers its middle": the sample host's navigation controller lays iOS 26's scroll edge effect over session a's top left, so the step that checks a blur stays the session's never runs. Aim the tap at a point of a's ground below the bar, or teach the agent's cover test that the edge effect takes no touches.
- **`justify-items` on a block container: the kernel ignores it, Chrome does not** (2026-10-19, round 1 of the flex-on-block warning's review). CSS Box Alignment 3 makes a block container's `justify-items` its block children's default `justify-self`, and Chrome lays `<div style="width:100px;justify-items:center"><div style="width:20px;height:10px">` with the child at x=40; `view width=100 justify-items="center"` › `view width=20 height=10` lays it at x=0. The vendored taffy's block layout aligns a child only by the legacy `text-align` (`compute/block.rs`); doing it means `justify-self` on block-level children (with `auto` margins winning, as in Chrome) and a parity fixture.
- **Library platform inputs not checked** (2026-10-20, LLP 1139 D6, stage 12 C3; theme2's ruling (3)). `lower-library-platform` refuses a library package's `exactDelivery`/`exactSurface` resources and `exactViewport().displayMode`; two inputs D6 lists are limits: an `inject` whose provider reads the host (a provider in the package is itself held to the rule; one outside it is the app's), and a package file named as an `app.json` `host.<platform>.entry` or per-platform variants of one component (no Contract syntax names a host entry from a package). Doing it means following an inject to its provider across files, and reading app.json host entries against package roots.
- **Base names and length tokens inside a Contract literal shadow row** (2026-10-20, LLP 1121 stage 12 B3, theme2's Q1). `box-shadow="0 space.md 4px black"` written on the row, not as a token, is refused, pointing to a shadow token (`x: box-shadow = 0 space.md 4px black`), which reads it. Taking it on the row means the kernel's shadow parse reading theme lengths at run time, each read recorded, and the same per-column check at build as a shadow token's; a literal stays px until an app needs it.
- **Linux: a keyboard cannot scroll a grouped alert's long message** (2026-10-19, the alert parts, `contract/lower/src/alert_parts.rs`). The retained alert's key handler (`host/linux/src/presenter/alert.rs`, `alert_key`) acts on Escape, Tab, Enter and Space and takes every other key, so the arrow and page keys do not reach the message box, which the wheel scrolls; nor can Tab reach it, as the web's alert-message.js makes it a stop while it overflows.
- **A Dynamic Type opt-in for the base scale** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 11 ruling 6, the lead's assignment to B's first commit). The base scale (`space.*`, `radius.*`, the schema's `scale`) is px (points) in every column and never scales with Dynamic Type, as a measured UIKit inset or corner does not. An app that wants its spacing to follow the text size has no switch for it: an opt-in (a design-level flag, or `rem`-based base values under it) scaling the space steps, never the corners, with a test per host at an accessibility size.
- **A 3,000-token × 64-context design cannot import: share a context's tree** (2026-10-19, iosfix's x2/import-rest; theme2's 19:07 ruling). The budget is calibrated to the isolate (`budget.rs`: `IMPORT` 3 × 2²⁴, `REIMPORT` 3 × 2²³, about 600 and 750 MB), so a design of about 650 tokens over 64 contexts re-imports and about 880 import fresh; 3,000 × 64 is refused with the budget's message (the export inlines every token into every context: 93 MB, 3.4 GB of RSS to re-import whole). The standard route: the DTCG Resolver Module's sets and modifiers can carry a context's differences instead of inlining every token per context, so the export shrinks within the format we already write (no new wire), and re-import reads one shared tree plus each context's differences. Needs its own plan and theme2's ruling: the export's shape (a base set, each modifier context its overrides), what a client sends back, and the budget measured again.
- **Tamber importers: the super-linear paths left behind the isolation** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 §5 stage 10b E2+E3 review round 5; owner rt1; the lead's 12:31Z ruling). Every import is now planned in a watched process of its own (`scripts/tamber-isolate.mjs`: about 1 GiB, 10 s, a 2 MiB answer, one at a time), so these cost a client its own import, not the server; `tamber/src/budget.rs` is a partial second layer (the DTCG importer and re-import; the CSS importers are bounded by their own caps, iosfix's x2/import-rest). A held re-import's marks still run in the shared serve, on operations the server built. Still to make linear, from Astra's round 5 (`llp/reviews/code-2026-10-19-s10b-e23-r5.astra.md`): generated dotted paths uncharged (`export.rs` `flatten`, `dtcg.rs` `path.join`); composite alias object keys uncharged (`kinds.rs` `inner`); resolver sibling overrides copied uncharged (`dtcg.rs` `deref`) and `deep`'s `a.clone()` charged after; `type_of` uncharged (its walk is linear now, iosfix's x2/import-linear, which also made re-import judge a proposal whole and look tokens up by name). From Grok's round 5 (`…-r5.grok.md`): `subsets` in `export.rs` materializes every subset of many one-value axes (walk combinations, cap axes); `reaches` in `dtcg.rs` per colour token; `deep`'s `token_like` clones the destination before it is charged. Left after round 9 (the lead's 14:09Z review cap; `llp/reviews/code-2026-10-19-s10b-e23-r9.*.md`): an import's per-client count is released when planning ends, not when its hold does (held proposals are capped at 64 and 32 MiB in all, a client below `write` making room only from its own); the design read's 2 s answer leaves its I/O running; the design's identity is the manifest's at the import, not the serve's own target (a rewritten app.json mid-run); the CLI's sources read synchronously before the planner (its own process, no server's); a probe in flight past the next tick could kill without waiting out `ps` (RSS overshoot during one probe); tests for slow snapshot readers, the Linux launch and a large real design, and for the design read's loop (a forced short read, a file growing mid-read, the 2 s deadline: theme2's 21:26 sign-off accepts them untested). (Done: an import stops with its request, its client's revocation or the server's close, leaving the queue at once; the design read to its end; `kept()` capped and never read through a link.)
- **`sortBy` with a computed direction** (2026-10-19, the euxy port's friction). The VM already takes the direction as a run-time bool (`Stdlib::SortBy`), so lowering could compile `dir == "desc"`; what stops it is the semantics, whose `sortDir` (`semantics/Contract/Eval.lean`, `Big.lean`'s `.sortBy` rule, `Lower.lean` and their proofs) reads a literal. Until then the refusal names `dir == "desc" ? sortBy(xs, f, "desc") : sortBy(xs, f)`.
- **The Figma plugin's leftovers after its review cap** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 §5 stage 10b E4, owner rt1; the lead's 14:09Z cap: after round 9 only security or data-loss findings block; `llp/reviews/code-2026-10-19-s10b-e4-r9.*.md`). In `tools/figma-tamber/src/code.test.ts`: rollback tests that snapshot mode ids, values and stored parts after a refused pull (the undo counts are asserted for the refusals the leftovers commit touched: the room refusals, the personal default, the migration). The real-Figma run (undo history, the 40-character and mode-limit errors) is the gate's. (Done: drops named by each live variable in each collection, a type change tested; the room refusal read once undone, naming the person's collection and modes, a personal default to drag below another, the parked default by its name; a replaced collection's mode said to delete, its values going; a person's mode named as an incoming context preflighted since round 18.)
- **A `pen` importer for Pencil's `.pen` files** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 §5 stage 10b E5, `~/x2theme/rt1-stage10b-e5-spike.md`). Pencil (pen.dev) keeps designs as JSON `.pen` files in the repo, with variables and themes, so `import {kind: "pen"}` could read one directly: its variables and themes to tokens and axes, as the Figma `<Collection>.<Mode>` importer maps, and a re-import's dials and pins as `dtcg`'s. The format is not documented publicly enough to map blind. Precondition: a sample `.pen` with themes from a real project. Until then, the agent bridge in `tools/figma-tamber/README.md` (Paper and Pencil) covers Pencil and Paper (Paper has no file, only a desktop-local MCP server).
- **Apple: a `tree` read straight after launch can precede the launch autofocus** (2026-10-18, LLP 1132 lane B's macOS starter proof). The presenter autofocuses the turn after the first activation (`releaseLaunchAutofocus`, `Accessibility.swift`), and `clock settle` waits for that (`launchQueued`, `Agent.swift`), but `tree`, `state` and `layout` do not: a fresh macOS session read Play as unfocused in its tree and as the logical focus in the `state` that followed. The game starter settles before its focus check; `tree` could drain launch work as settle does, so a first read agrees with the next.
- **Mac: an untinted path icon beside an untinted SF Symbol may draw in two greys** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 9 B on A, theme2 and the lead). A path icon with no tint and no written colour draws in its inherited colour (stage 9 A's rule), an SF Symbol in the platform's unsaid symbol colour (`unsaidSymbolTint`, `secondaryLabelColor` on the Mac; stage 9 B's), so one toolbar or row holding both can show two greys. Measure it on the Mac; if they differ visibly, untinted path icons on Apple take the platform's unsaid symbol colour too, or rule why not.
- **If the decorations editor stays: a decorated textarea's agent inspection on the web** (2026-10-19, the synthetic-decorations fix's review, Astra and Grok; LLP 1114.007 is under rework, Charlie bbcaf15d49, and the contentEditable editor may go). The JS target's tree reads a disabled editor (a div) as `accessibilityDisabled`, reports the editor's own `role="textbox"` as authored, and the wasm page's focus does not count the editor as the field (fixes reviewed and kept on branch `x2/deco-polish`). Neither web target reports applied ranges and provisional state in `state` (Linux's `state.decorations` does); the JS tree leaves out the `decorations` record; `select()` on the editor waits out the agent's 2 s select-all fallback; both contentEditable editors overwrite an authored `aria-disabled`.
- **Warm Beacons Rust-edit proof budgets** (LLP 1127.001, 2026-10-18): instrumented warm before/after samples were Linux 2.697 / 4.823 s and web 17.861 / 17.933 s, with maximum loads 38.18 / 39.86 and 35.93 / 37.63 respectively (sampled throughout, all below 50). The <3 s / <10 s budgets remain open; the earlier 4.362 / 25.396 s observations remain misses. Reusing the JS target's existing input-checked editor/text-flow products removes repeated Cargo/wasm-opt work: that phase fell from 1.038 to 0.170 s, without changing proof steps. There is no demonstrated full-loop speedup. Web's receipt/build span is 7.256 s (GPU 1.565, host 0.275, host wasm-opt 2.106, bindgen 0.174, input scans 0.402, shared leaves 0.170; the remaining 2.564 is tool/bake/packaging balance). Its proof is 10.518 s, including two browser opens 1.364 s (Chrome 0.846, page ready 0.149), 23 typing steps 6.868 s, 13 settles 0.274 s, other clock steps 0.158 s, taps 0.661 s and screenshots 0.564 s. Nested spans must not be added twice. Linux's receipt/build is 2.244 s (GPU 1.462, scans 0.501), proof 2.403 s; first Play varied from 0.177 to 1.805 s, with no cause established. Profile typing and first Play next; do not weaken the proof or change the budgets without Charlie's amendment.
- **App-supplied highlight ranges on LLP 1045's Markdown editor, for T3 Code's composer** (2026-10-18, LLP 1114.007 r3, Charlie's ruling). T3 Code's composer moves to `textarea markup="markdown"`, which refuses `decorations`; what it needs from app-computed ranges there (a language tag, a mention) is LLP 1045's to design, over that editor's own styling, never a second editor. Chips are LLP 1045.000's inline atoms (accepted 2026-10-23), built in its stages: 0, the TextKit 2 gate, measured 2026-10-23 (its §5.1: the view provider fails its lifecycle check on macOS, so the Apple plan waits on a ruling between the routes §5.1 proposes); 1, crates, Contract, kernel and the web editor and reader; 2, the Apple editor and reader, after that ruling; 3, the clone's adoption.
- **Paint a textarea's decorations on Android and the terminal** (2026-10-18, LLP 1114.007 r3 D5). Both keep the source and editing and paint nothing; Android as Compose `SpanStyle` colour over the field's `TextFieldValue` without changing its text, the terminal as each cell's colour and underline. Waits for a consumer on either.
- **`text-shadow` in a decoration on native hosts** (2026-10-18, LLP 1114.007 r3 D5). Only the web paints it. Check whether TextKit 2 draws a `.shadow` rendering attribute (iOS, macOS); the Linux field painter has no shadow for a run.
- **Status-lock recovery reads the lock's inode and its owner in two steps** (2026-10-18, LLP 1132 lane A, round 3). Between the reads an owner can release, another acquire and die, so the observer pairs the old inode with the new dead pid and can tombstone a live successor under another name. One immutable acquisition token (or the OS lock helper `withProofBuild` uses) would close it; test a generation change between the two reads.
- **Qualify the skinned/sprites fixture continuation-save pins** (2026-10-18, LLP 1132.A round 1). Linux and web produce identical saves, also byte-identical to direct source-directory Linux proofs, but `pins.json` expects different digests: skinned `432f2afd…` versus `13e8c9b1…`, sprites `bfe4bea3…` versus `4d451ecd…`. Tick, pixel, residency and restore assertions pass. Determine the intended save change and check every mode/profile before replacing these baselines.
- D2 quiet measurement: three cold `gpu-dev` runs under load 5 with `game/bench/cold-build/probe.py`; wire `keptGameCrates` into `buildBake` if the eligible registry chain is over a quarter (LLP 1132 D2).

- **Water's shafts pass under a lid** (2026-10-19, LLP 1139.002 D4). The shafts are screen space: their source is the surface where the frame sees it, so an opaque lid above the water (hidden behind the surface from below) shades the floor and its caustics but not the shafts. Sampling the sun's shadow cascades at each march step's surface point (binding group 1 in `shafts.wgsl`, one comparison a step) would stop them; measure its cost on a phone first.
- **Game baseline release builds remain dominated by path compilation and LTO.** LLP 1132.A2 measured 902.1 s under load 71–222; the cache-eligible registry dependency chain was 62.6 s (6.9%), so release does not use the registry cache. Measure those path/LTO phases on a quiet machine before choosing the next build change. The web dev producer's profile measurement belongs with LLP 1127.001 D1.
- **Finish dev-server build cancellation.** The resident loop owns both startup build trees. The ordinary JS loop (`host/web-js/dev.mjs`) still installs signal handlers after its first build, and the resident loop's later `hostBuildChild` rebuild receives only a PID-level SIGTERM. A signal after the startup builds but before `startup.handoff(stop)` exits without `killCompiler`, so producers started by `startCompiler()` can survive. Terminate those build trees and exercise shutdown during compilation.
- **iOS spinner, an AccentColor role under an authored accent: what the tests do not cover** (2026-10-18, LLP 1121 stage 9 F1, theme2's sign-off). `ProgressIOSTests` covers an authored colour and a system colour above the role in one view tree; not covered: presented content (a sheet, a popover, a pushed route, a grouped list's carried row: the walk takes their authored parents), an authoring view and the spinner in different appearances (resolved in the spinner's), and Highlight's `@tint/0.2` under a view tint (the authoring view's tint).
- **The Hermes installer refuses to run from a worktree inside another checkout** (2026-10-18). The command `exact new` and `build.mjs` print, `cargo run --manifest-path vendor/ibex/crates/hermes-lean-sys-installer/Cargo.toml`, fails in `.claude/worktrees/<lane>`: the lane's root `Cargo.toml` excludes `vendor`, so Cargo walks on to the enclosing checkout's workspace, which does not, and stops with "current package believes it's in a workspace when it's not". A copy of the crate with an empty `[workspace]` table and its `#[path]` made absolute installed the iOS simulator bundle. Give the vendored crate an empty `[workspace]` table (recorded in `vendor/ibex/EXACT-PATCHES.md`), or have the printed command build it outside the checkout.
- **iOS `tree --ax` lists views UIKit's accessibility leaves out** (2026-10-18, #721). XCTest's snapshot of a running app (what VoiceOver is served) dropped a zero-height view and everything under it, and kept them once the view was 1 point tall; the agent's walk (`AgentAccessibility.swift`, `children(of:)`) falls back to every subview, so it listed the buttons UIKit did not. Mirroring UIKit's rule would let `tree --ax` show the next such gap; the rule's extent (a zero width, a transformed or alpha-0 view) is unmeasured.
- **Apple: a node's own press colour removed while it is down is not taken over by a press above** (2026-10-18, LLP 1061 D7, stage 9 G4's review). A style write that drops `press_color` from a pressed node shows its engine colour, not the colour of a press still down above it, until its release (which re-inks from the nearest press above, `liveLender`). The rework under a new style deliberately re-inks nothing (re-inking there re-entered `presented`); if it matters, re-ink after the style is applied.
- **`exact-js`'s socket tests miss their 100 ms budgets under load** (2026-10-21, reload1's health sweep; was the WP14 workspace entry, whose weatherlight and exact-linux halves are fixed). In a full `cargo test --workspace --tests --no-fail-fast` beside other work, `socket::what_the_handle_refuses_sends_nothing_it_refused`, `what_an_answer_may_not_send` (`say`/`ask` took 107 ms, over the 100 ms budget) and `what_a_mapper_may_not_do` fail; alone they pass 3 of 3. A debug build's wall-clock budget is a load test: measure the budget's work, or give the tests a budget the debug build keeps under load.
- **A transport-driven timeline, so a playhead needs no re-sync task** (2026-10-18, euxy playing: 9–10% CPU with a frame task, 0.1% with `docs/contract-for-agents.md`'s "Following the transport"). The guide's lights are loops on a clock timeline (LLP 1055.002) whose phase the app sets from `exactTransport()` once per anchor: a gated `task … key=anchor` reads `performanceNow()`, and each light carries a negative delay. What that leaves to the app: a pause shows the head drawn as static nodes (the loops are removed); a one-off change at a beat restarts by alternating two identical `keyframes` names. A timeline whose time is the transport's position (`animation-timeline=transport`, ms along the beat grid, holding while stopped) would remove the task, the delays and the pause path. It adds a value to `animation-timeline`, so it needs Charlie's ruling.
- **iOS: a cover image whose decode moved its aspect still stretches while UIKit resizes it** (2026-10-18, #684's review, Astra and Sol). `applyImageLayer` (`IOS/BoxLayerIOS.swift`) crops `object-fit: cover` with `resizeAspectFill`, so it keeps its aspect through a UIKit resize animation, except where the decoded bitmap's rounded pixel sizes (`RasterDecodePlan`) move its aspect off the natural one by half a point or more across the fitted rect: those crop with an interpolated `contentsRect`, which stretches the picture mid-animation (about 11% halfway from 40×40 to 160×80 for a 4000×1001 picture decoded for 40 pt), and a resize that crosses the threshold switches the gravity at its start. A clip layer over a sublayer of the whole fitted rect (both animated by UIKit) would keep every fit exact.
- **A motion value's change is classed `rebuild` without knowing whether a row reads it** (2026-10-17, LLP 1121 stage 7 B, review round 2; the rest of that entry closed by stage 9 C). The safe class: a motion token or param no row reads still rebuilds. `uses`' instance sites (stage 9 C2) could tell; worth it only if a design tool measures a needless rebuild.
- **The iOS suite on an iPad simulator: 8 failures the iPhone run does not have, and one flaky sheet test** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 9 gate, on main `aa5cb16b1e`, iPad Pro 11-inch (M5), iOS 27). Failing on iPad only: FaceSymbolIOSTests.testNativeTabsPathIsTheirTabBarItemsAndSegmentsImage; InspectIOSTests.testATapOnTheTabBarSelectsTheTabUnderItAndItsEmptySpaceNothing, testATapSelectsTheNodeItLandsOnAndTheBarsDrawnTitleButNotUIKitsBack and testInspectModeEndsEditingAndTheStatusBarsScrollToTopTillItEnds; NavigationBasicsIOSTests.testARoutePushedWhileTheTablistIsHiddenHidesTheTabBar and testTheRootsTablistHiddenHidesTheTabBarAndShownBringsItBack; NavigationTabsIOSTests.testAppearanceNoneKeepsRootTabsAuthoredAndTheirStacksRetained; GroupedRevealTests.testRevealReachesACustomButtonInsertedByASheet. All but the last are the tab bar, which iPadOS draws as its own floating bar. On iPhone, PressFocusIOSTests.testADismissedSheetHandsNothingOn (hosted) failed once and passed in the iPad run and the next iPhone run: a sheet-dismissal timing flake.
- **`PressFeedbackIOSTests.testThePressEasesOnTheRenderServerFromWhatShows` is flaky on the iPhone simulator** (2026-10-20, iPhone 17 Pro, iOS 27.0): it failed once in four full `build.mjs --test --ios` runs on the same sources and passed in the other three. It times a press's easing on the render server; find what it waits on before trusting a red.
- **Lowering checks an attribute in an `if` arm it can prove is never taken** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 9 H, theme2 02:15). After a component is inlined with a constant prop, an element under `if symbol != ""` whose caller passes `symbol=""` is still checked, so a value only that dead arm would build (here `symbol:${symbol}` folding to `symbol:`) is refused. Stage 9 H made an empty `symbol:` name mean no symbol, as an empty `sf/` one already did, which covers that case. The general fix would skip value checks in an arm whose condition folds to false. It is broader than it looks, because it would also hide real value errors written there, so it waits for a decision on how dead arms should be reported.
- **History of a template row: a key a node keeps across versions** (2026-10-18, LLP 1121 stage 9 D2, theme2 17:18). `history` and `revert` read a `.contract` file's state rows and a style's own rows by name; a node's own attribute, which `set-row` writes at its place, has no key that survives a commit (lines move, no node id is kept), so a commit of such edits is code: left out of `history`, and `revert` can't invert it (the session's undo still gives the bytes back). A key of the component, the tag path to the node and the attribute's name, refused where ambiguous, would let both read it. A component's `params` lines (stage 9 D3's `add-param` and `remove-param`) are code in history too: their revert needs the template row that reads them, so they wait for that key, and then join by (component, name).
- **Apple: two icon tokens with one SVG name a face's path by the first declared** (2026-10-18, LLP 1121 stage 9 A2, theme2's 21:49 ruling). The source form carries no icon name (the compiler and the runner's bridge expand it away), so `icon.reply` and `icon.respond` on one file give one form, and the agent's `symbol` names the first declared. The cheaper real fix: a build lint when two icon tokens have identical sources (they are one icon; alias one to the other), which makes the name unambiguous without changing the source form.
- **Geolocation's options composed across readers on Apple** (LLP 1133.002 D5). Each request writes its own `desiredAccuracy`, `distanceFilter` and `activityType` to the one manager; D5 asks for the finest accuracy, the smallest filter, and `geolocation-options-conflict` for two readers' different `activityType`. Wants the runner to hand the module every live reader's options.
- **Geolocation's substitute has no approximate ceiling** (LLP 1133.002 D7, D14). Under the approximate line the substitute raises a `precision` ticket for `requestPreciseLocation`, which `allow` answers `precise`; the Apple and Android modules answer the status and ask nothing. D14 says an approximate app sees its ceiling under the agent; the substitute's `raise` wants a condition on the grant line.
- **Weatherlight's sky leaves a frame on Android** (Pixel 10a, Android 16, 2026-10-20). The sky `canvas` has `padding=26` and `background-color="#183e56"`; on Android the surface draws inside the padding, so a 26 px band of that colour frames the sky, where on iOS the sky fills the card to its rounded edge. One host disagrees with the other about the box a canvas surface fills.
- **Module use found in Rust and TypeScript** (LLP 1133 D6 rule 6). The plan names a module only when the Contract calls one of its names; a data-code-only caller's Contract has to read one, or the binding is missing and the runner refuses the call.
- **A routeless screen's `header` gets no warning that it is not the navigation bar** (2026-10-18, round 22's household-list). The builder started from `pattern rest`, dropped its `navigationKey` for one screen, and kept the `header` (a level-1 heading, a count, the persona button): with no route it is the author's own flex row, drawn in the page as written, and at accessibility-XXXL its count is cut off and the persona button runs off the screen, where a route's header would be UIKit's bar. `lower-header-nested` warns when a route's header is one box too deep; nothing warns when there is no route. A warning beside it (a `header` holding a `role="heading"` at the head of the root view of an app with no `navigationKey`) would name the repair; `contract/lower/src/warnings.rs` is at its line cap, so the check needs its own module and a line found there.
- **verify web's largest-text pass reports a header iOS draws as its navigation bar** (2026-10-18, round 22's apps). On the web a route's `header` is the author's row, so at a 53 px root its large title and buttons spill ("Flashcards" 207 px past its box, a sheet's Cancel and Create cut), and verify reports them; on iOS the same header is UIKit's bar, which truncates a title and sizes its buttons, and `verify ios` says nothing spills. Builders see findings on the web that the phone does not have (book-club, expense-tracker, flashcards and notes each did) and learn to discount the pass. The web's tree names neither a node's element (`header`) nor a route's screen (`navigationKey`), so verify cannot leave those out; with both it could skip what iOS's bar draws.
- **The macOS input's `Field`** (2026-10-18, LLP 1121 D9, stage 8 C's measurements, `docs/control-roles-measured.md`). AppKit's bezeled `NSTextField` draws its own ground on macOS 27: it ignores `backgroundColor`, and `drawsBackground = false` still leaves the bezel's white. So a design's `Field` reaches the Mac's textarea but not its input (`none`, tamber-unreachable names it). Possible: when `Field` is overridden, drop the bezel and let Exact paint the input's box, as an authored `background-color` already does. That changes the input's chrome (border, focus ring), which is theme2's call.
- **Flex line breaking compares floats where Chrome compares 1/64px layout units** (Chrome 151.0.7922.34, 2026-10-18, after Taffy patch 47). Each case is a `display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;align-content:flex-start` row in normal flow holding two `height:30px;flex-shrink:0` items. A `width:100px` row with `width:50.01px` items: Chrome 100×30 (one line, since 50.01 and 100 floor to 50 and 100 layout units), kernel 100×60. A `width:99.99951171875px` row with `width:50px` items: Chrome 100×60 (the width floors to 99.984375), kernel 99.9995×30; `width:99.9990234375px` likewise. With `width:50.02px` items in 100px, both give 100×60. Patch 47's fit test allows 1/1024px of excess so that decimal sizes (three `60.4px` items and a `0.1px` gap in `181.4px`, which Chrome keeps on one line) do not wrap on f32 roundoff. Comparing the line length and the limit as Chrome does, each item's outer size and the gaps floored to 1/64px before summing, would match all of these and would replace that tolerance. The same gap reaches Taffy patch 49's stretch test, which truncates the item's natural and stretched cross sizes to 1/64px but sums the natural height in f32: a normal-flow or absolute `display:flex;width:100px;max-height:110.105px` row holding a `width:100px;aspect-ratio:2` item whose children are `width:20px;height:100%` then two `width:20px;height:30.055px`. Chrome 151 gives the item 110.09375 and the percentage child 50, because Blink truncates each length (30.046875) before summing, so the cap equals the natural height. The kernel gives 110.105 and 110.105, treating the cap as a stretch; it was 50 before patch 49. Tried and parked on 2026-10-18 (branch `q24/flex-layout-units`, four commits, four review rounds): converting each item's components and the line limit to layout units inside `collect_flex_lines` fixes every case above, but Taffy only has the f32 sums, and each attempt to recover Blink's components from them regressed a case main and Chrome agree on. Measured in Chrome 151, branch against main: a `width:100px` block holding an auto-width wrapping row with `margin-left:.01px` and two `width:50px;height:30px;flex-shrink:0;margin-right:-.01px` items (Chrome and main one line, the branch two, because the parent's 99.99px arrives as an f32 border box); a `width:100px` wrapping row of two `display:block;flex-basis:50.01px;flex-shrink:0;height:30px` items each holding a `width:50.015625px;height:1px;margin:0 -.01px` child (Chrome and main two lines, the branch one, because a measured automatic minimum is f32); `aspect-ratio:0.7` items 100px high in a `139.984375px` row (an f32 ratio widened to f64 drops a unit); a flexed row whose f32 width equals its authored width by chance. Doing it right means carrying the sizes' layout-unit components from style resolution and from the parent (block layout's sums, `LayoutInput`'s known dimensions, measured content) into the flex algorithm, not reconstructing them in flexbox.rs; the branch's tests (`flex_line_*` in flexbox.rs's intrinsic_tests) and 17 Chrome rows are reusable.
- **A growing auto-height ratio text leaf freezes at its height cap** (Chrome 151.0.7922.34, 2026-10-19). A `display:flex;width:200px` row holding a text leaf with `aspect-ratio:1;max-height:60px;flex-grow:1` and an unbreakable word measuring 120px: Chrome gives the item 200×60; the kernel gives 60×60 on both `origin/main` at 8c841107e and the ratio max-height branch. The same row with a block child containing a 120×10 box grows to 200×60.
- **A definite-height ratio text leaf in a flex, grid or absolute chain is refused by exclusion flow** (2026-10-19, found by the review of Taffy patch 57, reproduced on main 8c841107e). A block holds a `position:absolute;wrap-flow:both;top:0;left:0;width:40px;height:40px` exclusion and a `display:flex;width:50px` row whose item is a text leaf with `height:80px;aspect-ratio:1;max-height:60px` and twelve `a`s: the item is 120×60, but `flow_skipped` names the leaf and its `flow_shapes` are cleared, so the paragraph is painted across the exclusion and the receipt asks for a height the text already has. Grid and absolute chains, and an exclusion whose parent is the flex or grid container (`FlowRefusal::Context`), fail the same way. The cause: the ratio content probe measures the leaf with an unknown height, which sets the leaf's `height_measured` flag (`kernel/src/layout.rs`) for the rest of the pass, and `kernel/src/flow.rs` then treats the paragraph as auto-height. Keeping the known height on the probe hides the text's content width (the 120), so the fix is to measure the leaf's width while leaving its height known, or to clear the flag once the final layout knows the height.
- **A ratio root grows to a stretched ratio item's overflowing content** (Chrome 151.0.7922.34, 2026-10-19). An auto-width `display:flex;aspect-ratio:1` root offered 50px, holding an `aspect-ratio:1` block with a `width:10px;height:70px` child: Chrome gives root and item 50×50, with the child overflowing. Main c8f5d6c96 gives root 50×70 and item 10×70; after patch 56 the item is 50×70 and the root remains 50×70. A grid root also predates patch 56: main gives root 50×70 and item 70×70 where Chrome gives both 50×50.
- **Percentage heights in an intrinsic ratio flex root resolve against no height** (Chrome 151.0.7922.34, 2026-10-19, found by the review of Taffy patch 60, measured on main b2b03034e and after the patch). Offered max-content (`Offer::MAX_CONTENT`; Chrome: a `width:max-content` wrapper), a `display:flex;aspect-ratio:1;max-width:200px` root holding one `width:60px;height:50%;flex-shrink:0` item: Chrome gives root 60×60 and item 60×30; the kernel gives the item 60×0 (main 60×0 too, with its root wrong as well), and 60×10 with `min-height:10px`. A `height:100%` item in a `display:flex;aspect-ratio:1` root: Chrome 60×60, kernel 60×0. With `width:60px` on the root both agree. Patch 60 refreshes the root's ratio height after intrinsic row sizing, but items were generated, and their percentage sizes resolved, while that height was still unknown; re-running `compute_preliminary` with the resolved outer width (as the min-content branch does) would let items see it.
- **The reader never tells a driver it is ready when the page draws nothing** (2026-10-19, `oracle.mjs --phone`). `AgentDrive` answers `ready` after `ExactView`'s first frame with ops, so an app whose first screen is all bars (a header and a tablist the bars take, an empty page) leaves `agent.mjs android --reader` waiting 20 s and failing. The Material specimens draw a line of page text outside their crops to step round it.
- **The Material oracle's findings, and what it still owes** (2026-10-18, LLP 1115.001.001 §3; `bun host/android/reference/oracle.mjs` on `expo-build-1000`). Found and open: the navigation bar places its destinations from the left under a right-to-left direction (`Layout::bottom` is told none), and where no palette is told its indicator and container are Exact's `ButtonFace` and grouped background, not Material's baseline (review `code-2026-10-18-material-navbar-r1`); a field's label floats on a 150 ms ease where Material 3 1.4 springs it (`MotionSchemeKeyTokens.FastSpatial`); a field's texts are 0.6 to 3.6 dp narrower than Material's (its type ramp's tracking, 1115.001's reported type ramp).
