Fixed: a disabled field's container; the navigation bar's colours, gaps and 56 dp indicator; a field's indicator on the pixel grid; the top bar's title after the Up arrow (56 dp, not 52); the checkbox, the radio and the filled button are Material's, drawn; a checkbox and radio lay out at 24 dp and every Material control takes a touch over 48 dp, apart from layout (§5 Q5, ruled 2026-10-19); the tonal, outlined and text buttons are not yet (D9); the door names a field's painted texts; the oracle reads Robolectric's Roboto as `fonts.xml` does (the "bold" title was Medium drawn for 400). O2 calibrated on one Pixel (thresholds in `oracle.mjs`; layout and text geometry not yet measured on the phone, nor a second Pixel). Owed: O3, accessibility and motion; O4, the hourly job with its release watch.
- **Material's 48 dp touch target, what three reviews left** (2026-10-19, LLP 1115.001.001 §5 Q5, `Presenter::touch_target`; reviews `code-2026-10-19-material-q5-r1` to `-r3`). (1) The target is chosen at the release (`press_at_after_down`) while a contact keeps only the geometric hit from the down: a slide from inside a checkbox into its own margin, under touch slop, changes the hit to the parent and cancels the press, and a control a `pointerdown` handler reveals beside the finger can take the release. Compose decides the target at the down and keeps it. Keep the touch target on the contact from the down, apart from the hit that gesture discovery and press feedback use. (2) A finger on no layout box (a 24 dp checkbox that is a whole root) reaches nothing, since a contact starts only on a hit; an earlier fallback that made the control the contact's hit broke press feedback and started its ancestors' pans from outside every box. (1) is the place to answer (2).
- **A Contract edit in an app with a Rust data crate still recompiles its archive** (2026-10-18; Caltrain, iOS simulator, a one-word edit: Release 58–64 s, development 7.5–8.7 s). A TypeScript app's Apple build bakes its plan before Cargo and links it (`scripts/linked-plan.mjs`, `exact_apple::baked`), and its archive compiles nothing for such an edit (Euxy Release 48 → 6.6 s). An app whose plan bakes through its own Rust (`contract::bake(plan, caltrain_data::Caltrain)` in `apps/*/apple/build.rs`, `exact_js_bake::build_mixed*`) still bakes in its build script, which Cargo reruns, and Cargo compiles again every crate whose build script ran. The same split would need that bake run outside Cargo: the build-script binary Cargo already compiled, run with its own OUT_DIR, and each such build.rs taking a baked directory as `exact_js_bake::build` does; the agent driver's plan-only path (`buildRefusal`) could then take these apps too.
- **Android module views: two gaps the module tap and event seam leaves** (2026-10-18, #707's review). (1) A dev reload or delivered update that gives a different control the same view id, slot, generation and module keeps the old `Natives` element and its event binding, so a popup opened before the restart sends its `change` to the new control; carrying a session epoch in the module props, the `under` words and `Native.moduleEvent` would retire them. (2) A second contact that lands before exact2 answers the first's `under` replays the first release as an ordinary one (`ExactView.forgetModuleTap`), so that tap's `performClick()` is lost; keeping each completed contact until its own answer, without forwarding the second down ahead of the first up, would keep it.
- **Android reader agent locale** (LLP 1126 D6): device configuration overrides `--locale`; honor the drive locale or make the refusal explicit. Per-app Android locales currently drive locale checks.
- **Linux desktop locale for dates** (LLP 1126 D6): `time` and `formatDate` format in English whatever the viewer locale.

- **`apps/llp`, `apps/exact-live` and `apps/markdown-stress` still draw a pipe table row by row** (2026-10-17). Each row is its own `row` of `view flex=1` cells, and a cell is never narrower than its longest word (CSS's `min-width: auto`), so a row whose words outgrow an equal share widens those cells and narrows the rest: columns stop lining up between rows, and a header of long words runs past the rows' rules. `apps/markdown` draws a table as one block and one grid (`markdown_parse::value::into_blocks_with_tables`, its `table` branch); these would line up the same way by reading that form, and `apps/markdown-stress` would then stress what `apps/markdown` draws.
- **Mac accessibility-client tests lose the first notification intermittently** (2026-10-17, macOS 27.0.1, LLP 1126 extraction verification). On unchanged `8d0e2303f` and the extraction, `AccessibilityClientMacTests` can receive no native `AXSheetCreated` at line 606 or no `AXMenuOpened` at line 400; the full baseline run can also pass. The isolated menu test fails identically on both. Investigate observer readiness and replace the fixed notification window with an event-based wait before treating these as presenter regressions.
- **What Android 10 and 11 still lack** (2026-10-17, a Galaxy Note 9 and a Pixel 5; `host/android/README.md`, "Android 10 and 11"). (1) Android 10's overscroll glow is not drawn, so a scroller's end shows nothing where a hand-built Android 10 app glows: `EdgeEffect` draws the glow below API 31, only its distance and the stretch are Android 12's (`Scrolling.STRETCH`). (2) Outline text carries 1 to 4 % more ink (by coverage) than `drawGlyphs`' atlas glyphs on the same phone, differing along glyph edges only. (3) A COLRv1 glyph is drawn from its plain outline below API 31 (swash renders COLR version 0 and bitmap strikes), which Noto Color Emoji's COLRv1 build leaves empty; Android 10 and 11's own emoji are CBDT and draw. (4) Not tried: Android 11 (no device), and a TypeScript data module, whose Android Hermes bundle is built at API 30.
- **A flex text's lines, what `Kernel::text_line_offset` leaves** (2026-10-19; the Apple hosts and the Linux painter place a flex-container text's lines as CSS's anonymous flex item now, so a grouped section's bare `text` row is centred). Left: a parent's `align-items: baseline` reads the text's baseline before the offset, so a centred flex text beside a baseline-aligned sibling sits off it; a text that is a grid container draws from its top; a flex text fragmented across columns takes no offset.
- **Mac: a native list row's pressed paint** (2026-10-17, LLP 1061 D7's gap). A row of a list drawn as an `NSTableView` shows the table's selection on a press, never its `-exact-press-opacity`/`-background-color`/`-color`: the table tracks the click (`TableRowCell.hitTest` → `TableFeed.keepsClick`, `pressRow`, double-click), so `NodeView.pressed` never turns. Drive the row's `pressed` from the table's click tracking and suppress only its selection highlight, keeping selection, first responder and `pressRow`.
- **What a package's design can't do yet** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 11 A1; theme2's rulings Q1, Q6 and Q7; its icon tokens landed in stage 13). (2) References across packages: a package token names its own package's tokens, roles and base names only, never another package's (`tamber-unknown-token`), even one it depends on. (3) Scoped namespaces: a package's tokens are named by the last part of its name, so two linked packages `@a/reading` and `@b/reading` that both ship a design are refused (`tamber-package-name`); if a real case appears, names could carry the scope. (4) DTCG export: `export` folds the app's design file alone, so an app whose design names a package token can't export (`tamber-unknown-token`); an app-only document needs the join with the package tokens left out.
- **A package's multicolour (non-template) SVG icon on the web** (2026-10-22, LLP 1121 stage 13; theme2's 03:22 ruling Q2 (a)). A package's icon SVG is read from `<package root>/assets/`, and in v1 it must be a one-colour template (`tamber-icon-svg` otherwise), drawn from the plan's inline path on every host. The web alone draws a non-template file as written, as `assets/<svg>` from the app's staged `assets/`, and no web build stages a package's `assets/`. When a package needs a multicolour icon: stage each package's `assets/` under `assets/packages/<ns>/` (host/web/build.mjs, dev.mjs and serve.mjs, host/web-js/build.mjs) and give the plan's `svg` field that path. On Apple a multicolour file still needs an `sf` source (the Apple entry below).
- **The packages' path icons, to icon tokens** (2026-10-22; theme2's 03:17 and package-icons sign-off). `@exact/cards`' repost, pin and reply marks and ProfileHeader's alert pills and badge, and `@exact/actions`' row and composer icons, are path functions in each `common.contract` drawn with `Glyph`; with package icon tokens landed, both move to icon tokens in their `.tamber` in one change (x2-perf4 with iosfix, 2 rounds, pixels identical on the copy's profile screens and library-gate's cells).
- **A package SVG read across three swaps** (2026-10-22, LLP 1121 stage 13's narrow re-check, Astra RE-3; contrived). `contract/lower/src/icons/confined.rs` opens a package's SVG, resolves its path again and compares the resolved file's device and inode with the descriptor's. An attacker who can write the package's `assets/` and swaps the path three times inside a few system calls (a link out of the package when it is opened, a file inside when it is resolved, a link out again before the resolved file's metadata is read) passes the check: the resolved path is the SVG's own, whose metadata, read after the third swap, follows the link to the file already opened, so the outside bytes are read. Binding the walk with `O_NOFOLLOW` and `openat` per component (or `lstat` of the resolved path against the descriptor) would close it, if a real case appears.
- **Package-file layouts outside the read-only guard's definition** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 11 A2 part 2; theme2's ruling 21:32 defines a package file by real path: a named package.json above it in the app's tree, a node_modules segment, or a root the app's load resolved; the server also refuses under any directory a link inside a node_modules points to, walking the app's tree and the node_modules above it, failing closed past 400,000 directories or on a directory it can't list). Not covered, by that ruling: (1) `app/node_modules` itself a link to a directory outside the app whose entries link back into it (`app/node_modules → /shared/deps`, `/shared/deps/@acme/reading → app/vendor/reading`, no manifest, app.json's "tamber" naming `vendor/reading/reading.tamber`): writable. (2) A Contract source under such a manifest-less linked directory, imported by a relative `use`: stage 8 D's `owned()` (contract/cli/src/tamber_contract.rs) checks only the app's own top-level node_modules, so `set-row`/`set-state`/`add-param` write it. (3) Journal completion after a crash renames an already-checked write without checking again, so a link made in that window isn't seen. Each needs a layout no resolver produces for the app; fix if one appears in practice.
- **Apple: what euxy's tabs still cost over its UIKit port, about 1.8 MB** (2026-10-17, iOS 27 simulator, Release; idle → every tab shown → back on the first: Exact +9.2 MB, UIKit +7.4). Per kernel node, the host's `Mirror` (`host/apple/src/host.rs`) holds its style JSON shared with every view that heard the same text (2026-10-18, `StyleTexts`; a screen whose styles differ per node still pays about 640 bytes a paragraph), and taffy's per-node layout cache is 128 bytes plus the measure slots used (448 before 2026-10-18). Per `NodeView`, its `registerForTraitChanges` costs UIKit about 0.8 KB (registry, registration, records). Measured with `heap` and `MallocStackLogging=lite` against a tour that switches tabs through `ExactTabs` (AXe's taps load UIAccessibility into the app, which then walks every view: +10–14 MB in both apps).

- **Android native fields omit tabular-digit styling.** `host/linux/src/fields.rs`'s `FieldFace` and Android's `Fields.kt` do not forward `font-variant-numeric` to hosted `EditText` measurement or drawing. Painted text applies `tnum`; native fields need the same feature carried through both paths.
- **conform's `synthetic-media` seek count differs between the wasm and JS targets in a full run** (2026-10-17, a 119-target `host/web-js/conform.mjs` run on 757cf1917). `tap other` reported `slots.seeks` wasm 4, JS 3 (and `slots.fresh` wasm false, JS true), with the tree's seek counter following; the target passed when run alone (`--synthetic --only media`). A timing flake under the full run's load, not yet traced: likely a `seeked` event landing either side of the step's state read.

- **The hosts disagree on pressing a link with no `press`** (2026-10-18, LLP 1061, Charlie's; found in LLP 1121 stage 8 E1, theme2's 11:48 ruling). A pressable link (`link` or `button` with an `href`, no `press`): Apple sets `pressed` for it (`handlers.contains("press") || defaultLink != nil`), so D7's pressed paint shows, and the platform's dim when no press look is written; Linux and Android press only a node with a `press` handler (`handler_target(view, EventKind::Press)`); the web presses its `<a>` only by touch (the input glue's pointerdown selector), not by mouse. A `text` link is pressed nowhere. So `:active` on a pressless pressable link shows on Apple and on a touch browser only; `lower-state-dead` says so. `hold active` follows a `press` handler on the web (and, with stage 8 E2 and E3, each host's real press: Apple's takes the link). One rule for all hosts is LLP 1061's to decide.
- **A request across a dist swap can mix two builds** (2026-10-18, rt1/theme2; the swap itself is one step now, `replaceBuild`'s directory exchange). A page that reads several files of a build (its index, then its assets, plan or module) while a dev loop puts the next build in place can get the new index with the old build's files, or the reverse. It was so before the exchange too. A generation pointer (`dist` a link to a numbered directory, each request resolving it once) would close it, but the filesystem helper opens every root with `O_NOFOLLOW` and refuses a linked dist, so about fifteen callers would pass `realpath(dist)` first (rt1's survey, 14:19).
- **A multicolour (non-template) SVG icon on Apple** (2026-10-18, LLP 1121 D10, stage 9 A; theme2's 17:03 (a)). Apple draws an icon's template path or its SF Symbol; a file of more than one colour still needs an `sf` source for Apple targets, because Apple has no SVG file renderer outside an asset catalog. Rasterise the file at build time into the asset catalog (each scale) so `multicolor` draws it on Apple as the web does.
- **Every `exact.mjs ios` of an outside app replays two Cargo warnings of Exact's: 2.8 KB of a 3.3 KB warm build's output** (2026-10-17, LLP 1116.021 D3's lane; `host/apple/quiet.mjs` takes out only Exact's Swift warnings). Cargo replays a path dependency's build-script warnings on every build: ibex2's `-Wreorder-ctor` at `vendor/ibex/crates/ibex2/src/bindings/install.cc:284` (the `Integrity` initializer list is not in declaration order; reordering it changes nothing, members initialise in declaration order) and the generated `[patch.crates-io] wgpu-hal` that an app with no GPU module never uses ("was not used in the crate graph"). The bake's Cargo output is inherited (`scripts/app.mjs` `buildBake`), so neither can be filtered there without piping it.
- **A running transition is not cancelled when its `transition-property` stops covering it** (2026-10-16, CSS Transitions §3). `Engine::observe` keeps a running or played transition whose target is unchanged whatever the declaration says now. Cancelling it needs the kernel to tell `transition: none` from an unset row: both reach the engine as an empty list (`kernel/src/motion.rs`), and an unset row is CSS's `all 0s`, which still covers the property and keeps the transition. The web hosts' spring lowering (`host/web/src/motion.rs`, `host/web-js/motion/src/lib.rs`) cancels a WAAPI animation only when the value changes, so a cancellation at an unchanged target would leave the browser's animation running.
- **Apple: a played `d` morph is not taken back when its path stops being one Core Animation can draw** (2026-10-16). `svg_lower::hand_over_paths` judges only running transitions, so markers or a paint server added to a path while its morph plays, with `d` unchanged, leave the Core Animation spec on it while the markers are placed at the target. An unlanded draft LLP (written as 1126, a number LLP 1126, system surfaces, has since taken) takes up when a render-server animation's context changes.
- **A Rust update that changes the Rust blanks the app's Rust-drawn canvases until the next binary** (2026-10-16, Caltrain on an iPhone through AppDrop: the line map lost its white background, stations and line while release 2 renamed a station in Rust). A replaced module is a `Swappable` with no embedded crate, and its `draw` answers "a replaced Rust module does not draw Canvas 2D yet" (stage 1 owes, (2) below). Since `d1de59c31` an update whose Rust is the binary's own runs the native crate and draws, so only an update that changes the Rust shows it. The fix: a draw operation on the logic ABI (a new ABI version; older modules keep the rest): the module records with `exact_canvas`'s recorder and returns the list, which the host replays as it replays a TypeScript draw (`exact_canvas::seam`). Then check Caltrain's line map with a Rust update on macOS (native dylib) and iOS (wasm).
- **A GPU module beyond the primary that owns shaders breaks the primary on Linux and Android** (2026-10-16, Astra's review of the Android shader packaging). The shader pack is the app's whole inventory (`scripts/app.mjs` `shaderFiles`), and both hosts register all of it into the primary module only: Linux's rendered path through `surfaces/shaders.rs` (`read_pack`, `artifact.is_empty()`), Android's through `surfaces/android.rs` `load`. A module refuses a name it does not reflect (`Module::set_shader`), so a shader that only an extra module owns makes the primary's registration fail, and the extra module gets none. Android also stages no extra module's library (`buildApk`). The fix needs a per-module shader roster, from the bake's compat or an ABI call.
- **A per-axis `scale` (`scale="0 1"`, CSS's x and y)** (2026-10-16, euxy's keep bar, FRICTION 9). Refused on a row (`expected number`) and in a keyframe (`lower-keyframes`, which says so): the kernel's `scale` row is one `f32` (`kernel/tables/schema.json` bit 61) and `exact_motion::Property::Scale` a scalar, so it is the row's codec (vec2), the motion value, the present op's wire, and each host's transform (`PresenterIOS`/`PresenterMac` `v.scale`, Linux `paint/presented.rs`, the web hosts), with press scale folded per axis. The workaround a porter takes is a bar translated inside a clip.
- **The Lean components semantics omit a child's device-fact resources** (2026-10-20, Astra's review of children reading `exactViewport()`/`exactPage()`). The inliner hoists a child's device-fact resource into the root as `name@Component` (`contract/syntax/src/inline.rs` `hoisted`); `contract lean` emits it, but `contract lean --components` (`contract/cli/src/lean/components.rs`) emits resources for the root only and leaves the child reading its own name, and the Lean expander has no hoisting, so an expansion comparison of such a program disagrees. `contract-difftest` generates none yet.
- **A hand-written swipe row in a grouped list, the forms still styled on the scroll** (2026-10-19, `contract/lower/src/swipe_rows.rs` `written`). A grouped section's `scroll swipeContent=…` whose content is a direct child with the same `id` expression is styled as `delete=`'s is: the content is the row (its inset, subtitle and accessory rules), the scroll takes its box in the card. Content inside wrappers (Messages' inbox shape) is read too, by lowering and by the kernel's `grouped/separator.rs` `swiped`. Content in an `if` or `match` arm is read too. Still styled as the row on the scroll: content produced by a `for` in the scroll (several elements of one `id`), an `id` that is another expression evaluating to the same name (known only when it runs), a scroll whose author padded its leading edge or that has a `class`, and two elements with the same `id`.
- **The agent's `--touch platform` tap on a header search field is refused** (2026-10-16, LLP 1116.019, round 17's contacts): "tap #17: GroupedCollectionView, in node #18, covers its middle". It aims at the field's frame in the page, not UIKit's `UISearchBar` in the bar; a plain `type` sets the value without activating the search controller. The contacts fix was seen with the touch runner's taps at points (`host/apple/touches.mjs`, `{"op":"tap"}`), on the keyboard too.
- **Check `3af249aa7`'s native module settle against what a parallel build's reviews found** (2026-10-16; branch `q1013/w5-native-module-settle` on the Mac Studio built the same thing and was parked after four review rounds). Each was reproduced there; whether `3af249aa7` has it is not checked: a state change between the candidate's preparation and its commit is overwritten by the prepared carry (`count` 1 back to 0); the local mixed-module receipt is rebuilt with `JSONSerialization` without `.sortedKeys`, so a retried pending load's identity can change; several busy sessions wait one after another (`applyTogether` returns at the first pending one), so N sessions take N seconds; and an established, delivered stream is counted as a request in flight in the let-go log (`pending()` where `in_flight()` is meant).
- **Android's pickers and Kotlin hatches, what LLP 1115.001.000 leaves** (2026-10-16, on a Nothing Phone (4a) Pro). (1) A Material field's press is a state layer, not the ripple (1115.001 D9's press, owed for every control). (2) A view a Kotlin hatch hosts takes no touches (as a native module's view on this host). (3) Where Compose is not linked, the framework's dialogs ignore `accent-color`. (4) Not measured on a Pixel, nor with a date control on an app's first screen. (5) `contract verify apps/native-fixture/app.contract` diverges at boot on main too (the `tabs` element: runner `tabs`, Lean `route-second`).
- **LLP 1125 stage 3 (MIDI), what was not run** (2026-10-16): nothing was sent to a real device or the macOS IAC bus (none present), Chrome's Web MIDI was not tried by hand, and the Apple arm's 3 s watchdog and iOS foreground re-enumeration have no test. Two edge cases the last review confirmation left (LLP 1125 As built, stage 3): the Apple arm and `midi-glue.js` forget handed note-on ids after about a second, so a retraction delayed past that (a stop begun while the main thread stalls) retracts a note-off whose note-on already sounded — keep ids until their paired note-off is sent; and an edit at exactly the beat where a full queue parked the fill keeps that beat's skip (`transport.rs` `fill`, `transport.js` `fill`), so a note the edit retracted there is not re-issued — reset the skip when the edit retracts at that beat.
- **The data module's Hermes heap: what is left after boot's collections and the between-calls cap** (2026-10-17, `js/src/shim.cc`, `Adapter::harden`). Hades keeps every young-generation page it has touched and no `GCConfig` field bounds it, so the shim collects before the freeze, between its guard and `harden.js` (vendor/ibex/EXACT-PATCHES.md), after it, and between calls once the module has allocated at least 512 KB and as much as it holds live. At idle after launch the young generation keeps 528 KB in the template and in euxy (Release, iPhone 17 simulator), about 80 KB of it the segment's own mark bits and cards, the rest the guard's and `harden.js`'s 0.45 MB each; a session of dice rolls in euxy holds it near 720 KB where it reached 3.6 MB. A collection right after the runtime is created saves nothing (measured): the runtime's own 0.26 MB is mostly old generation. Left: (1) the render host's fresh realm per page pays the boot collections (made while a worker is idle) and, for a page whose module allocates past 512 KB, one collection on the request path, with no memory to save since the realm is dropped; measure RealWorld's CPU a page and skip them there if it shows. (2) An answer's reply is serialized with the capture replacer (`ok` in `js/src/prelude.js`), which makes a key string per array element and an ancestor record per object: euxy's seeded library (22 KB as the runner keeps it) allocates 151 KB to settle. A plain `JSON.stringify` first, falling back to the replacer only when the text is 64 Ki characters or longer, would avoid that, but runs getters and `toJSON` twice for such replies. (3) Below 528 KB idle needs a smaller guard or `harden.js` allocation, or a lean VM built with a smaller `HERMESVM_LOG_HEAP_SEGMENT_SIZE` (a CMake cache variable, default 22; objects past a segment go to jumbo segments). Measured 2026-10-18 on euxy (Release, iPhone 17 simulator, the iOS-simulator lean VM rebuilt from the pinned receipt's flags plus that option, linked through `HERMES_LEAN_SYS_DIR_aarch64_apple_ios_sim`): the heap's two segments 1120 KB dirty at 22, 928 KB at 20, 864 KB at 19, and `EUXY_TTI` unchanged (five interleaved launches each, medians 1284, 1285, 1288 ms). Shipping it is a release outside this repo: in expo/ibex, add `-DHERMESVM_LOG_HEAP_SEGMENT_SIZE=20` to the common CMake arguments of `scripts/build-hermes-vanilla-release.sh` and `scripts/build-hermes-windows-vanilla-release.ps1` (it is one option for both VMs), move `RELEASE_TAG` in `.github/workflows/hermes-vanilla-publish.yml` to `-v6`, dispatch `hermes-vanilla-build.yml` on main and let the publisher (environment `hermes-vanilla-release`) attest and publish; then here, the new tag and eleven digests in `vendor/ibex/crates/hermes-lean-sys/build_support.rs` (`RELEASE_TAG`, `PINNED_BUNDLES`), the bundle line of `vendor/ibex/EXACT-PATCHES.md`, and the tests that name the tag. No `GCConfig` setting releases young-generation pages: `ShouldReleaseUnused` is read nowhere in `lib/VM`, and `HadesGC::youngGenCollection` resets the segment with `AdviseUnused::No`; patched to `Yes`, the dirty size did not move. (4) Hardening's readers are not all captured before bootstrap: `harden.js` reads `Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor`, `getOwnPropertyNames` and the rest when it starts, and the deferred intrinsic snapshot its readers when `capture_intrinsics` runs, so a bootstrap that replaces one with a function that lies is believed (the guard's are captured when `install_with` builds `Reachability`). Capturing them at adapter construction and refusing to harden when the live ones differ would close it (Grok, review of the 2026-10-17 memory change). (5) Why `AdviseUnused::Yes` moved nothing: the lean VM's `oscompat::vm_unused` is `madvise(…, MADV_FREE)` (5, disassembled from the pinned iOS-simulator `libhermesvmlean_a.a`), which on Darwin leaves the pages in the footprint until the system needs them; `MADV_FREE_REUSABLE` (7) takes them out at once, as `releaseStackBelowCaller` does. A VM patch that advises the young generation unused with that after the shim's full collections could give back most of what it has touched at idle (euxy port 12, 2026-10-19: 53 of its segment's pages, 848 KB, about 80 KB of them its own mark bits and cards). That is a Hermes release outside this repo, as (3) is, for about three times (3)'s saving.
- **What euxy's first screen still costs on iOS that its UIKit port does not** (2026-10-16, euxy memory lane, Release, iPhone 17 simulator, idle 10 s after launch: Exact 26.7 MB, UIKit 21.1; later 2026-10-18, a box of flat boxes is one layer tree (LLP 1068 §6.3): `NodeView`s 243 → 116, 0.3 MB less; measure on a simulator no agent drive has touched since it was erased, since a drive leaves accessibility loaded in every app it launches after (about 8 MB more for each, UIKit's port included); 2026-10-18, 25.7 MB against 20.4 (medians of 10 and 5): of the 5.3 MB, malloc 2.75 MB (Rust and Hermes heap 2.85 MB more than UIKit's, NodeView 292 KB, style dictionaries and strings about 0.3 MB, against UIKit's Auto Layout), the Hermes heap 1.1 MB, thread stacks 0.4 MB (an 8 MB owner stack 176 KB dirty, two raster workers, three Rust threads), the executable's `__DATA` and `__DATA_CONST` 0.54 MB, a vImage colour-conversion cache 128 KB that a `CABackdropLayer` filter image makes at commit, and `Network` 80 KB (the app hatch's `onLine` fact starts the path monitor). Text rasters equal UIKit's label backing stores. The larger Rust items, after the 2026-10-18 cuts (the mirrors' style texts shared, 322 KB to 122 KB; the site index's constant cells kept only for the bindings evaluated, 96 KB; taffy's caches allocating measure slots as used, 137 KB to 50 KB): the plan's tables and 1,331 strings decoded from the linked plan (0.21 MB: 64, 48 and 32 KB table vectors, 40 KB of strings in 1,331 allocations under a 32 KB `Vec<String>`; a pooled `Strings` type would save about 45 KB and reading them in place from the static bytes 80 KB, across some 60 `plan.strings` sites, most in tests), the runner's site index (0.19 MB) and realized instances (0.3 MB), the kernel's 94 interned `StyleProps` at 896 bytes (82 KB), the node arena's and taffy's node vectors (0.23 MB, up to half of it growth slack); Swift's: CSS animation specs kept as `[String: Any]` per animated node (115 dictionaries, 0.1 MB, and their lowered arrays).) Beyond the Hermes heap (above; 2.4 MB dirty then, 1.4 MB since 2026-10-17, plus 0.6 MB of its malloc) and the executable's dirtied `__DATA`/`__DATA_CONST` (1.3 MB more), one smaller thing is Exact's own: each `NodeView` registers for trait changes itself (`_UITraitChangeRegistry`, about 64 KB over euxy's 134 views). Text rasters are not it: ink-cropped BGRA bitmaps (0.74 MB) against UILabel backing stores, 1.18 MB of both kinds of pixels to UIKit's 0.99 (A8 needs a mask layer, below). 2026-10-19, after the bindings' bytecode read where it is linked (91 KB), the owner's stack under the boot given back (144 KB), the plan's strings read in its bytes and the VM's string values made as read (the binary's own malloc 2.87 to 2.68 MB): 26,210 to 25,730 KB (`footprint` bytes, medians of 5 interleaved launches on a quiet machine; 26,210 to 25,874 over 10 pairs), `EUXY_TTI` 1181 to 1175 ms (medians of 10). Measured there and left: the app's own `apple/src/lib.rs` still passes `BYTECODE.to_vec()` (76 KB; an app made before 8471fb5f9 keeps copying until its entry passes `APP_BYTECODE`, and `exact.mjs update` does not rewrite it); the executable's `__DATA_CONST` (304 KB) and `__DATA` (19 of 23 pages) are dirtied by fixups and ObjC metadata, so only fewer pointer-bearing constants would cut them; the runtime `Entry` is one 48 KB allocation (`Bridge` holds `Host` and a `PreparedHost` inline); taffy's slotmaps grow by doubling in `new_leaf` (80, 20 and 14 KB buffers); the kernel's 94 interned `StyleProps` at 896 B; Hermes keeps 105 chunks of pinned JSI values (47 KB) from the boot's peak; two raster workers start with the first image source and sit on 16 KB each. 2026-10-19, later, against the SwiftUI port on one fresh simulator (`footprint`, medians of 5 launches in 5 interleaved rounds): 25.17 MiB to 24.33, SwiftUI's 24.91; `EUXY_TTI` 1153 to 1133 ms (medians of 10, three rounds); playing 0.1% CPU either way. The cuts, in `malloc_history` against SwiftUI's: CoreUI kept every symbol the kernel measured, its glyph's SVG documents about 120 KB a symbol (SwiftUI's whole CoreUI is 1.05 MB), so symbols in a box covered whole (the closed pattern menu's six) are measured when a layout places it, and the root tablist a tab bar replaces is covered from boot (`closed.rs` `cover_root_tablist`; the presenter adopts the cover or gives it back), which also ends `ButtonMeasureCache`'s tab probes; `GCMouse` and its notifications are asked only on an iPad or a Mac (GameController's keyboard and mouse service, 0.26 MB of malloc and 90 KB of `__DATA`); the raster workers and Ibex's host-task workers give their stacks back when they go idle (Ibex local patch 17); the launch image's digest is CommonCrypto's (CryptoKit's `__DATA`, 40 KB). What Exact still spends beyond SwiftUI, by `footprint` category: text rasters in `CG Raster Data` 0.62 MB, less SwiftUI's 0.48 MB more `CoreAnimation` backing stores; malloc 0.34 MB (`NodeView` 285 × 1,280 B, 365 KB, its stored properties beyond `UIView`'s; Rust 1.5 MB and Hermes 0.4 MB, of which taffy's slotmap doubling 0.16 MB, 30 hashbrown tables 0.15 MB, the kernel's interned `StyleProps` 93 KB, the runtime `Entry` 48 KB, Hermes' pinned-value and weak-slot chunks 92 KB; `Presenter.setLanguage` gives every view an `accessibilityLanguage`, an associated object each, 59 KB); stacks 0.18 MB (the owner's, the raster workers', Hades', Hermes' finalizer executor's, `exact-io-0`'s); the vImage cache 128 KB; the executable's `__DATA_CONST` 0.13 MB more. SwiftUI's own `__DATA` is 1.6 MB more than Exact's. `Network` (80 KB of `__DATA`) is the app hatch's: `appFacts` reads `onLine`, which starts the path monitor, whether or not a hatch reads it. 2026-10-19, euxy port 12 against its UIKit port on one fresh simulator (25,138 against 20,162 KB, `footprint` medians of 5): the Hermes heap 1.37 MB and its malloc 0.4 MB (its young generation's touched pages are (5) of the entry above); Rust's malloc 1.42 MB (runner 340 KB, kernel 323, taffy 208, plan 168, `exact_apple` 158, triomphe 113, `exact_num` 58, Ibex 52); the Sequencer tab's navigation controller 0.93 MB (the entry at the top); the presenter's Swift objects about 0.9 MB (`NodeView` 250 × 1,280 B, style dictionaries and their `BatchValue` arrays 186 KB, CSS animation specs 70 KB, strings 102 KB) against 0.3 MB more of UIKitCore's own in the UIKit port; Swift's runtime metadata 0.35 MB, mostly the navigation controller's SwiftUI; the executable's `__DATA` and `__DATA_CONST` 0.64 MB; stacks 0.16 MB; the keyboard machinery 0.11 to 0.22 MB (the first-responder entry below); the vImage cache 128 KB; `Network` 80 KB. Text is in Exact's favour: its `CG Raster Data` 0.4 MB more, the UIKit port's label backing stores 1.0 MB more. The UIKit port loads its audio engine at launch (about 0.2 MB) and links no GameController, whose images cost Exact nothing dirty. 2026-10-19, later (euxy port 12, Release, a fresh iPhone 17 simulator, `footprint` medians of 5 interleaved launches, each change against the one before): 25,090 to 24,626 KB in four cuts. The path monitor starts at the first read of `onLine` (the plan's, or a module's `app.onLine` through the hatch host table's `on_line`), so `Network` is no longer loaded: -161 KB (`__DATA` -109). A node answers `accessibilityLanguage` from its presenter, as UIKit stored it per view: -80 KB (20 KB of it malloc). A `NodeView` is a 768-byte block, from 1,024 (its flags together, the transform, image, paint ghosts and scroll extent in `NodeAside`, the paragraph raster in `TextSpecs`, one shared first-draw receipt): -80 KB (102 KB of malloc). A box's CSS animations are kept as `CssAnimation` structs, not the batch's dictionaries: -144 KB (70 KB of malloc). `EUXY_TTI` 1265 to 1251 ms (medians of 10), playing CPU 0.075 to 0.05 %. Tried and dropped: reserving a batch's creates in the node arena and Taffy measured 176 KB worse, since the first screen's batch is followed by smaller ones that double the exact capacity past the power of two the pushes reached; it needs the final count, which the kernel does not know at the first batch. 2026-10-20, euxy port 15 against its UIKit port on one fresh simulator (`footprint` bytes, medians of 5 interleaved launches: UIKit 21,317,936, Exact 24,643,960): only a `color-scheme` boundary overrides its appearance (the root's scheme was inherited by every node and each of 312 views carried its own trait overrides, trait collections and associated object) and `applyProps` sets accessibility properties only on change: 24,021,368, `EUXY_TTI` 1167 to 1165 ms (medians of 10), playing CPU 0.1% either way. Tried and dropped: watching a tab stack's pops at its first show and laying out only loaded stacks in `replayHatches`, so a tab not shown loads no navigation view: 166 KB less live malloc (`malloc_history`), yet the footprint median 130 KB higher over 10 interleaved pairs. What is left beyond the UIKit port, by `footprint` category: malloc 2.1 MB (Rust 1.8 MB: runner 565 KB, kernel 518, plan 208, `exact_apple` 195, taffy 177, `exact_js_value` 129; Hermes 0.4 MB; NodeView instances 312 × 768 B); the Hermes heap 1.1 MB (the entry above); the executable's dirty `__DATA` and `__DATA_CONST` 0.57 MB more than the port's (Swift type metadata about 0.3 MB; Rust's relro 144 KB, of which the kernel's generated `METRICS`, 171 rows of `&str` cells, is 34 KB: names and text cells as offsets into one string would leave it in `__TEXT`); stacks 0.26 MB (`exact.owner` 64 KB, two raster workers 32 KB each, `ibex2-host-task` 32 KB, `exact-io-0`, `exact.kept`, Hades and Hermes' finalizer executor 16 KB each, all idle after boot); the vImage cache 128 KB, which is UIKit's scroll edge effect (a 1 × 384 Generic Gray mask converted at commit) under euxy's scroller, the platform's own. The larger Rust items: the plan's 27 table vectors decoded from the linked plan (153 KB; read in place they would cost nothing), the node arena's columns and Taffy's three slotmaps grown by doubling (85 and 114 KB), the site index's `Deps` (186 KB), the data module's reply values (110 KB of triomphe arrays), the kept answer copied into `Store::values` (48 KB), the 90 interned `StyleProps` at 896 B (88 KB). Of the hashbrown tables, Taffy's `node_context_data` is the largest (48 and 12 KB: a `SparseSecondaryMap` of 72-byte `MeasureContext`s, one per measured leaf), then the theme index (24 KB) and the measurer's memo (20 KB). Text is in Exact's favour (`CG Raster Data` and `CoreAnimation` 0.9 MB against the port's 1.65 MB).
- **What euxy's playing costs on iOS that its UIKit port's does not, about 0.7 MB** (2026-10-20, port 17, Release, a fresh iPhone 17 simulator; idle → playing 10 s: Exact +1.0 MB before the 2026-10-20 cuts, +0.7 after, UIKit +0.0). Flat over 80 s of playing, so nothing accumulates: not the sampler, the MIDI queue, `sent`, Hermes or the runner's per-tick state. It is what `if live` realizes: 141 step lights (ember and lit), each a box with a `box-shadow` and its own `animation` on a clock timeline, about 7 KB of footprint each; UIKit's port draws its strip with Core Graphics. Of the malloc growth (MallocStackLogging, play − idle, 1.5 MB before the cuts): the kernel's styles 0.37 MB (each light's `animation` delay makes its whole style unique: an 896-byte `StyleProps`, a 640-byte cold group carrying the animation and the shadow, the animation's own vectors), the runner's instances 0.16 MB, the motion engine's plays 0.15 MB, shadows 0.2 MB (now a container, its mask and two path copies Core Animation makes on `setShadowPath`/`setPath`, about 0.9 KB a box), `NodeView`s and their layers 0.1 MB, taffy's per-node replay records and a table doubling 0.09 MB, UIKit rebuilding its main menu's default commands 0.13 MB, and the wake lock's scene settings 0.07 MB (UIKit's port pays that too). The next cut is the styles: the `animation` row held apart from the interned style (as `Rare` and `Cold` are, interned by itself), so lights that differ only in delay share one `StyleProps` and cold group, about 0.2 MB here.
- **Euxy port 18's non-object malloc on iOS, what is left after the 2026-10-21 cuts** (Release, a fresh iPhone 17 simulator, idle 10 s after launch; `heap -s` against the UIKit port: non-object 5.00 MB in 25,250 blocks against 2.72 MB; `MallocStackLogging=lite`, `malloc_history -allBySize` through `c++filt`). The difference is Rust (1.9 MB before the cuts) and Hermes' own malloc (about 0.4 MB: code blocks 100 KB, pinned-value and weak-slot chunks 90 KB, the identifier table 48 KB). The default zone also holds 3.2 MB free in pages it keeps against the UIKit port's 2.4 MB; `malloc_zone_pressure_relief` returns none of it (no page is wholly free), and an untouched `calloc` (the MIDI input ring, 80 KB) costs nothing. Cut: the dependency table holds each distinct read set once (194 to 78 KB), the plan's `bindings` and `nodes` are read in the linked plan's bytes (162 to 34 KB), a node's `notEnabled` trait is set only on change; footprint 24,430,968 to 24,160,608 bytes idle and 25,373,036 to 25,094,508 playing, `EUXY_TTI` 1232 to 1229 ms. Then a realized `Child` at 128 bytes: Apple's allocator rounds a request above 128 bytes up to a class (160, 192, 224, …), and about 450 one-child vectors moved down one; footprint 24,217,976 to 23,996,780 bytes idle (medians of 12) and 25,233,784 to 24,906,104 playing (9), warm `EUXY_TTI` 1227 to 1222 ms. How to measure: install each build in turn over one bundle id (one data container) and rotate the order each round. The same binary under two bundle ids read up to 350 KB apart, and the first build of a round read 50 to 200 KB high; footprint moves in 16 KB pages with a spread of about 150 KB, so a cut under about 100 KB shows only in `heap -s`. Tried and not kept: Hermes' `kReleaseUnusedYoungOnFull` (this Hades has no path that releases memory: no change); a full collection between calls after every 32 KB allocated (Hermes' dirty 1,728 to 1,504 KB, the default zone up about as much: footprint flat); every Rust allocation in a malloc zone of its own (the default zone 1.7 MB less dirty, the Rust zone 2.6 MB at 39% free: 0.9 MB worse, so the default zone's free space is mostly not Rust's); Swift's style decoder sharing nested values and long strings across styles (`BatchValue` arrays 571 to 162, string storage 1,297 to 799, live heap 77 KB less, footprint 150 KB more). 2026-10-21, later, euxy port 20 (one bundle id, rotated order; live heap by `heap -s` with no stack logging, since `MallocStackLogging` moves blocks up a size class; medians of 12): a node keeps the last values of only the bindings that can change (3,652 of euxy's 3,974 are constant and read from the site index), -45 KB idle and -98 KB playing; `aspect_ratio`, `column_width` and `placeholder_color` are rare rows (`StyleProps` 864 B, the 896-byte class), -27 and -23; a `Mirror` is 128 B with static prop names, -34 and -40; Swift's `StyleCache` keeps two hashes of a style's bytes, not the bytes, -26 and -43; Taffy's grid fields and `multicol` are allocated only when set (`Style` 256 B, EXACT PATCH 62), -20 and -28. Together -155 KB idle and -232 KB playing; `footprint` 23,832,940 to 23,398,764 bytes idle and 24,643,936 to 24,201,580 playing (spread about 150 KB); `EUXY_TTI` 1,138.5 to 1,136.5 ms (medians of 20). Tried and dropped: reserving 512 slots in the arena columns and Taffy's slotmaps at the first create measured no different (medians of 8), so the doubling slack in those large blocks is not resident: their untouched pages are never dirtied, and only the bytes written count. Left, by owner (KB): Hermes' young generation, whose pages Hades keeps at their high-water (960 dirty, set by Ibex's hardening, 966 KB allocated in two steps, and euxy's `factoryPatterns` call, 550 KB); the runner's realized children 125 (a `Child` is 128 B, the `NodeInst` inline; its rarely set `last_surface`, `collection` and `implied` behind one pointer would bring it to 104) and its constant values 39 (a `BTreeMap` of the bindings evaluated), the kernel's interned styles 82 (88 `StyleProps` of 864 B; under 752 B they would take the 768-byte class, but what is left is common or inherited, `FontVariations` 68 B inline by design), Taffy's caches 86 and replay records 48 (one hashbrown table of `Replay`s, each a whole `LayoutInput` and `LayoutOutput`), the host's mirrors 64 and their props maps 25, the host's `StyleTexts` 48 (the presenter's cache no longer copies them), the store's kept bytes 48 (two values read whole, beside their parsed copies), the data module's reply values 77, the plan's other decoded tables 34 (`LENT_TABLES` in plan/build.rs takes more), the runtime `Entry` 48, the theme index 24. Swift's typed objects are the rest of the gap: `NodeView` 256 × 768 B, style dictionaries and `BatchValue` arrays 175 KB, and the associated object table at 96 KB (UIKit associates one per first `addSubview` of a view, so it follows the view count). 2026-10-21, later still, euxy port 21 (same method, medians of 10 to 12): a realized `Child` is 112 B, its list engine, surface inputs and implied rows in one boxed `Rare` (-12 KB idle, -18 to -25 playing); a style's own arrays and objects, its keys and long ASCII strings are shared across styles by their bytes in `BatchReader`, and a style with an escape is no longer cached (-50 and -60; footprint flat within its spread, as the earlier two-bundle-id trial's 150 KB more was not); a parent whose children all paint at rank 0 keeps no `DensePaintRanks` table (115 on the first screen; -15 and -12). `footprint` 23,267,680 to 23,128,428 bytes idle and 24,643,948 to 24,291,692 playing; `EUXY_TTI` 1,234.5 to 1,229.5 ms (spread about 20). Measured and left: the kernel's interned `StyleProps` reach the 768-byte class only at 752 B or less, which no single row move gives; `Paint::Url`'s id and fallback boxed (-32 for `fill` and `stroke`), `MarkerRef` thin (-24) and `font_variation_settings` boxed in the style row (-60, though runs carry `FontVariations` by value) together make 744 B, for about 12 KB. Taffy's `Replay` table holds about 220 records of 120 B in a 256-bucket table (33 KB written): boxing the record saves under 5 KB. The runner's constants as a sorted vector would save about 15 KB at an O(n) insert per constant. `TextSpecs` is 1,280 B (25 on euxy) for its two inline `Spec`s, each with an inline strut `Run`.
- **CSS font weights as exact `wght` copies cost euxy about 300 KB of heap on iOS** (2026-10-21, port 21, Release, iPhone 17 simulator, one bundle id, rotated order, `heap -s` medians of 12). `TextEngine.cssWeight` gives `font-weight` 500 and 600 on the system face the `wght` 500 and 600 Chrome and WebKit set, where UIKit's medium and semibold are 510 and 590 (text 0.2% narrower than Chrome's). Each copy is a CoreText variation instance of its own name, and CoreText keeps per name what it shares among the named weights: the shaping-glyph list decompressed from the font (a 20 KB `NSConcreteData`: 8 of them, 2 without the copies, 3 in the UIKit port) and other per-font caches. With the copies skipped (a probe, not landed): live heap 11,587,292 to 11,278,172 bytes idle and 13,542,604 to 13,244,676 playing, `footprint` about 240 KB less idle and no different playing. Charlie decides: the named instances (UIKit's own answer, a tell no one could see, and widths that no longer match the web oracle's by 0.2%) or the parity.
- **Euxy's footprint grows 14 MB when its Patterns tab first opens** (2026-10-17, development build on an iPhone 17 simulator, driven by the agent: 26.9 MB on the Sequencer tab after playing, 40 to 45 MB once Patterns and Settings have been shown, about 42 MB after; Hermes is not it). Unmeasured on a Release build and not yet attributed: find what the list's first presentation keeps (`vmmap` by region, then `malloc_history`).
- **The session's view becomes first responder at every iOS launch** (2026-10-16, euxy memory lane): so its key commands work, but in an app that declares no `aria-keyshortcuts` this sets up UIKit's keyboard machinery for nothing (about 190 KB malloc in euxy). A change that took the focus only while a shortcut is declared (`canBecomeFirstResponder` returning that; take focus after each batch, blur and window join when nothing else in the window, view or view controller, is first responder and `MenuFocus` is not holding it aside; release it when the last shortcut goes) went three review rounds and was dropped with one case open: with nothing focused, `MenuFocus.setAside()` records no suspension, so a shortcut introduced while a native menu is open takes the focus over the menu. Track menu suspension apart from the saved responder. With nothing first responder, the app delegate's ⌘D still opened the dev menu (Release euxy, hardware keyboard). Measured 2026-10-19 (euxy port 12, launches that skip the `becomeFirstResponder`): 110 to 220 KB, all malloc (`UITextEffectsWindow`, the keyboard input scene, TextInput's preferences); the empty `inputView` costs nothing at launch.
- **Files in a share on Android, and what LLP 1125 D12 left unseen** (2026-10-16, stage 5): Android refuses `share(files=…)` by name (`host/linux/src/presenter/requests.rs`); carrying it needs a `FileProvider` in the Canvas host's manifest, the presenter's app roots as content URIs on an `ACTION_SEND_MULTIPLE`, and a picture from the Canvas renderer. Not seen: a real browser's `navigator.share({files})` (only a fake navigator), the outcome line of a file picked in a real Apple sheet, and Safari's activation window after the store reads. An Apple share's scratch PNG directory stays in the temporary directory when the app quits with the sheet open. Two sessions of one app in one process (the sample host, LLP 1031) share one set of pinned app roots, keyed by the roots' paths: if the app's data directory is swapped between their activations, the first session's share reads the second's pinned tree (Astra's confirmation of `46efcc152`; keep the handles per storage activation).
- **Lean navigation tabs still need reload carry** (2026-10-17, LLP 1075.003 §9.14). Model `carry_panels`: retain a built panel’s component state across reload even when that panel is not rebuilt at the reload, including successive reloads before its next selection. `runner/tests/it/tabs.rs` covers it; the semantics has no reload at all (`Contract/Runtime.lean`, `Contract/CompSem.lean`, the observation's events), so a reload comes first. Prerender's `build_every_tab` is modelled (`Oracle.everyTab`, the corpus's `(prerendered)` variants).
- **LLP 1125 D11's test step `expect wakelock held|released` is not in** (2026-10-16, stage 1b): authored test steps are parsed (`contract/syntax`, as `expect sound` is), not table-driven, so it was left out; the driver's `state.wakelock` carries the same. Android's kind-8 `setKeepScreenOn` was written and not run on a device or emulator, and neither web target's lock was seen held in a real browser's wake lock (the glue's test stands a counting `navigator.wakeLock` in). Natively an element the host shows as its own control (a header in the navigation bar, a confirmation as a native alert, a menu as a native menu) holds nothing, because a whole host cover marks both what the host hides and what it shows natively and the kernel cannot tell them apart; the web holds there.
- **iOS custom invokers, the edges left** (2026-10-17, the reviews that landed them; each no worse than before, when such an invoker opened nothing). (1) Enter or Space on a handlerless custom invoker, and the agent's `type`, take the invoker path only when its target already resolves (`activateKey` gates on `invocation(of:)`), so a target its ancestor's `press` mounts stays closed; gate as `MenuHost.invoker(from:)` does, on a button naming a target. (2) VoiceOver's activation of a handlerless invoker hands the press's focus on with the press target as the handoff's control, not the focused invoker, so an autofocus its ancestor's press mounts is refused (`PressHandoffIOS.pressHandsOff`), as for a pass-through activation before. (3) A stationary agent contact under a `pan` ancestor with no press handler captures no `reach` (`recognizedPan`), so its lift invokes nothing, where a real touch would. (4) A pressable non-button carrying `popovertarget` inside a custom invoker is taken as the nearest invoker and then refused by kind, so the enclosing button is not invoked: require a button in `invoker(from:)`. (5) An inline run's or an SVG element's own press inside an invoker returns before `MenuHost.activating`, so the invoker is not invoked. (6) The production overlays (`MenuHost.sync`'s pull-down and confirmation buttons) press no ancestor of a handlerless invoker, where the web's click bubbles and the agent's painted path does.
- **The iOS agent's press, what a real touch still does differently** (2026-10-20, review of the in-page press keeping its cancellation; `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/AgentPanIOS.swift`). (1) A target the press's own focus callback disables and restores still presses: after the focus an in-page press walks its live ancestry, where a real contact stays cancelled (`cancelledByReparenting` is false, so `movedByFocus` does not apply); tell a reparent from other cancellations and recheck after the focus. (2) An inline run, a link and an SVG element's activation keep no path, so their host disabled and restored between down and up still activates them. (3) A held synthetic down sets the target's `pressed` look with no `PressContact`, so a node on its path disabled or made inert does not clear the look until the up. (4) A contact that reaches no press target and no invoker keeps no path, so a node only its focus settles, disabled and restored between down and up, still takes the focus. (5) A handlerless in-page invoker keeps only its invocation path, which `rearm` does not take again after `pointerdown`: its own `pointerdown` disabling and restoring it refuses the activation a real contact (which begins after) makes.
- **A `button` invoker without `press` presses no pressable ancestor on macOS or as a native button on iOS** (2026-10-17, scratch app: `button commandfor=… command="show-modal"` inside a pressable `main`: web +1, macOS and iOS 0). The web's click bubbles past the invoker to the ancestor's `press` and also runs the invocation; macOS counts an invoker as `pressable` (`NodeViewMac`) and a native iOS button invoker `takesPress`, so the press stops there. docs/contract-grammar.md says a button with no `press` presses the nearest ancestor's on every host. Built and not landed after three review rounds (2026-10-20; local branch `item2-r3`, one commit on 6ac72bc688, in the worktree `.claude/worktrees/agent-acc7bb23bd57ba852`): on macOS `Presenter.press` resolves a handlerless button invoker's nearest listener after committing the pending edit (the click's landing point for its bounds, none for keys), presses it once, then runs the button's own invocation; on iOS a native invoker and a custom closer no longer take the press, the originating button keeps invocation ownership across focus, and the agent, keys, shortcuts, VoiceOver and the native pull-down follow it. The last round left two regressions: dragging off a handlerless custom closer and releasing inside the listener still closes its dialog (the listener, not the closer, now tracks the contact's bounds), and macOS shortcut discovery, which uses `pressable`, lets a handlerless button naming a target that does not exist claim its shortcut (a `Meta+c` there hides Copy). Edges no worse than main: clearing the Mac invoker's target during the edit commit still presses the button's own id, not its listener.
- **The wasm page still loses a `play` or `ended` an autoplaying element fires before its data executor is ready** (2026-10-16). The media glue now attaches once `moduleReady` resolves and reports the metadata, duration, opening seek, `canplay` and an error the element already has (Video Player's lost opening duration, reproduced on main with activation delayed 1.5 s: 37 failures, 0 after). The presenter sets `autoplay` as an attribute at once, so a muted autoplaying element can start, and a short one end, before then; before this change the report callback dropped those too. Holding the attribute until attaching does not work (HTML acts on `autoplay` only on reaching HAVE_ENOUGH_DATA); reporting `play`/`ended` on attaching would.
  Tried 2026-10-16 and parked (branch `q1013/w5-wasm-autoplay-events` on the Mac Studio, four review rounds). Inferring what already happened from `paused`/`ended`/`readyState`/`played`/`seeking` at attach and suppressing the queued duplicates failed every round (a `waiting` before attach, seeks, a pause during attach, HTML's completion interval). Recording the events instead (a recorder set where `glue.js` and `host/web-js/media.js` give the element `autoplay`, replayed in order by `media-glue.js`) is small and fixes those, but replay still needs: recorded history dropped when the source changes or reloads (else an old source's `error` reaches the new one), recorded `timeupdate` not written back through a `currentTime` binding (it rewinds the playhead), the attach-time `ended` guard not consuming a `paused` true→false the app set before attach, and no `currentTime` write while `readyState` is 0 during replay. Take the recorder, with those four, as the next attempt.
  Tried again 2026-10-17 with the recorder and parked (branch `q24/wasm-media-early-play` on expo-build-450b, four commits, four review rounds). The recorder holds: a one-line `recordMedia` at the end of `host/web/navigation.js` (the only boot module with room; glue.js is at 1,499 lines), replay through `media-glue.js`'s listener path, history dropped on a `src` change, `load()` or `emptied`, a replayed `timeupdate` kept from seeking the player back, retirement during replay, the opening seek reported as main reports it; 47 media tests pass on the branch. What did not converge is replaying `play`/`playing`/`pause`/`ended` into handlers that write `paused`: the replay arrives after the app moved, so history and the app's newer request collide, and every rule tried (per-event versions, a pending-request counter) met a new case in review: a request made by a replay handler during the replay, a request the element already meets (no fresh `play()`/`pause()` event follows), a `pause`/`ended` HTML still has queued when the listeners attach. Next attempt, if any: deliver the early reports in order with the app's own inputs (the runner or glue queues them while `inputReady` is false, as one timeline) instead of replaying them at attach.

- **The iOS agent's tap sends pointer events only on the plain press path** (2026-10-16): a tap that lands on a native control, a segment, a grouped list's row, a swipe action or a bar item activates it with no `pointerdown`/`pointerup` to an authored ancestor that hears them, which a real touch on that control would deliver (`PointerRecognizer` is on the node). Rare in practice; send them before each of those activations if an app needs it.
- **iOS cold start: what a first frame still does that a hand-built UIKit app would not** (2026-10-16, euxy port, Release on an iPhone 17 simulator, Time Profiler). (1) A closed `dialog` and a closed `popover` are laid out and measured at boot though iOS shows neither until it opens, and an alert-shaped one or a `role="menu"` one never shows Exact's views (the platform's alert and `UIMenu`): euxy's pattern menu, rename alert and its field are 8 of the boot's 11 button faces and its one field's chrome, about 15 ms on main inside runner + layout, and the alert's `input` makes a `UITextField` in the first frame. A closed top-layer box replaced whole until it opens, laid out in the batch that opens it, would end it; the agent shows them, so it needs the open path to lay out before presenting. (2) The boot lays the page out in the safe area, then the tab container takes the whole view (`refitForBars`), and `fit` sends `screen`, `regions` and `resize` as three batches, each a presenter apply and a forced UIKit layout (about 8 ms on main), before the first frame. (3) Background-loading SwiftUI, which `UINavigationBar` loads on first use (30 to 60 ms in the simulator), does not help: its image registration holds the Objective-C runtime lock, and main waited the whole load wherever it started.
- **iOS cold start: euxy's Exact port still reaches its boot animation's end later than its SwiftUI and UIKit ports** (2026-10-16, Release, a fresh iPhone 17 simulator, 10 interleaved launches to `EUXY_TTI`: Exact 1455 ms, SwiftUI 1221, UIKit 1317; process start to `main` is not it: Exact 173 to 187 ms, UIKit 3 to 8 ms less, SwiftUI about 20 ms more, with the same 506 images loaded as UIKit's; what Exact has more is its executable's own 34,000 chained fixups against 6,600, mostly Rust and Swift pointer tables in `__DATA_CONST`, and stripping its symbols changes nothing measurable). Exact's first draw is about 195 ms after `main`, the boot animation's 900 ms follow it. On main in that first frame, by Time Profiler: (1) `UINavigationBar` loads SwiftUI on first use (30 to 60 ms in the simulator); background-loading it does not help, its image registration holding the Objective-C runtime lock while main waits. (2) After the boot's `fit`, `OwnerBlocks.publish` tells the routes' blocks as a batch of its own, whose apply forces a UIKit layout of the hierarchy (`NavigationHost.sync`, about 8 ms): they could go to the runtime with the view's change (`exact_view`). (3) euxy's module builds an `AVAudioPlayerNode` and sets the audio session's category in `init`, made with its `firstFrame` views (about 15 ms; `docs/recipes/native-views.md` now says not to).
- **Exact's game holds 160 MB of graphics memory on a Pixel 5 against the WebView's 40** (2026-10-16, `game/bench/README.md`, "Memory and CPU at 60 Hz"). The direct path's 4× MSAA targets are already `TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT`, but this driver (2020-11-30) has no lazily allocated memory type and no `VK_EXT_multisampled_render_to_single_sampled`, the Vulkan form of the GLES extension that keeps the WebView's MSAA in tile memory; a newer Adreno driver may have either, and wgpu-hal would need the second added. The hardware-buffer ring's six slots are another 60 MB: five collapse 80,000 cubes to 26 fps.
- **Exact's occlusion at 256,000 cubes on a Pixel 5: 30.6 fps exact, 35.0 with unshrunk occluders** (2026-10-16, `game/render/README.md`, "Occlusion"). Shrinking each occluder by a texel keeps the frame identical but costs a texel-wide rim of every small far cube: 42,000 cubes drawn against 18,000. Closing the gap without giving up exactness: occluders drawn from an 8-vertex mesh (the pass's 4.7 ms is vertex work), a cull that reads less than two 40-byte transform records per item (it is memory-bound: 9.1 ms), and presenting the canvas's buffers through `ASurfaceControl` rather than drawing them as a bitmap in the reader's pass (2 ms of GPU a frame, measured by not drawing it).
- **A 60 Hz tick of 256,000 moving entities takes 5–6 ms on a Pixel 5, bound by memory** (2026-10-16, the cubes bench's `tick`): 56 bytes read and 40 written per entity, and its 10 MB upload another 3 ms. Two ticks and two uploads then fill a display frame. Copying the pages into staging on another core while the next tick runs is the next lever.
- **Castle Town faults the Pixel 5's GPU, and Android does not recover a lost device** (2026-10-17, castle-fixgpu). Since round 10 the Adreno driver reports recovered GPU faults in the module's context (`IOCTL_KGSL_GPU_COMMAND ... errno 71`, the error KGSL gives a context's next submission after a fault): cold starts with `screenrecord` running, round 9's freeplay build 0 of 5 runs, round 10's 3 of 5, rounds 13–16 and this branch every run (2 to 8 a run; fewer without `screenrecord`). About one cold start in nine the device is then lost; that used to abort the process (vendored wgpu-hal patch 12 fixes the abort), and now the canvas stops, because the Android presenter never calls `gpu_recover` nor attaches its windows again. Round 10's "absent shadows on launch" and round 13's "buffered canvases stop producing frames" below may be the same faults; the round 16 SPIR-V validation errors (workgroup array strides) are a lead. Bisect round 10 (`c96ac1c9d` render scale, `15edcd90d` ground cut, or what they exposed), and give the Android presenter recovery as Apple's has (`Module::recover` reattaches Apple layers; an Android canvas would need its `ANativeWindow` attached again). Logcats: the castle-fixgpu lane's scratchpad `fixgpu-phone/`.
- **What castle-fixgpu left in the GPU module** (2026-10-17). (1) Under the agent's clock a canvas acquires on the module's thread and waits for the image, so an Android `surfaceDestroyed` arriving then waits on the compositor through `Owner.sync`; live frames no longer do (detach hangs up on the acquisition thread, a resize waits for its image to land). Attaching a window joins retired acquisition threads first (Android refuses a second swapchain on a window still connected to one), bounded by wgpu's one-second acquire timeout. (2) The cached sun-shadow copy kept for GL and WebGL2 (`shadow_cache::seeds`) is tested only by forcing it on Metal; exact2 compiles no GL backend today.
- **A GPU artifact can be unloaded while its acquisition threads still run** (2026-10-17, castle-fixgpu2; Astra's review of castle-fixgpu, finding 2). `Module::drop` joins only acquisition threads that have ended and lets the rest go (`gpu/src/release.rs`), so `gpu_unload` can return while a thread is still inside an acquisition in the artifact's code; the Linux host's `Abi` then drops its `Library` (`host/linux/src/surfaces.rs`, `dlclose`). Android keeps the primary artifact loaded for the process; a secondary artifact has no such hold. Keep an artifact loaded until its threads have ended, distinguishing a canvas's detach, which must not wait, from the module's final shutdown.
- **WebGPU canvases configure as opaque whatever they promise** (2026-10-17, castle-fixgpu2; Astra's review of castle-fixgpu, finding 4). The pinned wgpu 30.0.1 WebGPU backend lists only `Opaque` in a canvas's capabilities (`src/backend/webgpu.rs:4176`) although its `configure` maps `PreMultiplied` to the browser's premultiplied mode (`:4231`), so `target::alpha_mode` picks `Opaque` for every browser canvas and a translucent surface loses its alpha over page content. List `PreMultiplied` there (a vendored patch, as for wgpu-hal), keeping the module's choice among listed modes.
- **Castle Town 90 Hz headroom** (2026-10-17, `game/bench/README.md`, round 16). In-pass sun-cache seeding (`e922c05f7`) saves 0.184/0.182 ms in views 0/1 and preserves fixed-pose pixels. Final full-resolution 1080×2340, 24 players: view 0 is 89.39 fps with five missed slots in ten seconds, view 7 misses one, all others miss none. View 0 mean delivery is 11.187 ms, about 0.076 ms over the nominal budget before variance headroom. Omitting avatars plus hulls removes 0.772 ms of its 1.266 ms camera-cost difference against view 2; separate body and hull cost next. COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT acquire waits leave the median ~0.23 ms GPU gap and do not improve fps; latency 3 allocates five images (default already four) and regresses. Keep the prepass, Depth32Float, 35 m shadows and full resolution. Hardware utilization and the remaining gap's cause remain unmeasured.
- **Pixel 5 Vulkan SPIR-V validation errors in the existing game shaders** (2026-10-17, Castle round 16). Khronos Android validation 1.4.363.0 reports `VUID-StandaloneSpirv-None-10684` for workgroup array strides and `VUID-VkShaderModuleCreateInfo-pCode-08737` for a vector constant with mismatched component types. Both occur with the original TOP_OF_PIPE acquire path as well as the rejected COLOR path; synchronization validation reports no SYNC hazards. Reproduce and fix the generated SPIR-V, then rerun validation. Evidence: `castle-port:game/games/castle-town/artifacts/perf/r16/validation-summary.json` and its logcats.
- **Android buffered GPU canvases sometimes stop producing frames** (2026-10-17, Castle round 13). The baseline attempt, 30 m trial at view 8 and original-assets 35 m tour at view 5 stalled while the app stayed in front and the phone awake; CPU fell to about 52 ms/s and no next tour log arrived. Relaunches run normally. No cause is established; invalid rows are excluded. Three SurfaceView tours completed in round 13; no foreground stall recurred in round 14, whose bench captures debuggerd and logcat before restarting a stalled run. Evidence: `castle-port:game/games/castle-town/artifacts/perf/r13/final-buffer-stall-logcat.txt` and the receipt.
- **Castle Town intermittent absent shadows on launch** (2026-10-17, round 10). The first 0.9-scale tour lacked shadows; the identical APK restored them on relaunch. The forward shaders match the full-size build, and the failed launch still reports a sun pass. Its performance rows are marked visually rejected. Investigate startup/resource ordering; no cause is established. Evidence: `castle-port:game/games/castle-town/artifacts/perf/r10/{shadow-compare,shadow-repeat}.png`.
- **A GPU canvas over children draws nothing on the Android reader** (2026-10-16, Pixel 5). With Caltrain's module now packaged (`agent-android.mjs build caltrain --apk`), its sky (`glass`, a canvas whose content is its children) is blank and logs nothing, while the cubes game's world (a canvas without children) draws. Likely cause, read and not confirmed: the Linux host's presenter hands each child to `gpu_child_view` with no pixels (`host/linux/src/surfaces.rs`), and Android runs that presenter. Capture the children's pixels there, or have the reader hand the module its own layer of them.
- **Document watches still owed after LLP 1069.010.000 stage 1** (2026-10-16): iOS has none (`NSFilePresenter` on the document's URL, a vnode source for local files; `host/apple/src/doc_watch.rs` is FSEvents on macOS only); a Rust source's `doc:` watch on the web JS target announces nothing, because no Rust topic is wired there (`native.watch` from Rust is not either); the agent cannot announce a change (`changed <path>`, D2); `exactPage().canWatchFiles` is not built (see the RFC's "As built").
- **LLP 1069.010.000 stage 2 on Linux** (2026-10-18; ticket `20261015-chosen-documents-outlast-the-launch`): the freedesktop recent-files store (`recently-used.xbel`) as the list, the path with its device and inode as the handle, nothing kept outside a desktop session; `exact_data::documents::kept` is the host-independent half, `host/apple/src/documents.rs` the macOS host's.
- **LLP 1069.010.000 stage 2 on the web** (2026-10-18): `FileSystemHandle`s in IndexedDB, `requestPermission(id, path, mode)` under a press (D7), and `recentDocuments` / `forgetDocument` in the glue and the web JS target's prelude, which today has neither (a TypeScript call throws there).
- **Kept documents, what macOS stage 2 left** (2026-10-18): a TypeScript `atomicWriteFile` of a kept file does not carry the entry to the new file (the Rust storage does; the prelude is at its line cap), so another launch forgets it; D4's hard rows driven by the Markdown editor with a fixture that replaces the file from another program; Clear Menu clicked in the real menu bar (the agent drives none).
- **`test-rust` on Windows sometimes fails to write or launch a test executable** (2026-10-09, the same diary): `LNK1104` while linking twice, and Windows error 32 (file in use) launching one; each passed on a retry with no change. Antivirus or the search indexer holding a new `.exe` is the usual cause, but none was seen. The same runs twice failed a render doctest with `E0463` (the logic crate not found), which a focused rebuild passed; those runs shared the SDK's `game/target`, which `test-rust` no longer defaults to. If either recurs, retry a launch-only sharing violation once, and say which file was held.
- **A bar item's drawn faces register a dark variant UIKit may size from the light one** (2026-10-15, chrome lane): a header `ChipFace` (`BarFaceIOS.swift`) drawn as a light/dark `UIImageAsset` sat in a dark capsule three times its width on iOS 27, so it is drawn for the page's scheme instead. `BadgeFace` (`NavigationBarIOS.swift`) still registers both; its item hides the capsule, so nothing showed. Draw it per scheme too, or measure it in dark.
- **The Mac's toolbar has no painted bar-item face** (2026-10-15, chrome lane): iOS draws a header button's dot-and-label or authored-font face as written (`ChipFace`); the Mac's header is projected whole or painted whole (LLP 1115.002 D2), so such a header button there still becomes a plain `NSToolbarItem` title. A toolbar item with an image drawn the same way would match. A header readout (`role="status"`, LLP 1075.003 §9.15) keeps the Mac's header painted for the same reason; an `NSToolbarItem` with the readout's view would place it, and Android's top app bar leaves one out.
- **A painted swipe row's leading full swipe is not driven** (2026-10-15, chrome lane): `PaintedSwipesIOS.swift` was driven on a simulator with real touches for trailing actions (open, one open at a time, full swipe deleting the row); a `swipeLeading` row with painted actions has the same code but no drive or XCTest.

- **LLP 1123 (#627, 8d38b64a2): what a stale slice leaves behind after a commit** (2026-10-15; found while checking Charlie's Bluesky "repaints only after a tap", which it does not explain: these paths need a generation change while a slice is in flight, which only an OTA activation (`ExactUpdates.activate`) or a dev reload makes). `landFill` (`host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/Session.swift`) now runs a stale slice's waiters of the current generation and drops older ones. That is right for a pump (a commit swaps in a new executor, `abi.rs` `commit_plan`), but: (1) the stale path still skips `presenter.collections.landed`, so reports the slice held wait for the next input; (2) a NativeModule intrinsic flush queued before the commit is dropped, and its `intrinsicFlushPending` never clears, so that module's intrinsic sizes never flush again; (3) iOS navigation's reset-time cover clears (`NavigationIOS.reset` → `onCovers`) are queued under the new generation and run inline during the commit's own apply, before the new tree. Repro sketch: a list scrolling (slice in flight) when a dev reload or `deliveryActivate` commits; then a native module's intrinsic size change never reaches layout. A reference implementation reviewed over three Astra/Grok rounds (before #627) is on branch `x2/afterfill-v1`: it binds presenter-view work to a `viewsEpoch` renewed after each reset, runs `collections.landed` for a stale slice, and defers its waiters to the next turn.
- **Warn about statically competing `transformDragFor` targets** (2026-10-15, from bsky5's Bluesky b45 lightbox fix). LLP 1071 allows one owner (first valid, never stolen). Add a compiler or `analyze` warning where two bindings are statically visible together, such as `transformDragFor` inside a list's row template.
- **The page's frames drop to one a second after `host/web-js/document-agent.test.mjs`'s first tap** (2026-10-15, at main `d1e73782d`; Chrome for Testing headless from Terminal, display on). The page draws at 60 fps until `tap('open-detail')`, then at one frame a second (`requestAnimationFrame` gaps of 1,016 ms) with the page visible, no view transition and no running animations, until a later resize. The agent's resize waits at most 250 ms for two frames, so the 420 px layout's media-query `change` has not fired when the test reads the tree, and the test failed 10 of 12 runs here. `--disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows`, `--disable-renderer-backgrounding` and `--disable-background-timer-throttling` do not prevent it. The glue step leaves the file out (`scripts/async.mjs`) until it passes. Find what the tap starts that slows the frames before changing the agent's resize wait.
- **Chrome's `Page.captureScreenshot` sometimes takes 9–10 s while other Chromes are busy** (2026-10-14, the glue tests at 6 workers). About 1 to 3% of captures in a probe and 4 of 40 in `host/web/agent-check.test.mjs`, whose 10 s test then takes 20 s; every agent `screenshot` and `check` can stall so. A rAF loop during the capture, `fromSurface: false` and `optimizeForSpeed` do not prevent it. Sampled during a stall (2026-10-15), the headless browser's main thread is busy in AppKit and SwiftUI, laying out and creating its hidden `NSWindow`s and their title-bar chrome, while Chrome's capture waits on a forced redraw (`RenderWidgetHostImpl::GetSnapshotFromBrowser`). Playwright's `chrome-headless-shell`, which makes no AppKit windows, never answered a capture of Caltrain's page. Find what makes the browser lay out its windows, perhaps with a symbolized Chrome, before changing `scripts/agent.mjs`.
- **iOS: a dialog sheet's scroll does not reach the runner until something else does** (2026-10-20, review of the sheet in the runner's top layer). `DialogSheets.presented` reads the dialog's box and the card's clip when the runner asks (`openTopLayer`), but the sheet's scroller reports no movement: after a read, scrolling a tall sheet can bring an `intersect` target into view with no enter event, and a timer's action can read the box as it was. Publish the presentation's geometry as the sheet scrolls or lays out, and wake pending intersections.
- **iOS: a dialog presented again after its route closes, what is left** (2026-10-20, reviews of closing a route under a dialog's sheet; `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/DialogSheetIOS.swift`, `ModalIOS.swift`, `MenusIOS.swift`). A page-authored dialog or native confirmation over a route that closes is presented again over what is on top. (1) Moving a page dialog or prompt commits its field's edit and blurs it (`change`, `blur`, then `focus` again) while the dialog stays open; suppress that resignation for a dialog being moved. (2) A focus still pending in the dialog (its `autofocus` before the sheet reached the window) is dropped, and the new sheet does not autofocus. (3) The resumed dialog can take the root presenter before navigation presents a replacement route (`modalDidDismiss` runs after the resume is queued). (4) Closing the lower of two sheets dismisses only the upper (UIKit's `dismiss` on a presenting controller), leaving an empty sheet. (5) An embedder dismissing the stack under a native confirmation leaves it set (no disappearance cleanup for confirmations).
- **iOS: what a popover over a dialog's sheet still lacks** (2026-10-18). A popover a modal `dialog` holds is drawn over that dialog's UIKit sheet, in a `PopoverLayer` in the sheet's presentation container (`host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/PopoverLayerIOS.swift`); `SurfacesIOS.swift` lists the session's surfaces and resolves a view's controller, and whatever enumerates either goes through it. Only a dialog that holds a popover hosts it, which is narrower than HTML: inertness does not decide paint order, and a popover opened later outside the modal paints above it there though inert ([whatwg/html#11195](https://github.com/whatwg/html/issues/11195)); here it stays under the sheet. A native screen in such a popover has no parent controller: UIKit raises `UIViewControllerHierarchyInconsistency` for a parent whose view does not hold the layer. the popover follows its sheet's scroll or drag only at the next batch; inertness authored around the popover inside the dialog does not cross the lift; content taller than the room above the keyboard needs its own scrolling.
- **Native point reads omit a modal dialog's backdrop** (2026-10-16, Chrome/AppKit modal-read scratch comparison). Outside the front dialog's border box, Chrome's `elementFromPoint` names that dialog through its `::backdrop`, while the runner returns none; `elementsFromPoint` likewise omits the backdrop's dialog hit below a popover outside the dialog's box. The modal boundary now prevents reads reaching the page or a lower dialog. Backdrop hits still need to follow the host's actual presentation and pointer-event behavior.
- **Markdown Stress's Mac smoke fails on unchanged main** (2026-10-14, `ad4f84318` and Mac theme candidate `1e1ade948`).
