Manual 16 KiB passes mixed, paragraph, code and table with byte-identical captures, then fails `blocks: document must scroll` at `smoke.mjs:69`. Windowed 1 MiB blocks fails the revision assertion at line 62 on both in a matched repeat; the first main run reached the later timer assertion at line 125 instead. Investigate scroll reset and resource acknowledgement before changing assertions or adding waits; neither cause is established. Original failures and the single unchanged pair are in `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/theme-reach/current-main-mac-gaps-2026-10-14/{round1-apps,app-followup1,windowed-recheck1}/`.
- **Finish native clear-colour projection (LLP 1115.000 D2).** Button faces select title rows by value mask, losing explicit clear-only colour intent: UIKit retains accent and AppKit retains platform ink instead of the inherited colour. Preserve genuine absence as platform-owned. Qualify navigation/window titles and Mac toolbar ink too; they retained platform ink in the clear-app captures. Evidence at `c2f5c56f1`: `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/linux-neutral-chrome/integrated-clear-intent/c2f5c56f-native-source4/independent-native-review.txt` and `ordinary-visual-c2f5/independent-clear-mac-review.txt` beside it.
- **Hermetic conformance still has network TLS failures.** Paired `0d2d2e28e`/`c2f5c56f1` product steps agree, but CONNECT handshake failures vary: RealWorld appears in baseline Chrome only; Google in both WebKit runs and candidate Firefox. Identify the connecting process and verify its process-scoped trust of the generated proxy CA without bypassing certificate validation. Preserve unanswered requests and failed exits; no TLS fix is established. Reports: `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/linux-neutral-chrome/integrated-clear-intent/{baseline-0d2-conformance,candidate-c2f5-conformance}/<engine>/conformance/report.json`.
- **Current cross-browser conformance findings need classification.** Firefox reports `video-player`, `styles`, `focus-bubble` and `spacing`; WebKit reports `realworld`, `startend`, `reads`, `menus`, `navroot` and `focus-bubble`. Focused runs on `8339a0057` and the web-look removal at `2e7674324` produce identical failure tuples: 158 Firefox and 75 WebKit. The original full WebKit run had 73; the two additional selection findings occur on both paired sources. Inspect the recorded deliveries before treating a difference as an engine convention. Evidence: `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/web-neutral-only/paired-baseline-independent-review.txt`.
- **The optional asset-root bake fixture does not reach its asset checks** (`scripts/app-bake.test.mjs`, `EXACT_ASSET_BAKE_TEST=1`). At `178ae7237` its resolved manifest has the wrong app identity; the attempted fixture repair at `22e1a55e8` reaches generated code with a missing `exact-web-capabilities` dependency. Repairs stopped after three rounds and the fixture was restored unchanged; asset creation/rebake behavior remains unqualified. Original and attempted receipts: `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/web-neutral-only/baseline-178ae7237/three-failures.log` and `runtime-round-3/browser-settings-freshness.log` beside that baseline directory.
- **Structural reads during native animations report their destination** (2026-10-14, LLP 1123 stage 1). Owner placements and top-layer frames come from UIKit's model geometry (`UIView.convert`), so while a sheet presents, settles on a detent or a stack pushes, `frame` and `elementFromPoint` answer where the animation ends, as the runner answers a CSS transition's end. Wanted: sample the presentation layer (`layer.presentation()`) for the owners a native animation moves, each frame it runs, without per-frame work when nothing animates; a test that reads a sheet's card between a presentation's endpoints.
- **`hover` and `pan` on a presented route's logical ancestor are not carried into the sheet** (2026-10-14, LLP 1123 stage 1). Pointer observers, `contextmenu` and `dblclick` on an ancestor hear a sheet's touches through its mount (`MountRelay`); `hover` does not: the ancestor's own `UIHoverGestureRecognizer` ends as an iPad pointer moves onto the sheet, and a mount-level one would race it within the same event, so the ancestor would flicker between hovered and not. Wanted: one owner of the innermost-hover decision across the document and its presentations, then the relay. A `pan` on such an ancestor would compete with the sheet's own detent drag and scrollers; it needs arbitration rules before a relay, and the agent's pan follows the real touch's physical lookup meanwhile. A `swiperight` on such an ancestor stays out: it moves the node it is on, which is behind the modal sheet.
- **LLP 1123 stage 5 owes the keyboard design review's findings** (2026-10-13, Codex's review of stage 1's keyboard handling). Stage 1 only ends a sheet's route at its keyboard layout guide; the RFC's stage 5 lists what D7's executor must answer: scroller inset versus wrapper accommodation, owner-local occupancy for reveal and the toolbar, policy precedence before a sheet's keyboard may leave the document alone, undocked and floating keyboards, reveal for every editor, and composing with `PinnedRoom` and the toolbar.
- **The Mac's split pane is not mapped for the runner's geometry** (2026-10-13, found for LLP 1123 stage 1; stage 3 owes it). `SplitMac` gives each pane route a `.size` block (`blocks.show`) but reports no placement (`Runner::place_blocks`, `exact_host_covers` kind 6), so `frame()`, `measure` and `elementFromPoint` read a pane's nodes as if the pane were at the page's origin, off by the list column's width, and a point in the pane resolves in the list's space. Wanted: the pane publishes its rectangle in its root's parent's local coordinates as a placement next to `blocks.show`/`resize` (it keeps its place among its siblings), with a Mac test reading `frame` and `elementFromPoint` in it.
- **A Mac exit leaves the routes its panes and route sheet hold behind** (2026-10-14, found for LLP 1123 stage 1; stage 3 owes it). `Presenter.beginExit` on the Mac (`PresenceMac.swift`) walks the leaving view's subviews for the members it releases and the first responder it ends, but `BlockHostMac` moves a pane's route into the split's pane and `RouteSheetMac` a sheet's route into its sheet, outside their authored ancestors' views: a tab panel or root leaving with an exit animation keeps those routes' nodes registered and their editor focused. The iOS host collects the routes its native containers hold from its route controllers (`PresenceIOS.swift`). Wanted: the same on the Mac, from the block host's and route sheet's routes, with a Mac test that exits an ancestor of a pane's focused field.
- **An exit leaves the views the host lifts out of the leaving subtree behind** (2026-10-14, found reviewing LLP 1123 stage 1; read, not reproduced). `Presenter.beginExit` on iOS releases what it finds under the leaving view and under the routes its native containers hold (`PresenceIOS.swift`), and lands the flights in them, but the host also shows some nodes outside their authored parents' views: an open painted popover in its lift layer (`MenusIOS.lift`), a `dialog` in its sheet's scroller (`DialogSheetIOS`), a context menu's preview row in the menu (`ContextMenusIOS`). One of these open under a node that leaves with an exit animation would stay registered: the exit sends its parent, inside the leaving subtree, no more children. Wanted: retirement that reaches every view whose authored ancestry holds the leaving node, from one logical walk, with a test per kind.
- **The canvas through a trusted tunnel** (2026-10-19, stage 10 B2; theme2's Q3 ruling). `--trust-tunnel <port>` carries the design wire only: C's canvas gateway is TLS-only in stage 10, so a developer working through a tunnel edits the design without the canvas (LLP 1122 D12 allows it). Wanted, if asked for: a second tunnel port for the gateway (`--trust-tunnel <port>[,<canvas port>]`), with the gateway's grant and cookie rules unchanged.
- **Boxes between a placed block's parent and the view that holds it still clip it in point reads** (2026-10-14, LLP 1123 stage 1 review). A placement can be in an ancestor beyond the root's parent (`Scrolled::place`'s `within`: on iOS a tab's route is placed in the routes' owner, since UIKit's tab container holds the route beside its panel), and the runner shows the root in that ancestor directly (`shown_in`), so `frame` and the intersection observer leave the boxes between out, but `elementFromPoint`'s walk still descends through them, so a tab panel with `overflow` other than `visible` cuts its route where UIKit shows it whole (the part under a floating tab bar). Wanted: the walk enters such a root from the ancestor that holds it, past the clips of the boxes between, with a runner test of a clipping panel.
- **Apple stream completion can precede reservation release** (2026-10-16, unchanged `8201c148a`): 1 of 100 `cargo test -p exact-apple --lib executor::` runs under eight CPU-load processes failed `executor::core::stream::tests::three_messages_then_a_close_and_the_worker_stays_free` at `executor_stream_tests.rs:222`, "the stream's reservation ends with it", in the async-core subprocess. The other 99 runs passed. Check whether observing the terminal message also guarantees physical release before asserting `settled(&core)`. Evidence: `target/body-reader-audit/before-022.log` in the `q1013/w5-body-reader-limit` worktree.

- **SVG lengths, what the viewport-unit change leaves** (2026-10-13, SVG viewport review; main already has these differences). (a) Static math and environment geometry: `host/web/src/element.rs:767` omits dimensions other than points, percent and the four admitted viewport units. A nested SVG's `height="calc(10vh + 10px)"` or `height="env(viewport-segment-height 1 0, 25%)"` uses the initial viewport on the web, including its document, while native resolves the length. `min()`/`max()`/`clamp()`, safe-area `env()` and `sv`/`lv`/`dv` units have the same gap unless the kernel simplifies them to points or percent. Dynamic JS geometry keeps main's CSS path (`host/web-js/svg-transform.js:13`): a nested SVG's CSS `height` does not size its viewport, while a `foreignObject` takes it; browser support for segment `env()` fallbacks also differs. (b) Paint-server templates: omitting an undefined dimension (`host/web/src/element.rs:767`) exposes the `href` template's value. A radial gradient with `href="#base"` and `cx="env(viewport-segment-width 1 0)"`, where `base` has `cx="20%"`, uses 20% on the web when segment (1, 0) is absent; the kernel selects the authored row and uses its initial 50% (`kernel/src/svg/server.rs:136`, `kernel/src/svg/server.rs:176`). (c) Compact math: `kernel/src/style/calc/mod.rs:230` simplifies `calc(-10px)`, `min(-30px, -10px)` and `clamp(-20px, -10px, 20px)` to a literal. Used as `rx` beside `ry=10`, that value takes the negative-literal arm in `kernel/src/svg/shape.rs:71`, copying `ry` instead of clamping the calculation to 0. (d) Static font-relative geometry and hairlines: the kernel converts `em`/`rem` and `-exact-hairline` to points before `host/web/src/element.rs:748`; main emits these numbers rather than omitting them. The JS template (`host/web/src/template.rs:56`) keeps the build's pixels: a nested `svg height="2em"` stays 32 when its font size changes, and a pattern's static hairline stays 1 at 2x, while native and the wasm host recompute them. Dynamic JS hairlines use main's CSS resolution steps, so 1.25x uses 1 CSS px instead of native's 0.8.

- **Chrome retains SVG viewport attribute geometry after window resize** (2026-10-13, SVG viewport verification; browser limitation reproduced without Exact). In an 800x600 window, a fixed 400x400 SVG containing `<svg width="10vw" height="10vh"><rect width="100%" height="100%"/></svg>` draws at 80x60. After resizing the window to 200x400, Chrome's length API reports 20x40 but its drawn box remains 80x60; WebKit and Firefox update it. The literal viewport attributes written by `host/web/src/element.rs:754` and `host/web-js/svg-transform.js:12` inherit this limitation. Main omitted static viewport attributes and wrote dynamic geometry as CSS, so this is a limitation of the newly admitted form, not a regression of a working main case. Native scenes resolve against the resized window.

- **Refuse dynamic SVG attribute-only lengths at runtime** (2026-10-17; LLP 1055.000 D4). Contract refuses deferred units and function text in literals, template text and conditional literals on line/linearGradient `x1`/`y1`/`x2`/`y2`, radialGradient `fx`/`fy`/`fr`, marker `markerWidth`/`markerHeight`/`refX`/`refY`, and SVG text/tspan `x`/`y`/`dx`/`dy`. Bound values, incomplete template or concatenation tokens, and expressions beyond the static check’s 4,096-visit budget can still send relative lengths to the web that native `Length::parse` ignores. The SVG scene consumers have no warning path; add validation at a shared runtime boundary with a named diagnostic. CSS geometry, `offset`, `orient` and filter primitive attributes are separate.

- **Resolved: EX3 native unbound-handle logout — one-round waiver (2026-10-12).** The coordinator acting for the repo owner granted exactly one additional round after the RULES.md three-round limit, with a stop-if-NOT-READY instruction. Logout now retires matching origin/principal partitions even when the caller has no held partition. The real-server regression covers private cached reads, authenticated and manual-headers siblings, same-account relogin, and old-handle closure. Full live suite: 24 passed / 285 assertions; dev and serve scratch browser tests passed. Both fresh confirmation reviews are READY: [Codex](llp/reviews/1086-wave8-ex3-waiver.codex.md), [Muse](llp/reviews/1086-wave8-ex3-waiver.muse.md). The P1 is closed; no further round taken. This resolved record is retained at the coordinator's explicit request. Original finding: [round 3](llp/reviews/1086-wave8-ex3-r3.codex.md).
- **Wave 8 E5: an online-only Snapback driver over the browser journal.** Deferred from EX3: the current shared Rust device and native module have no device-free opening. Add an explicit online mode once the journal wire/identity contract is available to all Exact executors; it needs its own design and offline semantics, rather than silently bypassing the device. The app still builds the ~2.3 MB wasm meanwhile.
- **Wave 8 E9: grouped-list columns on day-two lane 006.** Deferred from EX3: reproduce on the same native presenter and app before changing cell projection; this workstream ran on Linux/Chrome and did not have the required Apple launch. The password helpers do not resolve inherited persona-specific tests; authors must update those tests when switching identity modes.

- **No runnable Exact+Snapback app in `apps/`** (2026-10-11, the sb4 farm's wave 2: 088; 051 found `select` driving only in the guestbook). The snapback4 README's guestbook builds and passes both its tests as written (extracted into a scratch app against 0.4.17, with `Snapback.switcher`, a `missing` and a positional testId), and `scripts/snapback-handshake.test.mjs` now extracts and rechecks both complete guestbooks against the pinned public-registry package (EX8, 2026-10-16). There is still no in-repo app: an `apps/` copy needs a backend, the device's wasm built from this checkout and a running `snapback4 dev`, which neither the gate nor the async lane has, and a README-extraction test would need Chrome as well. Which, if either, is a human's call (rules: agents add no apparatus).
- **Remaining Snapback media work (EX8, 2026-10-16).** Durable upload-and-place now uses the device outbox and sync round, including lost replies and reopen. Still owed from LLP 1108: retained/purged download caching, once-assets in memory, server upload inventory UI, and chunked custody above Exact’s current 4 MiB durable-upload limit.
- **A tap on a file `input` holds no picker under the agent** (2026-10-12, sb4 farm wave 7: 006, 3 minutes; E6). Only `showPicker("<id>")` from an action holds one, so `tap "<the input>"` then `pick` answers `held: none`; the snapback4 README now says to tap the button whose action calls `showPicker`. Holding a picker for a tapped file input (the web glue, as a person's click opens the system's) would make the obvious test work; 040 also had to join four references for a PDF picker (grammar, the file-pickers corpus, Documents, test pick syntax), which one worked example would cover.
- **A named agent store reported busy after an interrupted drive** (2026-10-11, wave 4: 053, 089; 3 minutes). Not reproduced here: an interrupted drive could not be staged (a drive's virtual clock ends it in seconds). The lock is a live page's Web Lock (`host/web/storage-fs.js`), so the likely holder is a Chrome from the earlier drive still running on that store's profile; a drive could refuse up front naming that Chrome's pid, from the profile's `SingletonLock`.
- **A shell's wrong directory still costs a build a minute** (2026-10-11, wave 2: 019 048 061 093 094 096 099). `exact.mjs` resolves a named file from either directory, but the slips left are shell redirects (`cat > app/app.contract` from inside the app): bash refuses them, and the next `contract build` checks the untouched file and prints `[]`. A stderr line naming the file checked and how long ago it changed (`app.contract, changed 14 min ago`) would contradict a write the agent believes it made; not done, as it adds a line to every call.
- **Selection rectangles for native reader-worker paragraphs:** ordinary text now reports `Selection.rects` (issue #274); paragraphs delegated to `RegionReaderParagraph` still return `[]`. Carry the worker's selected fragments as an event snapshot without shaping the whole paragraph on the UI thread.

- **The Mac's split, what is owed** (2026-10-13, LLP 1115.002 D4, stage 6b's as-built; `runner/src/runner/split.rs`, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/Mac/SplitMac.swift`, `SidebarViewsMac.swift`; records in `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/mac-split/`). (1) **The rule cannot tell a push from a Back or a sheet** when a row's `press` is an action: the plan holds an action's write set and no more, so a list whose rows only open sheets, or go back, splits with a pane that stays empty until its author says `navigationSplit="none"`. The route table also lacks an unmounted destination's presentation, and the recursive outer-press rule has no logical row-accessory boundary. Evaluate static modal destinations and nested row accessories across all hosts before changing this shared rule. The compiler knows which verb an action calls; the table's arrow key wants the same fact (below). (2) **Shelf now splits, but selecting another book pushes its route over the previous detail.** Its library is a `list` (LLP 1115.000 D16) and its unchanged test file passes 12 of 12 in both Mac chromes, including swipe deletion. Its `follow` still uses `go`; changing the selected book should use the list-detail pattern's `replace`. (3) **The page's view is the list's column**, so `vw`, and a width an app reads, are the column's while a stack splits (LLP 1115.002 D8.2 item 7 says the window's): the kernel's viewport is the page's view, and telling it the window's needs the root's block to be its pane. Chrome an app lays out around its stack inside the navigation root (a bar under every route) stays in the list's column for the same reason. (4) **The author's own placeholder** (`navigationSplit="placeholder"`, LLP 1116.006 D2) is not shown: the lowerer gives it `display: none` and a host sends no row; the pane says AppKit's "No Selection". (5) **A selected row's look is the table's**, so a list that is no table (the list-detail pattern's grouped form, a list in sections) splits and marks no row; a route is known to be a row's detail by a `link`, or by the table's own press, so a press row of a painted list that pushes over a detail gets the window's Back. (6) **Dark Mode's grounds differ in a split:** AppKit tints the window's own ground with the desktop (`#2c2a29` on this Mac) and a box painted in the window's background role is flat (`#1e1e1e`), so a detail that is a grouped form is darker than the list beside it. The same was found in a sheet (LLP 1115.002 D7.4); a painted role cannot be the tinted ground. (7) **The driver:** a plain `screenshot` is the page's view, the list's column; `check`, `sample`, `hover` and `tree --ax` were not run across the two panes; the pane's routes are the pane's accessibility children, not the page's. (8) **The other reading of Q10** is `SplitHost.listOwnsWindow`: run once on a scratch app with the constant turned (pictures in the records), held by no test. (9) Needs a person at a Mac: a drag of the divider and of the window's edge, the selected row's emphasis in a key window, the bar's items moving as a detail comes, a real Command-click to take the selection off, full screen, a 2x display, VoiceOver across the panes. (10) Not built: the iPad's half (the same rule, `UISplitViewController`). The web's stage 6d move and its test boundaries are recorded in LLP 1115.002 D4; integrated browser receipts live under `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/web-split-prep/`.

- **Native appearance assertions fail in the PR sweep on macOS 27.0.1 / iOS 27** (2026-10-13). (UIKit's eight HDR/image failures are fixed by 6f1d9020a.) macOS reports border/selected-text colour assertions and `PressFeedbackMacTests` (the focused run expected black at 0.16 alpha but received white at 0.18). Compare against a hand-built control in the same appearance before changing expectations. The retired Back toolbar-item crash found in the same run is fixed by #516; its focused Back/toolbar regressions pass.

- **Two things red on main that the list-rows lane met and left** (2026-10-13, on `40c6595df` with LLP 1115.000 D16 over it; none is in what that lane changed). (1) `bun scripts/smoke.mjs ios` on a freshly made iPhone 17 Pro simulator (iOS 27.0) reports seven failures, the same seven with the lane's press-path hooks taken out: Enter's key hint at the field, the canvas 1.25% beyond its reference's band, a tap under the keyboard, and four of the retained editor's focus. Not looked into; a new simulator's keyboard setting is the first thing to rule out. (2) Shelf's own test file fails now and then with `2 requests still in flight: works, changed` at a `clock data` step: once in four runs on the Mac under the driver's chrome (`a swipe deletes`) and once in six on iOS, at a first launch (`a book: progress clamps…`), each passing when run again. `works` is Discover's fetch to Open Library, asked at launch on every tab.
- **A list's rows as cells, what is owed** (2026-10-13, LLP 1115.000 D16's as-built row; `contract/lower/src/cells.rs`, `kernel/src/rows.rs`, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/RowCellsIOS.swift`, `host/web/neutral.css`, `host/linux/src/paint/neutral.rs`; records in `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/d16/`). (1) **The real UIKit-cell gate failed; ordinary lists remain painted.** The fixture implementation stays unlanded on `no-tells/ios-cells-gate-2026-10-13` at `f6f754da6`: the old collection survives the unchanged five-second release condition after session destruction and queued work, both in the combined 19-test run and alone with the identical binary. The feed releases; identify the collection's retaining owner before reopening the gate. Three lifetime repair rounds and seven extra are spent. **Ruled 2026-10-16** (Charlie asked for a decision; GPT-6 Astra, Grok 4.7 and Fable each recommended it): drawn rows stay, and the iOS tells on every screen go first (half-pixel edges, the status bar, the keyboard over a bar, type). Then a teardown-only investigation of three rounds names the retaining owner, starting from the grouped list's collection scroller (#502), before any adoption; drawn rows do not grow editing or multiple selection meanwhile, and VoiceOver list traits are worth a small slice if cheap. An earlier native-cell empty-editor hold also omitted AutoFill where the painted fixture and UIKit reference showed it. Latest-source delivered-touch reorder, paired release performance and the full landing matrix remain unqualified. Any later adoption must also qualify the bar/large title, `scrollIntoView`, restoration, keyboard avoidance, pull to refresh and `reachend` against the replacement scroll owner. (2) **iOS, what the drawn row does not do.** Its separators are redrawn after a batch that touched the list, so a row in a layout transition, an exit animation or a reorder drag moves without its line (no app in the repository has a reordered cell list: its two are card lists and say `appearance="none"`). A separator starts at the row's first text; a row whose first text is not its title starts there all the same. Not measured, so not matched: what a real touch's highlight does before it is a tap, an interactive or cancelled pop (written to follow the finger and to stay selected if cancelled, never run), an iPad's wider margin, 120 Hz, Increase Contrast, right to left. The driver's own `tap` presses without a touch, so it selects no row; a real touch (`--touch platform`) goes through the path that does, and its selected row was not pictured. (3) **Android.** The reader draws no divider, which is a Material 3 list item's default, read from Material's specification and not measured on a phone. A press shows nothing there, on a row as on any pressable (LLP 1115.001 D9), and a row keeps its author's height where Material's is 56, 72 or 88 dp. (4) **The web.** A box whose author wrote a top or bottom border carries `data-exact-said` whether or not it is a row, since an element's attributes are written from the node alone; telling it from what holds it would take the parent at the three places attributes are made (`document.rs`, `template.rs`, the live host). A row's line is under its children, so a child with an opaque ground at the row's bottom covers it. A child of a list that is out of the flow is still a row to the sheet and to the kernel. Not seen in Safari itself or on a phone's browser. (5) **The Linux desktop.** A row that is a link has no focus ring, as a link has none anywhere on that host. Not seen on a real display or with a real mouse; Windows compiled, never run. (6) **The rule.** A `display` or `flex-direction` that is no literal leaves a list unmarked. A list of `section`s is unmarked on every host (the grouped form's `listStyle="plain"` is the sectioned plain list). A plain list has no header or footer that is no row by the rule: Shelf's count and placeholders write an edge `none`, which is the author's "no separator here" and which only iOS needs; `header` and `footer` children of a `list` could be that. The terminal host draws nothing for the mark. (7) **Apps not converted.** Only Shelf's two were a `scroll` of hand-built rows turned into a `list`; other apps whose rows are hand-built in a `scroll` (look at `messages`, `fieldnotes`, `realworld`, `todo`) were not looked at one by one.
- **The Mac's table, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D5, stage 3b's as-built; `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/Mac/TableHostMac.swift`, `TableFeedMac.swift`, `TableInkMac.swift`; records in `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/mac-table/`). (1) **The frame budget on an idle Mac, and on a 120 Hz one.** The condition was met in thirteen runs of thirteen (the table's 95th percentile 8.9 to 11.9 ms against 13.33 at a 12,000 pt/s fling, release kernel), all in the quietest stretch a shared Mac gave (load 3.5 to 7.0), and an earlier cut went over in runs made while the load climbed to 9 and 20, where today's list did not; `sh ~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/mac-table/scripts/rerun.sh <checkout>` measures it again. Against a 120 Hz frame (8.33 ms) neither the table nor today's list holds its 95th percentile at that fling. (2) **What the compiler must say:** whether a row's `press` action writes the router. An arrow key that reaches a navigation row presses it (SwiftUI's list of navigation links, measured); the host knows a `link` to a route and nothing else, so a row whose action pushes is only selected. `Lowerer::unsplit` already works it out for the split, and stage 6b needs the same fact on the node. (3) **What the runner must still give the table:** a row's text when it is not mounted, for type-select (a stock table finds any row, this one mounted rows). The table now uses the runner's existing item index by key so an unmounted selection follows its item while the table does not have the keyboard; disappearance ends the selection. (4) **Kept painted, each said once in the log:** a list whose rows are in `section`s (AppKit's group rows), a list inside another list's row, a list with `reorderdrop` (the lift of a reordered row should be the table's own drag, D9), and a list that is no scroller of its own was written for and never run. (5) Items coming and going reload the table (`reloadData`): its row views are made again and a row's exit animation is cut, where `insertRows` and `removeRows` would keep them. (6) A row's chevron is not drawn but its box is still laid out, so a row's text ends that much earlier than a hand-built list's. (7) Not built: an inline run's own context menu in a row, `magnify` and a drop over a row (the table is what AppKit finds under the pointer), a native control's look in a selected row, an `svg`'s role paints in a selected row, and a swipe as a Mac user expects it (Delete, or the context menu) and multiple selection, which are vocabulary (LLP 1115.002 section 4). (8) Under load the table's tail has less room than a painted list's: its row views are made in the slice that builds rows, and the same travel back up at 5,000 pt/s was over the frame in two runs of six at the default quality of service (14.2 ms at worst).
- **A fitted text and text flow are not measured again when a typeface arrives after the first layout** (2026-10-12, found by the web look's second pass when it tried a face with `font-display: swap`; conformance's `synthetic-fit` then disagreed between the two web targets at boot by up to 41 px). Nothing in the tree can show it: LLP 1019 D5 allows a declared face only `optional` or behind a 100 ms barrier, so no face swaps in after paint, and the look's own typeface was withdrawn before it landed. The repair exists and is not landed, since it has no user: `d42768ade` on `worktree-agent-a0d07011df9723def` (both targets fit again on `document.fonts`' `loading` then `ready`, because WebKit never fires `loadingdone` for a stylesheet's face; `fit-faces.test.mjs` holds it in three engines). Take it with whatever first lets a face arrive late.
- **TalkBack on the Android reader: heard for reading, traversal, activation and an alert; four things not yet tried** (2026-10-13, LLP 1115.001 D13; Charlie, ten minutes with a Nothing Phone on Android 16: every item read once and in order by swipes, a double tap opens a book, the Remove alert reads and holds the focus). Still to try by hand, with TalkBack on and the reader's Shelf (`bun scripts/agent-android.mjs build shelf --apk`): typing in Add Book's fields (the field's name read as a hint, typed characters echoed); a checkbox or a switch saying its state when toggled; scrolling the library by two fingers and by the scroll action; the row menu. The phone used was his own, plugged into the black MacBook Pro; `com.exact.shelf.integ` was left installed on it (it was unplugged before it could be removed).
- **The not-found screen the compiler writes, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1116.009 D2; `contract/syntax/src/notfound.rs`). (1) Its words ("Not Found", "Page not found", "There is no page at", "Go Home") are English literals, outside LLP 1060's tables. (2) It is written only where the root view itself holds an `each` over a stack under the navigation root; one reached through a `use`d component gets no `notfound` row and no screen, and keeps the launch fallback to `/`. (3) The render host (`host/render/src/serve.rs`) now sees a `notfound` row for every such app, so a client-rendered app's unknown location is answered with the rendered screen and a 404 where it was the shell and a 200: check that is what a deploy wants. (4) iOS and the other native hosts were checked only through the runner's tests and the iOS simulator's `type <root> <location>`, not a cold launch at a location (no host launches a drive at one: `agent` has `--url` on the web only).
- **An app's `theme` is built on a branch and waits on one decision and one bake-off round** (2026-10-12, LLP 1095 D4 and its staging item 6, which holds what the port found; branch `worktree-agent-a3b55b60c37ad5d16`, not landed). Named colours work: Expose and the two readers ported with every compared screen identical. A role override reaches only what Exact paints from a string, so on iOS a themed app's grouped list, bar title, sheet and fields stay UIKit's (`~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/theme-proof/reach-shots/final-ios-light-1-grouped.png`). The decision: build LLP 1095 D1's reference table and the per-surface platform calls so an override reaches what the platform lets it, or land named colours alone and reword D4. For the round: four branded specs are drafted at `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/theme-proof/branded-specs.md` (a brand as values in both schemes, a typeface, a corner shape; graded on whether it holds on every screen, sheet and bar). **Ruled 2026-10-16** (as the cells entry above): first one brand on the web alone, Exact with Tamber (LLP 1121) against React, two builds and one blinded grade, graded on whether the brand holds where its author styled; the four-brand matrix only if it does, and what the builder could not say feeds Tamber's next stage. The unsaid web look stays (LLP 1115.000 D4 part B: a more designed neutral lost blind, 147 to 41). **The probe ran 2026-10-16** (`~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/theme-proof/probe-20261016/`: Fern & Fig, `gpt-6.1-sol` high building each from the round's prompts, Exact at `88bf8b8a5`; a Claude grader blind to which was which, judging only the running pages). Both built every feature (Exact 9.6 min, React 7.1). React won, 8 to 6 overall and 9 to 8 on the brand. Tamber held every colour in both schemes at both widths, declared once (8 pairs and 21 role aliases in `app.tamber`); what lost points was the brand's states and shapes, which these would close before the four-brand matrix: (1) a list row's hover and press wash is the neutral grey (`#F5F3EE`), not drawn from the brand's roles; (2) a button given a face of its own has no hover; (3) a modal sheet's corners are the host's 12 px with no way to say 14; (4) the focus ring's and the selection's colours cannot be said in Contract (`outline-color` and a selection colour are refused), so the builder wrote them in a web hatch; (5) `verify`'s separate relaunch reported stored data lost while a real relaunch kept it (a different origin from the test server's, the builder suspected). Its own bugs (Enter did not submit: `submit` exists and was not used; mixed date formats; a new plant marked overdue) are not Exact's. **LLP 1129 (2026-10-18) took (2), (4) and Enter** (the starter's title saves on Enter); (3) is said where to write it, (5) was fixed already, and (1) is an author's `:hover` on the row (LLP 1121 stage 8 B), which replaces the unsaid wash. **It ran again on 2026-10-18** (`probe-20261018/`, Exact at `38d90ee5f` against the same React build, a new blind grader): **Exact won, 8 to 7 overall**, React 9 to 8 on the brand; Exact 8, 9 on platform and robustness to React's 6, 8. The builder used `AccentColor`, `Highlight`, `submit` and the sheet route's radius, wrote no hatch, and took 7.0 minutes to 9.6. What Exact lost then, since fixed: Escape closed a sheet only by a Cancel with the id `navigationBack` names (the build's had none), and a `:hover` look drew under the unsaid wash. The builder's own: a pressed colour that inverts the button, no per-field message. Seen by that lane and not fixed: on the Mac a `filled` button was not accent-coloured in the driver's pictures, with or without a theme (the driver's window is never key, which may be all it is). The research behind this is `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/facet-research/facet-and-exact2.md`, which also says the looks Exact draws itself want their metrics as schema rows with a column per look: that is how LLP 1115.002's stage 4 and LLP 1115.000's D17 are to be built.
- **A warning for a field whose Enter does nothing beside its form's button** (LLP 1129 D5.2, set aside 2026-10-18 at the fix-loop limit). Built from printed expression trees, it kept misreading scope; built again on the analyzer's scoped slot reads (`contract/analyze/src/calls.rs` `slot_reads`), it names a one-line field (no `submit`, no `enterkeyhint="next"`, no key handler) when a button on the same route uses the field's value. The patch, its tests and four review rounds: `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/theme-proof/llp1129-field-no-submit/`. - **Fewer, later steps may cost GPT builders tokens** (2026-10-13, LLP 1116.012 as built). In the A/B of D1 (`~/bakeoff/ab_report.py ab1`), paired by spec, GPT's builds took 0.80× the commands and 1.20× the tokens (1.07–1.35 at ±1 SE, six web specs); Muse's 0.79× tokens on two. If round 15 or the next A/B shows the same for GPT, restore `verify` after each feature for it (start-here's workflow line), and measure again.
- **`SidebarMacTests.testTheAuthorsHideAndPlacementAndANarrowWindow` failed under load** (2026-10-12, found verifying #393): at load 300+ it failed at #393's merge base and on the branch alike (View ▸ Hide Sidebar left `collapsed` true but the page at 556 points, not 700, for the test's whole 10 s; the window is ordered in off screen); at load ~130 on the rebased branch it passed. Probably AppKit's collapse animation outlasting the wait; if it fails again, wait on the animation's end rather than a clock.
- **A root tablist switched to `appearance="none"` under a sheet has no edge cover at first** (2026-10-12, #393 review B, finding 5). `NavigationHost.reportCovers` measures the authored tablist against the session view, sheet or not, but `sync` returns before `reportCovers` while a presentation is in transition, and a tablist the tab bar replaced is laid out with no size, so the first measure after the switch finds no edge to cover and only a later one does. Wanted: a test that opens the sheet first, then sets the tablist to none, and finds its home-indicator cover (`testAuthoredTabsDoNotInsetASheetViewport` covers only none-then-sheet).
- **Native Fixture does not build for tvOS: its module names AppKit** (2026-10-22, after lane c mapped every app's `tvos` to the iOS manifest policy, QUEUE 263's receipt refusal). `bun host/apple/build.mjs --tvos --no-install native-fixture` now passes the receipt check and fails compiling `apps/native-fixture/modules/apple/Fixture.swift:190`: its tvOS branch names `NSMenu` and `UIView.menu`, which tvOS has not. Guard those behind `#if os(iOS)`/`canImport(AppKit)` or give tvOS its own branch; SVG Gallery builds and signs for tvOS already.
- **Content under a fixed control, what LLP 1131 leaves** (2026-10-19, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/ControlBarsIOS.swift`). On iOS a scroller under control bars (a switcher under an inline title, a composer, a pinned button row) runs under them with UIKit's edge effect. Left: (1) a large title over a fixed control that is no filter right above its list keeps the hard edge (LLP 1131 D2). A hand-built iOS 26 screen does better: SwiftUI's `safeAreaBar(edge: .top)` pins a row under a large title, which collapses (the bar 106 to 54 points) with the list scrolling under both (iOS 27.0, `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/large-title-bar-probe/`); Charlie ruled (2026-10-20) to band it. Lifting the exclusion in `PageEdgesIOS.swift` `routeBands` gets the collapsed end state right, but a short drag leaves the title half collapsed with the list back at its top: as the bar shrinks the route's cover changes, the kernel lays the row and list higher, and `setPageBand` rewrites the offset to keep `scrollTop`, which UIKit reads as a scroll back (Grok's review, filmed on the iPhone 18 Pro simulator; `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/large-title-band/`). Astra's review adds: hand a scroller back to the bar before its band's inset goes, and take one after (an open title closes otherwise), and on iPad the filter stays in the page, so it bands there. The banded scroller's inset has to follow the bar's height without moving its content, as UIKit's own adjusted inset does. A design that should hold: treat such a route as collapse mode does (`NavigationCoversIOS` `routeEdges` with `collapses`: no top cover, so layout never changes as the bar does), adopt the list as `collapseScroll` when only control bars lie between it and the header (`NavigationBarIOS` `collapse` adopts only the header's next child today), give it UIKit's automatic inset plus the bars' height as its band, and draw the bars at the safe area's top, which UIKit moves with the bar on each scroll tick and inside the snap's animation (a transform set from `viewSafeAreaInsetsDidChange`, as a view pinned to `safeAreaLayoutGuide.topAnchor` follows a hand-built bar). Tried for three rounds on 2026-10-20 and held back: `collapse` adopting the list under the bars, a `rideOffset` in `hostPlacement` set from the controller's safe-area top, and the band lifted for routes with riders. Fully collapsed it is right; at launch the title came up inline with the list scrolled, because the band's inset reaches the list after UIKit was handed it (`setPageBand` keeping `scrollTop` by an origin that does not count the bar's automatic inset); and `ControlBars.bars` reads `frame`, which a ride moves, so a riding bar stopped counting as one (it must read the laid-out frame, `center` and `bounds`). A fourth round (banding before the handoff) found two more: `ControlBars.isBar` refuses a transformed box, so a riding bar must pass when its transform is its ride alone (compare within a float's tolerance, as `applyTransform` composes it), else `collapse` adopts and drops the list on alternate calls; and with that fixed the band still alternated between 0 and the bars' height plus the bar's, as the route's top cover kept the bar's height (collapse mode's zero cover did not take hold before layout ran), so layout and the ride both moved the bars. Start from the covers: make them treat a route with riders as collapsing before anything is banded; a filter is the search's scope bar with a header search (LLP 1134), the large title's subtitle views without one (LLP 1135); (2) the web, Android, the Mac and tvOS; (3) a control bar's own text outside a control (a composer's caption, Messages Stress's) makes it no bar.
- **A phone's captures lack the keyboard, and `agent check` does not model sticky boxes** (2026-10-12, found verifying LLP 1116.008 D8). (1) `withKeyboard` (host/apple/devices.mjs) puts the keyboard into a simulator's window capture; a phone's (`--device`) has none, since the phone driver captures the app's window. Xcode 27.1's `devicectl device capture` captures the device's screen, keyboard included. (2) `AgentCheckIOS.swift` reads `style["position"]` for sticky and fixed, but the key is `position_type` (which has no `fixed`), so that test never matches. Reading `position_type` instead is not the fix: whether a covered control can be scrolled clear depends on how the covering box and the covered one each move under the scroller being judged. A box declared sticky may never stick (its natural and limit boxes are equal, its insets are `auto`, or it has an inset only on the other axis); a header with 10 points of sticky travel stops sticking before what it covers scrolls out; two controls in one stuck bar, or two bars stuck at the same edge, move together; a constraint can belong to an inner scroller while the outer one is judged; and sticking clears no sideways overflow. An approach that holds: over the room the scroller has in the clearing direction, move each box inside that scroller's content by the content's offset plus the change in `StickyConstraint.offset` of the box itself and of each sticky ancestor, counting only constraints whose scroller is that one (a box outside it stays put), and count the control reachable if at some offset its middle is inside the scroller's visible port and under no cover. A stuck row is not drawn over the form when the keyboard comes up (iPhone SE simulator, iOS 27.0, platform chrome and timing, 2026-10-16): a `position="sticky" bottom=0` row at the end of a long `scroll` sits just above the keys while a field above it has the focus.
- **RealWorld's app.js: hunt for link-by-use cuts** (2026-10-12, Charlie raised its budget 38 → 45 KiB with this line): 43.45 KiB brotli-11 at fe5b26798. Find runtime code shipped to every app that only some apps use (as the file-body path and Caltrain's grant/media/event families were moved), and lower the budget when a cut lands.
- **A subtree's containing block, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D8.2, stage 6a's proof). (1) A change inside a block is laid out from the block's root: the replay at a clipping box (`LayoutTree::prepare_boundaries`) is taken under the page's root only, because the page's pass sees every change first and a box under another engine root is none of its boundaries. The same has always held for a second arena root. A long list scrolling in a split's pane will want it. (2) The Mac reads the `block` op through the adapter path (`BatchOp.Kind.unknown`, `BlockHost.applied`), so no shared Swift moved; the iPad's half adds the kind to `Batch.swift` and takes `Runtime.blocks` out of `Mac/BlockHostMac.swift`, with the iOS tests and a tvOS build. (3) `env(safe-area-inset-*)` is one environment, the window's: an iPad sheet or column has a safe area of its own, and no block carries one. (4) A subtree in a container's view has left the page's key-view loop, its accessibility parent is the container's view, and the driver's `tap` and coordinates know one window (read, not run; the document's "As built"). Since stage 6b a split's pane is in the page's Tab order and the driver taps in it; its routes' accessibility parent is still the pane. (5) `browser_host_block.tsv` was measured by a script that is not in the repository, since an agent adds none: modelled on `fixtures/browser_paint_order.mjs`, it also reproduces `browser_containing_block.tsv`'s 155 cases. Beside the fixture it would let the cases be measured again when the rule changes. (6) A content region and a block are refused together; an exclusion shapes no text across a block's edge.
- **iOS content ends in a line at the keyboard's accessory bar, not under its glass keys** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.006 D6, round 8's unit converter): the viewport ends at the keys' top, the bar's included, as a hand-built screen pinned to `keyboardLayoutGuide` does; what shows behind the bar is now the colour the content's bottom edge goes on with (a grouped list's background), not the canvas's white band. A `UITableViewController`'s list instead runs on under the glass ± and Done, inset by the bar: the TO header was still cut in half at the line. Running the edge scroller under the bar would take the bar's height as a bottom safe-area inset (the route's cover, the scroller's adjusted inset, `reveal`'s port). Not seen: a tab container's route behind the bar, which the owner's frame extends under the keys and paints with the route's backdrop, not the list's.
- **The Mac's text and scrolling, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.000 D7.7, D7.9, D8.2, D8.5). (1) Selected text's ink, the rest: an underline under a part-transparent role colour (a label tier) stays part transparent when selected, where its glyphs are solid (the recolouring keeps the line's own alpha); and a content region's role colours are its pair's, resolved in Rust, where a paragraph's are AppKit's own (before this too). (2) AppKit's own selected-text menu, the rest (`TextMenuMac.swift`): the text view it is asked of lays the selection out itself, so Look Up's highlight is on the page's glyphs only for a selection inside one paragraph (its first line exactly, later lines by the paragraph's pitch); across paragraphs, or in a giant paragraph, it is near them. Nobody has opened the menu by hand, chosen Look Up, Translate, Share or a Service from it, or seen its Writing Tools items act on read-only text. (3) The localised standard menu titles are not built, and wait on Charlie's choice (LLP 1115.000 D7.9; measured 2026-10-12 with probe bundles run in Japanese for the process only, macOS 27.0.1). AppKit localises no title an app supplies, by action or otherwise: Xcode's own Mac template is a nib with English titles (`MainMenuApp.xib`) that its developer translates. What AppKit does give in the person's language is what it builds itself: the items it adds (Writing Tools, AutoFill, Start Dictation, Emoji & Symbols, Close All) and the menus it hands out (`NSTextView.defaultMenu`: Cut, Copy, Paste, Paste and Match Style, and the Spelling and Grammar, Substitutions, Transformations and Speech submenus whole; `NSFontManager`'s Font menu), which leaves the application menu, Undo, Redo, Delete, Select All, Find, File, View, Go, Window and Help in English. SwiftUI's `App` does build every standard title localised (About, Settings…, Services, Hide, Hide Others, Show All, Quit, the whole Edit menu with `TextEditingCommands`, Minimize, Zoom, Bring All to Front, Help), so the one supported way to all of them is to let SwiftUI own the main menu: the Mac executable's entry becomes an `App` and Exact's File, Go, shortcut and Develop items become its `Commands`, which is a rewrite of `DevMenuMac` and `ShortcutsMac`'s menu half and belongs with LLP 1115.002. The other ways are the two ruled out: ship translations, or read AppKit's and SwiftUI's tables by key (`MenuCommands` has 25 of 48). Beneath all of them: an Exact app's bundle declares its localisations only when it grants a device (`usageKeys`), so every other app runs in English whatever the person's language, and then AppKit's own strings are English too (its alerts, panels, the text menu). A bundle that declares the app's `lang` and its strings tables' tags is the first step of any of these. (4) AppKit's focus ring has never been seen around an Exact field: it is drawn only in a key window of an active app, and neither the driver's picture, `cacheDisplay` nor the window's layer tree holds it (probes outside the repository, macOS 27.0.1; a screen capture needs a permission the lanes do not have). So three things rest on AppKit's contract and want one look by hand in a key window: a bare field with a border its author wrote shows AppKit's exterior ring around its box and the border under it (LLP 1115.000 D7.9, built); a bare field with no written border shows Exact's two-point ring on its box and, if AppKit draws too, a second ring outside it (the audit's MAC-31 infers it; LLP 1104 D6's note that "the masks alone never drew" was from the driver's picture, which shows no AppKit ring on a native field either); and a native textarea, whose stand-in ring may double its scroll view's. If AppKit draws them, Exact's rings go. A textarea with a written border draws Exact's ring outside its box in AppKit's colour, since its text view's own ring is cut off by its clip view, which is also inferred. (5) Under the agent a wheel is applied at once, with no momentum, and a window has no opening animation; a live gesture test passed 22 or 23 of 27 runs and is kept outside the suite. (6) The page hit test from #327 costs about 10 ms a pointer move on a 400-paragraph page in an unoptimized build. (7) `CTRunDraw` draws no underline, so a paragraph with per-run shadows or a hidden run loses its underlines. (8) By hand on a Mac, none done: a trackpad scroll's momentum and bounce in Shelf's list and a mouse wheel's step; the I-beam over prose, the hand over links, the arrow over buttons; clicking a row, a card and a button in a background window; hover in a background window and after a drag; the window's opening animation and no Show Tab Bar; spelling marks in a plain field; keys in a key window, with a custom `DefaultKeyBinding.dict`.
- **The Mac's grouped form, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D6, stage 4's as-built). (1) Three controls in a row are not the reference's: a `select` is AppKit's pop-up button (its row 44 points where SwiftUI's menu-style picker makes 38), a number field keeps its field beside the stepper, and a text area keeps its bezel. A `button` row that is not `destructive` stays a row, where a Mac form draws every button as a push button: D6 ruled the destructive one only. (2) The destructive row's bezel is AppKit's push button drawn under Exact's pressable (`Mac/FormRowsMac.swift`), so the whole row takes the press where the reference takes it on the button; its key look, a real click and VoiceOver's reading were not seen by a person. (3) The card's corner is a circle's 10 points; the reference's is continuous (`corner-shape` could be one more metric). A lone field's text starts 2 points right of a row's title (the borderless cell's own inset). (4) The `metrics` table's web column is also what Linux, Windows and Android read until LLP 1115.000 D17 gives a painted host its own: when the neutral look or one of them gets a column, the grouped sheet's surfaces, its 26-point corner and its 52-point rows change there cell by cell, and `host/web/neutral.css`'s `!important` rules on a grouped list go (the entry below, item 3). The sheet's surfaces on the web are still literal pairs for that reason. (5) No fixture lays the Mac's column out beside a browser: the JS target compiles the web's column only, so the Mac's form is held by measurements against SwiftUI's and by the kernel's own tests.

- **The web's neutral look, what is owed now that it is every build's** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.000 D4 part B; the default changed on Charlie's word in `3c6a16360`; the sheet is `host/web/neutral.css`; its second pass after Expo's styleguide was graded blind and not adopted). (1) **The look's `min-width` on a platform button replaces a flex item's automatic minimum**, so an unsized button squeezed in a row cuts its title at its edge where it used to spill past the row (`agent-check.test.mjs` holds `clipped` for it). A size the author writes now wins: the host names a written `width` or `height` and the floor and padding step aside on that axis, which took `synthetic-grid` out of conformance's holds. (2) The schema's neutral column holds a grouped list's two grounds and its cards' corners since D17, and both web targets read that column, so the sheet's three `!important` rules over the compiler's sheet now repeat the row and can go, with a browser test that no pixel moves; a card's own fill is still the sheet's literal pair (`#fff / #1c1c1e`, a hair from the neutral surface), and its rows are iOS's 52 points. (3) The accent the kernel resolves itself (a motion between colours) is the table's blue; what CSS draws is the browser's hue at the table's lightness. (4) A context menu opens on the release of the press that asked for it, in Chrome on a Mac too, where it used to open on the press. (5) The Back button says "Back" only on a page in English; a word in another language wants a strings key the host can read. (6) A range draws no filled part before its thumb (only Firefox has the pseudo-element); a disabled date field's ink in WebKit is the engine's. (7) The author's split placeholder starts a pixel inside the list column's line and ends a pixel short of the window: the shell places it at the column's `100%`, and the look draws the divider as a border where the old one drew a shadow (`wide.test.mjs` pins 319 and says so). (8) Two lines of WebKit's cross-browser conformance are new under the look, and neither was looked into past its captures: `synthetic-startend`'s long list mounts one row more in Chrome than in WebKit after its wheel (20 lines; the two pages' rows were already 2 px apart, and the taller header moved the edge of the mounted window between them), and in `synthetic-menus` a right click on a checkbox focuses it in Chrome and its row in WebKit (4 lines, where the old look had 16 for the checkbox's own size; `known-webkit.json` names the same convention for a button, not for a checkbox). (9) The README's web pictures (`docs/screenshots/todo-web.webp`, which is nothing but unsaid controls, and the gallery) were captured in LLP 1116 D1's look: capture them again when the second pass has landed, once. (10) Not run: Safari itself (WebKit through Playwright stood in), a phone's browser, Windows, a real contrast theme (Chrome's emulation stood in), a coarse pointer beyond Chrome's emulation.
- **The neutral look on Linux and Windows, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.000 D17; the look's numbers are the schema's `metrics`, its switch `show_neutral_look` in `host/linux/src/app.rs`, its shapes `host/linux/src/paint/neutral.rs`; the pictures beside Chrome's are in `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/d17/`). (1) **Most chrome is still owed.** Direct route headers now get the Neutral ground, label ink and bottom border from schema-backed effective rows, and nested ordinary bordered buttons paint no edge. Neutral header padding, minimum height, sticky positioning and top now also come from schema metrics, with browser-measured geometry and CPU-painter tests on a Mac. Narrow tab bars now take the schema ground/top edge under the broad direct controlled-tab CSS predicate; ordinary bordered/gray tab faces follow selected, unselected, hovered, disabled and authored ink while retaining their boxes. Narrow native bordered/gray tabs now also use schema geometry with actual root-font context, measured against Chrome and checked by the Linux CPU painter on a Mac, including authored overrides and retained membership changes. Back presentation, sidebars, segmented controls, alerts, dialogs, menus and sheets remain. The kernel supplies header defaults below stored values and authored declarations, with live ancestry/prop invalidation. Other platform columns are unchanged. The tab-paint slice passed integrated Caltrain on Linux, Mac and iOS and Shelf on Mac/iOS at `5b1b2a8e5`. At `7d0be3297`, the tab-geometry slice passes the six checks, affected Linux/web-JS suites and Neutral CSS tests, ordinary Caltrain on Linux/Mac/iOS and Shelf in both Mac chromes and iOS. The four Linux fixture drives cover both widths and schemes, root-font changes and retained membership. Apple app drives use normal builds; iOS ran on an owned simulator that was deleted. Mac Caltrain still shows the previously recorded inverted text and dark-card contrast defects. A physical Linux display and Windows remain unqualified. The Linux host still presents no `dialog` or popover (LLP 1001, LLP 1021 D2). The CPU painter's translucent dark separator produces RGB 57 over RGB 18 for both implied and authored identical borders; Chrome produces 56. Keep that rasterization difference separate from header-rule equivalence. (2) **The unsaid hover and press of a pressable that is no control** (a card, a custom `button`, a link: D6) are not drawn on these hosts, nor a disabled custom button's half opacity; a painted control's hover and pressed washes are. (3) **An ordinary button's title is its ancestors' size but not their family**: the kernel computes its size and weight through inheritance, and the painter still sets the host's default family, style and letter spacing, where a page's button inherits all of them. A `line-height` written on a platform button is not read either. (4) **A button whose author wrote a width narrower than its padded title** wraps the title in the padded room while it is measured, where the page drops the padding: the measure cannot tell a written width from a stretched one (the painter can, and the box is the written one). (5) The accent is the table's blue: a browser draws its own hue at the table's lightness (item 4 above), and these hosts have none to fit. A selection's highlight in a field is still the painter's own blue pair. (6) A file field always says "No file chosen": the Linux picker hands the files to the app and keeps no name. A date field draws its ISO value in the kernel's box for Chrome's date control, with no picker's icon. A `type="search"` field is as wide as any field (Chrome's is 150 px where the others are 176), and a select is as wide as its widest option in the host's font. (7) A select's open menu is the look's raised surface with rows of its own height (the text and 8 px, at least `menu-row-height`), which no page was measured against: a browser opens its own menu there. (8) **Windows was compiled for its target and never run**: nothing of it was seen on a screen. Zig is not on the dev Mac, so the second lane's two cross-target checks were run with Zig 0.15.2 unpacked in a scratch directory. (9) Not seen: a Linux desktop's real display and mouse (the host ran headless on a Mac, under the driver); a coarse pointer (the `-coarse` rows are read by the sheet alone, since the kernel is not told a pointer's kind); a 2x display; a font other than the driver's.

Determine whether first initialization under a theme captures that app’s concrete colours. This is unmeasured. Tab-only admission now reads only its role-valued tab defaults and does not initialize the header table; no header-theme repair is included.

(1) `markdown`, `markdown-stress` and `exact-live` gained `role="heading"` with `font-size="1em" font-weight=400` on their heading blocks to keep their look, and none was run on a host. (2) A pale blob shows inside a sheet where the page's checked radio sits, and behind an alert on the base too: a system effect or a capture artefact, not told apart. (3) The `lower-alertdialog` refusal still says a confirmation "is dismissed by a tap outside it": D13.4's. - **Conformance after the Shelf bisect, what is owed** (2026-10-12). The lines Shelf and `synthetic-failed` gave are fixed, and were three things: the wasm page had no `showModal(id)` from an action (since `b968df0d8`), a tap the page refused left its reveal's scroll on the one page that was asked (since Shelf's first commit, `cf7196c0d`; the two pages never differed), and the JS target lost a resource's failure when a commit that had cleared it was refused (since `9ad0230e5`, #380, in the lane's own set). LLP 1116.005's "0 failures across 9 targets" named neither Shelf nor the synthetic plans: both were failing on that tree (`53205e43b`). Left: (1) The per-commit lane never drives Shelf: `scripts/async.mjs` `WEB_APPS` does not name it, so its lines stood for two days until two lanes ran it by hand. Adding it is a check, so it waits for a human's word, and Shelf is not ready for one: Discover fetches openlibrary.org for real, and in 2 of 9 runs the boot compare fell between the two pages' answers (21 lines at `boot`, `state resources.works…`, none after). (2) `conform.mjs` taps the JS page only when the wasm page took the tap, so whatever a tap the wasm page refuses leaves behind shows as a difference between pages that do not differ. The cause found is fixed in `reveal` (host/web/navigation.js); a target behind a modal `dialog`, inert with no `inert` attribute, is still scrolled to before its tap is refused as covered (read, not run). Tapping both pages and comparing the refusals would end the class. (3) On the dev Mac under load, runs of the lane's set give lines the next run does not: the wasm page's video in `video-player` has no duration where the JS page's has (37 lines, `state slots.duration: wasm 0 js 10`; here, and in a lane's run the day before); `harness: Chrome <pid> did not exit` after a drive that was equal at every step (ten targets in one run, at a load average of 90); and `synthetic-resize boot`, the wasm page's first size report in before the compare and the JS page's not. (4) The first run in a fresh checkout answers `linux-open: linux build is stale` for canvas-gallery, duo-lab and messages: the JS build writes `app.contract.d.ts` into the app's folder after `--build` made the Linux binary, whose dep-info names that folder (seen twice; a second run is clean). (5) The driver's rule counts `apps/<app>/android` as an input of the app's web build (`scripts/agent-launch.mjs` `webInputs` leaves out `apple`, `ios`, `macos` and `linux` only), so a change to Shelf's Android crate makes its web dist stale for a drive and for conformance. (6) On the web Shelf's swipe action is a 16 px `symbol:delete` drawn in the accent, though its button is `destructive`: a swipe moves the row 16 px aside, on both targets. (7) `scrollBy` is still the JS target's alone on the web, as `docs/contract-grammar.md` says; nothing holds either web target's commands to `HOST_COMMANDS`, which is how `showModal` was missed.
- **The focus follows the route on the painted hosts, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.000 D4.2 and ruling 5, `host/linux/src/presenter/arrive.rs`). (1) A heading or a route that takes the focus after a key's press shows no ring: the painted hosts ring controls and fields only, where the web shows `:focus-visible`'s outline there. (2) The presenter is Windows's and Android's reader's too, and the rule was seen on neither: on Android a finger's press leaves no ring by the rule (LLP 1115.001 D7.6) and TalkBack's focus is the reader's own. (3) A pointer's press gives up its control's focus before its handler runs (`dispatch_press`), so that control hears no `blur` when the route's heading then takes the focus; the web fires one. (4) A commit's `focus()` command runs after its commit here, so the heading holds the focus for a moment first; where it ends is the web's.
- **The web's routes as pages, what is owed** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.000 D4 part A). (1) A server-rendered page's `<title>` is the app's name until the runtime starts and sets the route's. (2) `tree --ax` lists a sheet's ancestors as "outside modal". (3) A menu does not take the focus when it opens; its arrows work once it has it. (4) A route that arrives without a press (a timer, Forward in the wide split) takes the focus only when nothing has it. (5) Arrows on a tab bar move the focus and do not press, since a tab's press can fetch; a tablist is one Tab stop. (6) Not run: Safari, Firefox and WebKit (`conform-firefox`, `conform-webkit` included), a screen reader's speech, a real finger; the focus ring's behaviour in Firefox and Safari is from HTML's rule for `:focus-visible`, not a run.
- **The filesystem helper's callers go one at a time, and a cold sixth check has two hundred of them** (2026-10-12, found repairing the wait they shared, LLP 1030.002 D3). Every Bun process that uses the helper runs its own `cargo build` of it under the build claim (`scripts/filesystem.mjs`), a no-op once it is built. With every app build script due and `exact-bake`'s tests beside it, 228 callers held the claim in turn: for 32 s at a load of 20 (median 0.13 s each) and for 68 s at a load of 165 to 205 (median 0.23 s). The cold build was 1.8 and 3.6 s of those, so the line, not the cold build, is the likelier place the old 60 s went (the failing gates, at loads of 300 to 800, were not measured). Nothing fails now, however long the line: a caller waits while the claim's owner runs. But the line is the check's time under load. A caller that saw another take the claim after it began waiting could use that caller's capture without building again: the sources would still have been checked during its own life.
- **A virtualized list at the page's top edge, what LLP 1130 leaves** (2026-10-18, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/PageEdgesIOS.swift`). It reports its box as its port (`CollectionIOS` `geometry`), so rows that have scrolled under the status bar are drawn only while the list keeps rows past its port; counting the band as port needs `keepStart`, `shift` and `correct` to convert offsets the same way.
- **A transformed box below a root does not reach the document's extent on iOS and the Mac** (2026-10-19, Astra's review of the root-overflow fix). `document_extent` (`host/apple/src/layout.rs`) takes boxes where layout put them, so a child moved by `translate` past the root's foot is not scrolled to, where CSS counts a transformed box's scrollable overflow (CSS Transforms 1 §3); the root's own transform is projected by the presenter. Projecting a descendant needs its resolved transform, which the kernel does not hand the host. The extent is a size from the root's origin, so overflow above or left of it that a root's own transform turns into the document (a `rotate=180` root holding a box at `top=-100`) is dropped too.
- **Linux's authored accent, what the painting scope does not reach** (2026-10-14, after `roles::authored_accent`, the painter's per-node scope for `AccentColor`, `LinkText` and Exact's tint roles). Not covered, each found by review and none a few lines: (1) an SVG descendant that authors its own `accent-color` paints under its `svg`'s accent (`svg_item` opens no scope); (2) a retained content region's replayed SVG and gradient stops resolve with no scope (`ink`, `Gradient::resolved` at replay), so under a region-local accent they are the app's tint; (3) a native button's face parts (title, subtitle, symbol) resolve under the button's accent, not a child's own; (4) a theme alias to a tint role (`CanvasText = "LinkText"`) with a theme `AccentColor` resolves to the theme's colour before the scope is read (`painted_override`); (5) `accent-color="AccentColor"` keeps the accent around only inside the walk, so a hosted field or a region capture under it, with a nearer accent above, reads the tint; (6) colour motion targets (`kernel/src/motion.rs` `color_targets`) still resolve with no scope, so a transition into `AccentColor` under an authored accent eases to the tint and snaps at its end (a fix must keep Apple's `@tint` set, not the web's); (7) a recorded row (Android's recording backend) under `accent-color="AccentColor"` keeps an ancestor accent that changes after it was recorded: invalidation stops at the authored row (`txn.rs`) and a recording's validity does not compare its effective accent (`paint/rows.rs`).
- **`lower-screen-by-state` misses screens swapped by sibling `when`s** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.005 D1.1): it reads a `when`/`else` chain or a `match`; round 5's event-rsvp swapped its list and detail with two `when`s, `wide or not phoneDetail` and `wide or phoneDetail`, a hand-built split keyed on the viewport too, so nothing warns. A pair of sibling `when`s over the same states with complementary arms, each a screen, could count as one swap; the viewport term would have to be read as the split's, not a loading view's.
- **iOS after wave 2's keyboard and bars, what is owed** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.000 D9 and D11, seen on an iPhone 17 Pro simulator, iOS 27.0). (1) A pushed route's large title arrives expanded now (`9b3e3c110`; LLP 1115.000's row for D11.1 says what UIKit needs). What is left of it: a single stack installed partway through the first batch, where a cover cannot be reported, still gets its first screen's header cover after UIKit's first layout; UIKit opens a stack's first title all the same (in UIKit alone on iOS 27, a root's scroller moved up to its controller's top a turn after the first layout), but it is the cause the pushed title had. A header's cover held back by a list's slice in flight (`Session.whenIdle`) would reach layout after the push had begun, and that push's title would arrive collapsed: read in the code, not reproduced. Under the driver's own drag (no `--touch platform`) a scroller cannot be pulled past its top, so a collapsed title does not open there; a real touch opens it. A route's scroller that is also its bottom-edge scroller (`reachBottom`), gives the bar up to a reason above it (chat-rooms' failed load) and gets it back when the reason goes, comes back scrolled (139 points on an iPhone 17 Pro) and closes the open title: while it is still under the bar its top inset is UIKit's, which `scrollTopInset` stops counting once it no longer collapses the title, so the position kept for it is off by that inset (2026-10-17, `pushed-title.contract`'s `swap-fail` pressed twice). A selected tab tapped again over content already at its top under a collapsed title (a scroller replaced in place leaves one, and so does an authored `scrollTop` of 0) does nothing (`scrollToTop`), where UIKit's status-bar tap scrolls on to the open title's inset and opens it (measured in UIKit alone, 2026-10-17); a scroller that never saw its title open holds only the collapsed inset in `scrollOrigin`, and an inset kept from another scroller goes stale when a hatch turns large titles off or a search field leaves the bar. (2) `user-select="text"` on iOS is built for a selection inside one paragraph (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.000's row "D11.6, `user-select`", `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/TextSelectionIOS.swift`). What it leaves: **(a) a selection across paragraphs.** UIKit's `UITextInteraction` takes a touch only when its view is the touch's hit view (measured: on a container whose text is in child views it never begins), so one selection over several paragraphs needs one view that takes every touch on their text and hands a tap on to the paragraph's own press and links; the document it would answer from is there (the Mac's order of paragraphs in `TextSelectionMac.swift`, the runner's `list_text` for a virtualized list's rows). The Markdown reader selects one block at a time until then. **(b) VoiceOver's text selection rotor**, which a `UITextView` offers because it is its own text input: the paragraph is a static text element. **(c) The highlight is over the glyphs**, a text view's under them: the container below the text is asked of the display interaction's delegate, which `UITextInteraction` owns (the reference's input was not asked for one). **(d) A triple tap** selects a paragraph in a text view; not measured here (the touch runner has no triple tap) and not seen. **(e) Linux, Windows, Android and tvOS take `text` as `auto`**, said in LLP 1001 and the guide but not by the host: one line in the Linux driver's log, as `font-stack-fallback` has, would say it where it happens. Selecting text there is the Mac's whole selection again (an anchor and a focus over the painted lines, the fill and the selected ink in the painter, a pointer drag, the clipboard), and `host/linux` has none of it. **(f) The driver's plain `tap` on text with a selection up** presses, where a finger's tap clears the selection and presses nothing; a real double tap needs the touch runner to make one (`XCUICoordinate.doubleTap`), and `tap <id> dblclick` is the recognized selection until then. **(g) Before iOS 18** nothing asks a text input whether it is editable; an empty input view is there to keep the keyboard away, on no simulator this Mac has. Not seen on a phone: the loupe under a finger, the haptic, a handle dragged, VoiceOver. (3) The page's own scroller (the viewport) puts no keyboard away on a drag, and no prop reaches it: D9.1's default is for a `scroll` or `list`. (4) An action's `focus("id")` or `blur("id")` does not reach a header's search field, which is UIKit's `UISearchBar` while its authored node is hidden; its Search key resigns it now. (5) Under the driver's frozen clock, `tap <grouped list> drag 0 -300` on a list under a large title collapses the title and leaves the list at its top (the collection view puts its offset back over the bar's change of inset, as `KeyboardAvoidanceIOS.swift` `reveal` already works around); a real touch scrolls it. (6) `exact new`, the patterns and most recipes still write `navigationScroll` on a route with one scroller, which iOS no longer needs. D11.4 (a borderless field in a grouped row) landed as LLP 1116.005 D2 while this lane built its own, which was dropped; one thing that one had and D2 has not: `appearance="auto"` written on a field in a row does not bring the bordered field back. (7) Not tried: a real finger pulling the keyboard down in a `resizes-content` page (the driver refuses a drag that leaves the viewport, which ends at the keys), VoiceOver speaking a link, the ⌘ overlay on an iPad with a hardware keyboard, pull-to-refresh on a grouped list by a finger.
- **A terminal paints a link run with no colour of its own in the link role's blue** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115 §3 meeting LLP 1101): `contract/lower/src/tags.rs` `link_color` gives a run with an `href` in its paragraph the `LinkText` role unless its own colour is written, and the terminal resolves the role's fallback (`#0088ff` in light, since LLP 1115.000 D3.2 re-measured the tint). The terminal work's test was written on a branch older than that rule and expected the parent's colour; when the two merged its expectation was moved to the role (`host/terminal/tests/it/cli.rs`, `inline_paint_carries_colours_decorations_and_links_without_crossing_siblings`). Whether a terminal should show the role, its own default ink with the underline and the OSC 8 link, or a colour from its palette is the terminal's decision, and it has not been made. Until it is, a terminal entry's block `link` of text is not given the role that LLP 1115.000 D3.5 gives one on every other host (`link_color` reads the lowering profile; `a_terminal_entrys_block_link_keeps_inheriting`).
- **The terminal host's own `eprintln!` can panic on a nonblocking terminal** (2026-10-18, grok's note on 5bad0f7bb): `host/terminal/src/term/unix.rs` `OutputFlags::nonblocking` sets `O_NONBLOCK` on stdout's file description, which stderr shares when both are the terminal. A write to stderr while output is backed up (a slow or stopped terminal) then returns `EAGAIN`, and `eprintln!` panics on it: `collection.rs:210`/`215`, `collection/focus.rs:123` and the commit trace at `host.rs:470` report errors this way. 5bad0f7bb fixed only the test child's readiness line. Fixed when the host's stderr writes cannot panic under the signal tests' blocked-output conditions (`host/terminal/tests/it/cli.rs`).

A graphic neither wraps nor scrolls; its remedies are to fit it (`width="100%"`) or clip it (`overflow="hidden"` on the box it fills). Leaving a decoration out was tried and dropped: a graphic hidden from assistive technology can still be what a person sees (an icon that is a button's only visible label), and inherited `aria-hidden` does not reach a presented route's views.

The split, iPad and Mac: (1) the kernel lays a split stack's root route out at the column's width and the top at the rest (a runner-side split read from the same rule and `navigationSplit`, not a host override the layout never sees), (2) the presenter shows the root route's controller beside a navigation controller of the routes above it (`UISplitViewController`'s double column, `NSSplitViewController` with a source list on the Mac) and moves the controllers across a size-class change with no router change, (3) the list-detail pattern's wide test then taps a second row while the first's detail shows, which a phone cannot, (4) while nothing is selected the detail column draws the platform's placeholder, or lays out the root route's box marked `navigationSplit="placeholder"` there in place of the `display: none` the lowerer gives it (LLP 1116.006 D2; the web's: index.html `data-exact-split-empty`).
