- **A `dialog`'s sheet on iOS, what is owed** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.000 D11.5, `DialogSheetIOS.swift`): a `dialog` that is not a confirmation is a UIKit sheet as tall as its content now. (1) The kernel lays the dialog out against the page, not the sheet. On an iPhone the two are the same width (iOS 27 floats a short sheet 8 points in and scales its 402-point content to fit), but on an iPad, where a sheet is narrower than the window, a `width="100%"` dialog is wider than its sheet and scrolls sideways; a host box for the dialog, as a route's is its sheet, would fix it. No iPad was tried. (2) With a field focused UIKit raises the sheet above the keyboard by itself; the page beneath is still laid out again above the keyboard though no one sees it, and a tap on the sheet's blank ground does not end editing as one on the page does. (3) Hardware Escape and VoiceOver's scrub are left to UIKit's sheet and were not tried. (4) macOS's laid-out `dialog` fires no `cancel` at Escape, which iOS's swipe, the web and the terminal do. (5) tvOS presents it over the full screen and was never run.
- **Android's accessibility, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.001 D13, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md), "Accessibility"; seen on a Pixel 10 Pro XL with Shelf and `apps/unsaid`). (1) **TalkBack itself has not read a screen.** The reader answers accessibility services and the framework serves its nodes (checked from outside the app with `uiautomator`); TalkBack found them and focused the first node of two screens. But a touch or key from `adb shell input` goes straight to the app on that phone and never reaches TalkBack, and its speech is not logged, so its swipe through a screen, its double tap, its scroll action, and its focus in an alert and a menu are unseen. It needs a person with the phone for ten minutes, or the owner's word on a virtual input device (`adb shell uinput`: a keyboard for TalkBack's Alt keys, gone when its process ends), which is not among the things a lane may do to a shared phone. Until then D13's gate is not passed. (2) TalkBack asks for the notification permission each time it is turned on there and the prompt does not close on Back: a lane that turns TalkBack on leaves that prompt in a background task unless someone answers it, which is the owner's to do. (3) `Tab` and `link` are English role descriptions: the reader has no strings by language. (4) With a service on, a scroll states the whole tree every frame; coalesce it to the scroll's end if TalkBack's own scroll stutters. (5) A swipe row's trailing action has no accessible action of its own (the row's menu has it). (6) A polite live region that arrives with a new route is spoken once where the app has no top app bar. (7) The reader's click, long click, scroll and set-progress actions were not reached on a phone (only accessibility focus was); a hosted web view or video under the provider was not tried.
- **Shelf's test file is 11 of 12 on the Android reader about one run in six, and the test that fails is not Discover** (2026-10-12; `bun scripts/agent.mjs android --reader --app shelf --test apps/shelf/app.test.contract`, two Pixel 10 Pro XLs). This entry said "discover shows an error, then tries again" passed on a reader built from `a0600e637` and failed on one from `79d468eaf`. Run again the same day it failed on neither, nor on `c48f4e0ea`: 26 runs of the whole file over the three builds (five with `79d468eaf`'s own driver) and 15 of that test alone or by hand, none failing. After `pass fetch` that test's retry is a live fetch of openlibrary.org from the phone (236 to 676 ms that day), so a network that stalls leaves `discover-error` in the tree as the entry described; "Android's bars" (8) below saw one such fetch time out. No commit broke it. What does fail, on all three builds (4 of the 26; 2 of 16 run alone), is "a book: progress clamps and moves the status, rating, notes" at its last step: `tap "detail-status-want"` straight after `type "notes" "Statues"` answers "view 316 is covered or not hit at its projected center". The keyboard that `type` raises is told to exact2 within about a second, between the driver's requests: the viewport goes from 443 by 896 to 443 by 548.5 and the page scrolls 137 points to keep Notes in view, so the control goes from y 212 to y 75. Of 16 drives that read the control's place and then tapped it, 12 pressed it where it was, 3 where it went, and 1 was refused. A person sees a correct screen throughout. Not found: which of the reader's turns the refused tap lands in (the window told, its paint, the scroll `reveal_owed` makes after that paint, that scroll's paint). `Presenter::boxes` does not repaint for an attached display, and the Canvas host's agent (`host/linux/src/canvas/agent.rs`) takes one display turn after a request and none before it. Find the turn, then either `type` answers only once the keyboard it raised has been told and shown, or the agent takes display turns until none is owed before it reads a request. A `CanvasHost` has no test in `exact-linux` (its boot sets the process's environment), so the fix wants a test binary of its own.

- **`agent ios` wheel on a grouped list stops short of its end** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.003): `Agent.scroll` clamps to the collection view's `contentSize`, which a self-sizing grouped list only estimates until its cells come in, so one `tap <list> wheel 0 20000` landed rows short (row 29 of 30 under the tab bar, row 30 unseen) where drags reach the end. Clamp again until the size settles, as `TabEdgeIOSTests.scrollToEnd` does.
- **Caltrain in dark mode: white text on its light glass card** (2026-10-11, `agent check dark`, 18 findings): the card's material or fill stays light in dark mode while its text follows the scheme; one of the repository apps the colour warning names (above).
- **A grouped list's cell kept across a batch that changed the list's height shows empty on iOS** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.003 D2.1): the keyboard batch puts every carried row back (`prepare`), then `mount` carries rows only into `visibleCells`, judged before the new layout; a cell left out but kept (not reused) comes back into the port empty, its row hidden beneath the list, until a batch touches the list. The keyboard's reveal carries rows after it scrolls (`carryRows`, KeyboardAvoidanceIOS.swift); a finger's scroll after the keys come up does not, nor the driver's: after Return moved focus and a submit added a row, `tap` on the next exercise's field answered "… covers its middle" (on f6769a64e too). Likely the same family as round 4's blank Finish row below.
- **The first `contract` call on a cold checkout builds the compiler for minutes** (2026-10-11, from closing `issues/closed/20261009-agent-port-cost-vs-swiftui.md`): `scripts/exact.mjs` builds `contract` on first use, so a new app's first `contract types`/`build` waits on a cold cargo build. `host/web-js/module.mjs --prebuild` warms only the web builder; a cached or prebuilt compiler would remove the wait from an agent's first minutes.
- **iOS scrolling's leftover suggestions** (2026-10-11, from closing `issues/closed/20261002-scroll-efficiency.md` §7–§8, whose three changes landed in 7098e9537, edaaa7c90, 1ff7407af, a1cd643d2): a 120 Hz device trace first; then per-frame checks driven by what is actually owed, the macOS PressClock on Core Animation's clock, and a per-frame budget for a fling.
- **Windows app-storage removal: the progress guard and a real batch test** (2026-10-11, review of the storage-results fix): `vendor/ibex/crates/ibex2/src/stdlib/windows_fs.rs`'s recursive `remove` drains `names_batch` until it is empty or a call errors, with no `require_progress` as Unix has (`app_fs_unix.rs`), so a file held open with `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` (delete pending, still listed) fails `fs.rm` though the pass made progress. `windows_fs_tests.rs`'s `recursive_remove_drains_more_than_one_directory_batch` uses 300 short names, under the 16 MiB listing cap, so it passes without the batching. Port the guard, make the test exceed the cap, and run both on the fleet's Windows machine.
- **Surface-control overflow-child multitouch and cancellation** (2026-10-11, review of #421): two fingers on the overflow child are not tracked because the child lacks `isMultipleTouchEnabled`; a wrapper disabled mid-press can lose cancellation; a focus callback moving the label before the touch id is stored can strand a held control; a link and a blank spot lifted in one `touchesEnded` can skip the link. The round-3 per-touch child route was descoped; design and test a UIKit-delivered route before restoring it.
- **This repository's apps earn `lower-colour-one-scheme`** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.002 D1): outside the docs and patterns, which earn none, the warning names 1,693 literal colours. 676 are in apps people see, the round-4 failure (light surfaces and grey text written as hex): realworld 121, examples/calendar 115, weatherlight 82, fieldnotes 63, expose 62, typetour 46, caltrain 43, messages 34, spark 26, shared-elements 23, video-player 19, carousel 13, sparkline 10, photo-editor 10, recorder 7, llp 1, exact-live 1. The rest are fixtures, conformance, corpus, galleries, labs, benches and two terminal entries, whose colours are what they test. Repaint the apps to roles and one accent (`apps/shelf` is the recipe); a media overlay is `color-scheme="dark"` with roles inside.
- **The test grammar has no colour scheme** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.002 D1.4): a pattern's test can't check dark mode, so `one-screen`'s test file carries the agent drive (`"prefer prefers-color-scheme dark"`). A launch line (`prefer prefers-color-scheme dark`, as the driver's op) would let a test screenshot or expect both schemes.
- **A `link` to a route with no `navigate` handler is refused on the native hosts** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.000 D4.3): on the web a link to a declared route and the browser's Forward are now the runner's own `go` (`Runner::host_go`, both web targets), as Back is its own `back`. iOS, macOS, Linux and the terminal still refuse a link or an opened URL there ("navigate refused: no navigation root handler"), so every recipe keeps `navigate=follow`. Calling `host_go` from each host's `follow` would let a `link` row need nothing else.
- **A grouped-row button with `commandfor=… command="show-modal"` can't be tapped on iOS by the agent** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.002): `tap delete-item` answered "confirmation #54 is unavailable, transitioning, or its source is no longer active"; the same row with `press=askRemove` and `showModal("remove")` works. Not tried with a real touch.
- **Round 4's workout-log: a blank Finish row on iOS, not reproduced** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.002 D2): the grader saw a blank, untappable Finish row after editing a set and adding a custom exercise; neither Maestro nor the agent reproduced it on `267d8b6a9`, with or without D2's fix (round 1's blank add-set row no longer reproduces). Replay the grader's exact edit-set and custom-exercise steps (`~/bakeoff/rounds/r4/grades/workout-log*`). `PresenterIOS.swift` is at 1,497 lines: split it before the next change there.
- **A grouped list's footer stays clipped after its text changes on iOS** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.002, start-here's Groceries): after the first add the footer's "1 item left" showed half cut off; maybe LLP 1116.002 D2's invalidation (the iOS lane).
- **`-exact-fit` is not fitted inside a content region** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.001 D1.4): the kernel's region layout (`kernel/src/region`) measures text by its own path, which `layout/fit.rs` does not reach, so a fitted text in a region keeps its size. Fit there when a consumer puts a readout in one.
- **VoiceOver on a grouped row's swipe actions is unverified** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.001 D1): the row's actions are its list cell's (`trailingSwipeActionsConfigurationProvider`), and its controls are hidden while the list carries it. Check with VoiceOver on a device that UIKit offers them as the cell's custom actions, as a table's one-row projection relied on.
- **A new grouped row rendered blank and untappable on iOS until the screen was left** (2026-10-10, bake-off round 1, workout-log): adding a third exercise's add-set row showed an empty, untappable row until navigating away and back (grader screenshots `~/bakeoff/rounds/r1/grades/workout-log/A-06*.png`, `A-08`, `A-09`; app `~/bakeoff/rounds/r1/apps/workout-log-exact2`). The same app showed an unprompted alphabetic keyboard and trash icons not drawn red. Reproduce from the app's contract.
- **The web agent's screenshot painted a stale frame** (2026-10-10, bake-off round 1, focus-timer): the header missing and text shifted up while DOM geometry and scroll were right; remounting, a real wait and a resize did not reliably fix it; the builder took one screenshot per fresh session (~5 min). App `~/bakeoff/rounds/r1/apps/focus-timer-exact2`.
- **CSS values builders reach for and Contract refuses** (2026-10-10, bake-off round 1): `clamp()` in `font-size`, a quoted unitless `line-height`, `min()` inside grid tracks (focus-timer, recipe-finder).
- **Snapback 4 client bugs from the bake-off** (2026-10-10, LLP 1116 §9; the bake-off moved to a plain REST server, so these are their own track): one partition per native device against the answer model (two resources on one partition refreshed in one action → "native call outside an answer", "pending on nothing"); a cold first sync of 176–650 ms over the 100 ms source budget (fresh launches sit on "Connecting…" until the poll); on iOS every successful write hung until relaunch while the 4 s refresh stopped (team-kanban); on the web reads stayed stale after the app's own writes (event-rsvp); the README's "Synced" and "Refused: CODE" lines were copied as UI text in 4 of 5 apps. Reproductions: `~/bakeoff/rounds/r1/analysis/batch-c.md` (9 bugs), apps `~/bakeoff/rounds/r1/apps/{team-kanban,chat-rooms,event-rsvp,household-list,polls}-exact2`.
- **A prerendered web page shows a persisted state's declared value until its module boots** (2026-10-10, LLP 1116 D5). The JS target reads `localStorage` as a persisted slot is made, so a page the build renders no document for (the dev loop, an `exact new` app) is right on its first paint; one rendered at build (`--render rust|js`) paints the declared value, then the module adopts the page and shows the kept one (a structural difference abandons adoption and builds afresh, journaled), and an `activate="interaction"` page waits for a tap. Either hide the root before first paint when an `exact.state.` key exists (the capture script) and show it at boot, or render no document for an app that persists a state. Neither is done: no app that persists renders one yet.
- **The checks' workspace `cargo test` skips exact-linux** (2026-10-10, the agent-tap review): `host/linux` is a workspace member but not in `default-members`, so `cargo test --lib --bins --tests` in the gate never runs its lib or pinned tests; a stale pinned test (`host/linux/tests/pinned/host.rs`) kept passing the gate while it failed under `cargo test -p exact-linux`. Proposal for Charlie: add exact-linux to the gate as host/web and host/apple were (or to `default-members`). Cost: its build and tests add minutes to every check run (measure before deciding).
- **`build.mjs --ios` takes `--sim`, not `--phone`, for a simulator, and picked a booted one by itself** (2026-10-09, the addressed-tap work): `--phone <udid>` is read only for `--device`, so a simulator build installed onto another drive's booted simulator (bench-exact-2). `simulator()` (`host/apple/devices.mjs`) now refuses to choose when several iPhones are booted; still open: make `--phone` on a simulator build mean `--sim` (or refuse it), and `agent ios` takes no `--phone` for a simulator either (EXACT_SIM).
- **What the Shelf recipe could not say natively** (2026-10-10, `apps/shelf`, LLP 1115). Each is drawn or worked around there and named in a comment: a stepper (two bordered buttons where iOS has `UIStepper`); context menus on `list appearance="auto"` rows (so the library is a plain `list`, whose rows are its platform's cells since LLP 1115.000 D16, and not a grouped one; a grouped row's swipe actions are UIKit's since LLP 1116.001 D1).
- **A handed-off module turn's outcome is not reserved** (2026-10-09, LLP 1041 §8.4 amendment, Astra's review). `next_job` charges a handed-off turn nothing more when it starts, and `complete` adds what its outcome retains without checking the ordered lane's 512 MiB, so several large outcomes can pass it. Bounded today only by the sixteen (a turn is an effect until drained). Reserve its ceiling at hand-off or backpressure the owner.
- **The re-ask herd** (2026-10-09, LLP 1041 §8.4 Q6). Each delivery re-asks every waiting answer: twenty rows awaiting one shared load take 62 pumps, 300 take 430 (`js/tests/it/admission.rs`). Ask again only the waiters whose promise chain moved (the prelude can tell after a drain), with tests for completed calls, unclaimed fetches, storage-head changes and the final "pending on nothing" check.
- **Storage reads at the sixteen** (2026-10-09, LLP 1041 §8.4 Q2). A TypeScript module's storage step is an opaque effect, so many answers each awaiting storage before a shared load (the Bluesky clone's `hiddenFirst`) still meet the sixteen. Classifying needs the seam to mark an identified read-only operation or batch.
- **`new Date()` works in a data module under bun tests but fails the iOS bake** (2026-10-08, the clone's group 7: muted words' expiry): the bun test environment does not match the data-module runtime. The shared prelude refuses ambient time on purpose (`js/src/prelude.js`), while a `Date` built from an explicit value works, so it is the bun tests that should refuse `new Date()` with no argument, as the bake does. The clone passes the wall time in (`noteWall`).
- **Two message rows drew on top of each other while a sheet was up over the conversation after the keyboard went down** (2026-10-08, the clone's conversation view): cleared when the sheet closed.
- **No way to read the host's root font size** (2026-10-08, the clone's font-size setting): `setRootFontSize("87.5%")`/`("125%")` now scale Dynamic Type instead of replacing it (LLP 1121 D6), so Smaller/Larger need no read; the size itself still can't be read back, if an app needs it.
- **Cross-browser conformance drives Chrome once per engine; a recorded Chrome drive could serve both** (2026-10-09). `conform.mjs --browser firefox|webkit` opens Chrome and the other engine for every target and drives them step by step together, so each async-lane commit drives Chrome three times per target: in the main run (wasm against JS) and in each cross-browser run. The main run's Chrome cannot stand in as it is: it pins only the media clock, the cross-browser runs also pin `line-height: 1.2` (`parityScript`), and it is compared against the wasm page, not recorded. The change: drive the JS page in Chrome once with the cross-browser parity, record each step's state, tree and layout, and have Firefox and WebKit each drive alone against that record. It takes Chrome out of both cross-browser runs, about half their work (Firefox 282 s and WebKit 176 s pooled). First read `drive()` for any step that depends on what Chrome shows mid-drive (an aim, a wait on its state). A smaller step: run Firefox and WebKit in one invocation sharing one Chrome per target, a third of the cross-browser drives.
- **The drift line can misname packages when two sources share a name and version** (2026-10-08, LLP 1086.000.000 §7; Astra's round-4 code review, left open by Charlie's ruling at the fix-loop cap). `lockDrift` (`game/app/shells.mjs`) matches a lock's dependency strings to packages not quite as Cargo does: an unqualified edge to a name and version both a path and a registry package hold follows both (Cargo prefers the path package), and a git-qualified edge may carry no commit fragment where the package's source does (Grok read Cargo the other way). Only the names in "exact2 moved … (added: …; updated: …)" are affected; whether the line appears, what `lock` writes and every build are not. Match Cargo's `encode.rs` lookup and add both regressions.
- **An agent's activation tap leaves a snapping pager between pages** (2026-10-08, the Bluesky clone's Home with three pinned feeds). `agent ios` `tap` scrolls the nearest horizontal scroller by the minimum that brings the target into view and stops there, ignoring `scroll-snap-type: x mandatory`, so Home rested 14 pt short of its page (`pagerX` 388 of 402), where no finger could leave it, and a screenshot read as a layout bug.
- **Scrolling a list to a row needs the list's id as a literal** (2026-10-08, the clone's group 3): `type-scroll-into-view` takes no computed id, so N pinned Home pages are named `p0`…`p11` and the logo's scroll-to-top is a chain of literal calls; a 13th page has none.
- **An indicator measured from a row's tabs goes stale when the tabs change and the row's box does not** (2026-10-08, the clone's Home tab row): `resize` follows the row's own box, as `ResizeObserver` does, so new labels of the same total width give no event; an app measuring its children needs to re-measure on the change itself (the clone measures again when its tabs change). A pitfalls line, if anything.
- **`agent ios` cannot tap a hidden tab button** (2026-10-08, the clone): `tap tab-me` answers "native segment is unavailable"; a drive must use the visible bottom-bar buttons (compare the Native Fixture segment entry below).
- **`--touch platform` occasionally times out** (2026-10-08, the clone): one tap answered "no touch reached the app's window within 1 s"; the retry worked.
- **An `agent ios` relaunch can fail on a stale console FIFO** (2026-10-08, the clone's group 4; again 2026-10-20): "Unable to establish FIFO … launch_console … File exists" (errno 17); the retry worked. On 2026-10-20 (Xcode 27.1, an iPhone 18 Pro simulator, iOS 27.0) it failed 2 of about 280 launches of the grouped-switch probe at `open`, as "the simulator launch exited before opening its agent socket". The FIFO (`data/tmp/launch_console-<pid>-1` under the device) is named by the launching process's pid, so one an earlier killed drive left may be what collides; not traced. The agent could launch again once when simctl says so.
- **One SF symbol drew at two sizes across renders of one sheet** (2026-10-08, the clone's interaction-settings sheet): the same `symbol:sf/circle` with the same `width`/`height` measured differently in two captures.
- **A list's data update is O(rows) in the size index** (2026-10-03, Expose's heavy list on Android). `SizeIndex::splice_keys` copies every row, rebuilds the `SumTree` and shifts every key's position, and `rekey_shared` walks the whole span between the shared prefix and suffix: a live tick that inserts one row at the top and bumps one reaction costs ~0.3 ms on an M-series Mac and ~8.5 ms on an Android phone's mid cores at rest, about 30 ms/s of a 10,000-row list's idle CPU (Views' adapter inserts one row). An order-statistics tree for positions and a splice per changed run would make it O(changes · log rows).
- **Pictures a GPU painter holds need no CPU copy** (2026-10-03, Expose on Android). A cached decoded picture is CPU pixels (`image/bitmap.rs`) and, once a GPU painter shows it, a texture too. Android's hardware bitmaps keep only the texture, so `VmRSS` (the list benchmark's peak RSS) leaves them out: Views' 164 MB of photos are graphics memory, Expose's 113 MB cache is native heap. The Canvas host's pictures already decode into `AHardwareBuffer`s its reader draws (`image::hardware_pictures`); a GPU painter could take the same buffers as textures (an `EGLImage`, a Vulkan import) instead of uploading CPU pixels.
- **Apple/Linux executor: a root swapped for a symlink between its check and its first open is pinned** (2026-10-08, review of the lazy app roots). `body::open` refuses a root that is a symbolic link, then `create_dir_all` and `AppDirectories::new` follow one put there in between (the same window existed when the roots opened at boot; storage's own `Session::configure` follows too). Closing it needs a no-follow root open (`O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW`) in ibex2's `AppDirectories`, used by both. The first open is also outside the request's deadline and abort (three `create_dir_all` calls in the app's container).
- **Apple: images linked by use** (2026-10-08, LLP 1047.001; measured, not built). Only an `image` node loads a bitmap (its `imageSource`), so the use rule is one line (a plan with an image node uses `images`; the web links nothing for it). But the move is all or nothing: Swift in the core calls the `exact_raster_*` exports (`RasterLoader`), and the core holds image results itself (`NativeRasterLease`/`RasterImage` on every node; `RasterGeometry`'s drawing in the modal, flights and box layer; `AnimatedRasters` in the session, video, agent and box layers). Worth it for image-free apps and embeds only: ~54 KB of `exact_raster` and ~45 KB of Swift by symbol gaps, perhaps 50 KB real (speed). Split `RasterGeometry` and a lease protocol into the core, move the rest to an `ExactImages` module, and emit `raster_exports!` by use.
- **Apple: list swipe actions in every app** (2026-10-08): `SwipeActionsHost` (~34 KB by symbol gaps) is in hello, which has no list. Find its use rule (a list row's swipe actions) and move it out as `ExactDrag` was.
- **Apple: the host's Rust drag and height sync run on every commit** (2026-10-08): `reconcile_height_handles`, `sync_height_owner`, `sync_height_transitions` and the transform drags are called from the commit path whether or not the plan has a drag; behind a const link (Drag) they would leave image-free, drag-free apps.
- **Standard-library sorts copied per call site** (2026-10-08, the duplicate audit): `quicksort`/`driftsort` are instantiated ~38 times in hello (~80 KB by symbol gaps), one per `sort_by`/`sort_by_key` closure and element type. A few shared non-generic helpers (sort by a key of one type) would fold most of them.
- **Web writes lost when a tab closes mid-write, redesign** (2026-10-08; **Charlie, 2026-10-08: leave it documented for now, no redesign; the bench grader stays as is** — the pitfall covers it). A storage.fs write still in its IndexedDB transaction when the tab closes is lost (4/4 on an immediate close; bench t5 on the Android round). The first fix (da6848b8f..29efdaad6, a per-tab localStorage journal replayed at next launch) was reverted after a fresh blind review (Astra: revert; Grok: keep with fixes): a replay can write a closed tab's older value over a newer one another tab committed, a page restored from the back-forward cache was treated as gone for good, a non-permanent replay error dropped every dead tab's journal, and a failed localStorage flush could let a committed write age out of the applied set and replay. A redesign needs: a replay only if the target is still the version the write was admitted against (a durable generation per path), pageshow clearing the gone state, per-entry failure handling, and stale-snapshot removal on a failed flush; its own LLP 1097 amendment and review before landing.
- **Opening a select's menu raised the software keyboard on an EAS iOS 26.5 Simulator** (2026-10-08, seen while verifying app farm 008): a real touch (agent-device) on a `select` with options opened its menu and the system keyboard, the first time with its "slide to type" card, on main `57eeaa18` and with the fix alike. `KeyboardFocusIOSTests.testNativeSelectMenuPreservesShortcutFocusAndAllowsTextEntry` passed on that commit (with the fix, which leaves focus alone), so its zero-height `inputView` is not what the launched app showed. Check on a local Simulator with no hardware keyboard connected, then on a phone.
- **A drive's page can lose a lazy chunk when another build replaces the output mid-drive** (2026-10-08, app farm round 1, territory-mapper-0429: the dev loop's watcher and a `test web` rebuild broke a hashed import). Concurrent builds no longer mix (`replaceBuild`, `host/web/serve.mjs`), but the agent's web carrier serves `target/web-dist` live, so a page booted from one build asks for `[name]-[hash].js` that a newer build with other inputs no longer has. Serve a drive from the build it booted (a copy, or the replaced tree kept until the drive ends).
- **LLP 1046.011 D4 and D3, ruled and unbuilt** (2026-10-07; D2 is built): `exact.mjs look`, an offscreen turntable of a `.glb` or `.model` through the engine's renderer as PNG, then the texture cap with the bake's per-model bill. LLP 1046.012 R5 asks that `look` also take a game-provided gallery (a roster of the game's own entities): comparing styles across Skirmish's whole roster is what made the art calls easy. `WorldSurface::settle` is the fixture loop it can use.
- **Native Fixture's header segment tap is unavailable under `--chrome platform`** (2026-10-07, LLP 1104 S4s drive): `tap segment-missed` answers `native segment #15 is unavailable`. `AgentIOS.tap` asks the content `SegmentHost` before `NavigationHost.activateChrome`; the header's segmented title view lives on its route controller instead. The authored-chrome drive and the title-view's UIKit value-change XCTest pass. Route agent activation to the header projection; this was found during verification and has not been attributed to step 4.

- **The JS edit loop exceeds its 100 ms budget on main and the merged button program** (2026-10-07, `scripts/metrics.mjs`, Mac Studio): unchanged main ca2ae9252 measured 354 ms p50 / 470 ms p95; the merged program measured 316 ms p50 / 344 ms p95 and, in a second run, 411 ms p50 / 543 ms p95. All runs took place during verification load; measure on a quiet machine and separate watcher delay, rebuild and browser reload before attributing a regression.

- **The full native-buttons fixture shrinks buttons on the web** (2026-10-07, LLP 1104 merged-tip drive): `scripts/fixtures/native-buttons.contract` at 420×900 puts its native buttons in a fixed-height scrolling flex column; their `overflow: hidden` permits `flex-shrink: 1` to collapse direct children below the title. The same heights reproduce with the pre-merge 934cfa08b stylesheet; unchanged origin/main ca2ae9252 also collapses a scrolling column of 60 native filled buttons to 14px each. The smaller conformance fixture does not cover this scroll case. Hold the intended intrinsic floor without changing CSS sizing semantics, or give the fixture nonshrinking rows.

- **iOS smoke intermittently loses the software keyboard between fixture launches** (2026-10-07, LLP 1104 merge verification; reproduced on `origin/main` at `69004a1b5`). On the lane's iOS simulator, the insets fixture gets keyboard height 0 and five failed assertions on main, while its keyboard-bar fixture gets height 328 and passes. The merged program also alternates between that failure and a missing keyboard in the keyboard-bar fixture; a cold simulator restart did not cure it, and `com.apple.keyboard.preferences AutomaticMinimizationEnabled` is unset. Both fixtures use `appearance="none"`. Investigate responder activation across the driver's separate app launches; the cause is not established.
- **canvas-gallery's 2D canvases are blank on the iOS simulator's screen** (2026-10-07, iPhone 17 Pro, iOS 27.0): launched by hand, development or production, `simctl io screenshot` shows the header and no canvas, 4 s and 14 s after launch. Yet `state` reports each canvas drawn (`draws: 1`, no error) and `agent.mjs … screenshot` shows every shape. A build of `28f29d821` (before LLP 1047.001's Surfaces move) is just as blank. Production Caltrain's GPU sky shows on the same simulator. Suspects: the replay's `layer.contents` on the on-screen path, against the agent's capture after `waitForReplays`.
- **Replay the garden bakeoff's exact2 lane through the look oracle** (LLP 1127.000, 2026-10-18). The in-tree garden's proof now looks with no tell on Linux, the web and macOS, but it has neither the brief's `meadow` model (the `#527b65` meadow) nor rain and snow, so RFC §4's acceptance case (the judged G4 and G9 frames failing on `particles-uncovered`, the meadow rendering green) needs the bakeoff's own lane (`game/bakeoff/garden/run.mjs`) run with the oracle. A `Material::hex("#527b65")` plane does read green in a look frame (`(124, 171, 155)` under the default sun and sky).
- **An HDR look frame with particles replaces the live frame's HDR attachments twice** (LLP 1127.000, 2026-10-18). Its count reads the scene's depth, so it needs that depth stored and sampleable; when the live frame's is transient (no depth prepass, soft particles, SSAO or final depth), `prepare_targets` makes retained ones for the look and the next live frame makes transient ones again: three textures each way at four samples, measured, with bloom's and SSAO's objects rebuilt. Neither keeps state between frames, so no picture changes; it is cost. A direct look frame already keeps attachments of its own (`look_direct`), released at the next live frame; the HDR path could do the same.
- **A look frame bypasses the static shadow cache** (LLP 1127.000, 2026-10-18). It draws every sun caster itself so that it leaves the cache as the live frames left it, so a defect only in the cache (a caster the cache dropped or kept stale) shows in live frames and escapes the oracle's `no-shadow-casters`. A look frame could read the cache's own maps as well, or count the live frame's texels.
- **`visible`'s reason names only the first non-mesh drawable that drew** (LLP 1127.000, 2026-10-18). `look.rs` `combine` answers `drawn` naming one drawable (`its sprite: …`) when a sprite and an emitter both drew; the reason could list every drawable's word.
- **No control has an emitter rescuing another drawable's rejection** (LLP 1127.000, 2026-10-18). `src/look_frame_tests.rs` covers a sprite that draws beside an empty emitter and beside a model out of view; an emitter whose particles draw beside a rejected mesh or sprite (`drawn`, naming the emitter) has no test.
- **Garden bakeoff follow-ups** (2026-10-06, historical run at `7716302242`; recheck on current main). exact2 placed third in the garden bakeoff (2026-10-06, [LLP 1046.010](llp/1046.010-garden-bakeoff.plan.md) §8): 81 fidelity at 3× Godot's cost. Engine frictions from the builder's diary: restore refuses components setup never inserted (only a fresh process catches it); the world hash includes the host clock; a Contract `every(50)` trips `TimerFireLimit` on an hour seek. Not yet attributed (engine or game?): a meadow rendered grey and rain particles alive but invisible. Discoverability: the builder never found `exactTime().epochAtZero`, so its game has no wall-clock away growth.

- **LLP 1108.001, slices 2 and 3 (`exactSaveTo`; `Blob`, object URLs, `blob:` sources): NOT READY after four design rounds** (2026-10-08). All four rounds of both families agree the write gate resolves the parent's "R2 needs admission"; each round found new concurrency and lifetime problems one layer deeper (r4: a stalled rename under the executor mutex, the wasm realm's Blob read as untracked work, UUID namespaces across engine reloads, a store mutex against GC-driven drops, gzip on Linux). r5 folds round 4 unreviewed and needs a fifth review, or a ruling that narrows scope (for example slice 2 alone first), before code lands. A slice-2 draft predating r3 is on the local branch `lane/media-slices-2-3`, worktree `~/projects/exact2-wt-media23`. When slice 2 lands and slice 1 (the Snapback client) has landed, switch the client's download to `exactSaveTo` with §1.6's staging split.
- **A 2 MB `ArrayBuffer` body still runs over a source's 100 ms step** (2026-10-08, the Bluesky clone): `fetch` base64-encodes a byte body in the prelude's JS (`toBase64`), in the same step as the call (a 2,000,000-byte `uploadBlob` took 106.8 ms on the iOS simulator). An upload from a file now goes by `exactBodyFrom` (LLP 1108 D6 R2), which moves no bytes through JS; a native encode would lift the byte body too.
- **macOS app smoke loses the deck iframe's pixels** (2026-10-07, reproduced on unchanged main `5e8528477`, macOS 26.6.2). After building Caltrain, `bun scripts/smoke.mjs macos --app-only` fails only with `the iframe screenshot has 0 guest-blue pixels in 600×300`; guest loading, messaging, outline and input checks pass, as do the three Contract tests and all layout comparisons. The same failure occurs with the saturation amendment. Investigate the software screenshot's WKWebView capture; the cause is not established. Web and iOS app smokes pass on this machine.

- **Driving apps on Android, stage 2** (2026-10-07, [LLP 1107](llp/1107-driving-an-app-on-android.rfc.md) §4). `agent.mjs android` drives an app's Linux host built for `aarch64-linux-android`, headless under adb (Caltrain's tests pass on the emulator). A TypeScript data module runs there with a locally built Hermes bundle (`bun scripts/hermes-android.mjs build`; LLP 1107 §4.1). Owed: that bundle in an Ibex release (Ibex branch `android-hermes-bundle`, local, for Charlie to send), `Intl` on Android (the bundle has Unicode through ICU but no Intl), a `linux/` crate in what `exact new` writes, and the bench's Android cells. (The carrier for the Canvas reader landed on 2026-10-11: `agent.mjs android --reader`, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md).)
- **Android is admitted: what that owes** (2026-10-07, `rules/DEFERRED.md` §Surfaces, Charlie: "we are working on android now -- it's becoming real"). The Android host work keeps landing from `android/explore` through its owner's lane (8 commits ahead of main on 2026-10-07; the Canvas host, LLP 1076 §3.3, and the GPU painter are mostly merged already). The Minikin arm, `android/text`, is 6 commits ahead. LLP 1085.000's stage 3 ran on the Pixels on 2026-10-07 (§7: no regression beyond noise; main is the Android arm, so `text/parley-android` is not needed). The Nothing Phone (4a) Pro was not connected and is still to be run. Hosting serves `/.well-known/assetlinks.json` beside Apple's association file (LLP 1082). Android joins the sweep: which of the six checks or the async lane builds `exact-linux` for `aarch64-linux-android`, and which smoke drives it, is to be decided. `exact-linux` is not a default member, so today nothing on the gate builds it at all (fb03fd196 fixed a Linux build that had been broken on main since 1eea26bdc).
- **Pull to refresh beneath the Bluesky clone’s overlaid header** (2026-10-07, LLP 1010 §6.9). Test the clone on iOS with its header over the list padding. `RefreshInsetIOSTests` passes on the simulator; the clone still needs a manual check.
- **A select's box on iOS, after #253** (2026-10-07, Grok's review of #253). `text-align` now moves a select's closed value, but: an unstyled select stays centred where CSS's initial `start` (and the iOS host's own `textAlignmentCode`) would start it at the inline-start edge; in a box wider than its widest option the `UIButton` keeps its natural width, so the rest of the authored box does not open the menu (the web's whole border box is the control; span the content box and align the label inside it); the popup indicator moves with the label where a browser keeps the arrow at the inline end; macOS (`NSPopUpButton`), Linux and tvOS still ignore `text-align` on a select.
- **Press states the web's way** (2026-10-07, from the Brooks port: its buttons' face moves 4 pt down-right in 70 ms on press, which its fork bolted onto `press-scale` as a translation, a duration and an easing). The web's answer is `:active` styles with a `transition`; Contract has no state selectors, only `press-scale` and `hover-effect`. A language question for Charlie, not a port; the fork's knobs are not taken.
- **A native module reading the app's current assets** (2026-10-07, Brooks port P2): `ExactModuleContext` has data, cache, temp, clock and change, but no resolver for the active asset generation, so a module's leaf pins embedded hashes and misses an asset-only update. A small API (bounded bytes or a file, with lifetime and cancellation); write it when a second app needs it or an app updates assets without a binary.
- **LLP 1081 stage 2: a symbol takes `color`** (2026-10-06, [LLP 1081](llp/1081-names-exact-invents.rfc.md) §4 stage 2, Q4; stage 1 built). A `symbol:` image's monochrome and hierarchical colour becomes its computed `color` on every host; `-exact-tint-color` stays for raster images only. Pixels change wherever an untinted symbol sits under a coloured ancestor, so it lands on its own with reviewed screenshots. §4 names every reader (`NodeViewIOS.swift`, `Affordances.swift`, `SegmentsIOS.swift`, `NavigationBarIOS.swift`, `NodeSymbolMac.swift`, both web symbol sheets); LLP 1095 §12 notes that `color`'s `CanvasText` default makes an unstyled symbol the label colour again.
- **Keyboard, the rest of #140** (2026-10-07, after `keyup`, `KeyboardEvent.code` and `.repeat` landed). Two parts wait on the owner's word: a capture-phase handler and its spelling (`keycapture=…`, ancestors outermost first, `stopPropagation()` keeping the key from the target), and a reserved held-modifier fact (`exactKeyboard`, reset on window blur, beside `keyup`; the web's way, keyup plus `exactPage().hasFocus`, is documented). A ⌘ chord during a composition now commits it and reaches the `key` handlers on macOS and iOS; not yet pressed with a real Korean or Japanese input source. Composing keys still differ by host: the web host hands every key Chrome sends while composing to `key` handlers (Contract has no `isComposing`, so a composer's Enter there can send mid-syllable), the native hosts keep them for the input method. Composed text is the same on every host since 2026-10-12 (LLP 1115.000 §5, ruling 6; `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/FieldComposition.swift`): `input` at each change with the marked text in the value, where AppKit had told it only once committed. What is left of that: (a) nobody has typed with a real Japanese, Chinese or Korean input method on macOS or iOS since (the tests drive `setMarkedText` and `insertText`); (b) an app's value never ends a composition, by Charlie's ruling of 2026-10-12 (LLP 1045 D5 stands): a different value written while text is marked is held and applied when the composition ends, since an action that rewrites what it hears would otherwise end one at its first keystroke and Contract gives it no way to know. macOS and iOS hold (`pendingValue`, `FieldComposition.swift`, each platform's `writeValue`), and so does the web's wasm target (`writeValue` in `host/web/glue.js`, `heldValues` until `compositionend`). Android and the web's JS target hold too since 2026-10-12: Android through the presenter (`Native.fieldComposing`, `presenter/hosted.rs`; not seen on a phone, which the entry on Android's text fields has), the JS target in `rt.js`'s `writeValue`, with the glue's rule and timing. Chrome does end a composition whose field's value is assigned: the marked text is replaced and the next keystroke begins another (`host/web/tests/compose-hold.test.mjs`, real compositions through CDP's `Input.imeSetComposition`, on both targets); (c) whether Chrome and UIKit fire an `input` at a commit that changes no text was not measured: macOS and iOS tell the app nothing then. Real keys (`orca computer`, events posted to the app's pid, macOS 26.6.2) gave Exact and Chrome 154 the same log: ⌘B is keydown Meta, keydown b, keyup b (with ⌘ still held), keyup Meta. A held key's auto-repeat is not yet pressed for real: the CLI cannot hold a key, and auto-repeat downs posted to a pid reached neither app; a keyboard by hand, or a HID-level post with the app frontmost, would show it. iOS reports no `repeat` (UIKit's presses carry none) and the terminal host sends no `keyup`.
- **Parley on Linux, what is left** (2026-10-07, [LLP 1085.000](llp/1085.000-parley-in-place-of-cosmic-text.plan.md) §7). An ltr line that starts in Hebrew keeps two visual-order inversions, as main did, under the first-strong base (LLP 1001 §1): it goes when the text-flow walker breaks an RTL run in an LTR paragraph and `shaping.rs` passes `BaseDirection::Ltr`. A Hebrew-in-ltr "…" pen sits 0.5 px off Chrome's, and emoji advances are 0.04–0.18 px narrower. The macOS and Windows fallback lists are cosmic-text's and unmeasured. Synthetic bold (fontique embolden) is not drawn. Tab stops count from the line start, not the block's start edge. Every vendored Parley patch (1–15) and fontique patch (1–3) is still to be sent upstream, and LLP 1085's questions to Nico Burns. Android: stage 3 ran on two Pixel 10 Pro XLs and a Pixel 10a (2026-10-07, §7); the Nothing Phone (4a) Pro, Expose and Windows are unmeasured. Crypto's `exact boot` slice is 1.6–2.0 ms longer with Parley on every phone (the cold start a user sees does not move); the slice has no sub-sections, so a simpleperf profile of both arms' boots is what would place it.
- **#84's journal lines, not ported** (2026-10-06; its Back and covered-edge fixes landed in 2cae1c92f and 74d782a36). A link that pops the stack, a testId repeated on covered routes, and a resource showing its answer to its previous arguments (LLP 1016 D3) were each to be said once. The testId scan read the wall clock (`performance.now()`, a 3 s window) and re-scanned `[data-testid]` on every projection in it; bound it by projection count or the agent's clock. The previous-answer line meets `rt.js`'s rewritten resource path (streams, build-time answers) at the cap.
- **Two checks red on main, seen in the PR sweep** (2026-10-06, at 856808161). On a Mac with Xcode's Metal toolchain installed (the Canvas 2D GPU module then builds), `smoke.mjs macos`'s canvas readback differs from its reference (1.33% of pixels beyond the band, mean 1.42, 840×240); and `smoke.mjs ios` on an iPhone 18 Pro Max has no reference picture (`canvas-sky.ios.1320x360.png`).
- **`overflow-anchor` and priority candidates** (2026-10-07, #138 X23d). The `overflow-anchor: none` opt-out and the priority candidates (a focused editable) are not built on any native host.
- **Linux scroll anchoring: input inside the frame that carries an anchor correction** (2026-10-19, the fourth review of the Linux anchoring lane, q24/linux-scroll-anchor). With a display attached, a correction waits for its picture's acknowledgement, and wheel, reader, command or feed input in that one frame moves only the pending model (`presenter/collection/anchoring.rs` `anchor_input`, `presenter/scroll_end.rs` `scroll_end_input`). Three edges are left: (1) the input's `scroll` handler runs at once at the old acknowledged offset, then again at the new one. In `scroll_anchor_tests/review_tests.rs` `input_that_only_moves_the_pending_model`, the wheel's order is `s648;s610;e;`, where it should be `s610;e;` (seen by asserting the whole string). (2) An input that exactly cancels the pending correction (+48 against a −48 fold) clears the model and records no `scrollend`. (3) A reader sample that moves only the model takes the host clock for its completion, not the shell's stamp, and an earlier sample's armed completion stays due, so one fling can end twice. (1) was reproduced. (2) and (3) are the reviewers' traces, not run. None of them reaches a scroller with no anchor correction pending: there `anchor_input` returns `None` and input takes main's path. Also not done: an anchor candidate whose transformed quad crosses behind the viewer (a `perspective` parent with `rotate: y 75deg`) is skipped instead of clipped to the visible half, as `placement::clipped_bounds` does.
- **`text-decoration-color` and `text-underline-offset`** (2026-10-06, the concept of #75 by Jon Samp, whose branch main outran; its third row, `cursor`, landed in c6136f39d and f7e010921). Two CSS longhands as schema rows: `text-decoration-color` not inherited, `currentcolor` by default; `text-underline-offset` inherited, `auto` by default. The `text-decoration` shorthand takes a colour, which `shorthands.rs` refuses today because native painters draw only solid `currentcolor`. Draw them natively rather than admitting web-only rows: Apple's `underlineColor`/`strikethroughColor` (an offset needs a drawn underline, or a named refusal; `Text.swift` is near the cap), Linux's `paint/text_decoration.rs`, the terminal's SGR 58. The line takes the decorating box's colour, as CSS does.
- **iPhone Duo: navigation bar items under the status capsule** (2026-10-06, from #90 by Jon Samp; set aside 2026-10-15 as UIKit's behavior, and measured again under Xcode 27.1, iOS 27.1, on 2026-10-19). On the cover panel, a navigation controller's image bar items go to the vertical bar on the trailing edge. With no scroll view above the controller, the vertical bar starts below the capsule (y 170); with a `UIScrollView` above it, in every configuration tried, it starts at y 24, under the capsule, in a plain UIKit app as in Exact, where the viewport is that scroll view: native-fixture's trailing item group sits partly under the capsule. The capsule is an occlusion `UIView.ReservedRegion`, not a safe-area inset, and `additionalSafeAreaInsets.top = 146` moved the horizontal bar and left the vertical one where it was, and no public API opts a scroll view's content out. Moving the controllers out of the viewport, as #90 did, breaks LLP 1083.000's paint order. Look again on a later release.
- **Server-render CPU per page** (2026-10-06, the concept of #81, which main outran; the web-framework-bench's weakest result, 3–21× the best competitor). Re-port on main: skip pre-compressing `dist/.gen` at bind and the number-text fast path (both cherry-pick clean from #81); one site index per plan shared across requests (`runner.rs` is at the cap); a decoded constant pool for `vm.rs`, without #81's load-and-field fusion, which changes the machine `Vm.lean` proves; re-profile the per-template style memo after StyleProps' shrink (71792e51d). Measure on a quiet machine and run the theme-flip test #81 skipped. Also seen: the render server serves `dist/.gen` (intermediates and the plan's source map) to anyone; `static_file` refuses no hidden path, and `/.exact/` must keep working.
- **web-js: 10,000-row list updates** (2026-10-06, the concept of #82, LLP 1071.000, goal approved 2026-09-30). Every commit scans the page for `[navigationBack]`, about 40% of an update's script at 10k rows; the emitter can say a plan has no navigation roots. Then: a clone cache in `h()` keyed by the whole site, text bindings writing `Text.data`, one write per `css()`, a single-element move, and the data seam's per-row conversion (re-check #82's value-to-type memo against streamed answers, `ts-stream.js`, which mutate). `rt.js` (1,499 lines) and `emit.rs` (1,495) are at the cap. #82's grid numbers: update every 10th of 10k rows 537–1,039 ms against Svelte's 442.
- **Wake-driven activation has no end-to-end test** (2026-10-06, reviews of #193). Its tests fire the wake by hand: nothing checks that a loader finishing alone wakes Linux's display loop or Apple's session and presents the activated frame. And `native::when_loaded` treats a missing digest or a poisoned lock as loaded (the retry then asks `preload` again).
- **A camera module** (2026-10-02, Charlie lifted "No camera anything", `rules/DEFERRED.md` §Components). An app native module in LLP 1067.000's shape, as `apps/recorder` is: `<camera-preview>` and `start`/`stop`/`capture`/`flip` on one object per session, `capture` writing to `app:/camera/`, the `device.camera` grant, a deterministic agent substitute (and a preview `snapshot` drawn from the last frame, since a capture layer is absent from `drawHierarchy`). Web, iOS and macOS first. Needs its RFC with an implementer and a date; open question: whether a camera can be shared across apps while an app has one module.
- **iOS: a pressable glass button misses real touches over a transform drag's handle** (2026-10-06, the Bluesky clone's lightbox, b08 and b09). Close and menu buttons with `backgroundMaterial="glass"`, siblings painted over a full-page `transformDragFor` handle (`press=tap`, `dblclick`), took no HID tap on the iOS 27 simulator (`axe touch`): the tap toggled the handle's `press` instead. The agent's taps reached them, and with the material removed the same HID tap pressed the button. Interactive glass returns its effect's `contentView` from `hitTest` (`NodeViewIOS.swift`, "Interactive glass … answers a finger itself") and relies on the responder chain reaching the node; a recognizer on the effect or the sibling handle probably takes the touch first. Reproduce with a glass button over a handle in a fixture and an XCTest real touch, then fix.
- **A timer overdue at a gesture's event starts its transitions at the event's time** (2026-10-06, Grok's r2 review of `bsky/release-clock`). `advance_for_input` fires the timers due by a release or geometry at their own runner times, but the hold update (or geometry's `engine.advance`) has already moved the motion engine to the input's time, so a transition a timer starts begins there, not at its due time (`Engine::observe` starts at `self.now`). Only a timer the host's own wake missed is overdue at an input, so the shift is normally under a frame; committing the timers' receipts before the hold or geometry seeks the engine would remove it.
- **Apple resize handlers run at the runner's last advance** (2026-10-06, the review of `bsky/release-clock`, rounds 1–2). `resize_rounds` (`host/apple/src/resize.rs`) dispatches with `runner.dispatch`, so a handler's `performanceNow()` and an `after` it arms are the runner's last advance, which a motion tick or a height hold leaves behind the host's clock; and a window resize reaches the runtime without its time (`Session.swift` `runtime.resize`, `host.rs` `resize`), so even the host's clock is old after an idle spell. Advancing inside the rounds is not enough: the timers' receipts must be laid out before the round gathers its notifications and moves its depth threshold (Astra r2). Carry the observation's time through the resize bridge and advance before the viewport settles.
- **Linux retained press/swipe and the terminal's keys still run at the runner's last advance** (2026-10-06, the review of `bsky/release-clock`, which moved the Apple and web gesture releases and geometry to the input's time). Linux `dispatch_retained` (`host/linux/src/host.rs`) calls `dispatch_bound` and sets `now_ms` after; the terminal dispatches a key after a poll of up to 250 ms that followed its advance (`host/terminal/src/term/unix.rs`). An action's `performanceNow()` and an `after` it arms are then that old; advance first, keeping Linux's rule that a refused binding takes neither the sample nor an ordinary action.
- **Android start as seen: the grey wash was the platform's focus highlight; nth-C's Extra Heavy needs the phone unlocked** (2026-10-06, android/xstart; Pixel 10 Pro XL, `~/bench/android/results/video/xstart-1`, five warm launches each; reader patch `~/bench/android/results/xstart/reader-xstart.patch` against the bench reader's fce7c67). The daily app's window looked grey to the end of a recording, not for the cross-fade's last 25 ms: its view is focusable (keys), a launch by `am start` leaves the window out of touch mode, and Android draws its default focus highlight over the whole focused view (paper 223 for 254). With `defaultFocusHighlightEnabled = false` (the app draws its own focus): content visible 201-231 -> 187-195 ms (Views 183-190, Compose 180-195), settled 283-306 -> 287-301 (281-295, 288-304); xheavy unchanged, 216-224 / 306-310 (Views 210-222 / 302-314). exact2's content frame is still first (daily 78-96 ms against 107-113, xheavy 95-107 against 133-149), but what shows is the system's cross-fade from the launch, the same for every app: an earlier frame inside it shows no sooner by `am start`. Not built: the app's own task-open animation (a theme's window animations) would shorten what `am start` shows and nothing a launcher's own icon animation does. On the Nothing Phone (4a) Pro (nth-C) v8's Extra Heavy fling never ran on exact2 or Views: the phone had restarted and not been unlocked (`dumpsys user`: RUNNING_LOCKED), and MapLibre's `FileSource` reads SharedPreferences from credential-encrypted storage in an AsyncTask, which throws until the first unlock and takes the process down as the first map row is made (Compose makes its map later). Unlocking the phone once clears it; making the apps survive it means redirecting MapLibre's preferences and files to device-protected storage in each app (it resolves the application context itself).
- **Android: Extra Heavy's 24k blank, its causes, and the measurements still owed** (2026-10-06, android/xblank on android/explore 1ac9a73e3; Pixel 10 Pro XL; reader patch `~/bench/android/results/xblank/reader-xblank.patch` against the bench reader's aaf2f06, tools in `results/xblank/tools`). Measured on diagnostic builds over 9c8ccbead, one to five runs each; **the before/after tables on the final builds (xheavy, heavy, crypto; blank, fps, CPU) were not taken: the phones' host dropped off the tailnet (its node key expired)**; `results/xblank/tools/measure.sh` runs them (`*-exactxb0` before, `*-exactxb` after, APKs in `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-xblank/apk`). Found with a frame tag (`tag=1`: the reader draws each frame's number in the header and logs its feed's rows, `tagjoin.py` joins a recording's blank frames to them and to a trace): (1) `blank.py` took the launch animation for the driver's start in some runs, so their "24k" window measured 12k: every "bimodal" low reading (crypto's 0.3% baseline, xheavy's 0.5%) was that; fixed in `~/bench/android` ea94a6e. (2) `cover=1` read "covered" because a row's recorded bounds were its boxes unclipped: the list's spacer for unmounted rows is a row over a million px tall that draws nothing, and a horizontal list's content box 239,160 px wide; fixed here (each box within its clip, 1x1 for a row that recorded nothing). (3) About half of xheavy's blank at 24k (8-11% before, Views 1.3%) was the frontier: one exact-thread turn of refine, paint and pictures ran 13-35 ms (32-38 turns over 12 ms in each 2 s), no stream left meanwhile, and the view ran past its last drawn row. A fast feed's pass now builds what its measured cost per row fits in 8 ms, the rest after the queued steps (the reader re-posts a pending pass): 4.0-5.6%. (4) The other half was WebView cards: the reader made a WebView for each and destroyed it with its row, white until its page painted, where RecyclerView hands Views a recycled one still showing its last page. The reader now keeps three released WebViews (paused) for the next rows: 0.8-1.6% at 24k, 0.1% at 12k, recorded fps 110 -> 117-118 (two runs; 6.5% in the same build with `webpool=0`). Measured and not kept: a pool of threads making a stream's pictures while the stream waits for them (4.8 ms of a long turn is `HardwareBuffer.create` and the copy): no change. Left: the long turns' paint (8.7 ms) and pictures (4.8 ms) still stall the stream (pictures made where they are decoded, or drawn into their slots when they land, would take the second out); crypto and heavy with the 8 ms pass are unmeasured (crypto's rows cost under 1 ms, so its passes should stay whole). Cold start on this phone (warm shader cache, five launches each, `results/video/xbstart-base`): content visible 216-227 ms against Views 311-322 and Compose 313-318, settled 306-313 against 453-472 and 472-476; both follow the system's launch transition, which starts at the app's first frame (73-84 ms, still blank; content is in the next, 88-100 ms, before the fade shows anything), so nothing in exact2's boot (19-22 ms of CPU) moves them here. On the Nothing Phone (4a) Pro (`results/video/vwc`) the starting window stays until content, exact2's content frame (207-232 ms) is level with Views' (227-230), and that is where a shorter boot would show.
- **Android: crypto's 24k blank fixed; what the extra paints cost and what is left** (2026-10-05, android/cblank on android/explore 829cac9d1; Pixel 10 Pro XL, `~/bench/android/results/cblank-*`). Cause (Perfetto, the reader's `cover=1`): a refine (every third scroll step, ~7 ms) mounts the rows a viewport past the view; they are out of view, so the pass is quiet and frames move the last paint until the next one, which came 0-4 frames later (median 17.6 ms after the refine); at 200 dp a frame the nearest of those rows is under two frames of travel away, so the list ended at the last drawn row for 2-4 frames of every five. The exact thread was not short of time (440 ms/s, the same before and after), and the main thread took the paint within 0.6 ms (median). Fix (`host/linux/src/canvas/travel.rs`): the host measures the feed's travel per step and since the last pass, and after a quiet pass paints by the frame its rows can first show (at once at 24k; ≤6k never sooner than before). Three runs each, interleaved, 12k/24k blank: before 0.0/19.8-24.2%, after 0.0/0.0%, Views 0.0/0.0%. Traced fling (two runs each): fps and janky frames unchanged at every speed; CPU and main ms/s unchanged to 12k; at 24k CPU 1,370 -> 1,455 ms/s and main 230 -> 253: the RenderThread now draws the fifth of the view that was blank (its drawing as at 12k), and paints go 25 -> ~47/s, each ingest costing the main thread ~0.9 ms whatever it carries (Views at 24k: 862 CPU, 341 main). xheavy (paints every frame, so unaffected): 24k blank 0.5-11.3% before, 8.3-10.7% after, five runs each; fps and CPU unchanged. Left: (1) the reader's per-stream ingest cost (or rows published without a paint: exact sends a new row's recording and place, the reader adds it to the feed's group) would take back the 24k main time. (2) `cover=1`'s counters read 0 while a fifth of the view was blank: not row reuse but the spacer row's bounds; fixed on android/xblank (the entry above). (3) A shifted window (`lead()` moved toward travel instead of grown) and a pass every step (`pre=1`, reader patch `~/bench/android/results/cblank-reader-pre.patch`) each left 2.8-3.2% at 24k; not kept. (4) The once-measured 0.3% crypto baseline was `blank.py`'s onset taking the launch animation for the driver's start (the entry above).
- **Android: blank areas while flinging fast — measured, the reader's share fixed, the rest owed** (2026-10-05, android/blank on 268878a98; Pixel 10 Pro XL; the reader is `~/bench/lists/exact/android/canvas`, outside the repo, patch `~/bench/android/results/blank/reader-blank.patch`). The measure: `~/bench/android/bin/bench.py blank <app>` records a fling with screenrecord and `bin/blank.py` takes, per driver segment, the share of the list's viewport in full-width one-colour bands ≥56 dp (no row there), time-weighted, the same arithmetic for every stack. Before (two runs each, 12k/24k dp/s): xheavy 2.5–3.4% / 23.1–23.4% (16–18% of 24k frames more than half blank), heavy 0.0% / 2.7–3.8%, crypto 0.3–0.5% / 24.0–24.2%; Views xheavy 1.6% / 1.0%, Compose 0.2% / 0.1%, Views heavy and crypto 0.0% (no frame more than half blank in either). The mechanism on xheavy: rows come into view as they are first recorded (median 2 ms after the stream that defines them), and the reader recorded every row not in view at paint time on its worker, so its group drew nothing there for a frame or more. With such rows recorded on main when within 33 ms of travel of the view, and a worker-made row's group recorded again before the next frame: xheavy 0.6–0.8% / 7.0–9.3% (2–5% of 24k frames more than half blank), heavy 0.0% / 0.0–0.1%; fps, late frames, main-thread ms/s and PSS unchanged, CPU within run spread. Owed: (1) crypto at 24k: landed, android/cblank (the entry above). (2) xheavy's rest at 24k: WebView cards not drawn yet (an empty bordered box, as Views shows) and a few whole-view blank frames. (3) Measured and not kept: a velocity lead (`refine(-1, v)`; the reader passed 0) made every report dearer and blanked more (xheavy 24k 18–19% vs 12–14% without, CPU +4–12%; a lead following the measured speed mounted and retired a row every report at 1k); keeping the velocity for passes between refines (`refine_slice` resets it to 0) tripled first recordings; moving the scrolled list to the front of the report queue (each pass takes two lists and every report queues every mounted list, inner ones too) measured no change; clamping the compositor's scroll to the drawn rows (as a browser clamps async scroll) left the blank (6.5–7.7%) and moved 98–99% of the distance. (4) The feed's group keeps rows at the list's start and end mounted (the reader saw rows millions of px away in every paint): memory and walk cost, not blank.

- **Image spools: work relative to the root's descriptor** (2026-10-06, the raster-spool review, Grok r3). `RasterSpool.establish` checks `tmp/exact-raster` is a real directory before and after taking `.namespace.lock`, then sweeps and makes its directory by path; another process of the same user swapping the root for a symlink between those steps could redirect them. Open the root `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` and use `openat`/`mkdirat`/`unlinkat` under it.
- **`rem`/`em` the JS target still bakes, or the browser resolves elsewhere** (2026-10-06, after #136): SVG geometry written as attributes (`svg_props`: `x`, `y`, `width`, `height`, `cx`, `cy`, `r` on viewports, uses, masks, patterns, filters and gradients) still carries the kernel's 16 px resolution; and a node whose element keeps the UA's font (`button[data-button-style]`'s `font: revert`, `select`, both ~13.33 px in Chrome) resolves an `em` width or padding against that size in the browser where the kernel uses the inherited one, so the JS target and the wasm page disagree there.
- **The terminal surface's next work** (2026-10-06, LLP 1101.001 §3/§7; the terminal is admitted): P7's whitespace lint (`lint-space-collapses`) and the pitfalls' box-model candidate; P12 kitty images in the live region by id; `scripts/agent.mjs terminal` over the headless verbs and a `record` verb; the `color` fact and quantization below truecolour (D5); the profile's admission holes (the `border` shorthand, `style` blocks, non-literal lengths; both 1101.000 reviews); a non-terminal entry refusing `ch`/`lh` until a GUI host resolves font-relative units (Astra); ibex2's lossless stream mode when a consumer streams unreproducible events through the executor (P4); LLP 1101.002's deferred P5 (retirement's view half in the runner, now that `printed` is a contract) and its Q2 (start-aligned buttons, `nowrap` rows in the terminal sheet).
- **The LLP terminal reader's leftovers** (2026-10-08, LLP 1101.004 §4): `scrollBy` on Apple (`IntoView.swift` beside `scrollIntoView`) and in the wasm web host (`host/web/glue.js`); find across a run boundary in `apps/llp/data/src/page.rs`; `press=` on an inline run on Linux and Apple (the terminal and `href` already follow).
- **LLP 1075.003.000.001, access hatches on every platform** (2026-10-06, accepted as a design; landing on main as it is built; its §13 is the as-built record). Done: the rename from hooks; of stage 1, on the web's JS target and on Apple, every call timed, `diagnostics` with its bounds, `state.hatches`' new shape, `perf hatches`, Save Trace's section and the web's throw rule. Also done: the per-platform `hatches` declaration with its typed keys, the plan's requirement, the cohort's capability, `classifyArtifacts`' branch, hosts that call only the words their module handles (`platform`, `unhandled`), late frames that name their hatches, the crash breadcrumb and `EXACT_HATCHES=off` on Apple. The plan's own per-platform row is built for Apple (a `hatches` table; a word not given to the platform reaches Swift unhatched). A node's calls are timed by its plan site on every host. Owed of stage 1: the wall-clock acceptance driven in a person's session (a late frame from a 30 ms hatch, read from Save Trace), and §9's cost series on a phone. Of stage 2, done on the web and Apple: the bounded act queue, `input(text)`, settle draining and naming a hatch that never stops, the app and window scopes with the embedder's two grants, the frame clock and `after` with the seek stopping at each hatch instant, and `?hatches=off` on the web. Root `data-*` words reach `app` as `app.data` on every host. Owed of stage 2: `input`'s remaining cases (a search controller keeping first responder, composition). Then: stage 3's leftovers (a part tap driven as a real touch on the simulator, and whether `owns` should take a `UIInteraction`, whose recognizers a hatch cannot declare today); Stage 4 is built on Linux (the trait, the overlay, observed input, the scopes, the frame clock, diagnostics, the breadcrumb); it builds, lints and passes the fixture smoke on a real Linux box too (x86_64, 2026-10-07); its Windows adapter is written and has never been compiled for Windows or run (a `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` from Linux stops at `ring`'s C build, so it needs a Windows machine or a Windows C toolchain, and a Windows crate for the fixture), the Linux `build.rs` of each Rust-data app includes its hatches (`rust_hatch_entry`), the six that bake through `exact_js_bake` (canvas-gallery, completion-storm, duo-lab, expose, messages, weatherlight) do not yet, and an overlay draws no images or text yet. Stage 5, the `hatch` verb, the template's section and the guides, is done. Stage 6: Android runs stage 4's hatches (its in-repo host is the Linux presenter built for Android; 38 of 38 on a Pixel 10 Pro XL and a Pixel 10a); a Kotlin module and a `view` for a node that is a View wait for the Canvas reader to be in this repository, with their own RFC, and `smoke.mjs` has no `android` host. The design's r4 had no review before the ruling, so review should look first at the mid-seek act drain, each host's `input` primitive and the plan's per-platform words.
- **`cargo test -p exact-linux --lib` and `--test pinned` segfault on a Linux box with a real GPU** (2026-10-07, minisforum: x86_64, Radeon 890M, Mesa Vulkan). The crash is in `libvulkan.so.1` on a `Vello shader init` thread while dozens of test threads each start a GPU painter; it is there at aece7f3a7 too, before the Linux hatches. `-- --test-threads=1` passes all of both (623 and 76). The async lane's builders have no GPU, so nothing reports it. Fix to measure: one Vulkan instance a process for the tests, or a lock around instance creation.
- **`agent macos` is not under App Transport Security** (2026-10-06, #106): the driver runs the bare `standalone/ExactMac`, which has no bundle `Info.plist`, so an `iframe` of `http://<named host>` loads in a drive and shows the ATS error in the `.app` unless `host.macos.appTransportSecurity` allows it; so does a named-host `http:` sub-resource of an app's own page (#135). `EXACT_MAC_BIN=<.app>/Contents/MacOS/ExactMac` drives the bundle and shows what a user sees, but skips the freshness check. Fix: drive the assembled bundle's executable by default, or link the plist into the bare executable (`-sectcreate __TEXT __info_plist`, unmeasured: whether ATS reads an embedded plist).
- **iOS: `share` under an app-owned tab container** (2026-10-07; the Brooks port's bar Share did nothing in its fork). Exact's own containers are fine: a native-fixture build with no agent pushed Detail, then shared from a timer (no source node, so `shareAnchor` falls back to the window's root view and the root controller presents, the path Brooks' hidden bar-item source takes), and the system sheet came up over the pushed route. Brooks uses its own tab container (`BrooksTabs`, through the `tabContainer` hatch, LLP 1075.003 §3.6); that is the case left to try, and the fork's `presenter(from:)` (present from the visible controller) is the fix to measure against it.
- **After LLP 1047.001.000 E and F** (2026-10-15, §7.1): the media picker and sign-in sheet are linked by use, and a template's simulator first draw went from 1,287 to 339 ms. Still owed: §6's eager-permitted list (the check holds only the absent state), the launch phases on iOS's `state`, M1's warm condition on an iPhone (§8 Q9), and M5's rest (a phone, an https callback, the sample host's two sessions).
- **Snapback4's first native open runs past the step budget** (2026-10-08, LLP 1108 slice 1 drives on macOS and the iOS simulator): opening a never-synced partition natively (the device adopting the server's backend) took about 570 ms in one `people` step against the 100 ms budget, so the answer fails and the next one opens it; the drive's app raised the budget (`Module::set_budget_ms`). Split the adoption across steps, or run it as the module's long work (`NativeModule::later`).
- **A retryable `E_CONFLICT` blocks the Snapback4 client's outbox** (2026-10-08, app farm round 3, 34 GPT builds): a conditional write that lost its race comes back `failed`/`E_CONFLICT`/`retryable: true`; `round.rs` keeps it queued and stops the flush (`Stop::Retry`), so it and every write behind it retry forever. Follow Snapback's answer in `issues/20261008-snapback4-retryable-conflict-held-pending.md` (snapback repo); meanwhile bounding resends per write would unblock the outbox.
- **Live Snapback reads without a timer** (2026-10-08, [LLP 1110](llp/1110-live-snapback-reads.rfc.md), Draft r2): every app in the app farm's client arm refreshed on a 2–15 s Contract task, because a held `poll(20)` holds `clock settle`. r2: a TypeScript source answers with an async iterator, and `Snapback.changes()` long-polls with no server change; SSE later behind the same call. Reviewed twice (round 2: Astra NOT READY, Grok READY WITH CHANGES); round 2's points are folded as design and marked *owed*. Next: fix the owed items on lane C's local branch `lane/iterator-streams` (`~/projects/exact2-wt-laneC` on this Mac; built and driven on every executor, not landed), then review round 3.
- **A stream that can send: Supabase Realtime and Convex without polling** (2026-10-05, Depot feedback: an equipment-lending app built on Snapback4, Convex and Supabase polled on all three). TypeScript sources send on a resource's socket from a mutation, its mapper or an answer on the web JS target, macOS, the iOS simulator and the Linux host run on a Mac (LLP 1016.000 D7; `issues/closed/20261009-runner-owned-bidirectional-streams.md`). Owed: Phoenix's join and Convex's sync protocol driven against Supabase and Convex as the proof; the web wasm host, whose module realm hands the mapper a handle that never opens (`open`, ordered and binary frames, `Closed`, the mutation flag and the op-14 door in `module-glue.js`); Rust sources, which have no handle; natively, a caller's abort ending the stream and mapping `Aborted` as the web JS target does (it only makes the handle stale: the prelude has no way to ask the runner to end a ticket, so it needs a door op, the runner and both native executors); a Chrome-oracle conformance case (text and binary both ways, order, close code and reason, `bufferedAmount` back to 0) and an async-lane echo test on the platform transport; and the X21 drive on a Linux machine, where the portable transport sends, and on an iPhone.
- **npm in `app.ts`, and backend SDKs** (2026-10-05, Depot feedback: three handwritten wire adapters, Supabase's session persistence and token refresh by hand). Two problems. Bundling: the bake refuses outside imports "until dependency capture is implemented" (`js/bake/src/lib.rs`, `sources`), and LLP 1091 D10's package linking for Contract is most of that capture. The model: an SDK lives between answers (refresh timers, a socket it writes, `localStorage`), and a data module runs only inside one, refusing a `fetch` or storage call outside it. Pure packages work once bundling does; an SDK needs a client lifetime beyond one answer or a first-party adapter. An RFC with supabase-js and the Convex client as the cases; offline (snapshot cache, purge on sign-out, the persisted half of the keep-last-value line below) follows from it.
- **CSS `visibility: hidden` on Apple, what is left** (2026-10-05, after e28279b3b, 3703cc461 and 2298d07e3; a held focus, tvOS focus, hidden-text targets and the agent's `layout` followed): a hidden box's `backgroundMaterial` view and Mac backdrop blur still show (gating `materialRequest` alone tears the effect down and reparents its children, which drops a visible descendant's focus on iOS, and lets a glass group form, `glassGroupConflict`); hiding a Mac field or text area that is being edited (`applyCssVisibility` sets its `isHidden`) ends the edit at once, and its `change` (`commitEdit` calls `onChange` directly) applies a batch inside the style batch; a Mac text selection keeps a hidden paragraph's text (filtering it in `TextSelection.selectable` would shift a virtual list's paragraph ordinals from the runner's projection); iOS background-colour keyframes on the layer (`SvgScene`) still paint; a visible inline link reads its whole range to VoiceOver (`InlineAccessibility`), hidden nested spans included.
- **An update store reopened while another thread spawns is refused** (2026-10-05, review of the exact-update test fix): a spawned child holds a copy of `owner.lock`'s descriptor until its exec closes it, so `Store::open` (d3c84c2ba) on one thread while another is inside `Command::spawn` finds an "exclusive owner". The hosts open the store once at launch and hold it, so nothing in the tree does this; an embedder that reopens would need to serialize the two, as the test harness does (`open_store`, `finished`).
- **Transitions and animations of SVG `points` and `d`** (2026-10-05, the Signal clone): Chrome interpolates a polygon's `points` and a path's `d` (`path()`) between shapes with matching commands, which makes Lottie-style morphs declarative; Contract has no such transition or keyframe value, so a morph is a canvas or a stack of crossfades.
- **LLP 1099's unmeasured inputs** (2026-10-05, [LLP 1099](llp/1099-uikit-springs.rfc.md) §4.6, stage 0): a device recording of one duration-form and one negative-bounce spring (tracked with the Signal clone's `motion-trace.mjs`); older iOS runtimes, with availability guards in `motion/tests/fixtures/uikit-springs/probe/`; mixed-form retargets; a dense duration-form sweep below u = 0.05 at ζ ≤ 0.1. None blocks stages 1–3; each would amend the fixtures.
- **A todo app's first native build compiles wgpu, vello, the game engine and canvas** (2026-10-04, the LLP 1086 fresh-agent trial). A fresh `exact new` app's first macOS and iOS builds took ~13 minutes each, building crates the app never uses ("GPU: no GPU crate" in the same log). The README now says ten to fifteen minutes; pay-for-what-you-use (LLP 1047) says they should not be built at all for such an app.
- **Journal a route whose overflow nothing scrolls** (2026-10-05, Exact-new iOS feedback; `lower-route-scroll` covers the static half). A runner log was built and pulled after GPT-6-Astra and Grok 4.7 reviews: it read every layout offer as the route's box, so a sheet detent or `resizes-content` keyboard flagged a covered screen and latched its key; covered retained routes, absolute and transformed descendants, and host covers (padding, which Taffy's content bottom omits) gave wrong answers; and it ran on the boot layout. A real one journals only the active top route, at the settled window offer, after first pixel, counting in-flow content against the padding edge, and clears its key when the route fits again.
- **Authoring-bench round 3 leftovers** (2026-10-05, LLP 1087, Claude and Codex builders on 15fae50b4/1811a9c98):
