  `text-decoration-line: line-through` on a `textarea` draws on the web, not on iOS; a test cannot assert list order (`expect state doing.0.id` is a syntax error; builders join ids in a derive); a `pan`/`panrelease` payload has no pointer position, so a drop target is computed from frame + offset + deltas + a hard-coded inset; `outline` is not admitted (inset `box-shadow` instead); a JS drive script kept in the app folder makes the web build stale on every edit to it.
- **Authoring-bench iOS round 6 leftovers** (2026-10-05, LLP 1087, web+ios on b6b3332a4): on iOS a SQLite-backed mutation lands a turn after the input, so a tap at the new row misses, and the miss names no in-flight request there (the hint is web-only because a native read on the error path could time out and close the transport, 3c27a248c), where a failed `expect` with no input before it does name them.
- **`tree --ax` `pressed` on native hosts** (2026-10-05, authoring bench): the Chrome path keeps an aria-pressed toggle's `pressed` state; UIKit (`toggleButton`, "0"/"1"/"2") and AppKit (`AXCheckBox`/`AXToggle`, which emits `checked` and drops mixed) do not, and `CAN`/`APPLIES` in `scripts/agent-ax.mjs` do not compare it.
- **Authoring-bench iOS round 13 polish** (2026-10-05, web+ios on cdfbd9e1e): a `button appearance="auto"` is tiny on the web until given a width and height (iOS sizes it); a `role="tablist"`'s authored background draws as a frame around the native segmented control on iOS; on iOS an `input type="date"` with an explicit width centres its control in the box (`text-align` has no effect; a `select` honours it since #253), and without one a select's empty-option title clips off the left; an empty date input shows today's date on iOS (UIDatePicker cannot be blank) where the web shows an empty field; under the agent a pushed screen's native bar is not presented, so a builder could not see it in screenshots.
- **A test's `reload` restarts at the start URL** (2026-10-05, authoring bench round 5, t3-recipes: ~10 min): it keeps the store but boots at the drive's launch URL, not the route the test reached, so "reload on a detail screen shows that recipe" cannot be an authored test; a browser's reload keeps the URL. Either `reload` keeps the current location (as a person's reload does) or the docs say it does not.
- **Under `--touch platform`, context menus are still painted** (2026-10-16, LLP 1080.000 §13): a long press (`tap <node> contextmenu`) is recognized and opens the painted popover, and a real touch through it is refused. UIKit's context menu needs a held real touch (P3) or a `press` drag of the runner's.
- **interaction-gallery's Linux conformance comparison stops where the hosts' fonts differ** (`LINUX_APART` in `host/web-js/conform.mjs`; it needs a ruling, not a fix). It stops at `drag sheet-handle 0 -150 300`: the drag and its snap agree (both snap to the full stop), but the full stop is the sheet bay's height, under three rows of buttons with 12 px text, which Chrome sets in system-ui (San Francisco on macOS, a 29 px button) and the Linux host on macOS in Arial (`text/fonts.rs`'s sans without fontconfig, 28 px), so 518 against 521 (2026-10-16). Comparing it needs one face on both hosts.
- **The Lean `stdlib`'s equations are near their heartbeat budget** (2026-10-04, impl/1088-lists, LLP 1088 §9.1). Lean realizes `Contract.stdlib`'s equation lemmas where a proof first unfolds it, under the command line's options (a `set_option` does not reach them), and its match over the roster's string names took about 200,000 heartbeats before `concat` and needs more after, so `semantics/lakefile.toml` raises `maxHeartbeats` for the library. Each roster entry grows it (origin/main's formats and `t` add more). Matching the router's thirteen entries in a function of their own, reached from one arm, would shrink the match; `stdlib_ty`'s and `stdlib_good`'s case analysis would follow.
- **`perf frames live` on Apple** (2026-10-04, fix/game, the platformer's diary R11). The web's wasm target lends the agent's clock to the wall for a window (LLP 1079 D4); macOS and iOS answer `virtual`. `FrameSampler.swift` already builds the reply; a live branch in `Agent.swift`'s `perf` would release `session.clock` for the window, turn the GPU module's seek off (`GpuModule` takes `agentFreezes`) and let the display link fire runner timers (`Session.swift` gates them on `!agentMode`), then take the clock back at the wall.

- **`z-index` across a non-stacking parent, as CSS** (2026-10-04, fix/geometry, chess #5, kanban, kanban2, LLP 1087 t4-kanban). LLP 1083.000 §3.1 holds every host to "`z-index` stays inside its holder" because Apple nests one view per node and `zPosition` orders only one layer's sublayers. The web would follow CSS by dropping isolation rule (z) (rules a and b keep native tree order for level 0); the Linux painter could defer an escaping box to its stacking context's phase with its ancestors' clip and transform (hit testing follows its box order); Apple would have to re-parent an escaping view to the context's view with the intermediate clips and transforms rebuilt, as flights and the Arrange lift do for one view. Deciding between one order everywhere and CSS where a host can is Charlie's; the pitfall and LLP 1001 say what holds now.
- **Native `frame()` reads transforms as committed** (2026-10-04, fix/geometry, LLP 1051.000 D1). A transform in flight, a 3D one's projection and a `-exact-layout-transition`'s offset are the web's alone (its client rect); natively the runner composes the rows. Hosts noting presented transforms as they note scroll offsets (`Runner::scrolled`) would close it, for the first consumer that reads geometry mid-motion.
- **Form attributes the web has and Contract refuses** (2026-10-04, authoring bench, rounds 1–2): `autocomplete` on `input`, and `label`/`for` (builders used `aria-label` plus a visible `text`). (`aria-invalid` and CSS-wide `inherit`, also met there, landed the same day.)
- **A Lean theorem for LLP 1089 D3** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1089](llp/1089-action-composition.rfc.md) §7.2). If no path reads a slot another frame assigned earlier on that path, loading each slot as the last assignment on the path (or its starting value) equals the snapshot load; derives, resources, `pending` and sends lie outside the statement. That would prove `analyze-call-stale-read` sound against `exec`. No implementer yet.
- **Rewrite the diaries' copies as calls** (2026-10-04, LLP 1089 §4). Files' four opens as `arrive(path)` (F27, which also fixes `openFocused`'s missing `anchorPath = ""`), and spreadsheet's close-the-editor, `followSelection` and `clipKey` → `move` (F21), each driven on web and macOS. A scratch copy of spreadsheet with `commitEdit()` passes its web tests.
- **A percentage of a fit-content sheet's height on an ancestor still feeds the measure** (2026-10-08, Astra's review of `bsky/detent-vh` at 1a9b86776, LLP 1075.003 §9.11). The trial takes the route's parent's published width, which a percentage height can tie to the sheet: an absolute parent `height="calc(400px - 100%)" aspect-ratio=1 min-width=100 max-width=300` around a route `left=0 right=0 aspect-ratio=1` with a 44-point row measures 300, 100, 300 as the sheet goes 100, 300, 100. The fix, as CSS has it: inside a fit-content sheet, a percentage height against the sheet resolves as indefinite (`auto`) in the measure and in the real layout alike.
- **A scrolling `fit-content` route measures its rows as laid out in the sheet** (2026-10-07, Astra's review of `bsky/content-detent`, LLP 1075.003 §9.11). Its detent reads the scroll extent, so rows that may shrink (`flex-shrink: 1`) hold the sheet short; the non-scrolling route is measured alone (`Kernel::fit_content_height`). A second extent on the `content` op for the detent would end the `flex-shrink: 0` advice.
- **tvOS has no test target** (2026-10-04, fix/impl1088, LLP 1088 D7.3). `canBecomeFocused` is false for an explicit negative `tabindex` (`RemoteTVOS.swift`), and it compiles (`bun host/apple/build.mjs --tvos`), but nothing runs it: `KeyboardFocusIOSTests` is `#if os(iOS)` and `build.mjs --test` has no `--tvos`. The D7.3 test list's tvOS line waits on one.
- **A `pan` area takes a mouse drag that starts on selectable text** (2026-10-04, fix/typo, the reader diary). The reader's paged mode pans its page area by pointer, so a mouse drag over a paragraph with `selectionchange` turns the page instead of selecting (web: `input-glue.js` `pan` prevents the pointerdown; scroll mode selects on web and macOS). A rule like rule 3's for controls — a mouse contact that starts on a `text` with `selectionchange` is the selection's, a touch still pans — is a gesture-arbitration decision (LLP 1057).
- **A fragmented paragraph is one view on Apple, not one layer per fragment** (2026-10-05, LLP 1093 D8, left when the reader port landed). `Columns.swift` draws the paragraph clipped per fragment inside its union-sized view, so a paragraph across a spread's gutter backs a layer two pages wide. Hits already refuse the gap, and the reader's page clips, so nothing in the port showed it.
- **Content regions take no `text-indent`, `hyphens: auto` or a soft hyphen's fit** (2026-10-04, fix/typo). `host/apple/src/content_region/wire.rs` sends neither row, and `RegionWorkerLayout` breaks without the indent and without the hyphen-fit rule `TextEngine.layout` has; justification and soft-hyphen ink are there. Thread both through the region wire when a region consumer wants book text.
- **The document window, what the studio pass left** (2026-10-04, fix/macdocs; LLP 1069.010 "As built: the document window"). A ⌘Q that a window's `beforeunload` keeps open is cancelled, not resumed once the app's question is answered (NSDocument's review continues the quit); `wheel` and `drop` have no iPadOS form (a trackpad's scroll, a drag from Files) and `title` no `UIToolTipInteraction`; a command's menu is by chord only, so Zoom to Fit ⌘9 stays in File beside View's zooms; the bake does not warn when a declared extension already belongs to another app's type on the Mac (Freeform's `.board`); repeating linear gradients and px stops are admitted by Charlie’s 2026-10-12 ruling 9 (LLP 1066 amendment); the open repeating-hatch issue tracks remaining host verification and deferred `background-attachment: local`.
- **A resource whose arguments change reads its shape's zero until the new reply** (2026-10-04, fix/tooling3, workout F6). Lift's diary reports its board resource's `ready: false` unmounting the whole screen on every search or filter change, a flash on real time and a zero board under a test's still clock; the web's data stacks (SWR, React Query's `keepPreviousData`) keep the last value while a refetch is in flight. Native `clock settle` already waits for every runner request (a parked storage turn included; the macOS drive could not reproduce F6's miss on this branch), so what is left is the semantics: keep the last answer, flagged pending, until the new one lands, on every host.
- **Hear LLP 1096 on a phone** (2026-10-04, impl/1096). The sound arm's interruption handling (a call, Siri: the engine stops, voices due meanwhile are journaled `sound skipped: interrupted`, the engine restarts) and `audio_session: "playback"` against the ring/silent switch were built and run on the iOS simulator only; drums on an iPhone, and tvOS's tier-2 build, are still to do.
- **`z-index="auto"` is refused** (2026-10-04, files F14, the lists lane). `z_index` is an `i32` row defaulting to 0, so a conditional `z-index` cannot fall back to CSS's `auto`, and `auto` and `0` differ in CSS (only `0` makes a stacking context). A lifted, dragged row writes `z-index=(lifted ? 2 : 0)` for now.
- **Should `clock settle` fire a pending one-shot timer?** (2026-10-04, authoring bench, LLP 1087 t7-wizard.) A `task boot mount` with `after(1, restore)` does not fire at `clock settle` alone (it waits for requests and advances to the end of motion, LLP 1012 §2, firing a timer only if it reaches it on the way); a builder needed `clock +50`. The docs said a timer lands at `clock settle` and now say what it does. Including the earliest pending `after` (not `every`, which has no fixed point) in settle's target would match what builders expect; Charlie's call.
- **Setup and the wasm build on a fresh Linux box** (2026-10-04, the authoring bench): With a private `CARGO_HOME` the `-Zbuild-std` path failed offline on std's own crates (`rustc-demangle`) until `cargo fetch --manifest-path <sysroot>/…/library/Cargo.toml` was run by hand, though `app.mjs` means to do that itself. `host/web/build.mjs`'s non-JS path (a game, `--wasm`, `--bake`; `exact deploy` creates the parent itself) still publishes into an `EXACT_WEB_DIST` whose parent is missing with ENOENT: `replaceBuild` (`host/web/serve.mjs`) writes `<dist>.lock` before anything creates the parent, which only the JS target's path does (`mkdirSync(dirname(dist))`, 6ddd0c07f7; checked 2026-10-22 by calling `replaceBuild` into a missing directory).
- **The chooser, what is left** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1021](llp/1021-menus.rfc.md) "The chooser", "The chooser on macOS", "Placement"). Four gaps remain. The native `UIMenu`/`NSMenu` presentations are covered only by XCTest: under the agent a menu paints (D4), and a real touch into one awaits LLP 1080.000 stage 3. The iOS sheet cannot checkmark the remembered row, because `UIAlertAction` has no public checked state. macOS still lists a menu's hide-only Cancel row as an item. `position-area` is unread by `UIMenu` and Linux.
- **A Mac canvas draws some of its native text upside down since cc60cf219** (2026-10-04; found driving Caltrain after a build-speed change, bisected on the M4 over 157 commits with a capture compared by pixels). Caltrain's header card (the name, "Change station", "Web deck", the theme row, the train), the search field's text and "Back" are each flipped top to bottom in place, on screen and in the agent's screenshot; plain text rows beside them ("Matches", "Palo Alto") are upright, and two dividers are gone. `cc60cf219` ("compose macOS canvas uploads in current paint order") is the first commit that shows it: `Capture.bitmap` stopped using `cacheDisplay` and renders a copied layer tree with `CALayer.render(in:)` (`paintOrderBitmap`), which does not draw an AppKit view's flipped contents the way AppKit does. `bun scripts/agent.mjs macos "tap change-station" "type station-search Palo" "clock settle" "screenshot out.png"` shows it. This belongs with the entry below: whichever capture is kept has to draw those views upright. It is also the macOS smoke's one failure on a 1x Mac: the sky fixture's "Zoom" and "Label" are upside down (1.58% of pixels beyond the band against `canvas-sky.macos.420x120.png`, which shows them upright). Ruled out (fix/health6, 2026-10-04, four tries reverted): `contentsAreFlipped()` does not tell the turned layers from the upright ones — Caltrain's header texts and its result rows are both `NSViewBackingLayer`s with `NSViewBackingLayerContents`, `contentsAreFlipped() == true` under the one geometry-flipped layer above the overlay's root, identity `contentsTransform`, the same gravity, rect and format, yet the copy draws the header turned and the rows upright. Turning every copied layer whose count of flipped ancestors differs fixes the header and turns the rows; redrawing every view before the copy changes nothing; holding the copy under a geometry-flipped layer mirrors the whole picture with the same split. What the window knows about each backing store that the copy loses is still to be found; the likeliest is an orientation `NSViewBackingLayerContents` carries from when it was drawn (the split differed between runs, the train image upright in one and turned in another), which the window server honours and `render(in:)` does not. The candidate fix is to give the copy each drawing view's own picture as a `CGImage` (its `draw(_:)` into a bitmap, as `cacheDisplay` did before cc60cf219) in place of its backing store; a single NSView fixture captured both ways would confirm it first.
  Two fixes tried 2026-10-16 and parked on the Mac Studio (branches `q1013/w2-mac-flip`, `q1013/w3-mac-flip`). (1) Going back to `cacheDisplay` routes canvas uploads through AppKit's `draw`, so nested canvases are read back during the capture; four review rounds each found a new re-entrancy defect (placements given to the wrong children while paint order is sorted, a readback changing the tree being walked, publications out of order, unbounded recapture, a stopped display link). (2) Keeping main's `paintOrderBitmap` and giving each drawing view's copied layer its own `draw(_:)` picture fixes the text (Caltrain's header and rows upright, a fixture failing first at 1× and 2×), but redrawing reimplements the backing store AppKit keeps and diverges from it: an editing search field captures blank (its field editor's text is not in the field's `draw`), each unclipped text view allocates the whole visible rect (thirty 7.7 MB bitmaps per capture for an 800×600 page at 2×) and a scaled one far more, `canDrawSubviewsIntoLayer` children that report `wantsUpdateLayer` vanish, a view fading out by its presentation opacity disappears when its model alpha reaches 0, and a bounds animation slices the wrong region. What both lack is the orientation the window server applies to an `NSViewBackingLayerContents`; find that (or a way to read the backing store upright) before a third attempt.
  Third attempt, 2026-10-22 (lane q1022/e, fixture `CaptureOrientationMacTests`, expected failures mark the defect): a copy of a subtree below the window's flipped content root flips every ordinary drawing store in it (CGImage and IOSurface contents too), while a copy that includes the window root, `cacheDisplay`, and `render(in:)` on the original or presentation tree are all upright; a fixture of a header beside a scroll view's rows does not reproduce Caltrain's mixed case, and the `isFlipped`/`isGeometryFlipped` chains of the header texts and the upright rows are identical. Rendering the original leaf layers in paint order (no copies) draws Caltrain upright but calls `draw(_:)` again, fails two `PaintCaptureMacTests` and leaves the sky readback at 1.53%. Next experiment: sample the header's and a row's backing-store image and content-layer state immediately before their first copy, without `cacheDisplay` or `render(in:)` first.
- **Two ordered macOS captures after LLP 1083.000 met main's capture fix** (2026-10-04). The agent's screenshot uses `Capture.picture` (`AgentMac.swift`, 4863d26d7: subviews re-sorted by `zPosition` around `cacheDisplay`, presented animation values, box fills hidden); canvas uploads use LLP 1083.000's `paintOrderBitmap` (`Mac/CaptureMac.swift`: an explicit compositor, filter pictures, model values with animated key paths overlaid). Both order by the paint rank and both pass the paint-order driver; keep one, with both sets of tests (`PaintCaptureMacTests`, the shop/spreadsheet captures).
- **CSS font generic mappings for cursive/fantasy/math/emoji/fangsong and CSS-wide font-family values** (2026-10-04, CSS lane): currently refused precisely so a generic is never silently treated as a local family.
- **CSS intrinsic flex-basis keywords (content/min-content/max-content/fit-content)** (2026-10-04, CSS lane): dimension rows need an intrinsic sizing representation; compiler now diagnoses the declared mode limit precisely.
- **CSS native interaction/painter gaps (diaries kanban F11, minesweeper F4, ledger F1, pomodoro F2)** (2026-10-04, CSS lane): resize handles; user-select contain on every host (text since 2026-10-12 and all since 2026-10-20 are honoured on the web, macOS and iOS; Linux, Windows and Android take both as auto); dotted/dashed and other border styles; decoration color/style/thickness and Linux underline skip-ink:auto. Current compiler/driver diagnoses each declared limit.
- **Linux authored glyph fallback cascades** (kanban F8, 2026-10-04): font lists select the first available family; cosmic-text still owns glyph fallback through its single Attrs.family. Preserve the rest of the authored list per shaping run before claiming full CSS per-glyph fallback parity.
- **Layout property transitions** (kanban F15, shop F5, 2026-10-04): CSS `transition="width 200ms ease"` is valid CSS, but the native engine cannot interpolate arbitrary layout rows or lay out per frame. The diagnostic names supported paint/transform/SVG properties and the admitted height path. Add width and other layout transitions only with an approved executor/layout design; `-exact-layout-transition` projects changes to the laid-out box today.
- **Viewport units on scalar CSS lengths** (kanban F9, 2026-10-04): dimension rows retain viewport units across wire and resolve on native resize; font-size, gap, spacing and border widths still use scalar rows. Extend their retained relative lengths and web emission before admitting viewport units there.

- **Clock timelines, what the reviews left** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1055.002](llp/1055.002-synced-animations.rfc.md) §5 "Known gaps"). Under the web agent clock an author's pause and resume is recorded once (`held`, as on main for every animation), so a resumed synced animation does not rejoin there, and one JS-agent seek that crosses a timer registers its joiner before older finite members are sought. `contract symbols` has no definition or reference for a `timeline`. Not yet driven after the review fixes: Lexy (its repository) on iOS and the web, macOS and Linux apps, Firefox, Core Animation across backgrounding.
- **`release` is every developer tool's profile too** (2026-10-04, the build-speed pass; LLP 1036.000 §5–7). The Linux host an agent drives and an app's render entry moved to `host-dev`, where a touched kernel line rebuilds in 3.6 s; at `release` (one codegen unit, thin LTO) it was 102 s on a Ryzen 3600, 58 of them the kernel on one core. So did the Contract compiler the smokes run for their fixtures. The web's tools are still `cargo run --release`: the dev loop's `exact-dev` and `exact-js-bake` (`host/web/dev.mjs`), `parity.mjs`, `logic/bake` (`scripts/rust.mjs`), the `metrics` bins, and conformance's wasm side. Each rebuilds through that link after any change under it. Moving them to `host-dev` is the same one-line change each, left for the web's pass.
- **What `release` could shed: measured, not built** (2026-10-04, Hetzner Ryzen 3600, `caltrain-linux` / `caltrain-render`, stripped). Thin LTO, one codegen unit, `opt-level = 3` (today): 17.91 / 8.00 MB, 134 s from nothing. Fat LTO: 16.44 / 7.31 MB (−8%), 207 s. `opt-level = "s"`: 14.80 / 6.40 MB (−17%), 94 s. Both: 12.85 / 5.65 MB (−28%), 155 s. `opt-level = 2`: 17.15 MB (−4%). Sixteen codegen units: 19.42 MB (+8.5%), 97 s. Fat LTO costs only build time (73 s more there; run again later that day 143 → 210 s and 18.04 → 16.57 MB, boot 50.7 and 50.9 ms and layout 21.9 ms both ways over twelve alternating launches), and a Linux bake could say it as the Apple one now does, on Cargo's command line: a bake builds with `--target`, so it shares no artifact with the tools above. Sixteen codegen units under fat LTO, which on Apple build faster than today's profile, do not here: 193 s for 17.36 MB (−3.8%). `"s"` trades the hosts' speed, unmeasured there. Both are Charlie's to rule with the other platforms. An Apple production bake does not move the same way (M4, Caltrain's `ExactMac`, as built / with `strip -x`): thin LTO 11.98 / 9.06 MB; fat LTO 11.82 / 8.62 MB (−1% / −5%); `opt-level = "s"` 13.60 / 10.78 MB, larger by 14%, and its runner and layout at boot 7% slower (16.1 ms against 15.1). Fat LTO is built there (the bake says it for an Apple target, which no tool builds for; LLP 1036.000 §8); `"s"` is a loss. The Swift side has the same trade and it is also left: `-Osize` makes Sparkline's stripped binary 7.8% smaller (8.73 → 8.04 MB, 12.6% with fat LTO) and its first apply of 295 views 7% slower (17.9 ms against 16.7, sixteen alternating launches).
- **A cold bake of an app with a GPU crate is two Cargo builds end to end: measured, not built** (2026-10-04, Cargo's `--timings`, Caltrain on the ten-core M4, before the registry crates were kept; LLP 1036.000 §7). The GPU crate first, 39 s for 146 unit-seconds, because it is one chain (naga 14 s → wgpu-core 13 s → wgpu 7 s); then the app's archive, 49 s for 366, whose chain is the kernel (33 s) and the app (3.5 s). They are in sequence because the app's build script binds the GPU product (LLP 1009 D6), and nothing else can warm the app's dependencies meanwhile: asked for by another selection they are other units (`-p exact-apple` recompiles 25 crates in a warm target, the kernel among them; with the GPU crate, 32). Overlapping them needs the GPU crate in its own target directory and the app's dependencies compiled before its build script may run, which is a change to what `buildBake` promises on every platform. It is worth less than the chains suggest on ten cores, where ExactKit's compile already fills the GPU phase's idle ones (the three together are 660 CPU-seconds, 66 s at best), and more on eighteen. In a checkout that starts from the kept registry crates (§11) the two builds are 12.9 s (wgpu-hal 3 s → wgpu-core 9 s → wgpu 5 s, then ours) and 37 s (the kernel, 30 s), so overlapping them is worth at most those 13 s of a 58 s first build.
- **What a second checkout still compiles** (2026-10-04; LLP 1036.000 §7, §10, §11). The host's two Rust modules and the registry crates under an app are now kept for the machine (`~/.cache/exact/apple-modules`, `~/.cache/exact/apple-crates`): a second checkout's first build is 78 → 58 s on an M4 and 38 crates, all of them path crates or standing on one. What is left, in the order it costs: the kernel (33 s in one rustc front end, the chain every build waits on); `wgpu-core` and `wgpu`, registry crates rebuilt in every checkout because the vendored `wgpu-hal` under them is a path crate (20 s of the GPU crate's build; a patched crate consumed as a registry or git source would be kept like the rest); ExactKit (150 CPU-seconds, beside the bake, and not what the Rust waits for: at a lower priority the build is the same, 57.7–58.9 s against 57.3–58.6). The kernel alone is 12.6 s on an idle eighteen-core M5, 10 s of it rustc and 7 of those LLVM over its sixteen codegen units, so it is the cores and not the front end. sccache was tried on the Linux host and is not it (95 → 79 s): what it cannot share is the same path crates.
- **A binary that takes updates ships every loaded module** (2026-10-04; LLP 1047 D8, "after the web target"; LLP 1036.000 §8). A production Apple build at store level 0 now leaves out each of the four its plan cannot reach (the bake's `loads`: the Canvas 2D GPU module, the SVG island module, the video and web arms; Sparkline's Mac bundle 16.3 → 11.6 MB), because that plan is the only one the binary will ever run. A binary at any other level carries all four (4.7 MB on a Mac) for a later bundle that might reach one, and dropping them there needs the linked set as a compatibility input, so a bundle that reaches a module its binary lacks is refused before it is applied (D6, D8).
- **An abort a listener suppresses leaves a browser-realm fetch pending until its reply** (2026-10-04, both families' round-5 reviews of the app-diary fixes, 4a8). Hermes watches a fetch's signal through Ibex's abort hooks; the wasm target's browser module realm loads prelude.js alone, so `watchAbort` is an `addEventListener` an earlier `stopImmediatePropagation` skips, and the fetch rejects only when its reply lands (fulfill rechecks `signal.aborted`). The default web build (the JS target) uses the browser's own fetch and is unaffected.
- **A translucent raster is darker on macOS than in Chrome** (2026-10-04, fix/images, seen in screenshots only). A 1×1 PNG of `rgba(255,0,0,0.5)` scaled to 40 pt paints pink over white in Chrome and a near-solid red in the macOS host (shown from a `data:` URL; a file asset with alpha not yet compared). Measure the pixels on macOS, iOS and Linux against Chrome's, then look at `RasterImage.decode`'s alpha handling (premultiplied or not) and the draw's blend.
- **A Rust source in a logic module is asked without its `Target`** (files F22, 2026-10-04, read in `logic/abi`, not reproduced). The ABI's ops 3/4 call `answer`/`parse`, whose defaults drop to `query` (the bake placeholder); in process (`Mixed`, `Storage`) the runner calls `answer_for`/`parse_for`. A source that parks a call by `Target` therefore answers its placeholder, silently, on the JS web target only. Carry the target across the ABI (both hosts' encoders, an ABI version), or refuse at load a module whose source overrides `answer_for` and not `answer`. No longer needed for `doc:` paths, which TypeScript reaches now (F2).
- **`contract types`' `Sources` demands the Rust half's sources from `app.ts`** (files F21, 2026-10-04). In a mixed app the generated `Sources` names every source, so the TypeScript half writes `Omit<Sources, …>` by hand. The composition that knows which sources are Rust's (`RUST_SOURCES`, `build_mixed_with`) is Rust code; `app.json` could name them, or the bake could emit a `TypeScriptSources` type from the composer it already runs.
- **Pointer events, what is left** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1056](llp/1056-canvas-2d.rfc.md) §8.6; the paint diary's F1). No `coalesced` list: a stroke is a polyline at the display's rate, and DOM's `getCoalescedEvents()` as a field needs a second record type (a record cannot list itself in the plan's types). An iPad's hovering pointer and pencil send no free `pointermove` (UIKit's hover recognizer). Linux delivers every move, as its `pan` does, not one a frame. The Mac agent's held drags read `pressedMouseButtons` beside the event's own button; a synthesized pen is never a tablet event, so pen pressure on macOS is untested by script. `exact new`'s template now passes the module's canvas roster; apps made before it (x2apps) have to add `.with_canvas_surfaces(CANVAS_SURFACES)` to their `apple/` and `web/` crates by hand.
- **An empty date still reads today's date to VoiceOver on iOS** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.000 D13.1): the pill shows the author's `placeholder` or no words, but UIKit's compact picker inside it is the element VoiceOver reaches, and `tree --ax` reads its button's value as today's date for a `value=""` (`button "Date Picker" value="Oct 12, 2026"`). `BoundDatePicker.accessibilityValue` is `""`, which nothing reads. While empty the picker would have to be the one element, its value the placeholder, with an activation that opens the calendar, for which UIKit has no public call.
- **CSS `outline`** (2026-10-04, x2apps spreadsheet F3): `outline-width/-color/-offset` are refused, and `outline`/`outline-style` take only `none`, on a text field or textarea, where it takes the field's ring off (LLP 1104 D6, #459). On the web they would be CSS (and `outline: none` takes the browser's focus ring off a focusable grid); natively nothing draws a focus ring on a box, so they need paint rows outside the border box, following `border-radius`, above the content, on Apple and Linux. Admit with the four rows and their shorthand, painted on every host, and a `:focus-visible` answer (none exists for any row yet).
- **Keys, what native hosts still lack** (2026-10-04, x2apps calc F3 / minesweeper F7; docs/contract-grammar.md#keys). `key` bubbles and `preventDefault()` claims a key on every host now, but on macOS and iOS a scroller does not scroll by arrow, Space or page keys (the web's default), and iOS carries no `aria-keyshortcuts` (`UIKeyCommand`s from the focused session's buttons, as `ShortcutsMac.swift` routes them). The iOS hardware-keyboard path (`TextField.pressesBegan`) is driven only through the agent's synthesized keys; no real keyboard has pressed it.
- **The JS target's data seam caps an answer at 16 MiB** (2026-10-04, LLP 1090 conformance). `rust-data.js` goes through the logic ABI, whose `MAX_MESSAGE` is `MAX_HOST_WORK_BYTES`; the runner's in-process data source has no cap, so an answer between 16 MiB and the runner's value bounds loads on wasm, native and Linux and fails on the JS target (docs/agent-pitfalls.md). Either bound answers alike on every host or let the JS seam carry what the runner does.
- **The JS jump lands what a timer sent at its last instant** (2026-10-04, LLP 1092 "As built"). `host/web-js/agent.js`'s `clock +N` waits for every reply after a stop and runs its `then`; the wasm glue, the Linux agent and Apple stop waiting once no timer is due by the jump's end, so a request a timer sends at exactly `to` lands there on real time and its `then` waits for the next operation. Conformance avoids it (`queue.contract`'s timer sends nothing); pick one behaviour and make the other hosts follow.
- **One-slot invariants of a queue mutation's slot** (2026-10-05, LLP 1092 D12, semantics/README.md "Queued sends and gated tasks"). `Reachable.slotIn` and its siblings are proved of slots no drain writes (`isQueue p x = false`). For a queue mutation's slot, carry the property of the answers its waiting sends will land (`∀ q ∈ c.queued, q.1 = x → ∀ v, V (.some v)`) through `runAction` and `nextCommit`. Lean's `pending(m)` of a waiting queue is false (the runner's is true): model it if a program needs it.
- **What the Lean semantics still leaves out** (2026-10-04, refreshed 2026-10-17; semantics/README.md "What the semantics leaves out"). Routes, `t(...)`, the `format*` entries, frame tasks, a mutation's `then` and form-control payloads landed. Left: geometry reads (`frame`, `measure`, `elementFromPoint`), `toLowerCase` (Unicode's case mapping, which the runner takes from Rust's `str::to_lowercase`), the host's `navigate` event, a reload, and `exactTransport`/`exactMIDI` reads. `difftest explore contract/corpus apps/*/app.contract` reports Metronome (`exactTransport`) and Shelf (`toLowerCase`) as outside it. A virtualized list's rows are left out of the observation (the runner windows them by layout), so a scripted tap on one is not compared meaningfully.
- **tvOS, what is owed** (2026-10-03, landed from Doug Lowder's `doug/tvos`; LLP 1008 §9 "tvOS"). iOS-only files that shared code names break it (twice in one landing: `PointerIOS.swift`, then `NavigationTitleIOS.swift` and `GroupedListIOS.swift`); the tier 2 async lane (`scripts/async.mjs --tier 2`, hourly) now builds tvOS and files what breaks, but nothing starts that lane on fleet hardware yet; since 2026-10-12 the per-commit lane compiles it too (`build.mjs --tvos --no-install`, which needs no simulator) for any range beyond documentation. `scripts/agent.mjs` has no tvOS target (it installs the iOS build), so `smoke.mjs` cannot drive a TV, and nothing here presses the Siri Remote by script (`simctl`/`devicectl` have no route; Doug drove it with Argent). No device builds, icons (layered brand assets) or Top Shelf. A grouped list's toggle row shows a checkmark but Select does not flip it.
- **A plan decoded inside wasm at activation** (2026-10-03, LLP 1029.000's per-byte fuel). Activation hands the data module the whole encoded plan, which it decodes at about 430 wasmi instructions a byte: Caltrain's 46 KB plan is about 20 million. Unmeasured: what that costs in wall time on the native interpreted path against the 100 ms cold-start budget. If it matters, activation gives the module only the tables it reads.
- **Hosts outside the repository** (2026-10-03, Charlie: "out-of-tree is for like Amazon's new TV operating system thing"; `rules/DEFERRED.md` §Surfaces). An RFC beside LLP 1036.001 (apps outside the repository): what a host with its own UI stack consumes from exact2 (the plan and kernel wire, the runner, the agent protocol), and what exact2 would have to publish for it (a stable host ABI, `host/web-js/conform.mjs` runnable outside this repository, drivers that do not assume `host/*`). Amazon's Vega OS is the motivating case; first find out what native surface its SDK gives an app.
- **A standing web competitor bench** (2026-10-04, `~/bench/dioxus`). The perf scoreboard measures iOS against SwiftUI and UIKit and never the web or a bounded window's shift. One afternoon against Dioxus Web found the feed shipped three times (cf12cb9ae), the whole-list tap (now a development-build warning, `host/web-js/seam.js`), a lazy source's first-shift hitch, and the clamp bug that threw readers back 55–59 rows at every window shift on web, macOS and iOS (ca83e4eaa, 04c1822ec). The harness (`web/bench.mjs`: load, bytes, heap, scroll at four speeds, taps; `web/progress.mjs`: content progress by row) and the Dioxus port (`heavy-dx/`) are there: give them a run on each landing that touches the web host or collections, beside the iOS series.
- **The Vello canvas keeps every decoded image** (2026-10-04, Blitz's crash). Dioxus Native's default renderer (vello_hybrid 0.0.9) panics with `AtlasLimitReached` after about 9,000 pt of a feed of 104 distinct photos. `canvas/vello/src/lib.rs`'s `images()` map holds every decoded image by the host's handle and nothing evicts it except `ecg_trim` (a memory warning on Apple). Whether `exact-vello`'s atlas behind it is bounded is untested. Drive a Canvas 2D surface through a few hundred distinct images and watch memory and errors.
- **Sub-second Rust edits without hot-patching** (2026-10-05, [LLP 1029.000.001](llp/1029.000.001-sub-second-rust-edits.rfc.md), Draft, awaiting Charlie). A one-line data-crate edit costs 2.3–3.2 s on the JS target's dev loop and 1.6–2.0 s with live replacement on native; the crate compiles in 0.13–0.55 s ([LLP 1029.000.000](llp/1029.000.000-hot-patching-rust-measured.research.md)). The RFC builds the dev module in `logic-dev`, swaps the module in the page for a Rust-only edit, and builds only the variants connected clients run, in parallel; Subsecond-style function patching was weighed and declined.
- **Grouped lists, what is left** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1084](llp/1084-native-grouped-lists.rfc.md) §4, §7). (The large title collapses with the collection view, and grouped rows take `delete=` and hand-written swipes: checked 2026-10-20.) The Signal Clone's `Section`/`Row`/`ActionRow` components can become a grouped list (its repository). `sidebar` styles wait for an iPad or Mac consumer.
- **Styleable materials** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1081](llp/1081-names-exact-invents.rfc.md) Q3). A `style` class cannot carry `backgroundMaterial` or `glassGroup` because they are props; LLP 1069.011 D12 already makes `buttonStyle` a styleable prop, and these two can join it. WebKit's `-apple-visual-effect` is not a browser name under LLP 1081 D1 (it is behind `useSystemAppearance`), so there is nothing to adopt.
- **Style names and their kinds in `schema.json`** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1081](llp/1081-names-exact-invents.rfc.md) Q5). LLP 1081 puts the author style names, with provenance, in Contract's `style_names.rs`. Moving them into the schema is a mapping design, not two new fields: names and rows are many to many (`gap` sets two rows, `translate` sets `Translate` and `TranslateZ`, `flex` sets three different values).
- **CamelCase for Contract's kebab-case props** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1081](llp/1081-names-exact-invents.rfc.md) Q6). `scroll-start`, `scroll-restoration`, `initial-item-count` and `bitmap-width`/`bitmap-height` look like CSS properties and are props; camelCase, Contract's spelling for its own props, would make a kebab-case name always mean CSS or an HTML attribute. Ask Charlie first about `bitmap-width`/`bitmap-height`, his "for now" spelling.
- **What `tree --ax` found on macOS and iOS** (2026-10-03, LLP 1080.002 §7). AppKit exposes the accessibility fixture's `aria-hidden` decoration and the button inside its `inert` column (`exposed-hidden` ×2, LLP 1001's "AppKit/Linux subtree enforcement remains open"), and its checkbox and password field are exposed through cells that answer no name (the label is set on the control view; whether VoiceOver speaks it is unverified). (On iOS a `role="img"` column is one image, its name its label, its button hidden, as on the web: checked 2026-10-20.) Not built: a projected segment's join to its tab, and the macOS window toolbar's walk (both reported as coverage limits). On iOS the read needs UIKit's accessibility runtime: `xcrun simctl spawn <udid> defaults write com.apple.Accessibility ApplicationAccessibilityEnabled -bool true` on a simulator, then relaunch; the driver could set it for an `ax` drive.
- **LLP 1079 on hardware, what is unrun** (2026-10-03, `perf`'s §As built). Linux's display-loop sampler and `SIGUSR1` trace were type-checked for `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu`, never run on a DRM display. iOS Save Trace was clicked on a simulator (2026-10-05: saved, Share… offers the file, `agent.mjs trace --phone <simulator>` copied it from the container and read it); on a phone neither the share sheet's AirDrop nor the driver's `devicectl` copy has been run, and macOS's ⌥⌘T was not clicked. The `--stress-url` p95 comparison, measuring on and off, was not run.
- **The JS target's development `perf` costs about 3.7 µs a commit** (2026-10-03, LLP 1079 §As built). 800 caltrain hover commits took 9.7 ms against 6.7 ms with `perf.js` stubbed out. Two-thirds is the mutation observer that gives `authored`/`created`/`retired`, the rest the `pf` wrappers; production carries neither. A cheaper count would need `rt.js` to stamp its own writes, which every production bundle would then carry.
- **JS-target grant parity, what is left** (2026-10-02, landed from `web/grants` (c4e02b00, 207c2c04, dcd938ed) after three review rounds; both final reviews still mark these). Unicode spellings the Rust parser accepts and the web treats differently: U+0085 in a module's grant text is refused at `host/web/module-glue.js` prepare; with a U+FEFF line a parser-accepted `auth.session` never installs on the JS target, and the wasm page's surface check admits a U+FEFF line native refuses. A `Response` with an empty URL (an opaque, `no-cors` answer) skips the final-origin check, so an ungranted redirect can succeed there. A redirect whose hop the browser itself rejects reports `Network`, not native's `Refused` (a declared deviation, LLP 1071 §7: the page cannot see the destination). A Rust-only page exposes its file commands before its grant set is installed. Two `Store` facade differences from native in `host/web-js/ts-data.js`. `apps/fieldnotes/web/tests/worker.rs` is not migrated to grant-set arguments. Out of scope by Charlie's ruling (grants on the web are parity, not a sandbox: the page's own code can always reach the browser's fetch): forged, self-sealed or accessor-backed grant objects, and app code replacing the fetch late host modules use.
- **Native bars, what Signal Clone still needs a module for** (2026-10-02, LLP 1075.003 in an outside app; its DIARY.md "build 5"; title views, `hidesBottomBarWhenPushed`, the barless route and content under the bar landed as §9.10). The tab bar's tint from the selected tab's authored `-exact-tint-color`; a symbol beside the heading in a drawn title (Signal's verified seal).
- **Scripted iOS gesture paths** (Mac; Charlie, 2026-10-22; LLP 1035.003 §3,
  LLP 1080.000 Q2). Extend `tap … drag` in `scripts/agent-drag.mjs` and
  `host/apple/touches.mjs` / `touches.swift` to carry down, move, hold,
  reverse and up in one XCTest synthesizer record, with reads at checkpoints.
  No contact stays down across separate requests; buffered still presses and
  their reads already play at the lift (§14). Done when a drag can be read
  while held, reversed, then released with the same touch identity. Keep §11's
  native swipe-action reveal drive on Messages and edge-swipe fix on a phone.
- **Animated scroll to the end on send, what is left** (2026-10-02, Signal Clone; iOS, macOS and the web host built 2026-10-03, LLP 1070.000 §11). Linux and the JS web target land a smooth correction at once. A plain `scroll` still jumps on a `scrollTop` write followed to the end.
- **Smooth collection jumps cross unbuilt rows** (2026-10-03, astra's review of the smooth-correction hosts, `llp/reviews/code-2026-10-03-signal-gaps.astra.md` #5). While a smooth correction animates, every host reports the destination: a mid-flight report told the runner that the reader had left the end, and that stopped the end-follow. So a smooth `scrollIntoView` farther than the mounted window crosses spacers no row fills. A fix needs the report to carry both the physical port, which is what to realize, and the destination, which is what to anchor. That is a wire change in `CollectionFacts`. A send's end-follow is within the window and never shows it.
- **A symbol image laid out at 0×0 after first layout, not reproduced** (2026-10-02, Signal Clone DIARY "build 4"; retried 2026-10-03). A composer camera or mic `symbol:` image that reappears after a send, with no explicit `width`/`height`, measured 0×0 while the app re-asked a resource every 250 ms. On current main it does not reproduce: four agent drives (`--timing platform`, send, layouts at 0/250/500/900 ms and after settle) give 26.33×20.33 every time. Real keyboard input is not drivable here (axe types into nothing without focus). The one path found by reading, never shown to be hit: `PresenterIOS.flushIntrinsicSizes` drops a queued symbol size whose `loadGeneration` moved on, and `loadImage` bumps the generation without re-queuing when `updateSymbol` finds the same `symbolKey`. The clone keeps explicit sizes. Next step: a real-touch drive (LLP 1080.000) of type, send and reappear, logging any dropped intrinsic.
- **Per-corner radii as a shape mask, not a CPU draw** (2026-10-02, Signal Clone). A box whose four corner radii differ (a chat bubble's 18/4 pt cluster corners) sets `boxDrawn` and paints its fill in `draw(_:)`, one CPU backing store per bubble, redrawn on every size change. Signal-iOS uses a solid colour or gradient view masked by a `CAShapeLayer` path (implicit animations off). `BoxLayerIOS` could do the same: layer background plus a shape-layer mask for non-uniform radii.
- **LLP 1075.003 (native platform control) owes** (2026-10-02, `lane/native-control`, §9.4). Stages 0–3 are built: hatches on an app's module (`navigation`, `route`, `routeEnded`, `tabs`, `tabContainer`) over Exact's own bar and retained tabs, declared `data-*`, host covers, write-on-change controls, multi-detent sheets, and a large title collapsing with its scroller. Owed: James's two cases at a pinned revision in his app; real touches (a finger on a bar item, a finger's collapse and pull to top, a held sheet drag, a minimizing tab bar); a cold launch filmed on a phone with and without hatches, and a memory warning; Stage 4 (TypeScript by experiment), which needs NativeScript's runtime, a dependency nobody approved; web and AppKit hatches; the `element` hatch and the named action (Stage 5, on demand); `back()` and `open(url)` on `ExactTabContents`; the development ownership check on an app's tab container. On the JS target `project` runs only from the router's effect, so a navigation root with no router slot is never projected there (its stacks, and now its panels, stay as authored); it predates the plan. LLP 1075.003.000 (hatches on every host, Stages 1–4 done; a hatched node in every row of the Extra Heavy feed costs a fling about 16% CPU and, on the iPhone, 5 fps, all of it the lost row reuse: the call and an unhatched build cost nothing measurable, §7.5) built the opt-in that takes that price back (`element.reusable`, §7.6); owes a development check on macOS and the web and for a base view's owned properties; hatches on the wasm web target, if a game or a fixture ever wants them; an LLP on which AppKit containers Exact projects.
- **A module view's input, twice** (2026-10-02, astra's delta review of the hatches' fixes). On both web targets a module view hears the page's own input as any element does (glue.js `attach`; rt.js `on` since e0ee5759f) and its module's reports (`event(code)`): a module whose own button reports `event(0)` for a click the page also sees delivers one press twice, and focus, blur and keys likewise. LLP 1024 should say which one a module view hears for each kind (the module's report only for what the page cannot see, or the module's only); Apple's hosts are the third to check.
- **Smoke checks that fail on main** (2026-10-02, seen at `220c2128e` and on the native-control landing alike). `smoke.mjs macos --app native-fixture`: "Tab reaches the module's editing descendant" (the responder stays `NodeView`, `editor` null). `smoke.mjs web --app recorder` (the JS target, seen at `ef01dcadd`): "the status is coarse, and answered by native.later" counts the wasm runner's `request N (status): POST exact-native:` journal lines, which the JS runtime does not write, so it counts 0; and `conform.mjs recorder` fails `tap toggle` and the next step because Chrome leaves the toggle focused on the JS page and not on the wasm page. Under load (the six checks beside a simulator or another build), `exact-update`'s store tests fail now and then and pass alone: `store::a_lower_seq_is_refused` (`update/tests/it/support.rs:230`), `store::a_head_naming_the_embedded_plan_and_assets_is_current_and_downloads_nothing` (`update/tests/it/store.rs:897`), `durability::an_unchanged_asset_is_stored_once_and_old_entries_are_collected`. **2026-10-12, the same smoke now stops earlier, at a tap:** `tap #18: its middle is outside the viewport` (`compose-home` laid out at x 421.6 in the 420-wide window), 49 of 51 checks. It is not from the Mac's sidebar, header, table or sheet work of that day: the day's first commit (`a0600e637`) fails the same way. Bisected with one build and one smoke a step: 97 of 97 at `94b6d995e` (2026-10-06); from `b0d2f145e` (2026-10-07) the drive stops at `view 123 is hidden or inert` with "a dialog initially focuses the native editor" failing beside it; and the stop at a tap outside the viewport begins in the fit-content series of 2026-10-07, one of `512c01bc8`, `878e6a051`, `c78a9c06b`, `ef64465db`, `42379e21a` (three of them do not build the fixture, so the bisect could not say which). Not looked into further.
- **Kept answers: repeated activation and slow first answers** (2026-10-02, [LLP 1027.005](llp/1027.005-resource-identity-and-request-context.rfc.md) D4/D5). Two issues reproduced during review, neither part of the `with` landing. (1) Consecutive `data_ready()` calls before an asynchronous reply issued tickets 1 and 2: a forced ask bypasses `enqueue`'s identical-request rule. No host calls it twice today (Apple and Linux guard with `data_activated`, wasm activates once per boot), so the fix is small and only for a future host. (2) Kept answers are admitted only for a source unready at boot and then remain visible while its asynchronous reply is pending: on the JS runtime, Fieldnotes's real TypeScript source showed the bake's "Opening your notebook…" until a controlled SQLite reply, and the runner does the same for any source ready at boot. The JS target also keeps nothing for a Rust module loaded after first paint. Whether a consumer needs either closed is follow-up 3 of the RFC.

- **Web motion followers** (2026-10-03). The release-follower lifecycle bug is fixed by a targeted change: post-commit reconciliation cancels a follower as soon as its consumer no longer resolves to that source. `web/motion2` (shelved on expo-build-1000) tried and abandoned compositor followers for CSS-eased sources: reproducing CSS animation semantics in retimed followers kept diverging, so main's per-frame seeking stays for that case. Still owed (the final review, codex sol): the real-plan test's later samples are corrected by the agent clock itself (each `clock` op reconciles timelines on both web targets), so it would stay green if the handed-off per-frame callback stopped running; add a check that lets the page's own `requestAnimationFrame` loop run without agent clock operations between samples.
- **Agent web drives, what the no-GPU fix left** (2026-10-02): `screenshot` settles the GPU before its two frame waits and image wait, not after; the JS target's agent settle does not await the GPU module's first import; `surfaceWork` ignores a pending `settled()`; `fallbackApplied` survives a successful module swap. None is the user-facing hang (fixed); each is a narrower race in the agent's drive.
- **JS dev reload carry, what is left** (2026-10-02): the logic revision hashes the app chunk and `app.module.wasm` only, so a native page module served at a stable URL (`./modules/index.js`) can change without dropping settled answers; a checkpoint is deleted before its page is written, so a retried GET boots fresh silently; the route-stack assertion would pass with router carry removed; a blurred autofocus field can take focus again after a carried reload (plausible); Caltrain's edit-to-frame is 273 ms p50 against the 100 ms budget (it was over before the carry).
- **Web test freshness and prerequisites from the build** (2026-10-02, `web/webtests` b8b93ed5, reverted before landing). One source of truth (`build.mjs --check` running the build's own checks; freshness roots from Cargo's dependency closure) is the right design, but that commit made the check a mandatory preflight that runs Cargo before a game's `.shells` workspace exists, breaking every clean game web build, and it still missed the render binary's and GPU crates' inputs and deleted inputs. `CHROME=''` is still taken as a browser path.
- **Firefox/WebKit carrier fidelity** (2026-10-02, the async `conform-firefox`/`conform-webkit` steps): keys go to an iframe guest before Chrome's browser-owned path and non-button keys take a different route than Chrome's; a wheel moves the pointer first (hover becomes part of the input, so the baselined RealWorld scroll gap is not a pure wheel-delta difference); a root with a `testId` double-counts document scroll; the public session advertises held contacts the carrier refuses; synthetic plans still compile after a launch failure. WebKit runs on a Mac (Linux lacks 36 system libraries and the builders have no sudo): first run 2026-10-02 on the M5 mini (WebKit 26.6, `bunx playwright@1.63.0 install webkit`, Chrome for Testing 153 as the oracle) matched Chrome's state on all six apps, 0 failures against `known-webkit.json` (a button is not focused by a click, native input widths). Real Safari through `safaridriver` stays optional: it needs `safaridriver --enable` once, by an admin.
- **CSS grid on the web, what is left** (2026-10-02, landed with the web program: every grid row lowers on both web targets, the kernel's grid grammar parses through cssparser, and `host/web-js/conformance/grid.contract` holds browser boxes to the kernel's with `--linux`). The final review's leftovers: a slot-bound grid value containing `var()`/`calc()` for placement, `grid-auto-flow` or `justify-items` is applied by the JS page but cleared by the kernel (track lists are checked); an auto-placed named span (`auto / span 2 foo`) lays out as `span 1` natively (Taffy resolves named spans only against an explicit line — declare it in LLP 1001 or patch Taffy); grids that resolve past Taffy's 10,000 tracks (`repeat(auto-fill, 1px)` in a wide box, `10000 / span 2`) are not refused; the JS range check splits serialized CSS on spaces, so an escaped numeric line name (`\31 99999`) is refused there.
- **A covered route column with a width of its own runs past the screen** (2026-10-17, guard-rail evaluation round 2, a grocery app on the iPhone Duo fully open and closed). While Exact's containers take the whole view, a route's sides are covered by the safe area (`routeEdges`), and the kernel adds a cover to the route's padding (`kernel/src/kernel/cover.rs`), which under `content-box` lies outside a width the author wrote: a route column with `width="100%"` beside the Duo's 84-point side strip is 84 points wider than the viewport, and its content runs under the strip; with `.automatic` route scrollers, a grouped list's cards there run past the screen's edge. A route written with insets and no width (`inset=0`), or with its width and `box-sizing="border-box"`, is laid out right; `inset=0` beside a `width="100%"` still overflows. Taking the cover out of the authored size instead was tried and dropped: a cover deliberately grows a box so its authored content keeps its size (an authored tablist over the home indicator), and rewriting the size breaks a ratio and an explicit `flex-basis`. LLP 1123 stage 2's wrapper (D3), which shrinks the containing block and leaves the authored box alone, is the fix.
- **A route column moved down a laptop fold keeps the window's top inset** (2026-10-20, Xcode 27.1, iOS 27.1 24A94425, the Duo simulator turned and folded to 130°, under UIKit's bars). The guidance evaluation's grocery app moves its route column to the lower segment (`top="env(viewport-segment-top 0 1)"`). With `navigationBar="none"` its header paints in the column, but 82 points below the segment's top, the window's top safe area with the device turned (`smoke-duo.mjs`'s `TURNED`), and the column, with a height of its own under `content-box`, grows by those 82 points, past the screen's bottom (one placed by `top` and `bottom` keeps its box and starts its content 82 points down). The host covers a route's top with its controller's top safe area wherever the route sits (`NavigationCoversIOS.routeEdges`), its sides likewise while the containers take the whole view, and its bottom only as far as the route reaches it. Covering each edge only as far as the route reaches it, as the authored tablist's cover does, would fix it. LLP 1123 stage 2 (D3, the entry above) does not fix it as specified: a route's clearance is its controller's view's safe area, and the inset wrapper is the containing block of an absolutely positioned root too, so the column would sit 82 points below the segment it names. Stage 2 needs the same remedy, with the reach measured with no clearance, as D3 measures a scroller's, and segment positions relative to the box a pane is placed in (the wrapper's inner box): `env(viewport-segment-*)` stays the window's (D2), so a pane placed by it inside a cleared route would move down by the clearance too. Meanwhile the foldables recipe keeps scrolling content in place, as UIKit's own list does, and warns that a route moved off an edge is still padded.
- **Form controls, what is left** (2026-10-02, landed: a control in block flow is shrink-to-fit as in Chrome, 960 recorded Chrome cases; Caltrain's station Back button's tap area is its label now, as on the web). Open: a Pressable whose `href` is set or cleared after creation is restyled by the kernel as the other element while the page keeps its first tag (`<button>`/`<a>` is chosen once); an authored `box-sizing` on a checkbox or switch is ignored (forced border-box); `appearance: none` is still measured as the native widget; input types the kernel has no control for (`color`, `number`, `month`, `week`, `hidden`) still lower as text fields while the page makes the real widget; on the JS target the reset's `input[type=…] { appearance: auto }` outranks an authored `.cN { appearance: none }` (specificity), so the author's `none` loses (this predates the change). Caltrain's station search field is 20 characters wide in its card on every host (the text-field rule of 2026-09-30); if it should fill the card, its plan says `width="100%"`.
- **`smoke.mjs web` does not pass on Linux** (2026-10-02, expo-build-1000, Chrome 154 for Testing): scroll limits come from Mac fonts (expected 652, Linux 612), the canvas reference is Mac's, and Chrome there has no working WebGPU adapter even on SwiftShader. Judge the web smoke on a Mac; a Linux variant would need its own references.
- **A real tap on a grouped row's detail button does nothing while UIKit rebuilds the button, and one on its switch whose down and lift reach the app together** (2026-10-19 and 2026-10-20, Xcode 27.1, an iPhone 18 Pro simulator, iOS 27.0). XCTest's automation loads UIKit's accessibility runtime into the app, and in the first seconds after a list appears its loader (`-[UICollectionViewListCellAccessibility _accessibilityLoadAccessibilityInformation]`) marks every list cell's accessories for update twice, about 1.3 s and 2.3 s after the cells are configured; each cell's next layout replaces its detail button with a new one. A touch that went down on the old button lifts with no view (`UITouch.view` nil) and runs no action. In 260 launches of the smoke's grouped fixture driven eight taps each, 9 of 660 logged detail taps were lost, each so; the smoke's first detail tap falls about 2.3 s after its launch. A plain UIKit list (`.detail(displayed: .always, actionHandler:)`, tapped through the same XCTest synthesizer) replaces its button at the same two moments and loses the tap the same way: 2 of 56, exactly the two whose touch spanned a replacement. Separately, a tap on the switch is lost when the window has its down and its lift together, the app's main thread busy until after the finger lifted: the switch takes them as no tap (its long-press recognizer fails). A plain UIKit list does the same: its main thread stalled 300 ms across a real tap, 10 of 10 were lost; across the down alone, 0 of 10. The fixture with a stall begun as the app answers the aim lost 10 of 10. Unstalled, neither lost a first switch tap 0.4 to 1.1 s after the cells were configured, beside a looping Duo smoke or not (the UIKit list 0 of 300, the fixture 0 of 277). Exact draws both as UIKit does. A real tap's reply says `viewGone` when its view had no lift and `downAfterLift` when the window had its down only after the lift (LLP 1080.000 D5), and the touch smoke taps either again when its reply says so and nothing changed. Of the detail button: in 120 launches of the fixture, eight taps each, 20 detail taps said `viewGone`, each with nothing changed, no other tap did, and none was lost silently (three in the last 30 launches, with the check of 2026-10-19, the others with earlier forms of it). Of the switch: with a stall injected on each launch's first tap, the smoke took it again and passed (2026-10-20). Whether the 3 first switch taps lost in 160 launches on 2026-10-19 were such stalls is not known: each came 0.40 s or more after configuration and left the long-press recognizer failed, but no dispatch time was recorded; a lost switch tap's reply now says whether its down came after its lift.
- **UIKit places alerts on a fold where Apple's talk says the other side** (2026-10-18, Xcode 27.1, iOS 27.1 simulator runtime 24A94425, a left-to-right layout). Partially folded like a book, a plain UIKit app's alert moves from the centre of the 951×669 window (`[273.7,241.3 320×152.3]`) to the middle of the left segment (`[67.7,241.3 320×152.3]`), presented there at 127.8° or folded to 127.8° and 90° while showing. Apple's Tech Talk [Strike a pose with adaptive layouts on iPhone Duo](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/tech-talks/111463/) says: "When it's partially folded, like a book, elements like alerts move to the trailing side. This keeps them closer to where they'll appear as the device closes and the experience continues on the outer display." Propped up like a laptop, the alert moves to the bottom segment (`[174.7,630 320×152.3]` in 669×951), where the talk says "The top region works well for content that benefits from visibility at a distance. The same alert can move here to remain easy to find." A page or form sheet moves from `[149,8 653×661]` to the left segment of a book fold (459.5 points wide presented there, 459.3 after folding while showing), its right edge about 12 points into the band, and keeps spanning a laptop fold. Right-to-left was not measured. Look again on a later release; `docs/recipes/foldables.md` keeps a pane on the trailing segment meanwhile.
- **`DueBeforeFrameIOSTests.testADriverOwnedClockAdvancesOnlyWhenTheDriverSaysSo` failed once in the parallel run** (2026-10-18, Xcode 27.1, the iPhone Duo simulator). In a full run of `build.mjs --test --ios`, after the agent's tap on its fixture's button returned, the session had no timer deadline ("XCTUnwrap failed … the press armed the timer"), 4.6 s into the test, where its two siblings, which share the fixture, took 0.4 s each. It passed in the full run before, and alone five times of five. Find whether the tap reached the button, and whether a timer it armed had already fired.
- **The Duo simulator's keyboard can crash the app on a fold** (2026-10-16, Xcode 27.1 with an earlier iOS 27.1 runtime; not seen in seven full Duo smoke runs with its current one, 24A94425, on 2026-10-19). In one of 39 attempted runs of the Duo smoke, `ab3-nosleep-4` (without the 300 and 200 ms waits the smoke then had around each `clock settle`, on a host whose `clock settle` also waited on `KeyboardMotion`, a variant that timing showed changes nothing), duo-lab ended with an uncaught `NSException` thrown in UIKit's keyboard: `-[TUIKeyplaneRow updateKeysForTransitionProgress:]` from `-[UIKeyboardLayoutStar _autoSplit:]` on a display-link callback, while the keyboard-bar leg folded from 90° to the closed panel after its keyboard had been dismissed and checked down. No Exact frame is above UIKit's in the backtrace. If it recurs, record the run here.
- **Android memory after a crypto fling, where it goes** (2026-10-05, Pixel 10 Pro XL, exact2 320 vs Views 196 MB PSS: graphics 200 vs 153, native heap 76 vs 31, Java 19–21 vs 6). `dumpsys gfxinfo`: Skia's GPU cache holds 88 MB of scratch surfaces in ~12,600 entries for exact2 against 62 MB in ~5,900 for Views (37 vs 12 MB purgeable); marking single-draw animated layers non-overlapping (`setHasOverlappingRendering(false)`) did not change it, so it is not the reader's opacity layers; candidates are rounded-rect/path clips (row clips, avatars) needing mask surfaces, the dashed sparkline re-recorded each draw-in frame, and even-odd `RING` paths. Native heap (heapprofd): HWUI's Vulkan pipelines 12.6 MB and textures ~9 MB (more distinct draw kinds than Views'), exact2's own ~16 MB (settled data values 5, plan 2.75, layout leaves 1.7, rows 1.2, shaping ~1.5). The Java heap is not the stream copies (recycling them left it unchanged).
- **Android CPU after `android/cpu2`** (2026-10-05, Pixel 10a, 6 runs a variant against Views in one session; cells are process CPU ms/s, exact / Views). Landed: the text cache's per-paint trim walks only when a budget could be over; a list row holding a scroller (easy's swipe-to-delete) is a kept row while the scroller stays put (every easy paint used to record every row that showed again); symbol pictures take their em square before their commit's layout (no second layout per row built); symbol paths parsed once; a symbol's font size read as one row, not a copied style; image visibility indexes the painted boxes once a paint; the reader (bench patch `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/cpu2-reader.patch`) draws a dashed stroke as its path's segments, and a layer's draw-in dash reads and measures its path once, not every frame (crypto's main thread −25% at 12k). easy fling 1k 499/643, 3k 783/605, 6k 636/560, 12k 683/684, 24k 754/913 (before: 562, 683, 975, 776, 986; the `exact` thread 63–274 from 91–334 ms/s; Mcycles/s now below Views at every speed but 3k). crypto fling 760/714, 836/734, 985/717, 1064/773, 1285/947; crypto rest 713/655. Left: (1) a row costs ~2.8 M instructions on crypto (a big-core probe, `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/crypto/probe`): binding evaluation in the VM (the chart's 48-point `points`, ~8%; decode ~20% of that), Taffy flexbox over five flex containers a row and text measured twice (max-content, then the definite width) after shaping (~20%), apply and its style cascade (~9%), motion's restatement of renewed nodes (~5%), the row's recording (~15%); Views' crypto row is a hand laid-out ViewGroup. (2) RenderThread runs on the little cores for whole segments (200–390 ms/s there against ~240 on the mid cores; Views' rarely does): the main thread's frames are short, so HWUI's hints ask for less; pinning it to the mid and big cores (tried: `fast_cores` by tid) kept it there but at lower clocks, mixed in ms/s, not kept. (3) crypto rest: the tick path is ~45% of the `exact` thread (the source's list copy, the old list's drop, `update_data`'s two passes over 5,000 items, conformance); a source that names the rows it changed would remove most of it (the runner takes `Changed { list, positions }` beside a value and skips the compare and rekey for the rest).
- **Android Canvas host against Views, by thread, on Parley** (2026-10-07, Pixel 10 Pro XL, ms/s exact - Views). crypto 6k: exact thread +255, RenderThread +27..40, hwuiTask +46..80, main -27; 12k: exact +350, RenderThread +29..43, hwuiTask +27, main -74. heavy 6k: exact +174, RenderThread +25, decode level (63 vs ~70 of the Views app's dispatcher and upload threads), main -59; 12k: exact +259, RenderThread +38, main -84. The gap is the exact thread less what the main thread saves; nothing else is worth a tenth of it. Probe shares of that thread at 6k-12k dp/s on Parley: layout 18.6% (48% of it text measuring, 22% shaping new strings, which paint needs anyway), the rebind's bindings 17% (the chart's `points` in the VM 6.3%; its parse at paint is 2.3%, so a parse cache buys little), recording rows 14% (the `svg` 6.4%), apply 7%, other commit effects 11%. RenderThread's excess on crypto: text placed again each frame (drawTextBlob 2.4 times the Views app's: Skia reuses a run's glyph placement only across whole-pixel moves, and the feed moves by fractions) and more path masks made (the software path renderer strokes 1.5 times as many contours; why is not found: the pulse ring and dot as rounded rects instead of four-cubic paths changed nothing). Rows and moved groups on whole pixels in the reader (`~/bench/lists/exact-c2v11/cpu2-snap.patch`) took 9-19 ms/s off RenderThread on both lists but added ~35 ms/s to heavy's main thread at 1k-6k, unexplained, so it is not merged.
- **Android Canvas host, a list row's cost while it scrolls: what the crypto probe shows** (2026-10-07, Pixel 10 Pro XL; `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/probe.sh`, `DP=1` for the bench driver's dp/s). After held spares and tracks with moves, at 6k-24k dp/s the exact thread is per-row work: layout 21% (the list is already the layout root and clean rows are cache hits; a rebound row is laid out whole because its five texts changed, ~7 measure callbacks a text of which ~3 reach the text engine, 45% of layout in measuring; a text whose box cannot change, nowrap in a stretched column, still dirties its ancestors), the rebind's bindings 18% (a third of it the VM building the chart's 48-point `points` string, which the kernel then parses again at paint), recording the row 14% (mostly the `svg`), apply 6.5%. Not tried: a row-local layout when a rebind changes no child order (the list's children change in the same commit, so the root is dirty); `points` as numbers end to end; recording only a rebound row's changed nodes (the recorder keeps a row whole, compared after it is walked). A report inside the window is still O(mounted rows) on both sides (the host sends every row's height each report, ~0.25 ms on a mid core, 80 a second).
- **`lead=1` on the Android reader (a window leading toward the travel) builds and destroys the lead every pass**: `refine_slice` resets the velocity to 0 when it returns, and the next unheld frame's pass (velocity 0) retires the rows the lead built (probe `VEL=1`: 2,093 rows built in 2 s at 24k px/s for 286 entering). Off by default, so no run measured it.
- **Android Canvas host, cold start: the first frame with content is three or four Vulkan pipelines** (easy on a Pixel 10 Pro XL: RenderThread 31-46 ms in `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`, PowerVR compiling shaders, for FillRectOp, FillRRectOp, CircularRRectOp and text; the Views app's first frame is 11 ms). The reader draws a uniform rounded rect (Skia's CircularRRectOp) and one with unequal corners (FillRRectOp) differently: one op for both would drop a pipeline. The benchmark force-stops within 3 s, before HWUI saves its pipeline cache. The engine's boot is 18 ms of which half is the list's 10,000 items (decode 21%, `rekey_full` 27%, shape check 4%), but first content waits for the frame after the boot ends, so a shorter boot moves it only by whole frames.
- **Android GPU canvas in hardware buffers: what is owed** (2026-10-07; the reader's part is `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/cpu2-gpubuffers.patch`, against bench e6e9118). Run on PowerVR (Pixel 10 Pro XL) only. Owed: (1) a run on Mali and Adreno with a screenshot of a shader row: the buffer is rendered by wgpu's Vulkan device and sampled by HWUI's with no queue-family transfer to `VK_QUEUE_FAMILY_FOREIGN_EXT` and no fence, only wgpu's word that the submission completed, which PowerVR accepts; `gpubuf=0` is the window path. (2) The reader's fallback when buffers are refused (`canvasFrame` -2: it swaps the element's view for a TextureView) has never run. (3) A frame is published at the exact thread's next frame after its GPU work completes and drawn at the reader's next: up to a frame later than the window path showed it. (4) What it bought is small (Extra Heavy 1k 386 -> 362 mW, 3k 393 -> 379): with canvases off entirely Extra Heavy is ~340 mW at 1k, so most of a canvas's cost is its own render and `vkQueueSubmit` (a third of the exact thread while one shows; PowerVR's `RGXKickTA` and fence merges in the call). The lever left is not submitting from the exact thread at all: the shader in the reader's pass (AGSL from the same source), or a submit thread.
- **`-exact-animation-trigger` on the hosts that do not have it** (2026-10-06, [LLP 1055](llp/1055-svg-shapes-and-css-animations.rfc.md) D13). `view` is the default on Linux and Android. Owed: run it on Apple (the held play reaches its engine through the same `motion_sync`; nobody has looked at a device); build it on the web's JS target (pause a row mounted out of the port, resume on intersection) and the wasm glue (`Kernel::animation_row`, `revealed`); a reveal with nothing else to say is a commit of its own, which crypto's probe shows (+8% of the exact thread's instructions with `view`, most of it the animations now drawn on screen): carry it on the next commit instead if a profile says the commit is the cost.
- **crypto and xheavy energy on the Pixel 10 Pro XL, what was tried** (2026-10-06, `bin/energy.py`, CPU+GPU rails). Fixed: a collection pass took the feed's whole snapshot twice to count the rows it built (+2% of crypto's probe since `android/xblank`). Not the cause or not worth it: (1) crypto's `hwuiTask` threads at 3k-24k (Skia's software path masks, ~250-340 ms/s against Views' ~150-200; ~10 at 1k) are the charts' draw-in: a new path a frame for every visible row. The animation starts at mount, a viewport ahead, so rows show the last half of it (long paths, ~625 us of masks a frame against Views' ~345, which shows the first half) and none of it at 1k. Drawing the drawn part as round-capped `drawLines` removes the masks but puts +180 ms/s on RenderThread and triples CPU power at 3k-6k (315 -> 960 mW): RenderThread time is the expensive kind. Skipping recordings under half a pixel of dash travel cut the masks 14% at 3k with nothing measurable in mW; not kept. (2) `fast=0` (the exact thread on any core) and `boost=0` (no ADPF hint) change neither mW nor frames on crypto. (3) xheavy at 1k: the shader canvas's wgpu submit and present are a third of the exact thread (~80 ms/s) and the GPU rail is ~90 mW against Views' ~58; a canvas out of view already draws nothing (skipping by viewport changed nothing). Drawing a GPU module's canvas in the reader's own pass is still the lever.
- **Android list CPU after `android/cpu2`'s structural pass** (2026-10-06, Pixel 10a; the bench reader's part is `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/cpu2-hwpictures.patch`). Done: StyleProps' 20 rare rows behind one shared pointer (`"rare": true` in schema.json), a decode under way finishing into the cache, JPEG decoded at its shown size by the platform, and platform-decoded pictures decoded straight into the `AHardwareBuffer` the reader wraps (`hardwarePictures`/`imageBuffer`; a heavy fling run made and filled ~1,900 buffers on the exact thread for 106 decodes, because the reader's copy went ten paints after a picture left). Tried and not worth it: a memo of apply's set-style patches (−0.1% of crypto's probe), the SVG cascade's clone (~0.3%), cosmic-text's `shape-run-cache` (−0.6% of heavy's probe: its words rarely repeat within the cache's life). Left, heavy at 24k against Views (~1,150 vs ~810 ms/s): the exact thread and main together (~530 vs Views' main ~290: layout 20% of the exact thread, text measure 12% of it; paint 16%; `sync_images_moved` 11%, of which a third is its own index rebuild at every commit and a third `Images::poll` walking every view), RenderThread (~230–330 vs ~200), and the decode threads (~150–350 vs ~70: the same libjpeg work a picture, plus the bilinear scale to the shown size, 17% of them; Views' loader lets the GPU scale). PNG pictures and canvases still take the copy route. `android/xblank`'s time-budgeted refine costs crypto's and easy's probes +2% (3.462 → 3.536 G instructions on crypto); not looked into.
- **Android list rows after `android/row`, second pass: what was tried and what a rebound row still costs** (2026-10-08, Pixel 10 Pro XL; probes and tools in `~/bench/lists/exact-rowv11`: `rprobe.sh`, `istat.sh`, `batchab.sh`, `prof/shares.py`, `abtable.py`). Landed first: the row layout memo (Taffy patch 29), restated values equal to the style's presenting nothing, a picture's arrival drawing its slot and not its row, the image sync's box index built when asked. Landed second: a collection pass waits for a quarter viewport of travel (`Travel::waits`), so rows mount two or more a commit below 12,000 dp/s: the exact thread, ms/s at 1k/3k/6k/12k/24k, easy 56/98/164/237/262 to 51/88/135/236/260, heavy 63/102/162/244/360 to 59/99/157/234/351 (a waiting pass still asks for the pictures that came into view, which is most of what a heavy pass costs: without that it was 50/85/144 and its placeholders showed for three points more of the view), crypto 96/174/254/351/484 to 69/133/216/340/481. Tried and not landed, each measured on the probe at 12,000 dp/s: (1) **each flex container in a row as its own memo root** (branch `android/row-nested`, 0963be075: a style starts deep, one trace a row, and turns shallow, a trace a container, when under half its boxes replay; traces by style and input, a bound a style): crypto replays 95% of its containers and the instructions are the same (1,955 to 1,946 M), heavy the same, easy +2.5%. Crypto's and heavy's layout time is text measure (shaping 8%, line breaking 6% on crypto), not the flex algorithm (7%), and a replay a container costs about what the flex it saves does. (2) `Engine::remove` by a mask of the properties a node holds, and an allocation's pin count read without its lock (branch `android/row-micro`): 0.3% of easy's instructions, cycles the same. (3) A bump arena for the transaction's scratch: allocation inside `apply_document` is 1.4% of the thread on easy and heavy; the 16% is spread over recording 3.7, the runner 3.4, commit effects 2.5, layout 1.5, no site over 0.6. Left: (a) **recording is 20% of easy's thread** (boxes, symbols and order 16.5, text 3.2). A rebound row whose frames equal the row it was could redraw only its text and symbol leaves in slots, as pictures now are; in easy a rebind keeps its height one time in three or four, so that is about 3.5%.
