- **LLP 1069.001 after slices 2–4** (2026-09-27; select, range and the date types landed): the fixture page under `scripts/fixtures/` and a `smoke.mjs` step (apparatus: a human's word); on Linux, dragging a range's thumb, typing into a date field (only the agent's `type` sets one) and keyboard Escape/arrows for an open select; on iOS the agent's `tap` opens no select menu or date picker (UIKit presents them under a finger; `performPrimaryAction` opened a menu the carrier could neither see nor close); `appearance: base-select` (D6's later step).
- **Streams after LLP 1069.004 slices 1–3** (2026-09-27). Owed: worker-placed TypeScript streams and their sockets' sends (a message there is a turn, and the runner keeps one ticket per target); the trial's person-judged half (Crew's chat through a forced drop, Decided 3); the rustls paths (SSE and the new WebSocket client) on a real Linux box (the Linux smoke ran on the Mac, on Darwin's transports); the public Exact Live job fixture redeployed with `/api/frames`; a Jetstream consumer app (outside the repo) — the public feed was only read by hand. Found: a build's document prerender (the render host) opens a stream answer and bakes its value after ~2 s (weatherlight probe) — decide whether a stream is ever a document's answer.
- **LLP 1069.010 slice 3's remainder** (2026-09-27): the LLP reader (`apps/llp/data`) still reads its corpus with `std::fs` and so still receives raw paths (only apps granted `fs.read doc:/` get `doc:` paths); moving its index to storage requests is owed by D1's "delete, don't deprecate". Under the App Sandbox a host route should mint the file alone, not its folder (D1 as built). A web `save-file` substitute keeps what the app wrote in memory; the driver cannot read it back.
- **LLP 1069.010 D4's cross-window notification** (2026-09-27, owed by slice 2, ruled Q3): Fieldnotes is still one window (`navigate-existing`), so a committed write announcing an LLP 1016.002 topic that the other session's readers re-ask is not built. It lands when Fieldnotes says `navigate-new`; the app owner, which holds both sessions, carries it.
- **The app's root font size, what #102 left** (2026-10-07, LLP 1069.000 D3 as built): a way to have the stored size at the first frame instead of one commit after a mount task. Wait for an app that asks. (The relative `setRootFontSize("125%")` landed with LLP 1121 stage 2's independent item.)
- **`rem`/`em` where the kernel still takes only `px`** (2026-09-27, LLP 1069.000 D3 as built): inside `calc()` and `env()`, `box-shadow`, `transform`/`translate`/`transform-origin`, `stroke-width` and the SVG number rows; `vw`/`ex`/`ch`. And a boot argument for the root font size, so a non-16 Dynamic Type costs no commit after boot. Wait for an app that writes one of them.
- **LLP 1069.007 after its first slice** (2026-09-27; the slice: the production gate, holds by ticket, `clock settle`'s `reason: "device"`, launch display preferences, the offset across DST). Owed: `--answer <capability>=<value>` fixtures (D4; an auth smoke answers with `type @t` instead); `prefer`'s `page`, `place` and `permission` groups (§5 item 5); `EXACT_DEVICE=real` (D8) beyond the Apple auth sheet and the seeded stream, where LLP 1069.006 built it, and Apple's page facts (#114). Retiring an HTTP-shaped hold with its ticket landed with the auth session. The picker (LLP 1069.002) now holds on every host; no smoke drives it yet.
- **LLP 1069.002 after its first build** (2026-09-27): a visible `input type="file"` presents only on the web (native hosts draw nothing; every consumer hides it); a video's HEVC-to-H.264 export (D6) is not built (only `preferredAssetRepresentationMode` on iOS); `video` does not take an `app:/` source (D7 is `image` only, and on the web only for this page's picks); a picked file's object URL on the web lives as long as the page; the Linux host has no HEIC decoder, so D6 there is a refusal; nothing manual yet on a phone with a real HEIC and a Cinematic video, nor a real NSOpenPanel/PHPicker pick; `apps/picker-fixture` and a `smoke.mjs` case were not added (Fieldnotes' import is the consumer).
- **LLP 1069.006 after its build** (2026-09-27): nobody has pressed the real sheet — the macOS `EXACT_DEVICE=real` drive opened an `ASWebAuthenticationSession` that no request left in 30 s, with no display to see it; a person should run `apps/auth-fixture` (`bun apps/auth-fixture/fixture.mjs`, then the app, Prepare, Sign in, Approve) on macOS and an iPhone, and once with an https callback (`.https`, `webcredentials:`, the AASA) on a signed build. Safari's popup rules are unmeasured (LLP 1069 §7 item 6). A Rust source on the web can't name its callback (`exact_data::auth::callback` needs the page's origin by import). Refresh (D6 step 8) has no consumer yet. The Bluesky port's changes are in the RFC's As built.
- **LLP 1069.005 D1b after its build** (2026-09-27): a Rust source on the web can generate and sign but not keep a key (`keep_key` refuses in the page's wasm, since a JWK in `localStorage` breaks Q3); keeping it means signing through the page's WebCrypto by import. The iOS link's size with `p256` is unmeasured. The host-held (Secure Enclave) key waits for its security review.
- **Switch xheavy's jump to scrollIntoView** (2026-09-27): its iOS probe can use `scrollIntoView` now (`BENCH_START_INDEX` through the command instead of the data source answering from row n).
- **LLP 1070 stage 4 owes** (2026-09-27): F4's report order (outer owed, inner owed, then leads) and F5's report-before-commit for a newly created inner port (the runner's bootstrap covers the outer port instead); a real-trackpad check of the macOS latch; xheavy's `innerfling` scenario against stage 0, now that `~/bench/xheavy/exact` can fill its filmstrip and inbox hooks with real nested lists.
- **LLP 1070 Q4: feel-test iOS edge chaining with real fingers** (2026-09-27, ruled provisionally: "lt's try your rec and see how it feels"). On an iPhone and the iPad, a drag that starts at an inner list's edge outward under `overscroll-behavior: auto` goes to the outer list with no inner rubber band; `contain` keeps the band. Try both on a strip and an inbox in a feed; if `auto` feels wrong, Charlie reverses it.
- **LLP 1070 Q3 (b): an option to anchor every size change on a horizontal list** (2026-09-27, Charlie: "(a) is ok for now, but we might want to allow an option for (b) at some point"). Row lists anchor only an estimate's first measurement; a re-measured card left of view shifts the strip, as Chrome does on the inline axis. (b) would hold it still, a declared deviation (LLP 1001). Wait for a consumer that sees the shift.
- **After LLP 1067.000 (Accepted 2026-09-27, one native module per app)**: the real microphone on Apple driven by a person (`EXACT_APP_DIR=apps/recorder bun host/apple/build.mjs --run`); grnl's move to `modules/apple` (the steps are in the RFC's "Moving an app to this shape").
- **No exact2 check touches the real Keychain** (2026-09-27, apple tests). `exact-apple`'s `fresh_preparation_reads_platform_secrets…` now stands in a shared memory store for the platform's (`store::PLATFORM`), because the login keychain is locked in a non-interactive session ("User interaction is not allowed"). The Keychain itself is `vendor/ibex/crates/ibex2`'s `secrets::darwin::tests::the_keychain_round_trips`, which nothing here runs (vendor is excluded from the workspace) and which needs an unlocked login keychain too. A hermetic real-Keychain test needs the shim (`darwin_keychain.mm`) to take a keychain (`kSecUseKeychain` on a temporary one the test creates and deletes); changing the user's default keychain would reach every other process.
- **Presence parity on iOS** (2026-09-30): resize parity is fixed on web/macOS/Linux (`issues/closed/20260927-presence-resize-diverges.md`). Sweep the shared 17-step timeline on iOS when its simulator supports the resize step. Linux's immediate exit removal is the declared LLP 1063 D8 limitation.
- **`ui-monospace` on Apple is not Chrome's SF Mono** (2026-09-27, `feat/backdrop-pre`, LLP 1053 §0.1). At 13 px CoreText's `monospacedSystemFont` advances 8.04 px a glyph where Chrome's `ui-monospace` advances 7.83, and its line box is 16 px against Chrome's 15 (`scripts/fixtures/pre.contract`'s code card). Every monospace string is 2.6% wider on Apple, not only a tab; find which SF Mono instance or tracking Chrome uses. The feed's code row declares Space Mono and is unaffected.
- **`backdrop-filter` owes** (2026-09-27, LLP 1053.000 §3). iOS: `.light` whatever σ (45–59/255 off on bare blurs); only a private layer does better. macOS: an authored `background-color` under a `backgroundMaterial` is hidden by the material's view.
- **Lower a box's transitions of `translate`, `scale`, `rotate` and `background-color` to Core Animation on iOS** (2026-09-27, LLP 1055.001 §3). Keyframes lower; a transition is still a running engine curve sampled per frame, so it wakes the display link while it runs. Needs the engine to keep a lowered curve's start and end without sampling it, a `CABasicAnimation` (or the spring's frames, as the web's `Element.animate` takes) per property, and the same eligibility.
- **Animated images: what LLP 1011.000 §4 leaves** (2026-09-27). Linux decodes no GIF or WebP at all; animated PNG, AVIF and HEICS paint their first frame on Apple; a player's frames (up to 4 MiB, else two) are outside the raster budget and never drop resolution under pressure.
- **Watch: agents writing `width`/`height` on a `canvas` meaning its bitmap** (2026-09-27, Charlie's ruling in LLP 1056 §10). Contract spells the bitmap size `bitmap-width`/`bitmap-height` because `width`/`height` are the box's CSS size. If agents or people stumble on it, make Contract name the mistake and revisit the web's spelling.
- **What perf/value16 left** (2026-09-27, the 16-byte `Value`: `Str` over arcstr, `Items` over triomphe's `ThinArc`). M1 iPad Pro, four interleaved rounds against origin/main ea4526fb (`results/v16-r1` in both benches; a few launches failed, three or four runs each). Heavy list, BENCH_REST=10: footprint at the first frame 40.5 -> 35.0 MB, fling peak 122.7 -> 111.9, fling end 93.4 -> 89.7, 10 s later 62.4 -> 52.7; ladder end 88.1 -> 80.5, 10 s later 55.5 -> 51.5; fps, busy/frame and CPU unchanged. Crypto list (`exact-cm`): rest 33.2 -> 29.8, fling peak 43.3 -> 40.5, live fling peak 42.9 -> 41.4, cold start 30.0 -> 29.6. With map/join (`exact-lmap`): rest 31.8 -> 28.2, fling peak 43.4 -> 39.7, live fling peak 43.7 -> 40.1, cold start 31.4 -> 28.2: now below exact-cm, as LLP 1017.003 predicted. Mac heap (a counting allocator over the baked value): heavy 21.1 -> 16.1 MB, lmap 8.5 -> 6.0, cm 5.4 -> 4.9; a thin `Rc` (slice-dst) came to 18.8 on the heavy list (its weak count, rounded to 16 bytes). Decode, boot, scroll and live ticks measured the same on the Mac, and so did the two crates with plain counts (atomics cost nothing measurable); the VM's map/join over 48 points is about 12% faster. Owed: (1) Caltrain's web core is 327.86 KiB brotli-11 on 23a7e269, over its 303 ceiling before this change (fix/web-core-size owns that); with this change it is 327.78 (+0.24 on ea4526fb, +0.04 on a5e74ec7: within brotli's noise). (2) Neither pointer has weak references: tests that watched a release through `Weak` now hold the value and read its count. (3) Apps outside the repo that build `Rc<[Value]>` or `Rc<str>` for a `Value` (the bench ports, Weird Castle) need `Items`/`Str` or `Value::record`/`Value::str`.
- **LLP 1056 (Canvas 2D) stage 1 owes** (2026-09-27, `feat/canvas2d-stage1`). (1) ABI 2's byte result: a TypeScript draw's lists cross the seam as JSON with base64 (`exact_canvas::seam`), and the Apple batch carries them as base64 too, where D1 and D7 ask for bytes and a typed buffer Rust owns until the batch is released; measure in stage 3 before building. (2) A worker-placed module and a replaced Rust module (`Swappable` with no embedded crate) report that they do not draw; the stale-reply path is built and tested in the runner but no host exercises it, so §4's worker sequence is not in the gallery. (3) The web module realm draws synchronously in the turn (a draw awaits nothing), not a deferred turn later as D4 expected; the first frame after activation is drawn. (4) Frames are drawn whether or not a canvas is on screen (`draw_canvases(&|_| true)` on every host); D4's on-screen rule needs each host's visibility. (5) and (6), `currentColor` and the wide colour forms, landed in stage 2. (7) The TypeScript recorder is a hand port held to the Rust one by `canvas/tests/cases.rs` (records compared to 1e-9), not generated from a rule table as D3 says. (8) landed in stage 2 (`Path2D` is built). (9) The runner's Canvas 2D code is linked into every app's wasm, not only a plan's with a 2D surface (LLP 1047); measure its bytes and move it behind a link if they count. (10) `smoke.mjs canvas` needs the gallery prebuilt natively (`cargo build --release -p canvas-gallery-linux`, `EXACT_APP_DIR=apps/canvas-gallery bun host/apple/build.mjs` and `--ios`; rebuild all three after a change to a replayer); its web builds go to `target/web-dist/<app>` and are built when missing (`EXACT_CANVAS_REBUILD=1` rebuilds). (11) `scripts/svgparity.mjs` keeps its copy of the comparator; it becomes a caller of `scripts/parity.mjs` once the SVG stages 8–10 lane lands. (12) A dev reload of a TypeScript module that draws is untested (§4's reload sequence).
- **LLP 1056 (Canvas 2D) stage 2 owes** (2026-09-27, `feat/canvas2d-stage2`, §8.2). (1) Apple text is held to a declared band (mean 8, 12%; Charlie's ruling, §8.2) because Skia's glyph masks carry less ink than a Core Graphics stroke of the same outlines; revisit if a consumer needs `strokeText` to match Chrome closer. (2) A Rust data crate drawing text on the web measures with `Estimate`: the wasm runner has no web text engine (an import calling the page's measure). (3) Canvas images use a cache of their own on every host, outside LLP 1010 §6's budget; Apple's is never evicted, Linux decodes PNG assets only and synchronously. (4) Apple ignores `fontStretch` and `textRendering` and draws oblique as italic; Linux draws colour glyphs as outlines. (5) A shadow under a clip-extent operator is not held to Chrome on Linux. (6) The `"font"` redraw is wired on the web but no fixture loads a face late. (7) `smoke.mjs canvas` captures the web at 1×, so the web's device-scale shadow path is proven only by review. (8) `EXACT_ABI_VERSION` stayed 10 though `exact_set_canvas_text` and `exact_canvas_image` were added (stage 1 added `exact_canvas_display` the same way).
- **`smoke.mjs svg` needs its fixture app prebuilt on every host** (2026-09-27). On a fresh worktree it stops at the first missing artifact: the gallery's web dist, then `cargo build --release -p svg-gallery-linux`, then `EXACT_APP_DIR=apps/svg-gallery bun host/apple/build.mjs` and `--ios`. The smoke could build what it needs, as the other smokes' callers do, or print all four commands at once.
- **What is left of the crypto SVG list's memory against SwiftUI on iOS** (2026-09-27, perf/crypto-mem3, M1 iPad Pro, three interleaved rounds against origin/main ef137e76 and SwiftUI, six for live fling and nine for rest, `results/cm3-r1`). perf/crypto-mem3 holds cold shaped text to two screens and keeps answers (metrics, line breaks, baselines) for what it lets go, drops a settled motion slot's presentation, moves a `NodeView`'s rarely set fields to a lazily made object, and gives the text residency's tables back at rest: fling peak 44.3 -> 40.3 MB (SwiftUI 33.0), live fling peak 44.4 -> 42.4 (33.1), rest 30.4 -> 30.9 (22.7; that is free-page noise: malloc in use at rest is 19.9 -> 19.5 MB in every run, `results/cm3-restvm`, and a four-run footprint recheck read 30.4 -> 30.35); fps, late frames, CPU at every ladder speed, jumps, blanks, cold start and pixels unchanged. What is left: (1) the port's data, about 9.3 MB at 5,000 coins (the entry below). (2) Text answers keep their `TextIdentity` alive (a `Spec` with a strut `Run`, the run array, a token: about 0.6 KB of the 1.2 KB an answer costs, Mac heap): a lean identity (the metric fields and UTF-8 only) would halve what a fling leaves; `fitShaped` is only wired on iOS (macOS has no rest trim). (3) Taffy's node store keeps its peak capacity: its slot map's keys are the kernel's node ids, so it cannot be compacted without renumbering every node, and rebuilding it drops every cached measurement; not attempted. (4) `NodeView` on macOS keeps its rarely set fields inline (the iOS split in `NodeExtrasIOS.swift` would carry over). (5) The collection index about 1.1 MB at 5,000 items: sharing the item's `Rc<str>` needs a key type of a tag plus the string through `HeightIndex`, the anchor, `mounted` and the snapshot JSON the hosts read, for about 30 bytes an item (0.15 MB here, 0.3 MB on the heavy list); not built. (6) The 16-byte `Value`: landed (perf/value16, below). Malloc's dirty-but-free pages swing 3-6 MB between runs, so rest and live-fling footprints need three or more rounds.
- **The expression VM costs about 6 ns an instruction** (2026-09-27, feat/contract-listmap). A crypto row's `points` is about 60 instructions and four string allocations a point: 23 us for 48 points, five times Rust's `format!`. Each instruction is decoded from bytes through the operand table (`vm::decode`), and a template's `+` allocates per part. A pre-decoded instruction array per code body, or an n-ary concatenation for templates, are the levers; measure on the crypto fling (row mounts) before building either.
- **Recoverable expression traps** (2026-09-27, LLP 1017.003 D3; Charlie agreed). A binding trap at run time (`IterationLimit`, `StringTooLong`, `ValueTooLarge`, `TypeMismatch`) poisons the runner, so the app stops updating. `map`/`filter` make data-dependent traps reachable: a view fine at 500 items can trap at 50,000 in production. Proposal: a trapping binding keeps its last good value (or its initial one), the journal and the agent's `state` report the trap with its pc and binding, and the rest of the tree keeps updating. Transaction work for every trap, not a map/filter change; not built in feat/contract-listmap. Tried and parked on 2026-10-22 (branch `q24/recoverable-traps` on expo-build-450a, four commits, four review rounds, 98 conformance checkpoints agreeing on wasm, JS and Linux in `host/web-js/conformance/binding-traps`). It recovers ordinary node bindings (props, styles, text, attributes, in rows too) of the four kinds on both executors: last good value, unset/empty on a later first mount, boot unchanged, one journal line `node N binding B: Trap(…); keeping last value`, `state.bindingTraps` with view, node, binding, kind and pc, cleared on success or unmount; collection creation reads `scrollFollowEnd` and `scroll-behavior` from the retained binding; JS aggregate readers (`liveTime`, `mo`, `mh`) take a default per trapping getter; LLP 1090 D6 and 1017.003 amended. Left when it parked, raised by both round-4 reviewers: when one update both starts a trap on an already-mounted view's binding and then poisons structurally, `host/web-js/rt.js`'s failed-update cleanup deletes that new record (its key was absent before the update) while the runner keeps it (`retain_binding_traps` keeps a trap whose view is in the kernel), so `state.bindingTraps` differs. The fix the reviewers gave: snapshot the view ids before `flush`, and on failure delete only records whose view was not mounted before; assert the record's node and binding, not empty keys, in js-runtime.test.mjs. Out of that lane either way: structural recovery (list subjects, row keys and initializers, conditional mounts, collection preview state, derives; a virtualized list's width, max-width, height, max-height and display at creation).
- **Watch: `sortBy`'s two spellings** (2026-10-17, LLP 1116.016 §5). Its direction is a literal, so a direction view state picks is a ternary of two calls (the euxy port's Patterns sort); and it is a function, as `sum` is, so `xs.filter(…).sortBy(…)` is refused for `sortBy(xs.filter(…), …)` (this lane's own corpus case was written the method way first). Record whether builders hit either, and whether a `"asc" | "desc"` value for the direction is worth its rule.
- **Watch: agents writing JS list idioms in Contract** (2026-09-27, LLP 1017.003 §Diagnostics). `xs.map(f)`, `xs.length`, `reduce`/`find`/`sort`/`slice`, `Math.min(...xs)`, `toFixed`, `map` as a view line or a callback returning a node, and truthy `filter` callbacks are each refused with the spelling that works (`contract_syntax::idioms`). Record, as with the canvas bitmap-width watch, whether agents building apps trip on these, which message got them out, and any idiom the table misses.
- **Text pixels on iOS without app-side bitmaps: measured, not built** (2026-09-27, perf/crypto-mem, M1 iPad Pro, 240 crypto-row labels drawn each way by a scratch probe). No public path draws a label's glyphs in the render server: `UILabel` and `CATextLayer` paint into in-process backing stores the size of the label (footprint +3.8 and +5.4 MB, against 1.9 MB of malloc for exact2's ink-cropped CGImages); both tint a private alpha image (`CATintedImage`) for single-colour text. Glyph paths in `CAShapeLayer`s are rasterized by the render server but hold 1.8 MB of paths and differ from CoreText (mean 4.5/255). An alpha-only (A8) raster is 3.5x smaller (2.56 -> 0.73 MB of pixels) and within one level of the BGRA one, but Core Animation shows A8 `contents` as white, so any other colour needs a `mask` layer, and 960 moving masked labels cost the render server 3.3x the CPU of plain contents (+115 against +35 samples/s over idle, all-process Time Profiler): not built. What would pay without a mask: white text (the crypto list's letter and change badge, about 0.4 MB at rest) as plain A8 contents, which matched the BGRA pixels exactly; it leans on undocumented Core Animation behaviour.
- **An `svg` scene still crosses as generic JSON** (2026-09-26, perf/crypto-svg, live-fling Time Profiler on the iPad): `BatchReader.value()` plus `BatchValue.any` (every path number boxed as an NSNumber, then unboxed by `nums`) is about 5% of main-thread time while a list of sparklines scrolls. A typed scene reader goes with feat/svg-complete's scene format, not before it.
- **What a live tick costs on the crypto list** (2026-09-26, perf/crypto-svg): per 100 ms tick on the iPad about 1 ms in the port's data source (`format!` per chart point, on main; worker placement, LLP 1027.002, would move it), about 1 ms of layout from the root with text measurement callbacks (the layout lane), and presenting the changed rows (a keyed flash is a text view created and destroyed per tick). Late frames on a live fling 181 -> 111 after the collection and scene changes (SwiftUI 292).
- **LLP 1057.001 (gesture precedence, pinch) owes** (2026-09-27, branch `gestures-phase-0-1`). iOS: rule 4's tie order between recognizers on one view is UIKit's; the pan + pinch pair, the failure requirement between nested drags and the press boundary are unverified with real fingers (no Simulator.app here; unit tests set recognizer phases; the agent's iOS pinch is `recognized` and the smoke's precedence case answers `unsupported`). Apple Arrange and Linux keep their own release velocity (UIKit's or a contact tracker). macOS smart-magnify and rotate are not wired. The smoke's precedence case (step 11b, `contract/corpus/precedence.contract`) was approved as apparatus by Charlie (2026-09-27).
- **120 Hz measured on hardware** (2026-09-26, LLP 1061 D4). Motion that moves things asks a ProMotion iPhone for its full rate and paint-only motion for 60 Hz, set but unmeasured on hardware (`perf frames` gives the sampler's target period and its late frames, and Save Trace keeps them, LLP 1079); the Mac's display link never asks for a frame-rate range.
- **The Bluesky port could use `contract rust`** (LLP 1054.000 R4): its `Session`, `Change` and `Loads` builders by position are what the generator replaces.

- **GPU canvases: what is left of an animated shader row's CPU** (LLP 1009 D7 and §3; re-traced 2026-09-30 with one Metal command buffer a canvas frame). At rest on an iPad Pro M1 the 19-kind feed's one animated shader row costs the main thread about 55 ms/s under the profiler (the starved pass: wgpu-core's `finish` 21, its submit 11, the render call 6; the tick 8) where UIKit's `MTKView` draw costs 35, and process CPU 245 ms/s against UIKit's 172 without the profiler (main 102 against 60). What is left is wgpu-core's bookkeeping for a pass and a submit, on the main thread, and a starved canvas's two passes a frame — D8 (the module on a thread of its own) is the design for the main thread; process CPU stays about 1.4× UIKit's for one canvas at 120 Hz with wgpu-core encoding the pass. In a fling the acquiring threads' `nextDrawable` is mostly new IOSurfaces for rows that scroll in (next line). Weird Castle's surface migrates by the patch kept with the lane (`~/bench/xheavy/gpusubmit/weird-castle-d7.patch`).
- **Upstream Naga's unnecessary f16 uniform-storage capability** (2026-10-17, `vendor/wgpu-hal/EXACT-PATCHES.md`, patch 11). Naga 30 emits both 16-bit buffer capabilities for function-local half arithmetic. Declare only capabilities the SPIR-V uses, then separate native arithmetic/storage from uniform-buffer support without weakening WebGPU's `shader-f16` guarantee. Exact's Android-only instance opt-in and uniform rejection keep Adreno usable in the meantime.
- **Upstream ibex2's queued-message count** (`vendor/ibex/EXACT-PATCHES.md`, "Socket queued-message count", 2026-10-15): `MessageSender::buffered_messages`, which LLP 1016.000 D7's publication uses to refuse a batch whole before the transport's 256-message limit; propose it to expo/ibex and drop the local patch when it lands.
- **Upstream wgpu-hal's lazy Metal command buffer** (`vendor/wgpu-hal/EXACT-PATCHES.md` patch 1, 2026-09-30): an encoding that encoded nothing makes and commits no `MTLCommandBuffer`. It changes nothing a caller can see and removes two or three committed buffers per render pass for every wgpu user on Metal; propose it to gfx-rs/wgpu against `wgpu-hal/src/metal/{command,mod}.rs`, and drop the local copy of that patch when a release carries it. Patch 2 (a frame's presentations riding its submit) would need an option on the surface configuration upstream and is not proposed yet.
- **A video's measured creation cost leaves out its presentation** (2026-09-28, LLP 1068 §5.1). `HeavyLeaves.make` times `VideoView(owner:)`, the player alone; the `AVPlayerViewController` (or layer view) is made in the first `update`, and AVKit's window entry happens at the next commit, so a video under AVKit is rarely measured over the frame and rarely held in a real fling. Time the first update with it. The probe's scripted scroll never holds either (neither drag nor deceleration); `coast` does.
- **What exact2 holds at rest on the Extra Heavy feed beyond SwiftUI** (2026-09-28, perf/xheavy-mem; iOS simulator `heap`/`malloc_history` at rest with BENCH_KINDS=video, then BENCH_VM device runs; `~/bench/xheavy/results/memory`). The gap is malloc (iPad 36 against 21.5 MB in use at the end of a fling; every other VM tag within 1 MB), and at rest it is: (1) the port's data: `xheavy-data` keeps its parsed `Raw` rows (6.7 MB) beside a `Value` for every row of the 19-kind feed (6.75 MB) and the video feed built from them (1 MB), where SwiftUI keeps two `[Row]` arrays (5.9 MB); (2) the port's first frame: it shows the baked 19-kind `initial` for 150 ms before `jump`, so the video feed mounts a shader canvas whose module keeps a 4.1 MB decoded photo and a wgpu device, and launch peaks at 81-83 MB against SwiftUI's 38 (the iPhone's footprint at the fling's start, 66-69 MB, is that launch not yet returned); SwiftUI filters before its first frame; (3) short text: built on perf/inline-str (Charlie, 2026-09-28, the safe inline variant, LLP 1017.003): video feed start/peak/end iPad 40.6/53.1/51.8 -> 35.7/48.8/47.5 MB, iPhone 71.0/76.7/46.1 -> 65.5/71.1/41.2, launch peak 86.7 -> 80.4 and 84.8 -> 74.5; (4) fonts: cut on perf/xheavy-mem (a bundled face's descriptor from its file, 1.9 MB; iPad video feed start/peak/end 40.0/52.9/51.4 -> 38.1/50.9/49.0 MB, iPhone 69.0/74.4/44.1 -> 65.6/71.3/41.3). (1) and (2) are the port's (`~/bench/xheavy/exact-kinds`), not exact2's; matching SwiftUI's app there is a port change.
- **MapKit draws each visible map on the main thread at twice SwiftUI's cost** (2026-09-27, LLP 1068 §0.3, xheavy on the M1 iPad Pro, 12k–24k pt/s): `renderSceneSync` 78–85 ms/s against SwiftUI's 42 with the same maps in view; reuse does not change it. Next: count live `MKMapView`s against SwiftUI's at the same offset (retained rows past the window may keep maps drawing) and check whether a map scrolled out of view but still mounted keeps its display link.
- **LLP 1055 / 1055.000 (SVG, completed in stages) owes** (stages 1–7 on main; 8, 9, 10b and 10c built on `feat/svg-stages-8-10`, §0 of 1055.000 records each): stage 10a (`image` inside SVG: each host's image pipeline for an element with no view) and stage 11 (`textPath`, and SMIL as ruled: native on the web on the agent clock, the lowered subset on native hosts); from stages 8–10, `feImage`, `width`/`height` and `d` animation on SVG elements, an animation inside an island sampled on Apple (it shows the model value), stripping `libexact_svg.dylib` (Xcode 27's strip breaks it; unstripped it is 487 KB), snapping Apple's filter islands to device pixels, Core Animation's blend colour space on macOS, and `mix-blend-mode` on boxes; animation events (D18); from stage 5, per-character `x`/`y`/`dx`/`dy` lists, `rotate`, `textLength`, and stroked or gradient text on Linux; from stage 6, hover and the other handlers on native hosts, `title`/`desc` and per-element accessibility, `a` inside SVG, text hit testing, the non-default `pointer-events` values on Apple, and an agent address for an SVG element (its tap goes through the `svg`'s centre); from stage 7, `context-fill`/`context-stroke` and `refX`/`refY` keywords; basic shapes and `shape-rendering` on SVG elements; `reflect`/`repeat` of a radial gradient with a moving focus on Apple (it pads); a change inside a referenced definition in another `svg` (a sprite) re-renders the referencing scene on native hosts, and a reference whose target's `id` changes re-emits on the web; SVG 2's `pathLength="0"` rule (an infinite scale), pinned against Chrome; percentages on `stroke-width` and dashes; the macOS agent's default screenshot shows model layers, so a paused or running CA animation needs `screenshot … window`. The parity smoke is `bun scripts/smoke.mjs svg` over `apps/svg-gallery`.
- **`transform` on HTML boxes** (LLP 1055.000 D5, §8 ruling 3): deferred until a consumer takes it. The row-equivalent subset landed (2026-10-19, the Brooks shop-app lane's `translate(0px, 25px) scale(0.8)`): a value that is `translate`, `rotate` and `scale`, each at most once, in CSS's individual-property order, lowers to those rows wherever the compiler sees the literal (inline, a class, each arm of a choice). The rest stays deferred and refused: `matrix`, skews, a per-axis or 3D scale, 3D functions, a function repeated or out of order, a bound string. Admitting it requires composition with the individual properties, hit testing and layout overflow on every host.
- **What is left of the heavy list's off-main CPU and late frames on iOS** (2026-09-26, perf/offmain, M1 iPad Pro Time Profiler and System Trace). Off-main is image decode (about two thirds at 6k pt/s and up) and worker text; inside a decode, ImageIO's own thumbnail (hardware JPEG decode, then a high-quality resample from full size) is nearly all of it, so what is left to cut is decode count, not decode cost. Four pieces: (1) a fifth of decodes still draw into a copy because the plan rounds a side up (`RasterDecodePlan.scaled`) where ImageIO rounds down, so the thumbnail is one pixel short and gets stretched; agreeing on rounding drops the draw but moves those images' pixels by that stretch. (2) Core Animation no longer copies image or text layer contents at commit (perf/memory: every raster is Core Graphics' own image, BGRA, 64-byte rows; a device Allocations trace shows no `create_image_by_copying` left). (3) The text-pixel cache (`TextRasterizer.kept`) is iOS only; the AppKit rasterizer has the same repeated labels. (4) With off-main load lower, the main thread at ±12k/24k pt/s spends more CPU-ms for the same rows (more samples on E cores, fewer P-core cycles per ms); total CPU still falls. The one late frame left per ladder run at the +12k start is the probe's segment checkpoint (6-12 ms of JSON written on main in `-[LBProbe emit:]`) plus rows the next frame needs; the harness could write it off main.

- **What is left of the heavy list's memory on iOS** (2026-09-27, perf/floor after perf/restmem, M1 iPad Pro 12.9", three interleaved rounds against origin/main 1c1802af and SwiftUI, BENCH_REST=10). Shared decoded objects cut the baked 10,000 messages' values 23 -> 18.8 MB (193k -> 139k objects), and the rest trim now also runs after launch (freed ImageIO blocks of the first decodes return). Fling end 103.7 -> 97.1 MB (SwiftUI 83.1), 10 s later 75.1 -> 67.9 (66.1); ladder end 121.0 -> 122.5 (72.3), 10 s later 66.0 -> 62.8 (57.6); footprint at the first frame 44.4 -> 40.0; cold start unchanged (157 vs 156.5 ms, six pairs). perf/crypto-mem3 (five rounds against origin/main ef137e76): ladder end 112.4 -> 87.0 MB, 10 s later 56.0 -> 52.2; fling end 94.0 -> 88.7, 10 s later 66.7 -> 63.1; fling peak unchanged (117.9 vs 117.8), late frames and CPU unchanged at every ladder speed. The footprint at the last frame still loses: cold shaped text (now two screens plus answers), freed ImageIO blocks that `malloc_zone_pressure_relief` does not return (16-19 MB, back about ten seconds later), CG image data, and the plan's values (records 14 MB, strings 4.9 MB; the 16-byte `Value` took 5 MB of the baked values, perf/value16). Still open: purgeable backing for cold images, the same rest trim on macOS (only the iOS scroll pump arms it).
- **`adopt` covers the compiled value only** (2026-09-26, perf/adopt, LLP 1027 D11). A value carried across a reload, a kept store-reader answer or a document's seed is not adopted, and the TypeScript executor and a worker-placed source ignore it (their values cross as copies). A source that edits what it answers still rebuilds its data after a dev reload (the heavy-list port parses its JSON on first edit then). Extend it when a consumer shows the cost. The heavy-list port also still bundles its 7 MB messages.json into the device binary for the bake's sake; untouched, those pages are clean and not charged to the footprint.
- **Weird Castle does not build against exact2 main** (2026-09-26, found checking perf/adopt against it). `weird-castle-data` matches `Outcome` without `Storage` and `Surface` (data/src/lib.rs, `parse_login` and `parse_switch`), and `weird-castle`'s src/lib.rs calls `Runner::boot` with 3 arguments (it takes 5 now: the viewport and the launch URL). Neither is from `adopt`; with the two matches given a fallback in a scratch copy, the data crate checks clean.
- **What is left of a slow heavy-list scroll on iOS** (2026-09-26, perf/slow-frame, Time Profiler on the M1 iPad Pro trace and the iPad Pro 13" simulator). At 1k pt/s, after the focus-search, text-scan, timer and travel-report fixes, the main thread is mostly building rows: `Host::commit` layout and `create_row` in Rust, `Presenter.apply` in Swift, and the CA commit. Three pieces are separable. An ellipsized paragraph (`text-overflow`) never rasters on a worker, so each new row's author name is shaped and drawn on main in `NodeView.draw` (`paragraphLayout`, ~2% of main on the iPad); a worker raster that truncates would take it off. Every commit re-serializes every mounted row's snapshot (`collections_json`) and Swift re-parses it (`applySnapshots`, `CollectionSnapshot.init`), ~3%, including the measurement-only report that follows each built row. And each report still carries every mounted row's height (`CollectionHost.height` walks each row's ancestors for `hidden`), which the runner now ignores when unchanged but the host still gathers and encodes.
- **A hardware keyboard on a headless simulator** (2026-09-26). This Xcode has no Simulator.app, so a simulator has no hardware keyboard and UIKit's focus system never starts. One HID keyboard event from SimulatorKit's `SimDeviceLegacyHIDClient` (`IndigoHIDMessageForKeyboardArbitrary`) attaches one for the rest of the boot, which is how perf/slow-frame reproduced the iPad's focus-search cost; the keys themselves did not reach the app, so a real Tab walk still needs a device or Simulator.app.

- **What is left of a heavy-list live tick** (2026-09-26, perf/live-ticks, `sample` on the iOS simulator in live mode). Keying and type-checking now follow what changed (the runner's share fell from about 17 to 1.3 ms a tick), and the timer callback is about 2.4 ms: the runner about 1 ms, of which `SumTree::new` reallocating and zeroing its 2×16,384-slot buffers on every splice is about 0.2 ms (reuse them when the base size holds, keeping the splice transactional); the host's commit and layout about 0.3 ms; and the Swift side (`ExactSession.apply`, `Batch.decode`) about 0.8 ms. A same-length reorder still keys, by position, every row between the moved ones.

- **iOS boxes on the pixel grid, what is owed** (2026-10-17, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/PixelGridIOS.swift`). Every node view and flat leaf is drawn with its absolute edges rounded half up to device pixels, as Chromium's `PixelSnappedIntRect` rounds them; text wraps and native editors and collections measure at layout's size (`layoutBounds`). Typetour's first screen at 3x has 20% fewer horizontal edges blended across two pixel rows (21,118 to 16,943); Shelf's first screen was on the grid already. Owed: (1) offsets UIKit's own views take, a grouped list's `UICollectionView` and a dialog sheet's scroller, and Exact's other programmatic writers (scroll anchoring, reading-position restore, keyboard avoidance, collection anchoring and its animated offsets), which can leave content a fraction of a pixel off; `writeScrollPosition` and content sizes are on it; (2) border widths: Chromium snaps a computed border width (under one device pixel to one, else down to whole pixels), which belongs in the kernel for every host; (3) what blurs inside a box: a paragraph's lines sit at fractional baselines; (4) a carried grouped-list row keeps its children's places from its authored parent, which can differ from Chromium's by a pixel; (5) the Mac draws where layout falls, as iOS did.

- **iOS's `line-height: normal` is not Chrome's, and no rule makes it so** (2026-09-26, same lane; Chrome is the oracle). Chrome keeps a face's ascent, descent and gap as whole pixels, rounded; macOS now does the same (TextParityMacTests). iOS's SF has other vertical metrics than the Mac's (15.23 + 3.86 at 16 px against 15.47 + 3.38), so Chrome's rule on iOS faces gives 16 at 14 px, not the 17 the scroll fixture's cross-host 652 needs; iOS keeps CoreText's sum rounded up once per paragraph, which is Chrome's height for one line at 12-15 px and 1-2 px taller at 11, 16 and 17 (a 16 px paragraph's lines are 19.09 apart, Chrome's 18 at 1x, 18.67 at 3x). Matching Chrome on iOS would take the Mac's SF metrics as constants, or a fixture that does not pin the Mac's font. For the record, Safari: iOS WebKit rounds each metric up (20 at 16 px, baseline 16; 17 at 13; 22 at 17), macOS WebKit to the nearest (as Chrome); Safari's underline sits `max(1, ceil(size/32))` below the baseline, `size/16` thick; Chrome's and CoreText's follow the font. Chrome renders `ui-monospace` in Times (unsupported).

- **`smoke.mjs web --app <name>` fails for an app with a data module** (2026-09-26, found running Messages for LLP 1047.000). The generic host fixtures reload a bare plan into the app's `EXACT_WEB_LINK=all` build, and `exact.reload` refuses with "module reload requires a paired generation" when the build carries a module. So Messages, Fieldnotes and the other TypeScript apps pass only `--app-only`. The fixtures could run on a build without the app's module, or the smoke could default to `--app-only` for such apps.

- **What LLP 1053's first tranche left of `direction`** (2026-09-25, feat/css-layout; the `aspect-ratio` half landed with LLP 1074, 2026-09-30). Under the initial `direction: ltr`, Apple and Linux take a paragraph's base direction from its first strong character, not ltr. That is because the Linux text-flow walker lays a Hebrew paragraph with a forced LTR base out as one unbreakable fragment: `exact_textflow` should break inside an RTL run in an LTR paragraph, then `shaping.rs` can pass `Some(false)`. G2, G4 and G5 of LLP 1053 §0 remain. The heavy benchmark's exact2 port can drop its viewport-derived photo sizes and per-run `when` font branches.
- **What LLP 1074 left** (2026-09-30, lane/taffy-css). (1) A height `aspect-ratio` derives is a floor, not a definite height: under a block or grid parent a child's `height: 100%` is `auto` where Chrome resolves it against the ratio's height (`min-height: 0` on the box is the way round it). The fix is two passes at the parent: measure the content, and give the box the ratio's height as definite when the content fits. Five cases in `kernel/tests/it/browser_position.rs` (`OWED`) fail the day it lands. (3) An exclusion (`wrap-flow: both`) under a static parent is placed against a box further up and wraps nothing; the compiler could say so (`wrap-flow: both` needs a positioned parent) where today the text is only refused auto height. (4) Apps outside the repo (Weird Castle, Interview) are not swept for `position: static`: the parent of each absolute box, and each box with an inset and no position, needs `position: relative`. (5) The one deviation left, that a clipping box is a containing block, goes only when native hosts paint by stacking context and re-parent a positioned view to its containing block (LLP 1074 §7); `position: fixed` waits on the same. (6) Patches 10, 11, 12, 14, 17, 18 and 20 are marked to upstream and none has been sent. (7) The schema's alignment enums lack `start`/`end`/`self-start`/`self-end`, which Taffy has (LLP 1074 §5).

- **The rest of LLP 1050.000 stage 1** (2026-09-25, perf/fill-hosts). iOS, macOS and web collections now report `velocity` and `limit` and fill in slices; a web authored jump builds its rows before it moves (stage 2), and macOS clamps `prepareContent(in:)` to the built rows (D5). Owed: D5 with real trackpad input (responsive scrolling takes window-server events; checked only by XCTest, in a session that cannot post them); a macOS authored `scrollTop` still reports on the next main-queue turn, not in its batch (whether AppKit can commit a frame between is unmeasured); the anchor rule while moving; pending intervals in the reply; and the cost memo (D3). Linux stays unlimited: it has no fling and its wheel steps are small. Give it a limit when it gains a fling (LLP 1015).

- **iOS scroll frames re-send the list snapshot** (2026-10-05, Signal clone, after `8e452961d`): a `scroll=` handler's commit carries a `collections` op on ~660 of ~1,000 scroll frames a fling, only because the list's `scrollSequence` advanced through the frame's feedback (`host/apple/src/layout.rs` emits the JSON whenever it differs). The runner's `scroll` dispatch is 0.41–0.45 ms an event and the host's snapshot path 0.30–0.32 ms on the simulator, ~0.7 ms a scroll frame; no late frames now. Not emitting a snapshot whose only change is `scrollSequence`, or not serializing collections on a commit that touched none, is LLP 1010/1050 semantics.
- **A chat's open builds its off-screen rows in the opening frame** (2026-10-05, Signal clone): the list's first report builds the window's lead (152 views) in the press's frame. Limiting a list's first report to what shows (`limit: 0` when `lastFacts` is nil, `Collection.swift`), the rest in later slices, measured 5–9 ms off a ~127 ms opening frame; left, since it changes how every list first fills. The rest of that frame is UIKit's push (~55 ms), the press batch's op loop (~13 ms) and the runner (~15 ms).
- **A list benchmark row is now mostly the runner and the batch** (2026-09-25, perf/rowcost, simulator Time Profiler over the fill slices; it was mostly UIKit). The iOS presenter's share of a built listbench row fell from about 1.5 to 0.75 ms (no window layout engine, no scroll view per closed swipe row, parked row views reused by shape: `NodePoolIOS.swift`). What is left per row: the runner's commit and a Taffy layout from the root on every report (about 0.7 ms, the perf/layout lane), `Batch.decode`'s JSON (about 0.25 ms), `CollectionSnapshot` parsing the whole snapshot each batch (about 0.12 ms, `Collection.swift`), and `UIImageView` resolving a symbol it is handed anew (about 0.08 ms). Layout from the collection, a binary batch, or a snapshot that sends only what changed would each go further. View reuse is iOS-only; macOS and web could follow the same contract.

- **The gallery's dismiss drag leaves the viewer open** (2026-09-28, LLP 1057.003 phase 1). At Fit a far or fast release sends the photo off on its spring and the backdrop fades to 0 with it, but the viewer stays, empty, until Back. Closing it when the exit ends needs an end-of-motion event (CSS's `transitionend`) or an action that can call the `command` prop; Contract has neither. C6's thrown card waits for "Deal again" for the same reason.
- **`timeline-scope: all` has no browser oracle** (2026-09-28, LLP 1057.003 phase 3). Chrome 154 drops the declaration (`CSS.supports('timeline-scope', 'all')` is false), so the kernel and the glue follow the specification's text, and the web's own CSS carries no `all`: phase 4's scroll timelines, which the browser resolves, would not see one. Recheck Chrome (and Safari) before phase 4, or refuse `all` until one parses it. The lookup's other 23 cases are Chrome's answers recorded in `kernel/tests/it/timeline_scope.rs` from a scratch page; `parity.html` rendering those trees with scroll timelines would make them a fixture.
- **A drag timeline's source eased by a CSS transition is sought per frame on the web** (2026-09-28, LLP 1057.003 phase 2). A spring release hands each consumer a follower animation and runs nothing per frame; a `translate` transition with a CSS easing (`0.4s ease-out`) is the browser's, so `motion-glue.js` seeks the consumers in each frame it runs (18–26 callbacks a release in the gallery). Composing it too means sampling the easing (a `KeyframeEffect` with no target answers its progress at any time) into the same `linear()` points; steps() would need its jumps.

- **Dropping the top visible row scrolls the List to follow it** (2026-09-25, the Apple reorder fix). The collection's scroll anchor is the key at the port's top (`capture_anchor`), and a reorder's data change restores it. When that key is the row just moved, the List jumps so the moved row is at the top again (seen on iOS and in `host/apple/src/arrange_tests.rs`, a `correction` of 80 points). This is the runner's, so every host does it. The anchor could skip the reorder's source.

- **A served RealWorld article is only partly adopted** (2026-09-24, the activation lane, LLP 1048/1047). At d73af7b0, booting a served `/article/…` page keeps 82 of the document's 113 elements; the rest are rebuilt. It happens on the tip, not only in the split prototype. Adoption should keep every element when the canonical documents match. Find what diverges (the Markdown body's projection is a likely suspect) and whether the digest or the element matching is wrong.

- **Presize the transaction's per-commit sets from the batch** (2026-09-24, the size lane): `txn::apply`'s `created` set, `Staged`'s maps (`created`, `parents`, `children`, the root sets) and `Detach`'s `seen` start empty on every commit and grow as its ops arrive, about seven doublings each for a batch that moves 220 children. Under a compact id map that growth cost 55–70% more cycles on a native churn bench (500 commits, each creating 20 rows, moving 220 and destroying 20: 203M → 313–348M cycles, for 1% more instructions), which is why hashbrown stays (LLP 1047 §10). hashbrown pays for the same growth, less. Sizing each set from the batch's op count before its first insert would take that growth off the commit path, on native and the web.

- **What RealWorld found in Contract** (2026-09-23, `apps/realworld`): (1) ~~Nothing runs when a mutation answers~~: `mutation … then action` (LLP 1016.001, 2026-09-26). Mutations, resources and tasks live only in the root, so a page's form state that touches data moves up.
- **`clock settle` doesn't see a request a source holds** (2026-09-23, jsexec lane): before the equal-arguments fix, a Fieldnotes web drive (a refresh while a library turn ran) left `library` ticket 5 in flight forever, held inside its Mixed set, yet `clock settle` answered `settled: true`; the host sees no work of its own. A future hold of that kind would pass a smoke unnoticed; settle could count the runner's pending requests too.
- **Surrogate keys that name only what shaped a page** (2026-09-23, render lane): the server keys a page by every source its render asked (LLP 1048.000 D11), so a source whose anonymous answer never changes (Interview's `loadDraft`, `accounts`) puts every page in its purge. Naming only the answers the document read needs the projection to report the resources it read; the runner doesn't track that.
- **`serve.mjs` doesn't serve the build's documents past `/`** (2026-09-23, render lane): `dist/404.html`, `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, `shell.html` and `<location>/index.html` (from `pages=`) aren't in its `PUBLIC_FILES`, and its fallback answers any app path with `/index.html`, now `/`'s document; so a static origin serves a listed page's URL, or an unknown one, `/`'s page (200), which the runtime then replaces. LLP 1048.000 D7 says a static host serves these files as they are; the render server does.
- **The web core's rich style codecs, linked by use** (2026-09-27, [LLP 1047.000 §9](llp/1047.000-staged-loading-by-capability.rfc.md)). RealWorld's core grew 70.7 KB in a day (238.9 → 309.6 KB brotli); Canvas 2D is now linked by use (`c1663d51`, −10.3 KB). What remains is style rows whose `CssValue` codecs parse CSS text (paint, marker, dash array, paint order, transform and its origin, filter, clip path, shape outside, aspect ratio, background image): the generated `StyleProps` code reaches every codec, so every app carries every parser. A throwaway build stubbing every `CssValue` parse and format call bounds the saving at **10.9 KB** (299,236 → 288,375 B), so the type-erased storage Charlie agreed to (rich rows behind a trait, parsers linked by use; `kernel/build/codec.rs`'s one template plus ~45 consumer sites across the SVG and paint lanes) waits until those lanes next reshape `StyleProps`. Also left: Canvas 2D's TypeScript draw seam and per-batch web turn (~3.5 KB), which need a registration in the generated entry. `metrics.mjs --long` reports each tracked app's core against a reference size (`WEB_CORE_KIB`), not gated since 852101fa73.
- **`app.wasm` across deploys, what is left** (2026-09-26, `6e0b8874`; [LLP 1047.000 §9](llp/1047.000-staged-loading-by-capability.rfc.md)). A returning reader now gets the next build as a delta (9.5 KB against 238.6 after an app edit) when the render server runs with `--generations`, which only the bench passes: hosting should. A page left open across a deploy still loads the new build and can skew against its checkpoint; the server keeps the earlier builds now, so it could serve each at its own `?v=` (LLP 1048.000 D10's "kept for pages still open"). The stage files are named by content but go `no-cache`; `immutable` would drop their revalidation.
- **GPU modules, what D6 left** (2026-09-23, split lane; [LLP 1009 D6](llp/1009-gpu-canvas.rfc.md)): natively a module gets no runtime shader assets (the ABI names no registry's shaders; the web asks `gpu_shader_names`), so a module compiles the WGSL it carries; natively every artifact links its own wgpu, naga and Metal backend (Weird Castle: 3.34 + 3.92 MB stripped against 4.06 MB for one module), which a shared wgpu library would remove; a destroyed device with two artifacts live has been reasoned and unit-tested, not driven.

- **Contract compiler follow-ups** (2026-09-22, branch `compiler/expansion`): Lowering canonicalizes and opens a file on every compile (6–14% of lowering, `lower_with_sites`); the resident compiler could keep it. Unoptimized `infer` takes ~10 KB of stack per level, which is what holds an expression to 100 deep (release has room for ten times as much); splitting its arms would raise the bound. `change=add` for a parameterless `add` reports only the unrelated `setDraft`'s uninferable parameter, since analysis's handler arity needs types first. Weird Castle does not compile at `c3b585bf` (`app.contract:289`: `iframe` has no attribute `postMessage`).

- **Native Markdown undo boundaries** (2026-09-22; [LLP 1045 D5](llp/1045-markdown-editor.rfc.md)): UIKit must retain its specialized per-editor undo manager; source undo/redo across natural run-loop turns and independent histories are verified with it. Still owed on native: a source replacement and formatting command in one automatic event group can undo together, skipping the intermediate source. Three bounded automatic-group repairs did not establish a safe boundary; the final attempt was reverted after scheduled run-loop closure exceptions on both platforms. Preserve native grouping semantics; the one-command-one-undo requirement remains incomplete. Physical phone input and intrinsic editor sizing remain unproven.

- **A link destination with `<…>` around a parenthesis** (2026-10-21, LLP 1045 D5 r6): in `[a](<b)c>)` the styler's link ends at the first `)`, inside the angle brackets, so `c>)` reads as text after the link on the web and on Apple alike. CommonMark's `<…>` destination may hold an unbalanced `)`; the bracket scan in `markdown/src/inline.rs` matches parentheses before it looks for `<`.
- **Markdown editor** (Charlie, 2026-09-21; [LLP 1045](llp/1045-markdown-editor.rfc.md)): source-backed editing now has shared Rust formatting/selection APIs, TextKit 2 adapters on iOS/macOS, and a dependency-free `contentEditable` web editor whose rules are `exact-markdown-editor`, an on-demand wasm (Charlie, 2026-09-22: CodeMirror removed). The stress app has a formatting toolbar and link field. Interview's separate consumer branch adds composition, source-preserving publication and durable account/prompt drafts. Native syntax stays visible at full advance but for link destinations, which Apple hides by `exact-markdown-editor`'s rules (LLP 1045 D5 r6, 2026-10-20); the web hides all of it (WYSIWYG). The DEFERRED take is approved. Owed: the Apple hosts adopting the rest of `exact-markdown-editor` (the other hidden syntax, its input rules), which waits behind LLP 1045.000's inline atoms (the take for their `rules/DEFERRED.md` move, 2026-10-23); phone composition/autocorrect/dictation/VoiceOver/selection ergonomics; Safari/Firefox/mobile-web evaluation; native intrinsic editor sizing and drawn list markers; reader paragraph details and Linux markup painting; the remaining media/highlighting/reader-migration slices. LLP 1045 remains Draft; it and its accepted child LLP 1045.000 are not linked into `llp/current/` (15 of 15).

- **`press` on a container holding its own input** (2026-09-23, buttons lane): Messages' two emoji-picker columns (`apps/messages/app.contract` `column press=showEmojiPicker`, lines 817 and 837) put `press` on a container that holds the picker's full-size transparent `input`, so neither can be a `button` (an input inside a `<button>` is interactive content in interactive content). Once they are restructured — the input could take the tap itself — `press` can belong to `button` and `link` alone and be a compile error elsewhere, where today it makes a `<div>` with a click listener and no keyboard access.

- **Context menus after LLP 1021 §5.1** (2026-10-05): the Signal clone moves its chat-list preview onto `contextPopover`/`contextPreview` (its agent has the syntax). Owed: a swipe row (the Signal chat list's) never parks in the iOS node pool, menu or not (its live scroll view), and that list is not a virtualized `list` anyway; macOS's `MenuHost.context` and right-mouse path have no test (`popUp` is modal); the compiler does not refuse a literal `contextPopover` naming no popover, or two `contextPreview` rows (iOS logs both, macOS the first, the web neither); no interactive content inside a preview (UIKit takes any touch on it as the commit), so Signal's reaction bar stays authored; after a bar item's menu, `ExactView`'s focus (key commands) comes back only at the next touch on the page, not when the menu ends (UIKit says nothing then), and any touch on such a bar blurs a focused node (UIKit exposes no bar item's view to tell a menu item's touch from another); Linux shows nothing; on the web a bound `contextPopover` that the node's own action changes opens the old popover when the commit is deferred (a view transition on the JS target, presence loading on the wasm host): resolve it after the applied commit, not in a timeout.
- **Keyboard access to a message's context menu** (2026-09-23, buttons lane): Messages' bubble (`apps/messages/message-parts.contract`) and the reply rows in Exact Live and messages-stress are containers with `swiperight`/`contextmenu`/`dblclick`, no longer `button`s (a message is not a button), so they are neither in the Tab order nor announced as buttons; their actions are the pointer's alone. A focusable message with a keyboard route to its actions (the context-menu key, Shift+F10) is owed.

- **iOS smoke: the deck iframe times out on a cold simulator** (2026-09-24, this Mac at load ~130): the first `smoke.mjs ios` after booting the simulator failed six checks, all the web deck's WKWebView never finishing its first load (`loading: true`, no `deck-ready`, an empty guest outline, a blank screenshot, then "guest frame is not ready" stopped the drive); the rerun on the warm simulator passed in 104 s. A first WebKit launch in a cold simulator under load outlasts the smoke's wait for the guest. Either wait for the guest's first load with a hang bound rather than a deadline, or warm WebKit before the fixture.

- **Reader memory** (2026-09-24, collection-parity lane): on a 3.2 MB corpus (75 LLP docs) the macOS reader holds 73–75 MB at open where Legend Markdown holds 49–50 MB; the gap is the small malloc zone (51 MB dirty to 25). About 19 MB of it was the document as plan values — 8,862 blocks, 51,462 runs at 208 B each (records and lists are now one `Rc<[Value]>` allocation each, perf/memory, not re-measured here) — and about 19 MB is freed-but-dirty pages left by 410 MB of allocation churn during the load (890k allocations for one document). Smoothness and startup already lead Legend there.

- **iOS keyboard focus, after 3a24683c** (2026-09-23, native lane): Tab moves the first responder but does not scroll a focused control into view (the web does); an open sheet does not confine the order (background nodes stay in it). The simulator XCTests (every `*IOSTests` class) now run in the async lane for commits under host/apple (87fa8b61; `bun host/apple/build.mjs --test --ios`).

- **No drive presses a real Tab on macOS** (2026-09-23, native lane): `agent.mjs macos … "type X key Tab"` (`AgentMac`'s `NSWindow.sendEvent`), a CGEvent posted to the agent app's pid, and even `NSWindow.keyDown` in XCTest leave the first responder where it was, from a button as from a paragraph; only `selectNextKeyView` moves it. The key-view loop (buttons in tree order; paragraphs selectable but out of it, 2026-09-23) is proven at that level in `AccessibilityTests`; a real-keyboard check on an active window is owed.

- **The macOS agent gives an app no native storage** (2026-09-23, the Interview lane): under `agent.mjs macos`, an app that opens native SQLite reports "native storage is unavailable". Interview's replica (`sqlite.open app:/data`) is one. So a signed-in member's own view can't be driven on macOS, with the switch off or on; the lane drove the public view instead. The launched app gets its storage; the agent's should get the same.

- **CPU nonfinite corner radius** (2026-09-21): a direct internal `Raster.fill` with radii `[4, NaN, 4, 4]`, scale 0.75 and a reflected transform panics in tiny-skia AA scan conversion. Reproduced on published `fb9f8b65`; no authored-app reproduction. Validate malformed internal geometry before path rasterization. Evidence: `/tmp/exact-rounded-interior-fb9f8b65/baseline-pixels.log`.

- **Text performance: LLP 1044.000 is paused** (Charlie, 2026-09-21, after S14): [LLP 1044.000](llp/1044.000-text-performance-claude.plan.md) §11 has every slice and the final scoreboard against Legend. **Deferred by Charlie, to revisit soon** (2026-09-21): the web's giant paragraph (S7's second half listed its unsupported cases — a narrower guarantee, our own breaker, or as is); rows too costly to build in a frame (a 256-column table: never-blank and never-stall conflict); a physical 120 Hz iPhone and Mac run (real touch input needs a person or an approved XCUITest runner). Also open: S14's small-text drawing on iOS (`TextRasterIOS.canRasterText`; unmeasured there); S9.
- **Reflow follow-ups** (2026-09-20, `apps/reflow`): the dragon is a hand-placed polygon; a traced silhouette (`shape-outside: <image>`, LLP 1043.000 stage 5) would let a real illustration be the exclusion. On the web, ordinary paragraphs (magazine, cards) are broken by the browser, so a column can end one line short of the cut; only flowed text goes through the shared walker there. Web `wasm-opt` was not on PATH for the numbers in the README.
- **Bounded resource answers** (Charlie, 2026-09-18, [LLP 1027.004](llp/1027.004-bounded-resource-answers.plan.md) r2): slices 1–2 are implemented. `list virtualized=true` has `reachstart`/`reachend`, Messages stress has a bounded mode, and the Messages conversation answer is at most 200 rows around a cursor. Still owed: the Grok code review (balance exhausted), a macOS/AppKit drive of the stress bounded mode. Shared views/buffers/heap and generic whole-value reconciliation remain deferred (LLP 1027.003 §9).
- **Module-wide liveness landed natively; confirm §13** (2026-10-04, hn-reader F7): an answer awaiting a promise another answer started (one memoized `fetch`, a queue behind another's storage) now waits on Hermes while the module has a fetch, storage step, stream or unbegun answer outstanding, and is asked again after each delivery ([LLP 1027.003.000](llp/1027.003.000-bounded-application-work-claude.plan.md) §13 option 1, which QUEUE recorded as Charlie's decision). Still per-answer: the web's wasm module realm (`host/web/module-glue.js` runs one answer at a time, so the wait would block the reply it waits for; the JS target, the web build, runs real promises) and a worker-placed source (its owner runs one turn to its end).
- **Bounded application work: plan r3, reviewed by two families** (Charlie, 2026-09-20): [LLP 1027.003.000](llp/1027.003.000-bounded-application-work-claude.plan.md) (Claude) and [LLP 1027.003.001](llp/1027.003.001-bounded-application-work-astra.plan.md) (Astra max) answer LLP 1027.003 §11–§13 independently. They agree: Fieldnotes first (a legal 1,000-note notebook cannot be opened), then Messages changed-record writes, then native filling; no heap, deltas or new apparatus. r2 §9 recommended 1027.003.001 as the spine with four amendments; r3 is the synthesis, reviewed by Astra max and Grok 4.6 xhigh (both MATERIAL FINDINGS, six defects raised by both families, dispositions in `llp/reviews/`) — this document's summary-only Fieldnotes answer with no pager, its sibling's chunked exact search with no stored folded column, the three-second idle sync tick as its own first Messages change, and hoisting `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` out of the per-operation path. 1027.003.001 §8 found what both repairs cannot fix: browser SQLite exports the whole database after every non-query op (`host/web/storage-worker.js:113-123,189`) and every `storage-fs` operation `getAll()`s every file with contents (`host/web/storage-fs.js:166`). A contract compiler fix landed (a bool `derive` could not be a ternary condition); Fieldnotes now fetches short prefixes for unfiltered previews, with bounded 32-row full-body fallback, and loads only the selected editor body. Exact search still scans every body. TypeScript reads and Rust backups now check the stored schema instead of issuing no-op CREATE writes; browser whole-database export on actual writes remains open. Filesystem operations now fetch their required records or subtree keys; removal reads no deleted-file contents, and rename loads only the moved subtree and records it validates. Messages idle sync now skips full-history reads/comparisons unless reconciliation is owed; reset/adoption, apply and settlement failures retain the obligation until successful read/publication. Browser idle metadata commits now skip persistence when a bounded SQLite comparison finds every final value unchanged. Unchanged reply timer ticks now skip full-model snapshot/diff work too. Durable source footprints now avoid full-history snapshot/diff and held-map copies on local edits. Receipt transitions now visit only outgoing messages still awaiting Read. Sends and contact edits now preserve existing person positions and select only affected contacts. Recovery footprints now include only selected messages and expiry candidates; due expiry metadata scans and whole-database export for actual browser writes remain.
- **Action spelling diagnostics** (LLP 1006 §3): handlers, action props and timers now use authored scopes; forwarded prop/provider errors link their supplied argument. Action-valued derived expressions and other untraceable substitution origins conservatively get no hint. Extend only with original binding provenance, never expanded-root name guesses.
- **Messages remaining write costs** (LLP 1027.004 D5): durable source footprints now compare only their declared records, detaching changed payloads before storage awaits and updating held entries after admission. Sends/contact edits select affected people with stable positions; exact-ID contact lookups reuse that index. Sends retain only one pending reply for rollback. Receipt transitions are proportional to outgoing messages still awaiting Read. Recovery footprints now avoid unrelated archived payloads, including global expiry candidates and selected messages only. Forward idle reply ticks now use a conservative next-event time without scanning/copying pending schedules. Non-idle footprint selection traverses pending directly and creates entries only for due events. It also identifies receipt-only ticks and no-op rewinds that need no pending-map rollback copy. Reply removals retain only removed entries and their original positions; refusal rebuilds schedule order before model restore. Single blocking/deletion removals now retain one activity and its ordinal through a key-only `forEach` scan, rebuilding order only on refusal. Small message deletions now update surviving thread indexes in place, preserving receipt order; deleting at least half rebuilds the fewer surviving rows. Transcript/affected-reply/receipt filtering remains linear. Recovery operations skip expiry metadata scans before a conservative earliest deadline and skip future buckets when another bucket is due; restore rebuilds the bound. Recently Deleted summaries use each archive’s maintained minimum expiry instead of walking its messages; contact enumeration remains. Deletion appends to the owned archive without copying its existing rows; footprint selection still scans that conversation’s archive metadata. Inbox, recipient matches, and Recently Deleted now decorate/transfer/mount at most 200 people through Previous/More controls. Deleted selection and Recover All targets/count still span all archived contacts; scanning, the global target string, and selected-token removal strings remain unbounded. Name/address searches, ordinal scans on actual blocking/deletion, scans on due ticks/rewinds/stale early deadlines, and row metadata scans within due archive buckets remain bounded-work targets. Startup, changed sync and exceptional rollback still restore the full model. Snapback's 512-record cap refuses oversized bulk edits whole; retain that atomic refusal while improving the explanation and preflight.
- **Linux popover presentation and Messages reply input** (2026-09-20): closed popovers are now hidden/inert and invokers report unsupported, fixing the hidden `permanentlyDelete` interception of ordinary inbox taps. Top-layer presentation, placement, light dismissal and closed normal-flow layout remain unimplemented (LLP 1021). The earlier reply drive loses its held contact, and the Linux carrier refuses context-menu input. The ordinary scroll-property path and the headless 215-send/earlier/later/latest drive are fixed. Evidence: `/tmp/exact-linux-popovers-306428c2/` and `/tmp/exact-scroll-commands-0a819c84/`.
- **Virtualize the Messages transcript** (LLP 1010 §6 consumer sweep; LLP 1027.004 D5): its horizontal timestamp-reveal wrapper is outside the collection's supported shape. Once virtualized, `reachstart`/`reachend` replace the explicit earlier/later rows with no source change.

What would make sense to do next, in rough order. This is not a spec and decides
nothing: `rules/RULES.md` §Scope says a spec without an implementer and a date isn't
written, so a line here is one to three lines — the thing, why, what it needs. When
someone picks one up it becomes an LLP with a name and a date (archive a link when
the working set is full); when it lands, delete the line. Anyone, agents
included, may add or remove a line. `rules/DEFERRED.md` still binds: an entry that
sits on that list carries the trade it would take.

## Next, in order (2026-08-29)

- **Game texture families in bundles and on the wire** (2026-09-23, textures lane; [LLP 1046.003](llp/1046.003-game-engine-as-built.explainer.md#compressed-textures-2026-09-23)): every baked texture ships as `x.tex` (RGBA8), `x.bc.tex` and `x.astc.tex`, and a device fetches one — on the web. Native bundles copy all three (the Fox's 5.6 MB becomes 8.4 MB); a macOS bundle needs BC plus the fallback, an iOS device ASTC plus the fallback, but the bake's compatibility inventory lists every asset, so thinning is a bake change, not a copy filter. Separately, `host/web/serve.mjs` sends `.tex`/`.model` as uncompressed `application/octet-stream`; RGBA8 payloads gzip 70× (the Fox's 5.6 MB → 80 KB) and blocks 1.2–17× — a compressible type for game assets would cut the fallback's download.
- **Game-engine comparison remaining gaps** (2026-09-17; LLP 1046.003 §7): full Lanterns games and fleet trials landed. Babylon/PlayCanvas qualify; eight external edit passes include one PlayCanvas repair-budget violation. Godot proves human win/loss but its sequential large-step adapter fails; Three.js/Cannon collision remains broken after the separately authorized extra sleep/reset round.
  Next: repair those two specific baselines before their withheld trials; measure target GPU/phone and native exports, complete adapter/setup cost, and actual vendor automation routes. Actively track Babylon + PlayCanvas; retain Godot/native and Three/renderer controls.
  Retained fixtures include a held-out change and negative controls; their repeated execution is not repeated agent success. Diagnostic only, no blocking check; LLP 1046.000 §6a remains the full-game bar.

- **List memory** (2026-09-18, Codex; LLP 1010 §6): fixed and measured-height
  runner/web windows and Apple geometry are implemented; Markdown now uses them,
  with logical selection/copy on Mac and web. Finish workspace validation after
  the macOS executable-startup block, Linux geometry, the full native interaction
  sweep, raster budget and Messages acceptance. Memory and construction cost trigger it now; further Messages
  decorative artwork/material/timing polish waits. The fixed-height web slice does not complete the native/consumer sweep.

- **Markdown performance comparison** (2026-09-18): displayed launch now favors
  Exact modestly on README and the 2.24 MB corpus; ordinary-wheel response is

  still has lower hitch time after the scalar-width reuse fix. Next: reduce

- **Text around shapes** (LLP 1043.000 §8): auto-height flow is built

  The web breaker is `textflow.wasm`, loaded on demand (`host/web-js/flow.js`).

- **Native verification gaps** (2026-09-15): Messages debug native tests can

  on the Air); investigate without weakening the limit. Interview's new Mac
  materials still need Reduce Transparency/Increase Contrast and older-OS pixels.

- **Markdown scrolling with real input** (2026-09-19): the separate upstream
  `4d91f9e` build measured ahead of Legend with in-process input on an M4 Pro
  at 60 Hz and an M5 Max Retina panel at 120 Hz (`apps/markdown/README.md`:
  inputs later than 8.33 ms, of 2,400 — about 2 against 84 at 60 Hz; 1, 1, 1
  against 1, 6, 4 at 120 Hz). The 120 Hz runs had a load average of 30–67;
  an Instruments trace under that load made app updates about four times as

- **A list paragraph is typeset twice** (2026-09-19): in black to be measured, then
  in its colours to be painted, because `TextShapeKey` carries the paint; only the
  line breaks are handed over. 0.7 ms at the median and 3 ms at worst, since
  2026-09-19 a fill unit of its own. A shape whose colour comes from the context at
  draw time would serve both, for a paragraph of one colour at least.
- **A row of many inline runs is the longest fill unit** (2026-09-19): 7 ms at the
  99th percentile against 2.6 at the median, about 0.5 ms in the runner and kernel
  and 0.35 ms in the presenter per created view, and every inline run is a
  `NodeView` that is never mounted.
- **Rationed list reports on iOS** (2026-09-19; LLP 1010 §6): UIKit also scrolls on
  the thread that lays out; `PresenterIOS` still reports a whole window inside the
  scroll callback and paints text when first seen. The runner and host halves are
  shared (`list_viewport_within`, one-call settle); the presenter half is not made.
- **A jump lands on rows built synchronously** (2026-09-19): a scroller drag or a
  250,000-point jump takes one main-thread period of 31–44 ms. The rows are never
  blank; the frame is late. The first report creates 68 views for six to eight
  visible rows (13 ms in the runner and kernel, 8.6 ms in the presenter); a second
  follows when measured heights move the landing (5 ms), then visible paragraphs
  paint. Shorten the per-row work before spreading the fill across frames, which
  would expose blank rows.

  D8.3). `ChainingScrollView` forces overlay and `kernel/src/style.rs` sets

  actions/editing without making the baked tree inert; Chrome verifies scrolling
  with the data module held or failed. Repeat the touch-scroll check on iPhone.
- **Dev program rebuild boundary** (2026-09-07): after the normal phone's live
  edit passed, a server rebuild reported web inputs changed / iOS inputs unchanged
  but the iPhone required a native relaunch. Check whether the shared program
  identity is invalidating unaffected native clients (1030.000).
