  compile inherits the iPhone SDK; target-specific macOS CXXFLAGS unblocked this run.
  Fix SDK selection in the sibling build script rather than relying on that override.

- **External dev latency** (2026-09-05): Weird Castle's first captured edit took
  300 ms to DOM acceptance (9 ms plan), above the 100 ms budget. Use the existing
  metrics drive to separate startup/preflight work from repeated edit latency.

- **Sample-host no-op input delay** (2026-09-04): `smoke.mjs host` passed but its
  final repeated light-scheme tap after destroying session a waited 74–131 seconds.
  Trace and GPU drawable samples are in the filesystem run; reduce the carrier/frame acknowledgement path.

1. **Linux host follow-ups** (LLP 1015 landed 2026-08-29; r2 the same day: vello on
   the GPU is the main painter, tiny-skia the fallback and pixel oracle; green on
   `expo-build-1000` (CPU), the minisforum (Vulkan/llvmpipe), and headless on macOS
   (Metal)). The DRM path ran on the minisforum 2026-08-29 (LLP 1015 §6: 1920×1080 @ 60 Hz on
   RADV, boot to first flip ~160 ms), seen and driven over the host's own VNC server
   (`EXACT_VNC=1`) since the KVM was unplugged; evdev itself has still carried no real
   event. Owed, in order: **a KMS surface for the GPU**
   (`VK_KHR_display`) so the frame is presented, not read back (17–19 ms of latency
   per frame on Metal today); `canvas` on this painter's device; the Chrome
   comparison of a pixel fixture (the font is pinned since 2026-08-29 — LLP 1015
   §3 — and `smoke linux` is green on a Mac and on the builder); a font cache when a machine's scan (25 ms
   for 787 faces on a Mac) matters; lifting the shared ~120 lines of orchestration
   out of `host/apple` and `host/linux`; one wgpu when vello moves to 30.
2. **Events, the rest** (the minimal set landed 2026-08-30: `hover`, `focus`,
   `blur`, `key` on web, macOS, iOS — LLP 1005 §3). Left out on purpose: pointer
   coordinates and moves (a drag), `keyup`, double-click, wheel offsets reaching
   the runner (the windowed-List line below). The Linux carrier has `tap … hover`
   and `type … key` now (2026-09-23); `smoke.mjs` step 4a still skips them on Linux;
   `key` inside a text field on macOS/iOS sees editing commands only (LLP 1008
   §5's declared deviation); and an action cannot branch on a key or hover
   payload — actions have assignment and command statements, no `if` and no
   string `match` — so `key=` today can only record the key.
3. **Weird Castle's asks** (Charlie, 2026-08-30: the app lives in `~/projects/weird-castle`
   on exact2 by path; `scripts/app.mjs`). In order: **asynchronous data settlement** —
   LLP 1016 (RFC, **Accepted** 2026-08-30 after a three-model panel: a `mutation` slot +
   `send`, `refresh` for queries; built the same day end to end — runner, compiler, the
   browser and `ibex2::host` executors, the agent's `settle`, Weird Castle's real `loginV2`
   against api.castle.xyz on three hosts; the Linux executor followed the same day (LLP 1016 §2,
   ibex OQ2 resolved), the token across launches is LLP 1018) for the runner's reserved
   shape (LLP 1005 §7): a data source
   answers now or hands back a request, the host executes it (`ibex2::host` on native,
   the browser's `fetch` on the web), one ABI call brings the answer in, a pending
   resource is `none` meanwhile, bake tolerates it — the first out-of-process resource
   is `loginV2` at api.castle.xyz; **password masking** and **Enter to submit** landed 2026-08-30 — the
   kernel rows are the web's `type` and `inputMode` (LLP 1017 P9's sweep spells them
   literally later), honored on web/macOS/iOS; `submit` is the seventh event, the web's
   implicit submission; a session across launches is LLP 1018 (another session's lane).
4. **Markdown viewer follow-ups** (LLP 1033, macOS milestone 2026-09-05):
   iOS selection and link gestures; iOS/web file-opening adapters; heading anchors,
   tables, and syntax highlighting. macOS drag selection across paragraphs and copy
   now use CoreText's existing lines. Keyboard selection extension and bidi selection
   geometry still need dedicated fixtures. Variable-height windowing and scroll
   anchoring now run; startup against Legend is still a tie, and scrolling is
   ahead on one 60 Hz machine (see `apps/markdown/README.md` and the 120 Hz item
   above). At a 420-point
   window width, the open folder pane leaves the text cramped and clips header
   controls; the reader needs a compact layout at that width.
   During verification the macOS timer-step smoke once read width 50 at t=1250
   instead of 75, then passed on repeat; investigate the intermittent clock fixture.

- **Rust replacement follow-ups** — **LLP 1029.000**: native/browser/Wasmi replacement
  and dev/prod controls are implemented. Next: physical-phone size/latency and
  20-edit/50-replacement measurements, normalized native-baseline reuse, and
  custom out-of-tree host composition; independent multi-module routing stays later.
  Parent **LLP 1029** is superseded for development and engine choice
  (Charlie, 2026-10-22); wasmi is ratified, per-source routing deferred, and
  Q5/Q7 remain open with no work waiting. Its Summary keeps the mixed-language
  rulings. LLP 1032's engine comparisons remain research for phone measurements.
- **The compatibility executor still says `wasmtime`** (LLP 1029 §8;
  Charlie, 2026-10-22). `bake/src/compat.rs` emits that label for `Swappable`
  and reads it to admit wasm. Rename it once, deliberately, including its
  readers: the compatibility ids containing the label change. Done when receipts name

- **Delivery, unified** — LLP 1030 r2 and LLP 1030.000 r2. Landed: stage 1 (the
  asset row), stage 2 (the manifest), the compatibility id, the `delivery`

  through `scripts/origin.mjs`), and the macOS and Linux hosts opening the

  signature seam holds; four structural properties the RFCs paid for are not
  what the code does — filesystem issues `issues/20260904-*.md` (anti-rollback
  floor is 0, stream files overwritten before the head, Linux next-launch
  ignores entry assets, snapshot is a git-status of the app dir, activation

  the dev policy folded onto the store (1026 D12); iOS driven; `BGAppRefreshTask`;
  Linux font and deck overrides from an entry; the sunset card shown by the
  app; the web host, which has no store (`L = A` in its `compat.json` is a lie
  until it does, or the manifest says `0` for it); `--watch`; the object-store
  adapter; the binary lanes.

- **LLP 1031 owed after the 2026-09-03 landing** (the handle, `ExactKit`, the macOS
  sample host, the manifest stage; the iOS sample host landed 2026-09-04 — each pane its
  own navigation controller, `smoke.mjs host-ios`): **content-height containment** (D3's
  guard is written; bounded only today); **the request and store crossings** (D4's
  contract; triggered by an adopter whose client cannot be wrapped); a request in flight under a destroy (needs a fetching app — Weird Castle — as the
  fixture); and the host smoke's remount step, which took ~3 s in some runs: measured 2026-09-04 in
  isolation at 42 ms (`clock settle` after the pop) and 7 ms (a's layout) — the seconds
  appeared only while two agent lanes were compiling on the same Mac, so it reads as a
  settle bound under load, not a remount cost; watch for it on a quiet machine.
  Weird Castle is on today's exact2 (2026-09-04: the registry shape, `host!` with
  `COMPAT`, `app.json`), but its `tests/app.rs` does not compile since LLP 1027 stage 3 —
  six assertions want `Answer::Now(...)` around a `Value` — and the file carries another
  session's uncommitted edits; whoever owns them wraps them.

## Cheap, any time

- **`lower-flex-on-block`: multi-column decided by name, not value** (2026-10-19; `contract/lower/src/warnings/container.rs:107`): `column-count="auto"`, `column-width="auto"`, `columns="auto"`, or a longhand resetting a class's count to `auto` is not multi-column, yet `justify-content`, `row-gap`, `gap` and `column-gap` stay exempt, so `view column-count="auto" justify-content="center"` is not warned. Exempt only when the winning count or width is not `auto` (a computed value keeps the benefit of the doubt).
- **Agent hold clock: round-3 review leftovers** (2026-10-19; `host/apple/touches.mjs:453`, `scripts/agent-drag.mjs:196`): (1) `down()` checks `finished` before its awaited log read, so a runner that fails during the read still lets `heldTick` seek the rest of a short hold (≤150 ms) in one step; recheck after the read (`if (finished) return false; const t = ours(await entries()); return !finished && t != null && !t.lifted`). (2) a `clock +100 real` step in flight keeps advancing after the lift; make the inner clock loop cancellable, check `down()` before each advancing request, and await it when the runner finishes. Both affect only failing or late-polled drives.
- **Porter-friction language lane: round-3 review leftovers** (2026-10-19): (1) `contract/types/src/routes.rs:270` suggests `path("day", k)` for a `bool` (or other non-string) interpolation, which `type-argument` refuses; keep the conversion (`` `${k}` ``) or omit the concrete form. (2) `contract/lower/src/warnings/root_replace.rs:198` counts every `go` as a push, so `select(go(nav, top(nav).url), "patterns")` (a no-op `go`) warns; suppress at least provable no-ops. (3) `contract/lower/src/controls.rs:749` repairs `-exact-press-haptic="none"` on a native button as `haptic("none")`, which `type-haptic` refuses; for `none`, say to remove the attribute.
- **The agent's `layout` could report each box's resolved corner radii** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 11 C; theme2's ruling (b)): `scripts/scale-check.mjs` fits a box's top-left corner radius from the host's screenshot (antialiased coverage along the corner's first rows, held to 1 point), which is enough for stage 11's gate. Each node's four resolved corner radii in `layout`'s reply (after CSS's reduction) would make the geometry exact, every corner's, and cheap to read on every host; the screenshot would still be what shows the painting honours it. It is an addition to the agent protocol, so it takes that window's version.
- **`style=` hint and grant scan: round-3 review leftovers** (2026-10-19): (1) `contract/lower/src/lint.rs:171` unquotes a number for string shorthands: `border: 0` suggests `border=0` and `columns: 2` `columns=2`, which `lower-css-shorthand` refuses; keep quotes where the attribute takes a shorthand string. (2) `lint.rs:211` `overlap` misses pairs that write the same rows without a hyphen prefix (`border-color`/`border-bottom-color`, `border-width`/`border-top`, `border-top`/`border-color`), so dropping `!important` changes the winner; treat names as overlapping when their bound `StyleId`s intersect (`shorthands::rows`). (3) `contract/cli/src/grants/literals.rs:100` ends a Rust `//` comment at U+2028/U+2029/`\r` in the join lookahead, unlike the lexer at :181; use `line_terminator(c) && (typescript || c == '\n')`.
- **Props named like an app `fn`: two review leftovers** (2026-10-19; `contract/types/src/component.rs:489,517`): (1) a value prop (`pick: number`) beside `fn pick` is not checked: inlining rewrites `pick(1)` in the child to the bound expression's head (`Seg(pick=other)` runs `other(1)`), while the checker and the Lean expander resolve the fn; preserve resolved fn names in substitution, or refuse the collision for every prop. (2) `type-prop-hides-fn` counts a `pick(…)` under a `let`, `match` or arrow binder named `pick`, which substitution leaves to the fn; count only calls whose head is free (`first_free`), still counting those under a node `for`/`match`.
- **Apple stepped keys: two review leftovers** (2026-10-18; `host/apple/src/svg_lower.rs:171,182`): (1) each key calls `curve.progress()`, a linear scan of the expanded stops, so `steps(65535)` builds 131,071 keys in ~17 billion comparisons; walk the stops with a cursor. (2) a delayed transition installs with `fillMode = .backwards` and its first key is `easing.progress(0)`, so `linear(0 25%, 1 75%)` or `steps(4, jump-start)` shows the eased start during the delay; check CSS Transitions' before-phase value against Chrome and the engine (`motion/src/transition.rs:342`), then leave `from` at time 0 in `transition_specs` if they hold it.
- **Android's `linear()` evaluator still has the old edge rules** (2026-10-19, the `linear()` reviews). `piecewise` in `host/android/canvas/src/main/java/dev/exact/canvas/Layers.kt` holds the outer stops and takes the earlier stop at a shared input; the engine's (`motion/src/easing.rs`, `piecewise`) answers as Chrome 155 does: at a stop's exact input, the last stop there; before the first stop or past the last, the outer segment extrapolated; before a first pair that shares an input, the second's output. Two that differ today: `linear(0 25%, 1 25%, 1)` at 0.1 (Chrome 1, Android 0) and `linear(0 25%, 1 75%)` at 0 (Chrome −0.5, Android 0). Port the rule and check it on a phone or the JVM.

- **Compiler recovery and fresh-store drives: three review leftovers** (2026-10-18): (1) `contract/cli/src/sources/refused.rs:45` `hides()` matches a refused name anywhere in a diagnostic's message, so a broken `state number =` hides an unrelated `type-operand` that mentions `number`; restrict it to missing-symbol diagnostics about that declaration. (2) An incomplete derive whose write was in a refused action is still reported as `type-derive-cycle` (`contract/types/src/component.rs:235`); drop it with `type-cannot-infer`. (3) `scripts/verify.mjs:228`: a fresh-store refusal calls `stop()`, which kills a `server-*` a crashed drive left answering; on refusal drop only this drive's lease.
- **Evaluate a component's computed prop once per instance** (2026-10-18; worth ~2 points of euxy's playing CPU): a prop read several times in a stateless one-box component is re-evaluated at every read. A memo in the inliner was tried and removed (review: it hoisted reads under `&&`/`||`, `?:`, expression `match` arms and arrow bodies, so `range(-1)` ran where the program skipped it). Redo it with the guaranteed-evaluation rule `contract/syntax/src/inline/derives.rs` `summarize` already uses, never memoize handler arguments or clock-reading props, and mirror it in `semantics/Contract/Expand.lean`; `semantics/corpus/components/prop-guarded.contract` and `prop-event-time.contract` are the cases it must keep agreeing on.
- **iOS: an open context-preview popover still lets the bar follow the scroller below it** (2026-10-18; `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/NavigationBarIOS.swift:940`): the top-scroller successor scan rejects a hidden view, and `MenusIOS.sync()` keeps an in-flow popover hidden while its context preview lays it out (`isLaidOut` true), so a route of header, in-flow popover, scroller keeps collapsing and insetting a scroller the popover pushed down. Scan by kernel flow participation and clear `collapseScroll` while the preview is open.
- **A large reply's owed Hermes collection can wait forever** (2026-10-17; `js/src/prelude.js:1051,1187`, `js/src/lib.rs:863,1084,1128`): the full collection a promoted large string owes runs only when no other answer is pending, so a fetch that never settles keeps a dropped 376 KB QR buffer for the life of the process; and a reply whose serialization throws (capture limit, cycle) leaves its call in `calls` as `done`, blocking every later owed collection. Run the owed collection on the reply and re-stamp pending calls' epochs; `__exact_forget` a call whose settle fails.
- **Nested buttons: only the innermost activates (Charlie's ruling, 2026-10-20), not landed** (`host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/InvokersIOS.swift`, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/Mac/`, `host/web-js/rt.js`). HTML gives a click one activation target: a click on or inside an inner button opens, closes or toggles only that button's target, never an outer invoker's, while the press is delivered as now. Today iOS runs a nested button's press and then opens the outer invoker (`CustomInvokerIOSTests.testPopoverBubblingInvokesWithAndWithoutAnInvokerHandler` encodes it), `MenuHost.pressAsTouched` presses an ancestor without `withoutInvocation(of:)` so an inner handlerless invoker inside an outer one opens both, and Chrome itself activates every invoker button on the click's path (measured: an inner `<button>` inside `<button popovertarget>` or `commandfor` opens both targets), so the web needs a runtime guard. Built for iOS, macOS and the web and not landed after three review rounds (local branch `item7-r3`, one commit on de4daad077, in the worktree `.claude/worktrees/agent-acc7bb23bd57ba852`; its tests fail on main and pass with it, but `NestedActivationIOSTests.testInlinePressInvokesItsButtonUnlessItsAncestryContainsALink` still fails in a bare run). The web guard neutralizes the outer invokers' attributes for that one click; the last review left two regressions in its handling of an outer `click()` the inner press handler makes during the same event: the outer's activation runs before an ancestor's press handler, and a bound write after a direct one can lose. Edges: an SVG shape's press inside an inner invoker does not activate it (macOS and the iOS agent). Separately: `interactive` yields to the host's own overflow `ScrollView` pan, so a `popovertarget` button with `overflow: auto` never opens its menu (skip the node's own `scroll`).
- **iOS invoker press look: two edges a second contact leaves** (2026-10-20, review of the overlay holding the look; `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/PointerIOS.swift`). (1) While the overlay is highlighted a handlerless invoker's `pressed` is true, so a second contact that went down through a handlerless child button and forwarded its down to a pressable ancestor is taken by the invoker at the up (`ownsPress = pressed`): the invoker's press runs, not the ancestor's, and the ancestor's contact stays armed. Record on `PressContact` whether its down was taken here and route the move, up and cancel by that. (2) That contact moving outside clears the look (`pressFollows(false)`) the overlay still holds, and nothing restores it at the up, since `pressed` and `overlayHoldsPress` stay true. Reconcile the look from the contact's inside state or the overlay's hold.
- **The driver's Release-reinstall note breaks on an apostrophe** (2026-10-17; `host/apple/devices.mjs:234,259`): the printed `xcrun simctl install … '<path>'` single-quotes the copied app's path without escaping a `'` in it (an app named `Charlie's Notes`), so the command is unmatched; the copy itself is kept. Escape embedded quotes in both notes and test spaces and apostrophes.
- **Bold Text on a Linux host's own chrome** (2026-10-17; LLP 1115.000 D12.3): the kernel steps every run and title it gives, but a weight the Linux host assigns after it is not stepped: a file field's button word, an alert's title, Material's clock and menu glyphs (they read `node.style`). Step them through `exact_kernel::bolder` where the run says `bold_text`, or take the weight from the kernel.
- **A store write that fails to persist is dropped** (2026-10-14): the web glue's and the JS target's `persist` swallow a `localStorage` error, and the Apple and Linux hosts likewise, while the runner keeps the value. Keep a failed write and retry it, or report it to the app. Keeping an unchanged value is no write, so a later identical `set` no longer retries it by chance.
- **Linux element reveals land at once** (2026-10-18; LLP 1070.000 §14, LLP 1115.000 D8.4; `host/linux/src/presenter/reveal.rs`): macOS and UIKit animate `scrollIntoView(id)` with omitted, `auto` or `smooth` behavior and hold the scroller's `scrollend` until the animation ends; the Linux host lands every element jump at once. Animate it there too, landing at once for `instant`, reduced motion and the frozen agent, a new request or `scrollTop` write replacing a running one.
- **scrollend gaps on the hosts that have it** (2026-10-13; LLP 1005):
  - Apple unbracketed moves use a 100 ms `DispatchWorkItem`; known instant commands and discrete wheel notches should complete without that quiet delay.
  - Apple visible layout clamps can arm an end through `scrollEndMoved`; classify clamps separately from scroll operations.
  - Apple hidden-scroller completion differs from Chrome: `take()` precedes `hasScrollLayoutBox`, so a hidden callback consumes an owed end, while hide/show before the callback can retain unfinished work; distinguish unfinished scrolling from an already queued end.
  - Apple settlement must include unfinished scrolling and queued completions: `Agent.nativeInFlight()` does not count `ScrollEnd.owed` or its quiet work item.
  - Web `clock settle` can return `settled: true` during smooth scrolling and before a `scrollend` handler's jump chain finishes, as measured by LLP 1005's runtime probes.
  - Verify web lifetime parity: `rt.js` suppresses `scrollend` for a disconnected element or a gone owner but still delivers `scroll`; the `glue.js` end listener has no equivalent guard.
  - Physical macOS momentum completion remains unqualified; verify one end after the coast with a real trackpad, as in the existing momentum entry below.
- **`scrollend` on the Linux and Android host: full timing and device qualification**
  (2026-10-15; [LLP 1005, Scroll completion](llp/1005-plan-and-runner-v1.spec.md#scroll-completion)):
  the Presenter now completes moving notches and instant jumps at its next delivery
  point (deferred notches share one completion after their frame's `scroll`),
  reader samples after 100 ms quiet, and owned edge scrolling when it stops.
  Lifetime cancellation and agent settle are covered; apps without handlers retain
  main's behavior. Declared gap: viewport resize can classify end-follow layout
  movement as a new scroll. A 100 px scroller with `scrollFollowEnd`, a child of
  height `100vh`, and a viewport growing from 500 to 600 px emits a completion.
  Remaining: the full deadline-before-later-timers rule and its
  snapshot/reentry budgets, plus Android device qualification. The first step keeps
  Host's catch-up and shell call order. The earlier full implementation remains
  parked on `q1013/scrollend-impl`; use the no-handler differential harness at
  `~/lanes/q1013/wt/scrolldiff` to guard any later timing work.
- **One presenter pass for the batches a frame lands together (iOS)** (2026-10-13, from the
  Bluesky clone's quotes fling): most fling frames apply four batches: the app's `scroll=` batch
  (the clone writes state every frame), one or two list fill slices that `landFill` lands inside
  it to keep the owner's order (T4), and a clock batch. Each that reaches `Presenter.apply`'s full
  pass (not a collections-only slice `applySnapshots` takes, nor a scroll batch `applyUnlessEmpty`
  skips) pays its fixed work whatever it holds: NavigationHost `batchApplied`/`prepare`, `onlyListRows`,
  `syncAccessibility`, `ControlHost.sync`, CollectionHost begin/end, about a quarter of the main
  thread's time in that fling (Time Profiler, 1136 samples). Merging the slices landed inside one
  apply into a single pass would pay it once. It touches the T4 ordering code, so parked until a
  device shows the cost.
- **Physical iPhone HDR qualification** (2026-10-13):
  On the iOS 27 simulator (iPhone 17 Pro), independent Apple API probes
  report `RGBA8Uint` for `.RGBA16Float` and `.gray8Uint` layer format hints,
  headroom 1 instead of 4 for gain-map HDR decodes, retained PQ/HLG for SDR
  decodes, and no ISO gain map when encoding the gain-map fixtures. The
  same requests work on macOS 27.0.1. Each declined assertion group records
  its fixture and observed result; storage, headroom and neutral-pixel
  comparisons still run. See [Apple's format hint](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/quartzcore/calayer/contentsformat)
  and [headroom semantics](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coreimage/ciimage/contentheadroom).
  Physical iOS remains unqualified: run `HDRColorTests`, `HDRImageTests`,
  `PickerColorTests` and `RasterColorTests` on a connected iPhone and require
  no declined assertion groups, skips or expected failures. Their colour
  fixtures live in `host/apple/tests/ExactKitTests/ColorFixtures` as regular
  files, copied into SwiftPM resources and loaded through `Bundle.module`.
  From the repository root, build the device archive and unsigned test bundle
  (the generic iOS build passed without a phone):

  ```sh
  export PATH=$HOME/.bun/bin:$HOME/.local/bin:$HOME/.cargo/bin:/opt/homebrew/bin:$PATH
  export DEVELOPER_DIR=/Applications/Xcode.app/Contents/Developer
  SDKROOT="$(xcrun --sdk iphoneos --show-sdk-path)" \
  HOST_CXXFLAGS="-isysroot $(xcrun --sdk macosx --show-sdk-path)" \
  MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=14.0 IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0 \
  EXACT_UPDATE_TRUST=development HERMES_LEAN_SYS_OFFLINE=1 \
    cargo rustc --crate-type staticlib --profile host-dev -p caltrain-apple --lib --target aarch64-apple-ios
  export EXACT_TESTS=1 EXACT_LIB=caltrain_apple
  export EXACT_LIB_DIR="$PWD/target/aarch64-apple-ios/host-dev"
  cd host/apple
  xcodebuild build-for-testing -scheme Exact -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' \
    -derivedDataPath ../../target/ios-hdr2/device-tests \
    CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO LIBRARY_SEARCH_PATHS="$EXACT_LIB_DIR"
  ```

  `xcodebuild test` cannot run these on a phone: a package's tests are
  tool-hosted, and Xcode refuses that on a device ("Tool-hosted testing is
  unavailable on device destinations", tried 2026-10-13 on an iPhone 17 Pro
  Max). Qualifying them needs a host app for the test bundle (as
  `host/apple/touches.mjs` makes for the UI tests), or a minimal app that runs
  the same ImageIO, Core Image and `CALayer.contentsFormat` probes and is
  launched with `xcrun devicectl device process launch --console` on an
  unlocked phone; a person at the phone is needed either way.
- **The date and place at launch on the remaining hosts** (2026-10-13; Apple, the Rust/Wasm web
  host and the Linux family's app boot boot under them, `exact_runner::time::launching_with`, and
  the JS target answers `exactTime` from the page): the render host, the content-region host,
  direct `Host::boot_stored`, and the Linux presenter's candidate reload (`restore_time` after the
  boot) never enter it, so their first settle reads the defaults (0, `en-US`, `UTC`, seed 0). The
  Linux family's date is sampled before the boot, but each loop's clock zero is its own instant
  (the display loop's `started`, before `Display::open`): the epoch is off by the time between
  them, as it was off by the boot when it was sampled after; pass each loop's zero to the boot.
- **Gaps the Bluesky clone's workers hit** (bsky5, b28–b31, 2026-10-12), as reported:
  - **A dynamic exit animation fails the web build only** (Linux; Charlie,
    2026-10-22, C and D). Support dynamic `-exact-exit-animation` on the JS
    target as ce583715bd (#809) supports state-dependent
    `-exact-layout-transition` (`host/web-js/src/style.rs`, rows and runtime).
    Make `bun exact.mjs contract build` run the JS emitter check for every
    app, including apps without `app.ts` + `app.json` and entries not named
    `app.contract` (`scripts/exact.mjs`, `checkTypes` / `typeDiagnostics`).
    Report emitter refusals as target refusals, not TypeScript errors on
    `app.ts`. Done when the dynamic exit runs on the JS target and a refused
    row fails each of those build forms with a target diagnostic. Not taken:
    moving the `contract` binary to its own package (compiler dependence on
    the web host), or a refusal registry in `contract` (a second authority).
  - **Contract tests:** `back` reports "back refused" on a sheet over a sheet while the route does
    pop; a test can't fake a data source, or open a URL.
  - **Vocabulary:** no vertical scroll snap (the clone hand-built a pager); no direction hand-off
    between nested drags; no single-tap versus double-tap; no "native only" condition; a `text`
    can't hold an inline badge (already in Charlie's asks).
- **More gaps from the clone's workers** (bsky5, b32–b33, 2026-10-12), as reported:
  - Toasts have no warning style; a child component can't hold a resource; `agent` can't hold a
    fetch, so loading states can't be staged; no leftward swipe (`swiperight` only); no `exp`
    function; no hit slop.
- **A dotted deep link whose last label is a file extension** (2026-10-13): the web servers
  answer a navigation to `/profile/jay.bsky.team` with the app's document, but `/profile/photos.zip`
  (`.zip` is a TLD) is taken for a missing file (`FILE_EXTENSIONS`, `scripts/origin.mjs`). Telling
  them apart needs the app's routes on the server: answer the document when the path matches a
  declared route template. External static hosts need their own rewrite either way.
- **macOS `scrollend` and trackpad momentum, unverified** (2026-10-13): `ScrollEndMac.swift` ends a
  live scroll at `NSScrollView.didEndLiveScrollNotification`, taken to come after the momentum
  (AppKit's 10.9 notes say it waits for an animation such as rubber banding, and do not name
  momentum); the tests post the notification themselves. Check with a real trackpad fling: one `scrollend`, after the coast.
- **Selected-document `readdir` and `mkdir` codes on native** (2026-10-13, from the
  compressImage review): since the Ibex refresh (f31c6e2ba), `readdir` and `mkdir` on a chosen
  *file* answer `PermissionDenied` (the prelude's `failed`) where they answered `ENOTDIR` and
  `EEXIST`, which the web still does (`vendor/ibex/crates/ibex2/src/stdlib/documents.rs`).
- **iOS long-press on an inline text run** (bsky5): a menu on a run inside a paragraph (a hashtag:
  See #tag posts, Mute #tag) acts as a tap. `NodeViewIOS.updateContextGestures` gives a long-press
  recognizer to node views only; runs get a menu on macOS (X77) and on the web.
- **The kept-answer cap** (device; LLP 1027 D4; Charlie, 2026-10-22).
  Measure the launch read on a physical phone at the current 256 KiB per
  answer and 512 KiB total (`runner/src/runner/kept.rs`). Record the read
  time in D4. Raise the total toward 1 MiB only if a real app needs it and
  that phone's read stays about 3 ms or less. No per-resource `keep <size>`.
- **Darwin WebSocket close failures under load** (2026-10-16): ibex2's
  `the_darwin_transport_holds_the_whole_conversation`, whose body is `conversation()` in
  `vendor/ibex/crates/ibex2/src/transport/websocket_tests.rs`, failed 3/100 unmodified runs
  with 72 `nice -n 10 yes` workers. A second 100-run loop, with only the failed peer report
  printed, failed once with `/echo gone` instead of a Close report and twice with
  `Closed { code: 1006, reason: "" }` instead of the peer's 1000/`peer`. The current Darwin
  transport marks `closing` under its lock and accounts/discards the following send;
  its concurrent send/close admission-order regression passes. The earlier diagnosis of
  a message arriving before Close does not explain these failures. Investigate the missing
  Close and abnormal peer-close result before changing the assertions. Build with
  `cargo test --manifest-path vendor/ibex/crates/ibex2/Cargo.toml -p ibex2 --lib --no-run`,
  then loop the test binary with `--exact
  transport::darwin_websocket::tests::the_darwin_transport_holds_the_whole_conversation`.
- **Reported iOS hit-testing under a positioned sibling; repro still needed** (bsky5, Bluesky clone
  b28 on 57ab536e3, 2026-10-12): the profile avatar (`position="absolute"`, `profile.contract`
  before clone 1be739e) remained visible, but `agent ios` refused its tap with "node #983 covers
  its middle"; `z-index=1` worked around it. The reported disagreement with the web is unconfirmed.
  On 2ba30e401 (2026-10-13), a small Contract fixture's absolute avatar, exposed info column and
  child each received their taps on Chrome, macOS and iOS. Native buttons, a flat leaf and a
  clipped parent with a shadow also agreed. Adding a positioned descendant to the later column
  raised it by LLP 1083.000's isolation rule on all three hosts: a transparent column left the
  avatar visible but took the tap there, and Chrome and iOS both refused the avatar tap as covered.
  Recover the original distinguishing shape before changing UIKit's hit order.
- **The root's bottom bar during an interactive zoom dismissal** (bsky5, b28): while UIKit's zoom
  (`ModalIOS.swift`, `preferredTransition = .zoom`) is dragged back, the view underneath has no
  tab bar, and it appears when the dismissal ends. Likely the fullscreen presentation's presenting
  view without the `UITabBarController`'s bar, or the bar's visibility following the route stack,
  which still has the photo on top until UIKit finishes.
- **The web JS target's build-time answer at a store holding secrets** (LLP 1027 D4,
  approved 2026-10-10): the Rust runner shows a store reader's declared `else` placeholder
  instead of the bake's empty-store answer when the store holds a granted secret and the
  source is not ready; `rt.js` still shows the build-time answer until its Rust module
  loads. It learns a store reader only from an answer (`ans.store`), so it needs the
  plan's reader marker emitted first.
- **The sky's capture on the phone** landed at the display's rate 2026-08-30 (LLP 1008
  §9): a shadow layer tree rendered by `CARenderer`, a nested canvas's readback cached,
  the texture handed to the module as it is (`gpu_texture_metal`, `gpu_sync`) — 119 fps
  on average scrolling with the sky on, a capture 7.1 ms, from 42–64 fps and 20–25 ms.
  Left: the deck's 48 per-child textures still cross as bytes (a `gpu_child_metal`
  would spare ~30 ms when the deck opens); the macOS presenter still captures on the
  CPU (`cacheDisplay`, 5–18 ms at 2×) — the same `Shadow` would serve it; the
  cross-queue wait (`gpu_sync`, ~3 ms of the 7.1) could be a shared Metal event if
  wgpu exposed one. Tried and declined on the way: capture at 2× (slower), `CARenderer`
  on the live overlay (crashes — a layer is in one tree only), `drawHierarchy` (no gain).
- **Canvas children** → LLP 1014 (RFC, Accepted) — built 2026-08-29, LLP 1014.000
  transcribes it: kernel, corpus, the web wrapper, the macOS overlay and capture with
  D4's four sources, the smoke's steps 6a, 9, and 10 with the readback fixture (native:
  `caltrain-gpu` `tests/children.rs`; per host: `scripts/fixtures/canvas-sky.*.png`,
  cross-host mean 1.48/255 as measured — a first number for 1009 §4.1); the line map's
  stations as children (the second use); the render tail measured and closed (0.5 ms
  steady; occluded windows now skip rendering); Accepted 2026-08-29, the §5 take applied
  (the three gradient rows are out — 1009's proposal for the same door). 2026-08-30:
  the app inside the sky (glass/ink/crt materials, the previous-children crossfade,
  nested canvases by readback) and D5 built — per-child textures, placements with
  depth, hit-testing and accessibility through them, the card deck (LLP 1014.000 §1a,
  §1b); two code reviews folded the same day (§1c: placements are the only place a
  placed child is; `clock`/`tap`/`type` settle the canvases before replying; a resize
  shows at once; the ABI checks its pointers). Left: Chrome's flag as the oracle for
  children *through* a surface; a hermetic test of the glass crossfade; LLP 1013 on
  this machinery. Delete this line then.

- **Menus** → LLP 1021 (RFC, Draft 2026-08-30): the Popover API by its HTML names
  (`popover`, `popovertarget`, `id`, `aria-checked`, `hr`), open state host state like
  scroll, the top layer outside every canvas capture; a menu-shaped popover may present
  as UIMenu/NSMenu with selection dispatching by view id, kernel-painted under
  `EXACT_AGENT`. First consumer: Weird Castle's account switcher (today a hand-rolled
  overlay with no light dismiss). M1 web+kernel · M2 Apple/Linux · M3 the switcher.
  Messages Add now preserves focus; its rich native attachment popover remains owed.
  Three UIKit probes stop short of keyboard-overlaid painting; matching the box
  alone passed falsely (`/tmp/messages-attachment-menu/probe/`, LLP 1021 D2).
- The untested-but-built list in LLP 1010 §5: horizontal scroll on macOS, `overflow:
  hidden` on the web, `hidden`/`visible` on a `ScrollView` on macOS — each a fixture.

## Declared gaps, by system (each spec's "Not in v1")

- **Kernel / layout** (LLP 1001, 1010): `position: fixed`; `text_align: start`;
  presentation transforms in scrollable overflow; `overscroll-behavior`; scroll
  snapping beyond the admitted horizontal subset; Linux scroll events.
- **Durable state** (LLP 1018 §5, built 2026-08-30 for Weird Castle's token): the
  `plain` tier — persisted slots, with Caltrain's "last station"; `me`; the iOS
  first-launch keychain wipe; the data-protection keychain on macOS (a bundle and a
  profile); the Linux file store when that host links ibex2.
- **Plan / runner** (LLP 1005 §8): per-instance derives or resources inside `each`.
- **Contract** (LLP 1006 §8): inlining budget; `contract` blocks as assertions;
  `cursor`; per-instance state; LSP.
- **Web host** (LLP 1007 §9): a text-measurement bridge (the kernel's layout does not
  run on the web — and only grid has a kernel-against-browser layout check,
  `host/web-js/conformance/grid.contract` with `--linux`; a general layout parity
  corpus would be the analog of `parity.mjs`); scroll position across a reload; a
  spring interrupted by an easing.
- **Apple host** (LLP 1008 §7): images; toggles; accessibility beyond `testId`,
  `accessibilityLabel` and macOS's button/link/image/heading roles (iOS has no
  image or header trait); justified text; rubber-banding on inner
  scroll nodes; a generated header. **iOS** (LLP 1008 §9): the agent API on a
  phone (`build.mjs --device --run` installs over USB or Wi-Fi, but nothing drives
  the app there); a
  synthesized touch for the agent's `tap` (UIKit's hit-test and the responder-chain
  rule today); a pan chaining out of a nested scroll view at its edge (UIKit's own;
  the agent's wheel chains).
- **The keyboard's blink on a field hand-off** (iOS): UIKit rebuilds the accessory bar
  when first responder moves between two `UITextField`s — one bar-less frame (308 →
  335), reproduced in a from-scratch two-field app with any traits and by re-traiting
  one field in place; a phone shows it faintly. Safari does not, so WebKit hides it
  somewhere below the responder (its `UITextInput`-conforming content view and its own
  input-assistant handling). Worth finding out how, if a form ever needs it gone
  (LLP 1008 §9).
- **Linux host** (LLP 1015 §7): evdev has carried no real event yet; `canvas`; libinput and
  xkbcommon (acceleration, touchpad gestures, hotplug, non-US keymaps); Wayland/X11
  windows; selection, IME, a caret blink; `text_decoration`, `font_family`, RTL;
  shadows, gradients, grid; JPEG, image URLs; accessibility; a font cache; text pixel
  fixtures against Chrome (the Linux tests pin a font, `host/linux/tests/pinned/`; Chrome draws the system's).
- **GPU** (LLP 1009 §4): readback bands for blending and MSAA; a generated module ABI
  once there is a second consumer; the `Custom(u16)` animatable-property extension
  when a surface needs it.

## Later

- **Shared elements, stage 2** (LLP 1013.000 §3; moved here 2026-10-19 as the take
  for LLP 1020 §11's iframe host updates, `rules/DEFERRED.md`; stage 1 built
  2026-10-04: the `sharedElement` attribute, live native flights on iOS/macOS, CSS
  View Transitions on the web's JS target, `apps/shared-elements`). Waits for a named
  consumer: velocity carried into a flight (a release's, an interrupted flight's: Q5),
  drag-to-dismiss handing a hold to the flight back, a name as the zoom's source
  (`navigationSource`), flights on Linux and on the wasm web target, and a prop
  changed on a live node pairing.

- **Web view rows: memory against SwiftUI** (2026-09-28, LLP 1020 §10, LLP 1068 §5.2; moved here 2026-10-23 as the take for LLP 1020.002, the browser shell, `rules/DEFERRED.md`). The feed's web view rows now tie SwiftUI on the iPhone (97.6 against 97.4 fps) and beat it on the iPad (100.5 against 95.8). Footprint is still higher: 70–76 MB against SwiftUI's 38–44 on the iPhone, 61–82 against 43–50 on the iPad. The two oddities are noise. Per-run peaks on the iPad are bimodal (about 62 or 81 MB) with and without near-only leaves, so the medians of three moved with the draw. System Trace's WebContent launch counts vary by ±100 between runs of one build (SwiftUI 290 and 479). A leaf, once made, stays until its row retires two viewports away, so it cannot churn at the quarter-viewport margin. Where the extra ~30 MB lives (the app's WebKit proxies, the text rasters, the pool) is unmeasured.

- **A followed end resolved by the host (deferred, 2026-10-05).** The
  proposal on `ide/doc/followed-end-one-target`
  (`issues/20261005-one-target-for-a-followed-end.md`): the correction names
  the end, and the host fits the extent with its own laid-out rows. LLP 1010
  §6.8's 2026-10-05 note does the cheap half instead: the measured row
  arrives 0.5 to 4 ms after the estimate, so a target before the motion's
  first frame is its one target. Take this up only if a measured report
  round trip is ever longer than a frame. Against it: a wire change on every
  host, and the extent with two sources of truth, so a later runner extent
  that disagrees (f32 rounding, a row whose measurement a cross-size check
  skipped) moves `contentSize` again at the end.

- **Chrome conformance steps for Paste and Match Style and for a clipboard event with nothing focused** (2026-10-21, PR #901; moved here 2026-10-23 as the take for LLP 1133.006's clipboard `write`, `rules/DEFERRED.md`). The driver's `type <id> paste` dispatches a synthetic `ClipboardEvent` at the focus or the target, so it never reaches `<body>`, and it has no Paste and Match Style; both were checked by hand in Chrome over CDP (`Input.dispatchKeyEvent` with `commands: ["paste"]` and `["pasteAndMatchStyle"]`). A driver operation for an edit command, sent as CDP's `commands`, would let `host/web-js/conformance` hold them.

- The serial runtime owner + env()/keyboard lane is parked on `parked/runtime-owner`
  (2583be3, base 17350d0 — pre-fonts) and the main worktree is clean of it. LLP 1022
  holds the measurements (fails the macOS smoke ~6 of 8; macOS boot +26 ms for a
  shell frame; iOS −27 ms from the prepare overlap), the four cherry-picks worth
  taking without the owner thread, and the conditions for reviving it. A revival

- LLP 1023 Stage 1 leftovers, each small: the physical-iPhone typed-URL run (the

- Messages fractional borders: compare device-pixel snapping at 1×/2×/3×. The shared `none`/`hidden`/`solid` semantics, default width and `currentColor` are implemented; Messages and the other existing bordered apps now declare solid explicitly. Native style/color switches and Fieldnotes web borders pass, and the Messages iOS comparison is pixel-identical (`/tmp/messages-border-semantics/`, LLP 1001 §1).

- Messages native ownership: authored `inert` now reaches the compiler and real DOM attribute; iOS subtree input/focus/accessibility, preserved geometry/drafts and restoration pass. Native confirmation now escapes inert ancestors and survives its invoker becoming inert; iOS/browser cases and four Messages cases per host pass. The third/final browser candidate passes explicit focus and inspection inside escaped modal dialogs, single confirmation dispatch, basic/active-route input exclusion and four rebuilt Messages cases. Forty browser identity/frame observations remain unchanged. AppKit/Linux, wider VoiceOver/IME/drag cases and implicit modal-inertness reporting remain (LLP 1035.001 D3; `/tmp/messages-inert-ownership/verification.json`). Back lookup now stays within the selected route; native pop/sheet permission and browser Escape checks pass, including refusal with retained sheet editing (`/tmp/messages-back-owner/`, LLP 1035.001 D1). Completed-sheet cleanup now releases the old modal-navigation slot after UIKit returns; retained/replacement routes and mid-gesture permission changes pass six physical cases (`/tmp/messages-dismissal-owner/`). Revocation after native commitment re-presents the selected route with its keyboard closed; continuous editing through that outcome remains unestablished. Unmount/destruction during a physical sheet drag now retire native owners without cancelling retained Compose; offscreen route replacement and queued focus survive remount (`/tmp/messages-unmounted-owner/`). Moving to a different UIKit controller in the same window now passes, including transfer during a physical sheet drag and queued focus after offscreen route replacement (`/tmp/messages-reparent-owner/`). Fullscreen/source ownership is now implemented under the approved LLP 1035.001 Messages exception; exact motion matching remains. A fresh native recording shows details zooming from the contact photo; a public UIKit over-full-screen zoom plus nested page sheet reproduces bar y=88 / field y=416 and passes cancellation, permission, completion and missing-source Back (`/tmp/messages-presentation-ancestry/`). R4/R5 pass the Messages flow, ten physical ownership cases, source replacement, nested teardown/reload and browser Escape (`/tmp/messages-fullscreen/`); one authorized continuation attempt remains unused.

- Investigate intermittent Linux update test `a_refused_initial_layout_releases_no_network_requests`: the workspace run observed one request after refused layout; isolated and full-workspace reruns passed (Messages compose validation, 2026-09-09).

- Messages symbols (1035.004): twelve schema-generated roles now render through native image leaves and browser masks; Messages has 19 symbol occurrences, and Fieldnotes uses Add/Close. Copy, Select and More now use native 17/400 images matching the reference’s intrinsic sizes; six physical icon taps and six browser actions pass with unchanged action frames and retained drafts. Reference-mask overlap improves, but exact raster alignment and Reply artwork remain open (`/tmp/messages-action-symbols/verification.json`). iOS physical controls and both apps’ web controls pass; natural/fixed/live-source fixtures pass on Apple/web. Finish pinned Messages glyph sizing/weight and VoiceOver comparison, material support reporting, and AppKit pixels (its current captures are transparent). Linux symbols remain unsupported (`/tmp/messages-symbol-integration/verification.json`). A stock-glass-button prototype passes eight physical actions and supplies native foreground treatment, but its Close raster error is worse than production; three calibration rounds stopped, no projection landed. Resolve that rendering difference before extending the native control boundary (`/tmp/messages-glass-button-candidate/verification.json`).
- Messages inbox gestures: finish trailing full-swipe confirmation, recovery styling/filter coverage, exact glyph size/artwork and transition comparison. Recently Deleted now archives individual/whole-thread deletions and provides local recovery/permanent deletion through the existing menu and confirmation owners; data order/reaction/reply retention and browser/iOS activation pass, but ordinary native return to Messages fails with a loading menu after recovery (`/tmp/messages-recovery/`). The three-candidate application loop is stopped; the source remains an incomplete candidate. Recover’s native default action remains black/gray rather than the reference’s blue; the browser selection outline and agent-mode ordinary-menu presentation also remain mismatched. Native partial and full deletion both ask first and cancel on outside tap; a public UIAlertController fixture proves deferred swipe completion/cancellation but differs in anchoring. Three presentation rounds stopped (the third failed compilation; its captures ran the preceding binary), preserved at `/tmp/messages-thread-deletion/`. No confirmation change landed. Explicit UIKit swipe rows now pass leading full Read/Unread, reversal, partial actions, Mute/Unmute, Delete, row switching, first-tap dismissal, draft/history retention and parent vertical scrolling (`/tmp/messages-swipe-activation/`). Callback eligibility follows the original authored ancestors, excluding UIKit’s temporarily disabled cell. Native action inspection and agent activation pass; the browser retains its authored controls. First-child scale/rotation capture and the pinned light/dark action colors are corrected (`/tmp/messages-swipe-glyphs/`); exact symbol paths remain owed. Open actions still retain their earlier appearance: two adaptive-image attempts passed action checks but corrupted surrounding avatar lettering and were removed at the repair limit. The ordinary light-to-dark control has pixel-identical surrounding rows with and without icon scaling; that comparison clears the scale change, not the adaptive experiment. Isolate the rendering failure before another adaptive capture attempt. Named inbox controls now retain their accessibility names and activation through native swipe cells; public activation and stale filtered-cell refusal pass, alongside eight physical swipe cases (`/tmp/messages-swipe-accessibility/verification.json`). Full VoiceOver interaction remains unverified. Real iOS gesture input still uses the temporary foreground-guarded helper.

- Apple line-clamp: the missing ellipsis is repaired and visible on a long Messages quote. Remaining: the token takes the last visible run's style instead of the paragraph's (the mixed bold/red link fixture differs in both token appearance and retained characters); the browser fixture does not show its right-aligned token. Compare those boundaries before claiming full parity (`/tmp/messages-line-clamp/verification.json`). Padding itself now paints at the content origin and wraps at its content width.

- Apple baseline precision: the kernel preserves fractional frames and `TextEngine` preserves authored fractional line heights, but intrinsic widths, normal paragraph heights and painted baselines still round to logical points. A same-font iOS 26.5 UILabel/CoreText/WebKit fixture found that removing that rounding improves some native labels but does not consistently match either UIKit or WebKit. Resolve line-box/baseline placement with a targeted comparison before changing painting globally; Removing all measurement ceilings changed Caltrain’s established scroll extent and canvas readback; investigate those intrinsic metrics separately. Authored line-height precision alone does not establish glyph fidelity.

- **Continuous release loop: bundle-only deploy rows** (LLP 1030.003 §6 Q2;
  Charlie, 2026-10-22). Make `scripts/deploy.mjs` classify against a signed

- **Continuous release loop: foreground interval checks** (LLP 1030.003 §6

- Shared-target filesystem helper: the ada0b93 workspace sweep failed with `spawnSync .../exact-filesystem ENOENT`; the working checkout’s async reader also intermittently returned 404 when its helper exited after one request. Reproduce helper executable identity/lifecycle across worktrees before claiming a full green sweep; evidence in `/tmp/interview-snapback4-20260913/exact2-checks/` and `/tmp/interview-snapback4-20260913/ui/`.
  LLP 1039's web smoke passed every assertion but retained its `exact-filesystem --serve-reads` child after printing success; closing that recorded child let Bun exit 0. Close the helper at the end of a finite bake/smoke process (`/tmp/lane-router/1039/smoke-web.log`, `launched-pids.txt`).

- Native development URL latency: Charlie observes about one second for Update Lab Contract edits on the physical iPhone while localhost clients update almost immediately (2026-09-14). Notification already uses SSE. `PlanURL` now fetches up to four verified payloads concurrently after the envelope; it still creates a fresh URLSession per fetch and refetches unchanged payloads. A physical-phone sample measured envelope 56.5 ms, payload fetches 1,347.8 ms, and prepare/commit 32.1 ms (`target/update-lab-phone-timing.json`). Payload fetching dominates this sample; isolate connection setup versus transfer, then consider a shared session and verified reuse by digest. Preserve byte limits, complete-generation validation and failed-candidate retention.

- Update completion event (Charlie, 2026-09-14): expose one app lifecycle notification after an admitted generation commits and its executors are ready, covering Contract, TypeScript and Rust. Include which artifact digests changed; refused/superseded candidates emit nothing, and initialization stays after first pixel. Update Lab now uses its existing 250 ms task to show executed probe versions automatically; an event would remove that sampling delay without making real apps poll. Define the shared Contract lifecycle hook before adding separate host callbacks.

- Worker placement follow-ups (LLP 1027.002, landed 2026-09-14): drive the placed Fieldnotes on an iOS simulator through the shared Apple host; a macOS frame-gap instrument (a display link's callback cadence in `state`) so §5 step 5 is judged on the device, not only in Chrome; a forgotten ticket's held call still takes its turn (D5 says it may be removed before it starts) — tell the composer when the runner forgets; Rust on a web Worker waits behind a consumer (the DEFERRED take); the development producer refreshes a Rust-owned resource's first-frame value only at a Cargo bake, and refuses a Rust source shared by several resources until then.

- Rust replacement latency follow-up (2026-09-14, LLP 1029.000): native image preparation now runs off the UI thread and the bake helper stays resident. Six Rust edit/restore cycles reached web, macOS, simulator and iPhone with carried state; three distinct new libraries took 2.6–4.6 s save-to-observed, with macOS probe round trips ≤48 ms. First executable launches can still wait on macOS assessment (including the filesystem helper at dev-server startup). Measure the remaining compiler/producer overhead toward the subsecond warm-edit target; the 20-edit timing and 50-replacement retention probes remain owed. Evidence: `target/update-lab-distinct-repeat-result.json`, `target/update-lab-baker-reuse-check.log`. Later normal-app preparation took 17.6 s behind a ~31 s load. Isolated real-library loads reproduced 70.7 s ad-hoc and 39.1 s development-signed inside `dlopen`, versus <1 ms write/sync in the ad-hoc case. Gatekeeper assessment and concurrent 30 s `syspolicyd` QUIC timeouts are recorded in `target/native-bottleneck-security.log`; loader stage diagnostics now separate queue/write/sync/load. Signing alone is not a fix. The opt-in stateless `tiered` executor now runs the new Wasm while native mapping proceeds, then promotes without a second reload (LLP §4.1); this leaves the OS loading cause and producer timing as follow-ups. A fresh Rust save in the Launch Services app then published in 2.93 s but spent 103.9 s in `dlopen`; the main thread stayed in its event loop. Restore preparation reused the mapping in 19.8 ms, with a separate 36.2 s producer/publication delay still to attribute. The tiered live proof subsequently passed with state/TS retained: 25 ms candidate preparation on two saves, plus 36 ms preparation while a normal-app native load took 54.3 s. Web/simulator/iPhone save-and-restore also passed; the earlier JS-engine-not-loaded diagnostic did not recur in the native traces (`target/update-lab-tiered-result.json`, `target/update-lab-tiered-cross-host-result.json`). Cold helper launches and shared Cargo locks still made initial builds take minutes; this is separate from tiered activation.

Reproduce the cancelled file operation/reload interaction before claiming the full workspace sweep green. Evidence: `target/router-test.log`, `target/router-test-retry.log` in `exact2-wt-router`.

- Interview router row 4 (LLP 1038 F10): seed an isolated agent-mode replica for the own-data/scroll swipe drive; first finish the static fixture’s held swipes with Simulator unobscured (other desktop apps blocked contact).
- Interview iPad-wide iOS (LLP 1038): drive the rail on a real wide simulator viewport; the iPhone agent ignores `--size` and reports 402 × 874 for both requested sizes.
- Interview backend list arguments (LLP 1038): let `/state` accept question/person/post ID lists and return every stacked record; the pre-replica path currently sends only each list’s first ID.

- **Re-measure VideoArm's position read with many autoplaying videos** (2026-10-09; moved here 2026-10-23 as the take for LLP 1133.003's video decode, `rules/DEFERRED.md`): VideoArm reads `currentTime` from the item's timebase instead of `-[AVPlayer currentTime]`, landed unmeasured from the Bluesky clone's round-1 `sample` (main in `VideoArm.send` → `-[AVPlayerItem currentTime]` waiting on a MediaToolbox lock, about 124 ms in 3 s of a Discover fling, gone with autoplay off). Neither today's Discover nor bsky4's 48-video fixture (`measure.py --feed-fixture videos`) reproduced it: 12 ms of VideoArm work on main in 30 s, no `currentTime` samples. Re-measure on a feed where many players autoplay at once, ideally on a phone, comparing Time Profiler samples under `VideoArm.send`.

# Graceful-overload consumers (2026-09-16)

LLP 1041 starts `messages-stress` and `completion-storm` as opt-in synthetic
workloads. Next: compare idle/loaded interaction samples on a real 120 Hz device,
then implement bounded completion pumping and admission/fairness where measured.
Keep module placement under LLP 1027.002; automatic windowing/image pressure and
physical-display native sweeps remain follow-ups; the first slice drives AppKit
and the Linux headless CPU renderer without claiming display frame timing.

- Linux textflow follow-up (LLP 1043.000 M3): carry resolved exclusions in immutable content-region worker requests and their accepted artifact identity. Ordinary definite-height text now flows; the opt-in content-region publication still owns its original unflowed geometry. Linux has no native text-selection UI, decoration painter or inline-link hit geometry yet; future consumers must use the accepted paragraph's fragments and original run metadata. Add discretionary-hyphen ink and clamp ellipsis to the flowed path (M3 clips at the band cap); justify currently uses start.

- Apple textflow follow-up (LLP 1043.000 M4): the shared walker now paints native paragraphs on macOS/iOS. A resolved flowed leaf retires an opt-in opaque content-region raster and returns the whole registration to native views, until a new registration. Carry exclusions and fragment geometry through the worker artifact before retaining region rastering for moving shapes; the current fallback trades that worker scheduling for correct fresh ink.

- Web textflow size review (LLP 1043.000 §6.2, M5): optimized textflow-web grew from the observed pre-M5 834,019 bytes to 905,398 bytes (+71,379), above the approximate 64 KB revisit threshold. Caltrain is currently 1,022,149 bytes; the orchestrator has the pre-program comparison. Review code/data size while preserving the ruling that the shared crate stays linked. Evidence: `target/textflow-scratch/m5/size-baseline.json`, `wasm-probe.json`.
- Public UI coverage (LLP 1035.006): select the first settings slice (labels, buttons, switches, checkboxes, radio groups, and text entry), choose each platform's disposition, then implement and hand-check the concrete examples. The ordered catalog distinguishes accepted support from reviewed deferrals; drafting it does not admit every excluded capability.

  explicit row-height list and upstream `virtualized` collection both build.
  Carry logical selection/full-document copy to the collection path, compare

  Do not infer a performance gain merely from combining the branches.

- Every deploy recompiles its app crates (2026-09-23, deploy lane): their build
  scripts write the run's receipts into a per-run `EXACT_BAKE_OUTPUT` they watch, so
  an unchanged deploy still reruns them and recompiles `caltrain-web`, both
  `caltrain-apple`s and `caltrain-linux` with LTO; nothing else rebuilds, and a warm
  Caltrain deploy took 265–450 s at load ~25. A receipt directory per Cargo cache,
  copied into the run after the build, would let an unchanged deploy reuse them. The
  `exact-filesystem` helper also rebuilds inside each deploy's capture, where its tool
  target sits.
- Default deploys republish the web root (2026-09-24, deploy lane): two cold deploys
  of unchanged Caltrain left every native stream `current` but changed the
  `.exact/install/` pages, which carry their build time, and `app.wasm` and
  `gpu_bg.wasm`, which recorded the run's target path until 113abeaa remapped it.
  Browsers fetch the new root; native clients are unaffected. The Rust module build
  remaps only its target directory: an app outside this repo with a module (none
  yet) would also need the capture root its exact2 path dependencies arrive under
  remapped, as `wasmRemapFlags` does for web builds.

- **macOS Cargo test launch directory (2026-10-02):** the Linux host's shared native transport tests time out before connecting when launched inside this checkout's large `target/debug/deps` directory. A sampled stack is in `CFBundleCopyBundleLocalizations` → `_CFIterateDirectory` while CFNetwork creates its first session, not in the executor or socket read. The identical binary in an otherwise empty temporary directory passes all 14 stream tests in 0.49 s; a fresh build with the original timeouts passes the full library suite (473 passed, one ignored). Keep the existing deadlines; isolate the native test executable's launch directory or address the build-directory accumulation. The two content-region budget failures found alongside these are fixed: their fixture explicitly positions the registered owner, as LLP 1074 requires. Evidence: `/tmp/exact-followup-stream-stack.txt`, `/tmp/exact-followup-stream-isolated.log`, `/tmp/exact-followup-region-final.log`.

- **Gaps the Ocho phone app hit on iOS** (2026-10-03, `apps/ocho-mobile` on branch `ocho/client`; each worked around in the app):
  - Data-source replies are parsed and committed on the main thread (`Session.swift`'s wake → `pump`): an 800 KB JSON reply every few seconds dropped frames while scrolling.
  - A re-ask with new arguments drops the request in flight and its reply is never parsed (LLP 1016 D5), but nothing tells a source that keeps its own in-flight flag; an equal request is kept (LLP 1054.000.000 D3), so a source's retry of a hung request waits on the same one.
  - `transition` cannot animate `filter` (only translate/scale/rotate/opacity); a `filter` entry in the list silently disables the whole transition.
  - A native module view cannot report its intended size; a growing composer reports height through `message` and the app sizes its box.
  - `backgroundMaterial="glass"` is a glass effect behind an Exact box, not UIKit's glass controls (`UIButton.Configuration.glass()`, glass containers that merge); the app uses native module views for buttons and its composer.
  - A back swipe painted only the part of the incoming route visible as it began; fixed by painting each frame of the transition, but a device report says half the screen still fills in late.

- **LLP 1097's parity fixture** (2026-10-04, impl/1097, §5). `host/web-js/conformance/storage-background` (a TypeScript source driven on the wasm target, the JS target and Linux, comparing journals and `state.background`) was not built: no TypeScript app with storage grants has a Linux host for `conform.mjs --linux`, and a fixture app with web and Linux crates would be new apparatus. - **Native macOS held-contact cancellation (2026-10-07, LLP 1104 F6):** down now records ownership and replies before AppKit tracking, and queued move/up ends tracking with one activation. `AgentMouseFormsMacTests.testHeldNativeButtonContactAcknowledgesDownThenMovesReleasesAndCancels` still observes `[1, 1]` after up then cancel, where `[1]` is required, despite a queued drag-away before cancel's release. Three fix rounds exhausted. Finish the cancel path and verify queued coordinates on an offset window; evidence: `target/f6-swift-after3.log`.

- **A substituted pick on the Firefox and WebKit carriers arrives empty** (2026-10-08, found driving LLP 1069.002 A1 through a scratch app): `type @1 <file>` answers the held `showPicker` (`device pick 1 answered: 1 file` in the journal), but the input's `change` hands the action `[]` on `agent firefox` and `agent webkit`, where `agent web` (Chrome) hands it the `Picked` record. The Playwright carrier's file delivery (`scripts/agent-playwright.mjs`) is the place to look.

- **An answer's storage step whose 30 s wait runs out lets the queue move on while it may still land** (2026-10-08, Astra's code review of LLP 1069.002 A1): `__exact_storage_failed` (`js/src/prelude.js`) settles the head and issues the next operation for any operation; an awaited `atomicWriteFile` stalled in `sync` past the wait can then rename over a write queued behind it. A1 made this safe for `compressImage` only (its right to write is taken away); other writes keep `origin/main`'s behaviour. The answer's remaining queued operations are also left with no owner to deliver them.
- **The Mac's sidebar does not follow a state-driven change of the tab bar's placement while it shows** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D3). Whether the author left the bar's placement unsaid is the kernel's to read (`exact_root_tabs`), since `flex_direction` does not cross to the Apple presenter. The host asks again when the root's tabs change, when the root's or the tablist's props or style are written, and when the root's level moves (a resize). A root that turns from a column to a row with nothing moving, the tablist being covered, is not seen until then. The web's `unsaidBar` is as sticky once a root is a sidebar. One way: let `flex_direction` cross for a navigation root.
- **View ▸ Show Sidebar follows the first window's session** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D3): `ShortcutHost.sync` shows or hides the item for the session the main menu was built with. An app with a window a document and a root tab bar in only some of them would show it wrongly.
- **A route pushed over a modal route is painted in the Mac's window, and so is the modal one** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D7.4): a sheet holds one route (`NavigationHost.sheetRoutes`; the log says so). SwiftUI's sheet holding a `NavigationStack` shows the pushed screen in the sheet with a Back beside its title. The sheet's content would be the top route of the modal route's own segment (`NavigationRules.segments`), and its title row would need Back.
- **In a Mac sheet in Dark Mode a ground an author wrote, and the grouped card's fill, are a window's** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D7.4): AppKit draws a sheet's ground lighter than a window's (about `#2d2b2a` over `#1e1e1e`), with a material no colour matches. A box given the window's background that no author wrote lets it show (`NodeView.standsOnSheetGround`); one whose author wrote `-exact-background` (a bar that covers what scrolls under it) paints the window's darker colour, and the grouped card's fill (`grouped-card-fill`, `#272727`, measured on a window) is darker than the sheet's ground where SwiftUI's is lighter (`#333030` on `#2b2727`). A box backed by AppKit's own sheet material would close the first; the second is a metric that knows its surface.
- **The Mac route sheet's two buttons carry no test id in `tree --ax`** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D7.4): they are AppKit's buttons standing for the authored Cancel and Save, which are hidden; a tap by test id reaches them (`native: "NSButton"`), but the accessibility tree names them by title only, as the window toolbar's items are not walked at all.
- **A Mac session whose first screen is a modal route may show it painted until its next batch** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D7.4; read in the code, not run): navigation names a route a sheet only while its view is in a window, and nothing asks again when the window arrives (`ExactView.viewDidMoveToWindow` could call `NavigationHost.resync`, once beginning a sheet on a window not yet on screen is shown to work).
- **The Mac driver's `resize` under a sidebar names the window, not the page** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D3): a window with a sidebar has a full-size content view, so `resize 900x700` (`NSWindow.setContentSize`, AgentMac.swift `resizeWindow`) makes the window 900 by 700 and the page 756 by 660, where `--size 900x700` at launch leaves the page 756 by 700 (the window keeps the height under its bars when the sidebar is composed) and a window with no sidebar gives the page all of 900 by 700. Either say it in the driver's help or size the content so that `contentLayoutRect` has the height asked for.
- **A Mac header with a subtitle, an avatar or a pressable title stays painted** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D2, "what the build settled"): iOS's bar shows the first text after the heading as a subtitle, a button drawn as a filled box as an image item, and a pressable around the heading as a tapped title; the Mac's toolbar has no place for them yet, so each keeps the whole header in the page. `NSWindow.subtitle` is the first (measured in the research: 13-point title over an 11-point subtitle, the bar still 52 points); the second is `BadgeFace` drawn for an `NSToolbarItem`; the third has no AppKit shape and may stay.
- **A Mac toolbar item whose popover is neither a menu nor an alert keeps its header painted** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D2): such a popover is placed against its button's box, which a bar item does not have. Stage 5's `NSPopover` can be shown relative to a toolbar item, and then the button can be an item.
- **The web page as the document's scroller** (2026-10-12, LLP 1116.011 D3, cut in r2; recipe-finder, rounds 7 and 13: "Content scrolls in an inner container rather than the document"). A route's page-filling scroller could be the document's, so the address bar, find, keyboard scrolling and full-page capture behave as on any website; it ships only with fixed chrome staying fixed, LLP 1038's per-route offsets kept, the content box iOS has, push-at-top and Back restoration, split panes scrolling apart and a sheet's scroll isolated (LLP 1116.005 D3 chose per-route scrollers for those reasons).

- **EX8 pre-load mutation counter (2026-10-16, Muse round 3):** the web runtime
  increments the diagnostic mutation counter before refusing a send while its
