- **Android Canvas transitions still expose partially cleared UI regions** (2026-10-17, Pixel 5). The Android host now retries only owed windows, records direct eligibility after rendering, and copies the current SurfaceView image before changing composition. Missing interior regions remain: 67/2,181 analyzed frames on `09f82dc75`, 61/2,106 after the host fixes (35-second seven-phase recordings, first five seconds excluded; this is not a meaningful improvement). The cold baseline also aborted in `vendor/wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/swapchain/native.rs:415`, destroying an acquire semaphore still held by a SurfaceTexture; the warmed baseline completed. GPU lifetime and HWUI buffer ownership remain to investigate with the GPU lane; no GPU files or detach waiting changed here. Rotation and background restoration recover; the native alert dims the world and HUD together. Local evidence: `target/castle-fixhost/{baseline-warm,seven-final}.mp4`, `*-frames-pixels.json`, `*-frames-contact.png`, `before-full-logcat.txt`, `lifecycle-result.json`, and `alert-open.png`. With the module settling a canvas before buffers replace its window (castle-fixgpu2, `2c7f7c97f`), the same recording finds 67/2,079: no change. In it and the host lane's two the bad frames fall throughout the three buffered phases (80% width, opacity 0.5, corner radius; 4 to 18 a 2.2-second phase), not at the switches between SurfaceView and buffers, and the missing regions are rectangular blocks of the page background inside the world, as if the reader drew a buffer whose frame the GPU had not finished or had begun again. A lead: patch 8 enables `VK_EXT_queue_family_foreign`, but nothing releases a buffer's image to `VK_QUEUE_FAMILY_FOREIGN_EXT` after its frame, which Vulkan's rules for external memory ask for before another API reads the image; another is a reader drawing a slot after the ring has freed it. Evidence: `target/castle-fixgpu2/` in that lane's worktree (`seven.mp4`, `seven-frames-pixels.json`, `seven-frames-contact.png`).
- **A game's web build is GPU-bound at about half its native rate on a Pixel 5** (2026-10-17, the Castle Town port). Its WebGPU timers put a frame at 17–22 ms (forward pass 15–20 ms) in Android WebView 136 and Chrome 155, where the native build presents every 11.1 ms frame; the CPU side is about 5 ms. Time the native build's passes the same way and compare pass by pass (Dawn's robust buffer access, WGSL's lack of a forced early depth test, attachments that cannot be transient where the browser lacks `TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT`, and no `shader-f16` in WebView 136 are candidates, none measured).

- Route Apple dev-client table-only generations to running GPU canvases, preserving world and tick; current whole-generation delivery resets surfaces (1127.001 D3).

- **Edit-loop acceptance still open (LLP 1127.001):** D2's two-second check figure is unqualified; D4 remains gated on the scene-language ruling; D5's SDK seed is unpublished. None is a pass.
- **`line-clamp: auto`** (2026-10-17, LLP 1126.000 WP4). A hand-built widget's `Text(...).lineLimit(4)` under a `Spacer` shows as many lines as fit the height and ellipsizes the last; Exact's `line-clamp=4` shows four and overflows the widget at XXXL (the open-question fixture's title and summary, `target/wp4/out-ios/widget-medium-answered-fullColor-light-XXXL.png` against the baseline's). CSS Overflow 4's `line-clamp: auto` (Chrome 141) is the web's answer; the kernel lacks it.

- **Linux parallel unit-test worker exhaustion** (2026-10-18, EX10 qualification): `cargo test -p exact-linux --lib --tests --no-fail-fast` exceeds the native executor worker limit under parallel scheduling. Plain upstream `511bdf55d` reproduces the failure class (five library failures; EX10 had eight, with varying test names). The EX10 library suite passes with `-- --test-threads=1`: 1,044 passed, 11 ignored. Isolate the shared worker budget so the ordinary parallel suite is reliable.

- **Scaffold test fixtures omit runtime dependencies** (2026-10-18, EX10 qualification): all 30 failed cases in a combined app-workspace, new-app, agent-launch and install-page run also fail on plain `49dd513fe`. Fixture SDK copies omit imports such as `linked-plan.mjs` and `port-lock.mjs`; the sibling-app dependency map also lacks `exact-apple-update`. Keep the fixture SDKs and dependency map complete when runtime imports change.
- **Touch smoke's grouped detail button sometimes leaves its counter unchanged** (2026-10-19, LLP 1126.001 WP14). At merge base `c5fefb2692`, both `smoke-touch.mjs --build` forms fail `info` with `false 0 0` instead of `false 1 0`, then `open` with `false 0 1` instead of `false 1 1`, on iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 27.0. The touch lands on `_UICollectionViewListAccessoryControl`. The WP14 explicit-device run reproduces both failures; its fresh-device run passes. Investigate the native accessory's action delivery.
- **The activity gallery's expanded leading width can preserve a label the Island truncates** (2026-10-19, LLP 1126.001 WP14). Caltrain's departure at Mountain View reports `Mountain View` in the gallery tree but `Mountain…` in SpringBoard's expanded element label on iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 27.0. Qualify the gallery's proposal against the system's region before extending the archive comparison beyond its Palo Alto fixture.
- **Two activities of one app show one at a time on the simulator** (2026-10-18, LLP 1126.001 WP10). With Caltrain's departure and service-alert activities both active, the extension rendered both (each from its own plan, `dev.exact.widgets` lines for both kinds in one process), but SpringBoard's Island and the Notification Center stack showed only the most recent one; ending it showed the other. Whether a device shows the second as the minimal view beside the first (the double Island) is WP15's device visual.

- **Notarize the granted helper** (2026-10-18, from issues/closed/20261009-macos-release-digest-entitlements.md): run the closed ticket's 2026-10-16 proof with a Developer ID Application identity and a notary profile, so a notarized bundle's granted helper gets `MAP_JIT` and the ungranted copy is refused. Until it passes, `docs/reference.md` claims no notarized support for helper exceptions.

- `perf <target> during` must carry a world incarnation and recording generation and refuse across remount or restore with equal counters (`scripts/agent-inspect.mjs`, `scripts/agent.mjs`).
- **A rendered JS page's extra faces of one font file, served uncached** (2026-10-21, the clone width fix's round 1, Grok). The JS target writes a face per declared weight, each an `@font-face` with `font-display: optional` (src/faces.rs). One variable file declared at several weights (the clone's Inter: 400, 500, 600, 700) is one preload, which serves one face; the other faces fetch the file again at their first use. The client shell now loads every face before its first render (build.mjs's main.js), but a rendered page (`--render`) paints its text before the module runs: served `no-store`, the other weights miss that paint and keep the fallback for the page view. A normal cache makes the second fetch a hit, so it shows with a no-store server (dev, conformance) or a cache-busting one. A fix: one `@font-face` per file with a weight range (`font-weight: 400 700`) for a variable file, one fetch for every weight, though an undeclared weight between them would then draw at its own `wght` where the wasm host's nearest declared face draws it at that face's.
- **A shaped inline run's `frame` on Linux** (2026-10-20, theme2's 17:27 ruling, Q3). `frame(id)` of an inline run inside a text (an `a` or a `span` in a paragraph) reads the native presenter's fragments on Apple (`InlineGeometry::frame`); Linux's reader (`host/linux/src/presenter/range_frame.rs`, which answers `rangeFrame` from the painted field) answers it unavailable. A reader would union the run's glyph boxes from the paragraph the walk painted (`walk.text`, by the run's source range), placed and bounded as the field's range is, published beside the fields.

- Game baseline D2 (LLP 1139.000): use one `gpu-dev` wasm with Off, Save and FreshGame reachable and call `Sim::paranoid` before every world's first tick. Keep release elimination through `option_env!`, require reported-mode/reconstruction evidence, and retain the Off-wasm-in-Save negative control. Completed continuous reuse measured 192 s total; raw hot-swap reuse and the latency oracle remain open.
- **Two JS dev loops of one GPU app on one target/ race on its GPU cache** (2026-10-22, the design-tool.test concurrency review, review2 04:42). `host/web-js/build.mjs` keeps `<target>/web-js-gpu/<app name>`, removes it when stale and rebuilds it in place, then copies it into the build's stage. A second loop of the same app on the same target (two checkouts sharing CARGO_TARGET_DIR, or two runs at once) can remove or rewrite it while the first copies it. The JS stage, the resident dist and the inspect table are each per loop since 239192d388; this cache is shared by design (a warm rebuild skips the GPU build), so its fix is a build into a stage of its own renamed over the cache under a lock, as `replaceBuild` does for dist. No app in the design tool's tests has a GPU crate.
- **The variant gallery draws a broken-image icon for an image prop** (2026-10-22, the design tool's second hands-on pass, finding 8). A library component's string prop that feeds an image source (PostCard's and AtCard's avatar and image) gets its name as its placeholder, as every string prop does (contract/cli/src/gallery), so the browser shows a broken image. The fix is a placeholder by use: a string prop that flows into an `image` src gets a neutral data: image (or the gallery hides an image whose src is a placeholder). Text props read as their names, which is fine.
- **"Back to the default" first proposes with a param's short name and gets a 400 just after a rebuild** (2026-10-22, the second hands-on pass, finding 9). Right after an Apply that rebuilds, the tool's proposal for `unset-param` names `PostCard.surface`; the design server, its param table not yet read again for the new plan, refuses it (400), and the next answer's re-read proposes it again and succeeds. It shows only in the console. The fix is the server's: keep the last plan's param names while the new ones are read (as `learnParams` keeps them for a selection), or the tool's: hold a param proposal until the plan's params are read.
- **`clock settle` never settles on the design tool, and its jumps wait on the tool's long poll** (2026-10-22, the design-tool test timing work, review2). The tool's `follow` task (`apps/design-tool/app.contract`, `after(1, advance)`) re-arms on every answer of the `changes` long poll, which the server holds 25 s (`scripts/tamber-server.mjs` POLL_MS). So `clock settle` runs its 16 rounds at about 25 s each and returns `settled: false` (about 400 s). Any jump that crosses a due timer also waits up to 20 s on that in-flight poll first (`host/web-js/agent.js`, the "what was in flight lands before a timer fires" rule, calendar F10). Every agent drive of the tool hits this. The design-tool tests now avoid settle there. "An agent's write" still pays the 20 s on each highlight fade (387 s), and so do the scrub tests' `+6000 real` windows. Fix options: the jump waits only on requests its own stops sent, not on a held long poll; or the tool marks its poll as background, so settle and jumps do not count it. It changes a deliberate agent rule, so it needs the lead's call and an owner.
- **"commit refuses …" got an ECONNRESET from the proxied `tamber serve` once under load** (2026-10-22, the design-tool test split, review2). In one of four `--parallel=4` runs, the harness proxy's fetch to the design server was reset (`scripts/design-tool-harness.mjs` `proxied`), and the drive failed. The test body is unchanged, and two later runs passed. If it recurs, find out whether the server crashed or closed a keep-alive socket under load. The run's log (machine-local) is ~/x2theme/logs/2026-10-22-dt-split-econnreset.log. Its excerpt: at `design-tool-harness.mjs:104`, the proxy's `fetch` to `http://127.0.0.1:<serve>/__tamber` failed with `TypeError: The socket connection was closed unexpectedly` (`ECONNRESET`, errno 0), then the test's drive (`design-tool.test.mjs:433`) failed. The case's run was 29 s, the whole run 479 s, and machine load peaked near 39.
- **Android's `iframe` ignores `sandbox`** (2026-10-21, LLP 1020.002 §10). `host/android/canvas/src/main/java/dev/exact/canvas/Natives.kt:513-519,693-698` makes a `WebView` with `javaScriptEnabled` and `allowFileAccess` and calls `loadUrl(src)`; nothing reads `sandbox`, so a sandboxed frame runs unsandboxed there, the fail-open class LLP 1020 §3.3 removed on the other hosts. Refuse a sandboxed `iframe` on Android by name, or serve it as Apple's wrapper does.
